# CoBacTyPhu — Project Status & Session Handoff

Read this file first in any new session on this project. It's the authoritative, up-to-date snapshot — more current than any AI memory of past sessions. Update it whenever a task's status changes.

## What this project is

**Cờ Tỷ Phú** — a multiplayer Monopoly-style board game. React frontend, Node/Express backend, Supabase (Postgres + Auth), 2–6 players, Socket.IO for realtime gameplay (now implemented, not yet connected to a live database). Working directory: `C:\Users\DELL\Downloads\CoBacTyPhu`.

## Documentation map (`docs/`, read in this order for full context)

1. `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` — core rules, turn/room/economy structure. Every rule tagged `[CONFIRMED]`/`[PROPOSED]`/`[OPEN DESIGN DECISION]`.
2. `BOARD_SPECIFICATION.md` — physical reference board reading, plus proposed new mechanics, **all four now shipped**: Flash Auction **[CONFIRMED]** V1 "Strategic Denial" (100% opening bid, entry fee, Near-Miss reward); Hostile Acquisition **[CONFIRMED]**, Phase 14 (unilateral 2.0×, no turn gate, no defense window); Rent Risk Choice **[CONFIRMED]**, 2026-08-21 (owner picks Standard/Gamble, 50/50 double-or-zero, 15s timeout to Standard — see "Backlog batch" below). Comeback Mechanics/Late-Game Escalation/Special Event Spaces remain `[PROPOSED]`, untouched.
3. `ADAPTIVE_BOARD_DESIGN.md` — Small (36 tiles, 2–4p) / Large (44 tiles, 5–6p) board sizing. **Fully approved**, locked numbers.
4. `GAME_STATE_MACHINE.md` — full state machine, timers, concurrency, idempotency design.
5. `ECONOMY_SPECIFICATION.md` — closed-economy model, finite Bank, zero-sum invariant.
6. `DATABASE_DESIGN.md` — 13-table Postgres schema, RLS strategy, ERD.
7. `API_CONTRACT.md` — REST endpoints (rooms + auth + game reads). Source of truth for every REST route actually implemented — read before touching `backend/src/api/`.
8. `SOCKET_CONTRACT.md` — **[SUPERSEDED — see `WEBSOCKET_API.md`]**. Kept for background reasoning only.
9. `WEBSOCKET_API.md` — **the real, implemented Socket.IO contract** (P09). Generic envelope design: `C2S_GAME_ACTION`/`S2C_STATE_UPDATE`/`S2C_ACTION_REJECTED`, plus `C2S_JOIN_ROOM`/`S2C_ROOM_JOINED`. Read this, not `SOCKET_CONTRACT.md`, before touching `backend/src/infrastructure/websocket/`.
10. `SECURITY_DESIGN.md` — 15-threat model + 4 real gaps found during review (see "Known gaps" below).
11. `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` — 57-task backlog. **Increasingly out of step with reality past Phase 07** — several implemented tasks (P09/P10) were driven by ad hoc "PXX-TXX" task requests that don't correspond to this document's own numbering. Treat this file (`PROJECT_STATUS.md`) as authoritative over the plan's phase/task numbers from Phase 08 onward.
12. `PROJECT_STATUS.md` — this file.

### Phase 07 (State machine) — complete
Core state machine, timers, idempotency layer, and Flash Auction were already logged (P07-T01–T04, see below). Since then, in the same session:
- **Event Card engine** (`backend/src/domain/eventDictionary.js`, `backend/src/engine/eventResolver.js`) — a "Decision & Moment System": `INSTANT` cards (flat payout) and `CHOICE` cards (options, one with a `PROBABILITY` intent). **Additive, not a replacement** — the older `domain/eventCard.js` (flat single-effect model, matches `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §13 exactly) still exists, untouched, unwired. Two parallel `EventCard` shapes now exist in the codebase by deliberate choice, not oversight.
- **Full wiring into `turnMachine.js`**: `DRAWING_CARD` now really draws from `GameState.eventDeck` and resolves INSTANT cards immediately or transitions to a new `AWAITING_EVENT_CHOICE` phase (`GameState.pendingEventCardId`) for CHOICE cards. `AWAITING_PURCHASE`'s decline path now charges `calculateAuctionFee` and starts a Flash Auction (`FLASH_AUCTION_ACTIVE`, `GameState.pendingAuction`) if the player can afford it. A new `applyIntents()` helper in `turnMachine.js` is the translation layer between `ADD_MONEY`/`REMOVE_MONEY`/`TRANSFER_PROPERTY` intents (what `auction.js`/`eventResolver.js` return) and real `applyTransaction()`/property-ownership calls — neither engine module applies its own intents, this bridge does.
- **Flash Auction full lifecycle**: `PLACE_BID`/`FOLD_AUCTION` actions in `FLASH_AUCTION_ACTIVE`; folding an auction down to ≤1 active bidders resolves it immediately (a new rule, not previously documented, supplementary to the timer-based ending); `AUCTION_TIMEOUT` forces resolution regardless of bidder count (the state-machine half of the timer-based ending — see the gap below).
- **Timer wiring**: `timers.js` now has a `FLASH_AUCTION_ACTIVE: 15` entry and a `buildDefaultAction` branch returning `{ type: 'AUCTION_TIMEOUT' }`. **15s is a placeholder, not `GAME_STATE_MACHINE.md` §0's real two-number design** (`FLASH_AUCTION_WINDOW_SECONDS = 12` initial + `FLASH_AUCTION_BID_RESET_SECONDS = 3` reset-per-bid) — `TimerManager`'s model is one fixed duration per phase with no "reset on activity" concept, so neither real number is actually implementable yet without extending that model.
- New `GameState` fields: `pendingAuction`, `pendingEventCardId`, `eventDeck`. New phases: `FLASH_AUCTION_ACTIVE`, `AWAITING_EVENT_CHOICE`.
- **Known gap inherited by everything downstream**: `resolveDrawingCard`/`eventDeck` uses one shared deck regardless of `chance` vs `fortune` tile type — `eventDictionary.js` has no deck-membership split (unlike `eventCard.js`'s `EVENT_CARD_DECKS`).

### Phase 08 (Room/Lobby REST) — complete, in-memory only
`backend/src/infrastructure/repositories/roomRepository.js` (in-memory `Map`, deep-clones on every read/write), `backend/src/api/{routes,controllers}/room.routes.js` / `room.controller.js`. Implements `API_CONTRACT.md`'s Rooms section except `leave`:
- `POST /api/v1/rooms` — create, host auto-joins as first player.
- `POST /api/v1/rooms/:code/join` — **by join code, not room id** (privacy: doesn't leak room existence by id).
- `GET /api/v1/rooms/:id` — 404 merges "not found" and "not a member" (never 403), per `API_CONTRACT.md`'s stated privacy rule.
- `PATCH /api/v1/rooms/:id/ready` — `{ ready: boolean }`, **an explicit target value, not a toggle** (idempotency reasoning: a toggle isn't safe against retried/duplicated requests). Recomputes whether all non-host players are ready and drives `Room.status` through `stateMachine/roomMachine.js`'s `transitionRoom()` (`ALL_PLAYERS_READY` / `PLAYER_UNREADY`) — `roomMachine.js` itself explicitly delegates "is everyone ready" computation to its caller; this is that caller, wired in for the first time.
- `POST /api/v1/rooms/:id/start` — host-only (`403 NOT_HOST`), validates player count `[2,6]` (`409 INVALID_PLAYER_COUNT`) and all-non-host-ready (`409 NOT_ALL_READY`, re-checked directly against the player list, not trusted from `status` alone), then `transitionRoom` twice (`ready_check → starting → in_progress`, matching the real `ROOM_STATUSES` enum — **not** `"PLAYING"`, which doesn't exist in the confirmed schema). Constructs the initial `GameState` via `initializeGameState()` (shuffled turn order, `STARTING_BALANCE=1500`/`BANK_RESERVE_INITIAL=20000`, both `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` "BALANCE TBD" placeholders) and attaches it to the room record. **`properties: []`, always** — no real board tile data is reachable (see Phase 10 below), so no property rows can be constructed yet.
- Auth: real `auth/authMiddleware.js`, mounted in `app.js` only if a JWT secret is configured (`createApp({ jwtSecret })` — gracefully skips mounting `/api/v1/rooms` otherwise, doesn't crash).
- No `gameRepository` — `GameState` lives bolted onto the room record in the same in-memory `Map`. Works today; will need a real split once persistence exists.

### Phase 09 (Socket.IO) — complete, wired into `server.js`
`backend/src/infrastructure/websocket/socketServer.js`. Implements `WEBSOCKET_API.md` (the new contract that supersedes `SOCKET_CONTRACT.md` — see that document's own top-of-file notice for the full reasoning): `createSocketAuthMiddleware` (JWT via `socket.handshake.auth.token`, reuses `verifyJwt.js`), `handleJoinRoom` (`C2S_JOIN_ROOM` → `S2C_ROOM_JOINED`/`S2C_ACTION_REJECTED`), `handleGameAction` (`C2S_GAME_ACTION` → `applyWithIdempotency`/`transitionTurn` → `S2C_STATE_UPDATE` broadcast or `S2C_ACTION_REJECTED` to sender). `server.js` now does `http.createServer(app)` + `initSocketServer(server, ...)` + `server.listen(...)`, sharing one HTTP server between REST and sockets.

**Two real bugs found and fixed while wiring this, not shipped**:
1. **Id-space mismatch.** `socket.user.id` (the JWT subject) is `profiles.id`; every engine/state-machine actor comparison (`auction.js`'s `activeBidders`, `turnMachine.js`'s player lookups) is keyed on `PlayerGameState.id` (`game_players.id`) — a different value. `handleGameAction` now resolves `socket.user.id` → the matching `PlayerGameState.id` via `gameState.players` before injecting it as the action's `playerId`, rather than injecting the raw auth id (which would have silently failed every `FLASH_AUCTION_ACTIVE` action).
2. **Server boot crash with no `SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET` configured** — true in this environment right now. `initSocketServer` used to call `createSocketAuthMiddleware()` unconditionally, which throws on a falsy secret; this crashed `node src/server.js` outright, unnoticed for two prior tasks because every unit test always supplied a secret. Found only by actually booting the server, not by the test suite. Fixed: `initSocketServer` now returns a real (but connection-inert) `io` instance instead of throwing when no secret is configured — same "missing infra makes the feature inert, not the whole process unable to start" posture as `createApp`'s `jwtSecret` handling.

### Phase 10 (Database & Persistence) — complete
`backend/src/infrastructure/database/supabaseClient.js` (service-role client, `null` until both `SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` are set), `boardRepository.js` (`fetchBoardTiles`, real `group_id`/`rent_table` columns, not the guessed `color_group`/`rent_levels`).

- **P10-T02 (Dynamic Persistence)**: replaced the in-memory room `Map` with a real Supabase-backed `roomRepository.js` (`rooms`/`room_players`, `profiles` joined in JS for display names) and a new `gameRepository.js`. Deliberately hybrid, not Supabase-per-action — GameState lives in an in-memory hot `Map` during play, durably snapshotted to `games`/`game_state_snapshots` only at the three schema-approved trigger points (`game_state_snapshots.reason`'s own `CHECK` only allows `turn_end`/`bankruptcy`/`game_over`), via a pure `deriveSnapshotReason()` helper — no `turnMachine.js` changes needed. Idempotency cache moved to an in-process `Map` in `socketServer.js`, never persisted (ephemeral by design, `GAME_STATE_MACHINE.md` §6). Open gap: a fresh game's *first* snapshot has no approved `reason` either; uses `'turn_end'` as the pragmatic closest fit.
- **P10-T03 (Reconnection)**: new `C2S_RECONNECT` — distinct from `C2S_JOIN_ROOM` by design (`GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §23's own phase split: lobby rejoin stays REST, in-progress reconnect needs a separate socket mechanism) — resyncs `GameState` and flips presence online; `handleDisconnect` flags offline (never removes) and broadcasts `S2C_PLAYER_DISCONNECTED`. Presence lives in its own in-process `Map`, never persisted (`DATABASE_DESIGN.md`'s ephemeral-state note excludes it explicitly). `WEBSOCKET_API.md` gained a new §5, closing 2 of its own previously-flagged open items.
- **P10-T04 (Turn Timeouts)**: wired the already-built-but-never-connected `stateMachine/timers.js` (`TimerManager`/`buildDefaultAction`, from Phase 07) into `socketServer.js` — a task brief asked for a *new* timer utility, one already existed, unused. New `deadlineAt` field on `S2C_STATE_UPDATE`. One mechanism covers both "AFK while online" and "genuinely disconnected" (`GAME_STATE_MACHINE.md` §5: the phase timeout fires regardless of connection status) — `RECONNECT_GRACE_SECONDS`/AFK-streak tracking deliberately not built (still `[PROPOSED]`). Real bug caught only by running the suite: wiring a real `TimerManager` leaked real 15–30s timers into the test process (<1s → ~65s) until every test in `socketServer.test.js` used `node:test`'s `mock.timers`.
- 320/320 tests passing by end of Phase 10; real boot smoke-tests passed throughout (`node src/server.js`, with and without Supabase configured).

### Phase 11 (Frontend + full-stack wiring) — in progress

**Numbering note**: task briefs this phase repeatedly self-labeled "P11-T02"/"P11-T03" for genuinely different tasks (three collisions total, matching `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s own noted numbering drift). Entries below are in session order, described by content rather than by the unreliable number.

- **Network/state layer**: added `zustand` + `socket.io-client`. New `frontend/src/store/gameStore.js` (field shapes mirror `WEBSOCKET_API.md` exactly: `connectionStatus`/`roomState`/`currentGameState`/`stateVersion`/`transactions`/`deadlineAt`/`offlinePlayerIds`/`lastError`/`staticBoard`), `network/api.js` (REST client), `network/socketClient.js` (a singleton, not a hook — wires every `S2C_*` event into the store; correctly emits `C2S_RECONNECT`, not `C2S_JOIN_ROOM`, on a transport reconnect — the task brief didn't call this out, but §5 requires it). `features/lobby/LobbyDiagnostic.jsx` — minimal create/join-room + connection-status + raw-state view. No router added (none exists in this project by design, per finding #8).
- **E2E smoke test** (`backend/scripts/e2e-smoke.js`, `npm run e2e:smoke`): a real 2-user client simulation (`MIN_PLAYERS=2` means one simulated client can never itself reach `S2C_STATE_UPDATE`) — auth → REST create/join/ready/start → two sockets → real `C2S_GAME_ACTION START_TURN` → asserts a real `S2C_STATE_UPDATE` (`players[]`/`phase`/`currentTurnIndex` — `GameState` has no `turn` field, unlike the brief's assumption). Reusable/idempotent: fixed test accounts `e2e-smoke-{host,guest}@cobactyphu.dev` / `E2eSmokeTest!12345`, sign-in-first-then-signUp-fallback. Chased three sequential real environment blockers to ground truth across several runs rather than declaring success early: email confirmation required → resolved by the user; then `unsupported_algorithm` (see next item); then `board_tiles` unseeded (see "Blocked" below) — each diagnosed from the actual failure, not assumed.
- **Auth algorithm fix (HS256 → ES256/JWKS)**: this Supabase project signs JWTs asymmetrically; `auth/verifyJwt.js` was built assuming HS256 (that file's own header comment had already flagged this exact scenario as the thing to watch for). Added `verifyJwtAsymmetric` + new `auth/jwks.js` (fetch/cache, native `node:crypto`, no new dependency — rejected the brief's `supabase.auth.getUser()`-per-request "preferred approach" as contradicting `API_CONTRACT.md`'s own already-approved "signature check, not a round-trip" rule) alongside the original `verifyJwt`, untouched. Zero breaking changes, verified empirically — every pre-existing test still passes unmodified; `createAuthMiddleware`/`createSocketAuthMiddleware`/`createApp`'s `jwtSecret` param now accepts either the original bare string or a `{ secret }`/`{ getKey }` config object. `server.js` prefers JWKS whenever `SUPABASE_URL` is set. Confirmed fixed against the real live project, not just tests — re-running `e2e-smoke.js` got past auth entirely and hit a different, deeper (already-tracked) blocker instead.
- **Board UI**: new `frontend/src/features/board/` (`GameBoard.jsx`/`BoardTile.jsx` + CSS Modules, no Tailwind, reusing `index.css`'s existing custom properties). Corrected the brief's assumed classic 40-tile layout — this project's own `ADAPTIVE_BOARD_DESIGN.md` locks 36 (Small, 2–4p)/44 (Large, 5–6p) tiles instead; `computeGridPosition()` is board-size-agnostic, derived from the real corner positions in `boards.sql` (0/9/18/27 Small, 0/11/22/33 Large) — "40" appears nowhere in the code. Verified live in browser via computed styles (not just visual inspection): a real 10×10 grid, all four corners at their exact expected coordinates, responsive at 375px (scrolls in its own container, page never gains horizontal overflow).
- **Static board data endpoint + seed deployed**: new `GET /api/v1/boards/:boardId`, reusing the existing startup-cached `fetchBoardTiles` result via `app.set('boardTilesByBoard', ...)` rather than querying Supabase again per request (`board_tiles` is static for a server's whole lifetime). Mounted behind the same auth middleware as `/rooms` — corrected the brief's "doesn't necessarily need auth" framing against `API_CONTRACT.md`'s actual "every endpoint except `/health`" rule. Frontend fetch trigger corrected too: the brief said `S2C_ROOM_JOINED` carries `board_id` — it doesn't (`rooms` has no such column; board size is only ever resolved at `start_game`), so `socketClient.js` fetches on first seeing `gameState.boardId` inside `S2C_STATE_UPDATE` instead, guarded against refetching on every subsequent action broadcast. `GameBoard.jsx` now reads `staticBoard` from the store exclusively; `mockGameState.json` deleted, per the task's own instruction.
- **`board_tiles` seed applied to live Supabase (2026-08-18)**: `backend/supabase/seed/boards.sql` executed against project `qpbyfgzujwzueprzuurk` via `npx supabase db query --file ... --linked`. Verified live: `GET /api/v1/boards/small` → `200 OK`, `tiles[].length === 36`. This was the **final blocker for Phase 11 Static Data**. `properties[]` (populated from tile data at `POST /start`) and the full board UI are now functional end to end.
- 360/360 backend tests passing (320 + 40 across this phase); frontend `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean throughout; both endpoints verified against the live Supabase project directly, not just mocked tests.

### Backend slice — property economy wired (`POST_ACTIONS`, `SECURITY_DESIGN.md` gap #1, 4 of 6 at the time)
`SECURITY_DESIGN.md`'s "Gaps found" #1 originally listed six `POST_ACTIONS` sub-actions with no Socket.IO event contract: `build_house`/`sell_house`/`mortgage`/`unmortgage`/`propose_trade`/`respond_trade`. `BUILD_HOUSE` was closed first (below); `SELL_HOUSE`/`MORTGAGE`/`UNMORTGAGE` closed in the same session, shortly after. **The trade system (see its own section below, same day) closed the remaining two — gap #1 is now fully closed, 6 of 6.**

**`SELL_HOUSE`/`MORTGAGE`/`UNMORTGAGE`** (same session as `BUILD_HOUSE`, immediately after):
- `handleSellHouse` mirrors `handleBuildHouse` exactly but inverted: refunds half `houseCost` (floored, `calculateSellHouse`), decrements `upgradeLevel`. The even-*sell* rule requires selling from whichever group member is currently at the group's **highest** `upgradeLevel` — the mirror of even-*build*'s lowest-level requirement — rejected as `UNEVEN_SELL`, a code deliberately distinct from `UNEVEN_BUILD` rather than reused, so client-side error branching can tell "can't build here" from "can't sell here" apart.
- `handleMortgage`'s precondition is **stricter than `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §12's original text** ("this property's own `upgradeLevel = 0`") — implemented per explicit instruction as "no property anywhere in the target's color group may have `upgradeLevel > 0`" (all houses in the group must be sold first), matching real Monopoly rules more closely than the original per-property-only reading. `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §12 updated in place, marked `[REVISED 2026-08-18]` — same category as the Flash Auction V0→V1 revision (finding #14): a deliberate rule change made on explicit instruction, flagged and reflected back into the design doc, not silently substituted either direction.
- `handleUnmortgage` is the one group-agnostic action of the four — only checks this property's own `mortgaged` flag and the payer's funds (`mortgageValue × 1.1`, ceiled, reuses the existing `INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE` code rather than a new `INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS` one, same reuse decision `BUILD_HOUSE` already made for the identical underlying condition).
- New shared `groupHoldingsFor()` helper in `turnMachine.js`, factored out of `handleBuildHouse`'s own inline group-lookup once a third caller needed the identical shape — `handleBuildHouse` itself now calls it too, no behavior change.
- `InvalidBuildError` **renamed to `InvalidPropertyActionError`** — it now covers all four property actions, not just builds; a mortgage rejection surfacing as `InvalidBuildError` would have misdescribed itself in code and logs. Every reference (`turnMachine.js`, `socketServer.js`'s `errorCodeFor`, `WEBSOCKET_API.md`, this file) updated together, not left half-migrated.
- New error codes: `NO_HOUSES_TO_SELL`, `UNEVEN_SELL`, `ALREADY_MORTGAGED`, `GROUP_HAS_HOUSES`, `NOT_MORTGAGED`. `NOT_OWNER`/`INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE` reused from the existing taxonomy.
- Tests: 15 new (`turnMachine.test.js`) — shared phase guard, happy paths, all new `InvalidPropertyActionError` reasons for each action, both even-sell directions, and both the self-has-houses and groupmate-has-houses branches of `GROUP_HAS_HOUSES`. One test bug caught by actually running the suite before concluding (an `UNEVEN_SELL` test used a property with 0 houses, which correctly hit `NO_HOUSES_TO_SELL` first instead — fixed by giving it houses to sell that were still short of the group max). 386/386 backend tests passing, no regressions.
- Turn-ownership gap (flagged below `BUILD_HOUSE`'s own writeup) explicitly **not** touched here either, per explicit instruction — same pre-existing gap, still open, still spans every turn-scoped action.

### `BUILD_HOUSE` wired (`POST_ACTIONS`, `SECURITY_DESIGN.md` gap #1, closed first)

- **`turnMachine.js`**: `VALID_ACTIONS_BY_PHASE.POST_ACTIONS` now accepts `BUILD_HOUSE` (`payload: { propertyId }`, current-turn player only) alongside `END_TURN`. Found `economy/propertyEconomy.js` already had `calculateBuildHouse()` built and tested, just never called — that file's own header explicitly declines to own business-rule validation ("belongs to a later orchestration/validation layer, not here"); new `handleBuildHouse` is that layer, validating (in order) property-is-a-house-eligible-tile, ownership, the upgrade-level ceiling (`MAX_UPGRADE_LEVEL`), full-color-group ownership with no mortgaged member, and the even-build rule (`GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §12, **[PROPOSED]**) before charging `houseCost` and incrementing `upgradeLevel`. Unlike `END_TURN`, a successful build does **not** advance the turn (§7: post-actions "may happen in any order... not a forced single slot") — stays in `POST_ACTIONS`.
- **House/hotel supply scarcity deliberately NOT enforced** — `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §12 flags `HOUSE_SUPPLY_TOTAL`/`HOTEL_SUPPLY_TOTAL` as a still-`[OPEN DESIGN DECISION]`, explicitly suggesting unlimited-supply as one legitimate default ("many digital adaptations skip it for simplicity"). Applied that default rather than guessing or blocking on it; still open in "Open design decisions" below, unchanged by this pass.
- **Full-group-ownership check on a tile with no `groupId`** (still true for all real seed content — "Property color-group assignment" remains open, see below) **correctly fails closed**, same graceful-false-on-missing-group-data convention `calculateRent.js`'s `hasGroupBonus` already uses — not treated as a trivial group-of-one. So `BUILD_HOUSE` is fully implemented and tested but, like the rest of the property system, not yet exercisable against real board content until color-group assignment is decided.
- New error class (`turnMachine.js`, same standing as `auction.js`'s `InvalidBidError`; originally named `InvalidBuildError`, **renamed to `InvalidPropertyActionError` once `SELL_HOUSE`/`MORTGAGE`/`UNMORTGAGE` joined it** — see below) with `.reason` codes `NOT_OWNER`/`INCOMPLETE_GROUP`/`GROUP_MORTGAGED`/`UNEVEN_BUILD`/`MAX_UPGRADE_LEVEL`; `INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE` reused from the existing taxonomy. `socketServer.js`'s `errorCodeFor()` and `WEBSOCKET_API.md`'s `actionType`/`errorCode` tables both updated to match — no `socketServer.js` routing changes needed beyond that one line, since `handleGameAction` already dispatches every `actionType` generically.
- **Flagged, not fixed (pre-existing, not introduced here)**: `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §27 states turn-scoped actions ("roll, buy, build, mortgage, end-turn") should "only be accepted from the socket matching `players[currentTurnIndex].userId`" — but neither `handleGameAction` nor any existing turn-scoped handler (`handleBuyProperty`, `handleJailAction`, and now `handleBuildHouse`) actually cross-checks the sender's resolved identity against `getCurrentPlayer(gameState)`; they all act "as" whoever the current player is, regardless of which participant's socket actually sent the message. `handleBuildHouse` follows the same established convention as its siblings for consistency rather than unilaterally hardening only the new action — this is a gap spanning every turn-scoped action, not something specific to `BUILD_HOUSE`, and is a candidate for its own dedicated pass (a `handleGameAction`-level check, not a per-handler one), not a silent fix bundled into this one.
- Tests: 11 new (`turnMachine.test.js`) — phase guard, happy path (charge + `upgradeLevel` + stays in `POST_ACTIONS`), all five rejection reasons (named `InvalidBuildError` at the time; see the rename above), and both even-build directions (rejects building further ahead, allows catching up). 371/371 backend tests passing at the time, no regressions.

### Backend slice — Trade System (`PROPOSE`/`COUNTER`/`ACCEPT`/`REJECT`/`CANCEL_TRADE`, closes `SECURITY_DESIGN.md` gap #1's last 2, `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §10 `[CONFIRMED]`)

Same day, immediately after the property-economy slice above. This one was a genuine "Phase 12" full-system request (`domain/trade.js`, `engine/trade.js`, `stateMachine/tradeMachine.js`), not a single-action addition — three real architecture questions came up before any code was written, and were resolved via `AskUserQuestion` rather than guessed, matching this project's own established "reserve stop-and-ask for genuine architecture-level forks" rule (see "Working style" below) precisely:

1. **Trade vs. the turn-phase gate — independent, confirmed.** Every other action is implicitly "the current-turn player, during a specific phase." Trade is fundamentally different (either player, any time) — confirmed to bypass `turnMachine.js`/`VALID_ACTIONS_BY_PHASE` entirely, not gated to the current player's own `POST_ACTIONS` window. New `stateMachine/tradeMachine.js`'s `applyTradeAction` is a second `transitionFn` `socketServer.js`'s `handleGameAction` dispatches to (same `C2S_GAME_ACTION` envelope, same `applyWithIdempotency` wrapper — confirmed genuinely generic before relying on it) whenever `actionType` is one of the five trade actions. `turnMachine.js` itself ended up completely untouched (see point 3 below for why the first draft briefly touched it, and why that was reverted).
2. **Asset locking — computed on the fly from `GameState.pendingTrades`, confirmed.** No `DATABASE_DESIGN.md` schema change, no `locked` column — every property/amount of money already offered in another active trade is unavailable to a new proposal, checked fresh at both propose- and accept-time. Same precedent `GameState.pendingAuction` already set.
3. **Expiration — lazy check, confirmed, not a new timer.** `stateMachine/timers.js`'s `TimerManager` only supports one active timer per room (its own documented invariant) — can't express several independently-expiring concurrent trades in one room without reworking an already-shipped module. `pruneExpiredTrades()` runs at the top of every trade action instead; an expired trade someone tries to act on reads as `TRADE_NOT_FOUND`.

**A real design bug caught only by running the tests, not by review** — the same "run tests before concluding" discipline this project has flagged before (findings #12/#19) caught it again: the first draft of `ACCEPT_TRADE`'s settlement reused `turnMachine.js`'s `applyIntents` (`ADD_MONEY`/`REMOVE_MONEY`), which is **hard-coded Bank-mediated** (every current caller — auction settlement, event-card payouts — is player-vs-Bank). Using it for a trade payment would have silently routed a direct player-to-player payment through two separate Bank-mediated legs (technically netting out the same balances, but producing two ledger rows through the Bank instead of one direct row, and violating `DATABASE_DESIGN.md` §12's "one row is one complete, atomic money movement" philosophy). Caught by a failing test assertion (`transactions.length`: expected 1, got 2), not by inspection. Fixed by following the *correct* existing precedent instead — `turnMachine.js`'s own `settleDebt()` (rent payments) calls `economy/applyTransaction.js` directly with the real `fromPlayerId`/`toPlayerId`; `tradeMachine.js`'s `ACCEPT_TRADE` now does the same, one `applyTransaction` call per money direction (up to 2) plus a direct `properties[]` update per property transfer. `turnMachine.js`'s `applyIntents` was briefly exported for the first (wrong) design and reverted back to module-private once this was fixed — `turnMachine.js` ships from this slice completely unchanged.

**Ruleset, confirmed on explicit instruction** (`GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §10 marked `[CONFIRMED]`, superseding its own `[OPEN DESIGN DECISION]`): strictly 1-vs-1, properties + money only (no jail-free cards), no conditional trades, counters allowed up to `MAX_COUNTER_DEPTH` (5), `TRADE_EXPIRY_SECONDS` = 60. `Trade.status` only ever reads `'PROPOSED'` for an entry actually sitting in `pendingTrades` today — the other five statuses (`COUNTERED`/`ACCEPTED`/`REJECTED`/`CANCELLED`/`EXPIRED`) are terminal and imply removal, not a value ever persisted anywhere (no `trades` table exists — kept on the type for schema completeness, per the brief's own explicit data-model requirement).

**Corrections made silently, flagged here** (unambiguous given already-established convention, not genuine forks): trade offers address properties by `properties.id` (game-scoped ownership row), not the brief's suggested `tileIndex` — matching `BUILD_HOUSE`/`SELL_HOUSE`/`MORTGAGE`/`UNMORTGAGE`'s existing convention exactly, avoiding a second parallel property-addressing scheme in the same file. Split into `domain/trade.js` (pure data shape) + `engine/trade.js` (pure logic/validation) rather than the brief's single `tradeManager.js` — matches `property.js`+propertyEconomy.js's/`auction.js`'s existing domain/engine split.

**Explicitly not touched, per instruction**: the pre-existing turn-ownership enforcement gap (`SECURITY_DESIGN.md`'s "Known gaps" #5, below) — out of scope for this PR by the brief's own stated constraint. Note it's genuinely orthogonal here: trade actions have their *own* actor-authorization model (`NOT_TARGET`/`NOT_PROPOSER`, checked against the resolved sender identity, since trade was never "implicitly the current-turn player" to begin with) — this slice didn't inherit the gap, it just doesn't fix it for the *other*, pre-existing turn-scoped actions either.

**Notable, not fixed (already-known gap, now concretely reachable)**: finding #15/#20's `applyTransaction.js` `idempotencyKey` (`${gameId}:${stateVersion}`) collision — a trade with money offered on *both* sides calls `applyTransaction` twice within one external action, before the idempotency layer's single end-of-action `stateVersion` bump, the exact scenario that gap describes. Previously only latent (via auction Near-Miss settlement, never exercised by a real persistence layer); now reachable through ordinary 1-vs-1 trade gameplay. Still not fixed here — same standing as before, tracked, not blocking.

Tests: 40 new — `domain/trade.test.js` (8, `createTrade` validation), `engine/trade.test.js` (15, including the brief's own explicitly-requested double-spend coverage: a property or amount of money already locked in one active trade correctly can't be offered again in a second), `stateMachine/tradeMachine.test.js` (15, full orchestration incl. two independent concurrent trades resolving independently, lazy expiry), `socketServer.test.js` (2, proving the actual `handleGameAction` routing works end-to-end and is genuinely phase-independent, not just the pure functions in isolation). 426/426 backend tests passing, no regressions.

### Frontend slice — Lobby UI + conditional view routing (P11-T05), and two real CORS bugs found doing it

**`frontend/src/features/lobby/Lobby.jsx`** (new) + **`App.jsx`** (three-way conditional: `LobbyDiagnostic` while no room joined yet → `Lobby` while waiting/ready-check → `GameBoard` once started). `network/api.js` already had `getRoom`/`setReady`/`startGame` built and unused — same recurring "pre-built-but-unwired" pattern as `calculateBuildHouse`/`TimerManager` before them (finding #22) — no changes needed there.

- **`roomState` (Zustand, mirrors `S2C_ROOM_JOINED`) only ever carries `members` as playerId *strings*** — no `displayName`/`isHost`/`isReady`. `Lobby.jsx` fetches the richer roster straight from `GET /api/v1/rooms/:id` (`API_CONTRACT.md`'s real shape: `players: [{ playerId, displayName, isReady, isHost }]`) as local component state instead, on a 3s poll while mounted — see the next point for why polling, not a push.
- **Real, previously-undiscovered gap**: `API_CONTRACT.md` itself already flagged that a REST ready/start mutation "should" push a lightweight Socket.IO notification to other lobby members ("payload TBD in the Socket.IO contract phase") — that event was never actually built. `WEBSOCKET_API.md` has no such event today. So a ready-toggle or the host's Start click is invisible to *other* connected clients until they poll or reconnect. Not fixed in this slice (a backend/socket-contract change, out of a frontend component's scope) — `Lobby.jsx`'s polling is the flagged, pragmatic stand-in, and the host's own "Start" click locally patches `roomState.roomStatus` so at least the *acting* player's own view transitions immediately rather than sitting stale after a real success.
- **Two real CORS bugs found only by actually clicking through a real browser** — same "smoke-test the real runtime" lesson Phase 09 already taught this project (finding #19), now extended to "a real browser specifically," not just a booted Node process: `node --test` and `e2e-smoke.js` (a Node.js script) don't enforce CORS at all, so neither ever could have caught this.
  1. `app.js` had **zero CORS headers configured, ever** — every REST route has always required Bearer auth (never relied on cookies/CORS for security), but a real browser blocks the cross-origin fetch (frontend `:5173`, backend `:5000`) at the OPTIONS preflight, before the request — and its Authorization header — ever reaches Express. This means **room creation/joining from a real browser has likely never actually worked**, since Phase 08 first built it — invisible until this session's first real click-through.
  2. Socket.IO's own transport (`new Server(httpServer)`, `socketServer.js`) handles `/socket.io/*` itself, separately from Express's middleware — and defaults to *rejecting* cross-origin handshakes in v4 unless `cors` is explicitly passed. Same root cause, a second, separate fix (`{ cors: { origin: true, methods: ['GET','POST'] } }`).
  - Both fixed by reflecting the request's own `Origin` header (no hardcoded origin, no new dependency — manual middleware, not the `cors` package, matching this backend's deliberately-minimal-dependencies stance) rather than a wildcard, and neither loosens any actual authorization — every route still requires its own valid JWT regardless of origin. **Verified live**: created a real room, saw `Lobby.jsx` render the real join code/roster/host badge/disabled-until-ready Start button, confirmed via real network requests (200s with real Socket.IO `sid`s), not just unit tests.
- Tests: 426/426 still passing (no backend regressions); frontend `npm run lint` clean. No new automated frontend tests added — this project has no frontend test runner set up yet (still true as of this session), so verification here was the live-browser click-through described above, not a test file.

### Frontend slice — `GameControls.jsx` + `GameView.jsx` (P11-T06, core turn loop UI)

**`frontend/src/features/game/GameControls.jsx` + `GameView.jsx`** (new) — `App.jsx`'s "game started" branch now renders `GameView` (`GameBoard` + `GameControls` composed together), not `GameBoard` alone.

- **`gameState.currentTurn` does not exist** — `domain/gameState.js`'s `GameState` is flat: `phase` (a `GAME_PHASES` string — `'ROLLING'`, not the brief's `'ROLL_DICE'`, which is actually the *actionType* sent to trigger a roll, not a phase name) and `currentTurnIndex` (a turnOrder *number*, not a playerId) both sit directly on `gameState`. Corrected without asking — same unambiguous-correction category as the `tileIndex`→`propertyId` fix in the Trade System slice, not a design fork.
- **"Is it my turn" needed the same id-space resolution Phase 09's real bug was about** (finding #19): the locally authenticated `user.id` (JWT/`profiles.id`) only ever matches `PlayerGameState.playerId`, never `PlayerGameState.id` (what `turnOrder`/`currentTurnIndex` comparisons are keyed on everywhere else in this codebase). Resolved as `me = gameState.players.find(p => p.playerId === user.id)`, then `me.turnOrder === gameState.currentTurnIndex` — not a direct `playerId`/`currentTurn` comparison the brief's own sketch implied.
- **`sendGameAction`'s real exported signature is `(actionType, payload)`, two positional args** (`network/socketClient.js`, already built in an earlier phase) — not the brief's suggested `sendGameAction({ type: 'ROLL_DICE' })` object form. Called as `sendGameAction('ROLL_DICE')`/`sendGameAction('END_TURN')`.
- **Scoped to exactly the three phases the brief's own "core loop" wording covers** — `TURN_START` (added beyond the brief's explicit list: the match's very first move needs an explicit `START_TURN` action once, or the game is permanently stuck before anyone can ever roll — every subsequent turn already lands past `TURN_START` automatically via `turnMachine.js`'s `advanceTurn`, so this only matters once per match, but omitting it would have shipped an unplayable first turn), `ROLLING`, `POST_ACTIONS`. Any other phase (jail, purchase, auction, event card — explicitly out of this task's stated scope) shows a neutral status line instead of nothing or a crash.
- **Player display names aren't on `GameState.players` at all** (no `displayName` field — only `playerId`/`turnOrder`/balances/position) — resolved via the same `GET /api/v1/rooms/:id` REST call `Lobby.jsx` already uses, fetched once on mount (no polling needed here — names don't change mid-match, unlike Lobby's ready-status freshness need).
- Click-guard (`busy`, disables the action button) resets on the next `stateVersion` *or* `lastError` change — not a guessed timeout — so a genuinely rejected action doesn't leave the button stuck disabled.
- **Verified live, not just read**: couldn't get a real second authenticated player into the same browser to test the "not my turn" path directly — Supabase Auth's session lives in `localStorage`, shared across same-origin tabs, so a second tab is the same logged-in user, not a second one. Instead, injected a realistic fake `S2C_STATE_UPDATE`-shaped `GameState` directly into the *real, running* Zustand store (`await import('/src/store/gameStore.js')` from the live page — the actual module instance the mounted app uses, not a mock) to drive all three phase branches plus the "waiting for X" branch. This incidentally verified more than a scripted click-through would have: clicking the real Roll Dice button while this fake state was active fired a genuine `C2S_GAME_ACTION` against the *real* room/socket (the fake `gameState.id` didn't change which real room the socket was attached to), and the real backend correctly rejected it (`ROOM_NOT_IN_PROGRESS` — the real room genuinely was still `waiting_for_players`, only ever having had one player) — round-tripping back through `S2C_ACTION_REJECTED` → `lastError` → this component's own error line, all for real. Confirms the id-resolution, phase-branching, and the actual socket call are all correct; does not substitute for an eventual real 2-player happy-path run once a second live account is easier to arrange.

### Frontend slice — `PropertyActionDrawer.jsx` (P11-T07, buy/decline property UI)

**`frontend/src/features/game/PropertyActionDrawer.jsx` + `.module.css`** (new), mounted alongside `GameControls` in `GameView.jsx` — a non-blocking, fixed-position card (this task's own "hybrid, not a full modal" architecture decision), not gated inside `GameControls` itself.

- **Same `gameState.currentTurn` correction as the previous slice** (no such field; real fields are flat `phase`/`currentTurnIndex`) — the brief repeated the identical wrong assumption from P11-T06, corrected the same way.
- **This task's own instruction was to verify the action names/payloads against `WEBSOCKET_API.md`, not guess — verification found the brief's own guesses wrong on both counts**: the real actionType for declining is `DECLINE_PURCHASE`, not the brief's suggested `SKIP_BUY`/`START_AUCTION` (there is no separate "skip" vs. "auction" action — whether declining actually starts a Flash Auction is a server-side decision based on affordability, `turnMachine.js`'s `handleDeclinePurchase`, not a client choice between two actions). And `BUY_PROPERTY`'s payload is genuinely empty `{}` — WEBSOCKET_API.md's own words, "tile is the sender's current position, not client-supplied" — not `{ propertyId }` as the brief's sketch had it; sending one would just be silently ignored server-side, but writing it would have been factually wrong.
- **No `propertyId`/`tileIndex` field exists anywhere on `GameState` to "extract"** — the tile under consideration is derived the same way the server derives it: the local player's own `currentPosition`, matched against `staticBoard.tiles` (an object `{ boardId, tiles }`, not itself an array — the brief's "staticBoard array" phrasing undersold its real shape slightly). No extra ownership/buyability re-check needed client-side either — `AWAITING_PURCHASE` being the current phase at all is already `resolveTile.js`'s own guarantee that this specific tile is fresh and unowned.
- **`GROUP_COLORS`/`CHANCE_FORTUNE_COLOR`/`TILE_ICON` factored out of `BoardTile.jsx` into a new `frontend/src/features/board/tileVisuals.js`** — needed the identical mappings for the card header (a mismatched color/icon between the drawer and the tile it represents would read as a bug), and exporting them straight from `BoardTile.jsx` tripped `oxlint`'s `react(only-export-components)` rule (breaks Vite Fast Refresh). Plain-constants file, not a component — same fix category as the `groupHoldingsFor()` extraction in the backend's own Trade System slice, done properly once the lint warning surfaced rather than left in place.
- **`GameControls.jsx` updated to exclude `AWAITING_PURCHASE` from its own generic "phase not supported yet" fallback line** — without this, both components would render simultaneously during a purchase decision: the real, working drawer *and* a stale "not supported" message next to it. Caught by checking the actual integrated result, not just reviewing the new component in isolation.
- **Verified live, same technique as the previous slice, with one new wrinkle**: mid-session, a rapid sequence of edits (removing `BoardTile.jsx`'s exports right after re-pointing `PropertyActionDrawer.jsx`'s import) left Vite's dev-server HMR in a transiently broken state (`SyntaxError: ... does not provide an export named 'CHANCE_FORTUNE_COLOR'`) — confirmed via `npm run build` (clean, 113 modules, no errors) that this was HMR staleness, not a real bug, then cleared it with a hard navigate. Separately, simulated button clicks (the `computer` tool) intermittently failed to register against this app during this session for unclear reasons (possibly timing-related, not chased further); calling `createRoom`/`connectSocket`/`sendGameAction` directly via `javascript_tool` against the real, running app worked reliably and gave an equally genuine result. With real seed content loaded (`GET /boards/small`), the drawer correctly rendered a real property's name/price, and a real `DECLINE_PURCHASE` round-tripped through the real socket to a real `ROOM_NOT_IN_PROGRESS` rejection (needed ~2.5s to arrive over the polling transport in this test, not the ~1s assumed in the previous slice's test — noted in case this matters for any future UI timeout/spinner logic).

### Frontend slice — `FlashAuction.jsx` (P11-T08, Flash Auction UI)

**`frontend/src/features/game/FlashAuction.jsx` + `.module.css`** (new), mounted alongside the other three in `GameView.jsx` — deliberately the *opposite* architecture from `PropertyActionDrawer.jsx`'s non-blocking corner card: a full-viewport centered modal (this task's own explicit "every player, not just the current-turn player, needs to react" reasoning), since a Flash Auction is the one phase where the current-turn player isn't the only relevant actor.

- **Two of the brief's own guesses were wrong, verified against `turnMachine.js`/`WEBSOCKET_API.md` before writing anything (this task's own explicit instruction)**: the fold action is `FOLD_AUCTION` — neither of the brief's two suggested names (`FOLD`, `LEAVE_AUCTION`) exist anywhere in this codebase. `PLACE_BID` (payload `{ amount }`) was the brief's other guess and was actually correct. `AUCTION_TIMEOUT` is a third legal phase action but is system-generated only (`WEBSOCKET_API.md` §1: "A real client must never send this") — deliberately not wired to any button.
- **`player.money` doesn't exist** — the real field, same as every other balance check already established this session (`PropertyActionDrawer.jsx`, `GameControls.jsx`), is `PlayerGameState.currentBalance`.
- **`auction.propertyId` is `Property.id` (the game-scoped ownership row), not a tile id or position** — unlike `PropertyActionDrawer.jsx` (which finds its tile via the local player's own `currentPosition`), this needed a genuine two-hop lookup the brief's "fetch... using propertyId and staticBoard" phrasing glossed over: `gameState.properties.find(propertyId) -> boardTileId -> staticBoard.tiles.find(boardTileId)`.
- **`activeBidders`/`highestBidderId`/`bids[].playerId` are all `PlayerGameState.id` (`game_players.id`)** — the same id-space `GameControls.jsx`/`PropertyActionDrawer.jsx` already established for `turnOrder`/`currentPosition` comparisons, confirmed again here rather than assumed, and NOT `useAuth()`'s `user.id` (`profiles.id`). Comparing the wrong id-space would have made "am I winning"/"can I still bid" silently always false.
- **`engine/auction.js`'s `AuctionState` has no stored "folded" list** — `foldBidder()` just removes an id from `activeBidders` with no trace kept. Folded participants are derived client-side as the complement of `activeBidders` within the real (non-bank, non-bankrupt) player roster; `highestBidderId` is looked up against that same full roster rather than just `activeBidders`, since `foldBidder()`'s own docstring confirms a folded player can still be the winning bidder — a naive "leader must be active" assumption would have hidden a real leader.
- Bid-button afford-check is per-button, not one shared disabled flag — the `+$10`/`+$50` increments can independently cross the caller's `currentBalance`, verified live (below) rather than assumed from the arithmetic alone.
- **Verified live, same store-injection technique as the previous two slices** (still no practical way to get a second real authenticated player into a second tab this session) — the process already listening on port 5173 turned out to be the user's own leftover dev-server instance again, confirmed via `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`'s `CommandLine` before reusing it rather than assumed. Injected a realistic `FLASH_AUCTION_ACTIVE` state directly into the live `gameStore` singleton: property name/color resolved correctly through the real two-hop lookup, the active/folded participant split and highest-bidder display were both correct (including a folded player still shown as leading, once tested), the `+$50` button correctly disabled itself at a balance sitting between the two increments while `+$10` stayed enabled, a real `PLACE_BID` fired through the real authenticated socket (busy-lock engaged correctly on click), and flipping `activeBidders` to exclude the local player correctly swapped the whole action area for the "already folded" status line. Injected state was reset and the tab hard-reloaded back to its original lobby screen afterward so the user's own running session wasn't left showing fake data.
- **New wrinkle this slice**: this repo's own `.claude/launch.json` (pinned to port 5174) turned out not to be what the preview tooling actually reads — it resolves `launch.json` relative to the harness's own primary working directory, a separate folder outside this repo entirely, which has its own separate multi-project `launch.json` (a `CoBacTyPhu-dev` entry at port 5173, alongside unrelated entries for a different local project). Worth remembering for any future preview/dev-server step in this environment: the repo-local `launch.json` is not what actually gets used.

### Frontend slice — `PropertyManager.jsx` (P11-T09, Property Management UI)

**`frontend/src/features/game/PropertyManager.jsx` + `.module.css`** (new), plus a click-to-select wire-up through `frontend/src/store/gameStore.js` (new `selectedPropertyId`/`selectProperty`) and `frontend/src/features/board/GameBoard.jsx`/`BoardTile.jsx` (new `properties`-to-tile lookup, `onClick`/`isSelected` props, a CSS selection glow). A top-right-anchored side panel, deliberately positioned so it can't visually collide with `PropertyActionDrawer.jsx`'s own bottom-right card if both happen to be mounted at once (selecting a tile is independent of turn/phase; only the panel's own action buttons are gated on `isMyTurn && phase === 'POST_ACTIONS'`).

- **`isMortgaged` doesn't exist** — the brief's own requirement #2 named it, but the real field (`domain/property.js`) is `property.mortgaged`. Same category of correction as `player.money`/`currentBalance` in the previous slice.
- **No `UPGRADE_PROPERTY` action exists** — the brief's own two guesses were `BUILD_HOUSE` or `UPGRADE_PROPERTY`; only `BUILD_HOUSE` is real (`turnMachine.js`'s `VALID_ACTIONS_BY_PHASE.POST_ACTIONS`). All four actions (`BUILD_HOUSE`/`SELL_HOUSE`/`MORTGAGE`/`UNMORTGAGE`) take `{ propertyId }` (`Property.id`, the game-scoped ownership row — not `boardTileId`, not `tileIndex`), confirmed against `WEBSOCKET_API.md` §1's own table rather than guessed.
- **Client-side validation was implemented as a full mirror of `turnMachine.js`'s real preconditions, not just the brief's three illustrative examples** (not-owner / not-mortgaged / `upgradeLevel > 0`) — full-group ownership, no mortgaged group member, the even-build/even-sell level comparison, and an affordability check are all reproduced client-side too, using the same `groupHoldingsFor`-style lookup (`staticBoard.tiles` + `gameState.properties`, matched by `groupId`) `turnMachine.js` itself uses server-side. Judged in-scope, not scope creep: the brief's own examples were explicitly "e.g.", and a Build/Sell button that only checked ownership would still let a player click it and get a confusing, entirely predictable rejection — which is exactly what "disable buttons if the action is logically impossible" was asking to prevent.
- **Real, already-known consequence of today's live seed data, reproduced correctly rather than glossed over**: `board_tiles.group_id` is null everywhere in the real seed data right now (an already-tracked open item, "Open design decisions" below) — `groupHoldingsFor` returns `[]` for any ungrouped tile, which makes the full-group-ownership check impossible to satisfy. **Build now correctly shows itself disabled against every real property on the live board today** ("Cần sở hữu trọn bộ nhóm màu"), not because of a frontend bug, but because it mirrors a real, currently-unsatisfiable backend precondition. Confirmed live with an injected property that *did* have a populated `groupId` and full-group ownership — Build correctly enabled itself once every real precondition was actually met, proving the validation logic itself is correct, not just conservatively always-off. Mortgage/Unmortgage are unaffected by this gap (their preconditions don't require group completeness) and work normally against the live board today.
- **A genuine, previously-unknown backend bug found while verifying `UNMORTGAGE`'s cost display, not by code review — since resolved, same day, see finding #26**: `economy/propertyEconomy.js`'s `calculateUnmortgage` (`Math.ceil(tile.mortgageValue * MORTGAGE_INTEREST_RATE)`, `MORTGAGE_INTEREST_RATE = 1.1`) was subject to a real IEEE-754 floating-point rounding error — `100 * 1.1` evaluates to `110.00000000000001` in JavaScript, not exactly `110`, so `Math.ceil` bumped it to `111`. Not a one-off: a sweep of `mortgageValue` from 1–500 found **25 affected integer values** (e.g. 50→56 instead of 55, 90→100 instead of 99, 100→111 instead of 110, 110→122 instead of 121 — always overcharging by exactly $1, never undercharging). At the time this was found, `PropertyManager.jsx` deliberately mirrored this formula bit-for-bit rather than "fixing" it client-side alone, specifically so the previewed cost never disagreed with what the server actually charged — both sides were fixed together in the same later same-day bug-fix pass (finding #26), not one ahead of the other.
- **Verified live, same store-injection technique as the previous two slices, with one new debugging wrinkle**: injecting state through a plain `await import('/src/store/gameStore.js')` (no query string) silently wrote to a *second, orphaned* module instance — Vite's dev server had already served the real app's own import of that file as `gameStore.js?t=<timestamp>` (its own HMR cache-busting query, bumped by this same slice's edits to that file), and the browser's ES-module cache keys strictly by exact URL including the query string, so the two resolved to two different `create()` calls entirely. Symptom was confusing at first (the store read back correctly via `getState()` immediately after writing, but the actual rendered page never changed, even after a hard navigate) — resolved by checking `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')` for the exact versioned URL the page's own script tags actually loaded, then importing *that* exact URL instead of the bare path. **Worth remembering for any future live-store-injection verification that happens in the same session as edits to the store file itself**: don't assume a bare dynamic `import()` path reaches the same module instance the mounted app is using — check the real loaded URL first if the injected state doesn't visibly take effect. Once fixed: clicking an ownable tile correctly opened the panel with the right property (including switching between two different selections, and toggling closed on a second click of the same tile); the CSS selection glow was confirmed via `getComputedStyle` (`outline: 2px solid` + a box-shadow, both in the real `--accent` color) rather than assumed from the CSS alone; three real ownership/state combinations (fully-group-owned-by-me, mortgaged-transport-owned-by-me, owned-by-opponent) each produced exactly the expected enabled/disabled pattern and per-button reason text; a real `BUILD_HOUSE` fired through the real authenticated socket with the busy-lock engaging correctly. Reset and hard-reloaded back to the clean lobby screen afterward, same courtesy as the previous two slices.

### Frontend slice — `TradeWindow.jsx` (P11-T10, full Trade System UI)

**`frontend/src/features/game/TradeWindow.jsx` + `TradeOfferColumn.jsx` (split out per this task's own "consider breaking it down" suggestion — one reusable split-view column, used for both parties in both edit and view mode) + both `.module.css` files** (new), plus a small always-visible "Giao dịch" opponent-button row added to `GameControls.jsx` (no in-game player-roster component existed anywhere in this codebase to hang a "Trade" button off of, and this task's own brief offered the entry point as an open "e.g." — building a whole new roster component was bigger scope than a trade-entry-point needed) and a new `tradeDraftTargetId` field/setter in `gameStore.js`. A fourth full-modal overlay alongside `FlashAuction.jsx`, at z-index 40 (between `PropertyManager.jsx`'s 15 and `FlashAuction.jsx`'s 50 — a 2-party trade deliberately doesn't outrank a whole-room auction event).

- **This task's own "do not guess proposerOffer/targetOffer" instruction was taken further than just reading `WEBSOCKET_API.md`**: `domain/trade.js`, `engine/trade.js`, `stateMachine/tradeMachine.js`, *and* `infrastructure/websocket/socketServer.js`'s actual dispatch code were all read before writing anything, since this task's payloads are security-sensitive (who's allowed to act as whom) in a way worth confirming against the real wire-up, not just the doc. Paid off: `socketServer.js`'s own action-construction code confirms the envelope's `playerId` is *always* server-overwritten with the sender's real resolved id for every action type including trade (the client never sends it, same as every action shipped this session), and — not obvious from the doc alone — `tradeId`/`newTradeId` for `PROPOSE_TRADE`/`COUNTER_TRADE` are *also* always server-overwritten with a fresh `crypto.randomUUID()`. The one id the client genuinely must supply itself is `COUNTER_TRADE`'s `tradeId`, identifying *which* existing trade is being countered — not in `socketServer.js`'s overwrite list. `WEBSOCKET_API.md`'s own documented payload shapes turned out to be accurate throughout — this was a confirmation pass, not a correction, but a thorough one given what was at stake if a client-supplied id or actor claim actually *had* been trusted somewhere.
- **`COUNTER_TRADE` flips proposer/target** (`engine/trade.js`'s `counterTrade`: `newProposerId = existingTrade.targetId`) — since only the current target may counter, *whoever submits a counter always becomes the new trade's proposer*. `TradeWindow.jsx` uses one `submitDraft()` for both a fresh `PROPOSE_TRADE` and a `COUNTER_TRADE` for exactly this reason: "my offer" always belongs in the new trade's `proposerOffer` slot and "their offer" in `targetOffer`, regardless of which of the two actions is actually being sent.
- **The brief's own "Fails if `counterDepth > 5`" phrasing doesn't quite match the real guard** (`engine/trade.js`: `existingTrade.counterDepth >= MAX_COUNTER_DEPTH`, i.e. `>= 5`, not `> 5`) — a trade already at depth 5 can never be countered again, one at depth 4 still can (producing depth 5). The "Mặc Cả" button is gated on the real `>=` condition; verified live by forcing `counterDepth` to 5 on an injected trade and confirming the button disappeared, leaving only Accept/Reject.
- **Asset locking (`lockedPropertyIds`/`lockedMoneyFor` in `TradeWindow.jsx`) is a direct mirror of `engine/trade.js`'s own same-named helpers** — every property/money amount already offered on *either* side of *any other* active trade in `gameState.pendingTrades` is unavailable, using the same `excludeTradeId` convention (null for a fresh proposal; the trade being countered, when countering). Verified live with a third, unrelated injected trade (between two NPC-role players, not involving the local player at all) that locked one property and $200 of one side's balance — the picker correctly disabled that property with a "🔒 Đã khoá" badge and the money input's `max` correctly dropped by exactly $200, entirely from a trade the local player wasn't even a party to.
- **UI framing decision, not explicitly specified by the brief**: the split view always shows "my offer" on the left and "their offer" on the right, regardless of whether the local player is the trade's `proposerId` or `targetId` — i.e. which named offer object (`proposerOffer` vs. `targetOffer`) maps to "left" flips depending on role. The brief's own "Left: Local Player's Offer, Right: Target Player's Offer" phrasing is written from an *initiating* player's perspective, but the same window also has to render an *incoming* trade the local player didn't initiate; always-mine-on-the-left is the one framing that stays coherent in both cases. Verified live in both roles.
- **Trade lookup, per the brief's own singular phrasing ("find *an* active trade")**: at most one trade involving the local player is surfaced at a time (`Array.prototype.find`, first match) — the "Giao dịch" opponent-button row hides itself (replaced by a one-line hint) whenever one already exists, rather than supporting a multi-trade queue UI, which wasn't asked for.
- **Verified live** (same store-injection technique as the previous three slices; `computer`-tool clicks intermittently failed to register again this round, same unresolved wrinkle noted in the `PropertyActionDrawer.jsx` slice — fell back to dispatching real DOM `click`/`input` events via `javascript_tool`, which worked reliably throughout): a fresh proposal draft correctly listed each side's real owned properties and real balances, correctly pre-disabled a property locked by an unrelated trade and reduced the counterparty's available money accordingly, and a real `PROPOSE_TRADE` fired through the real socket (busy-lock engaged) with exactly the selected properties/money. Injecting a real incoming trade correctly switched the window to view mode with the my-side/their-side mapping described above, the right button set for both the target role (Đồng Ý/Mặc Cả/Từ Chối) and the proposer role (Hủy Đề Xuất only, tested by flipping the same trade's roles), and the counter-draft pre-fill exactly reproduced the current offer flipped into the new roles. `tradeDraftTargetId` was also confirmed to auto-clear itself the moment a real trade involving the local player showed up in `gameState.pendingTrades`, so a stale draft flag can't linger once `PROPOSE_TRADE` actually lands. Reset and reloaded back to the clean lobby screen afterward.

### Frontend slice — Jail UI + `EventCardModal.jsx` (P11-T11, last core-loop UI gap)

**No new component for jail** — its three buttons went straight into `GameControls.jsx` (a new `JAIL_DECISION` block, same "small, turn-scoped action set" treatment already given to `TURN_START`/`ROLLING`/`POST_ACTIONS`), unlike `AWAITING_PURCHASE`/`FLASH_AUCTION_ACTIVE`/trade, which each got a dedicated file because their own briefs specifically called for a different architecture (non-blocking drawer / whole-room modal / cross-phase window). **`EventCardModal.jsx` + `.module.css`** (new) handles the other half — a real blocking modal for `AWAITING_EVENT_CHOICE`, and a separate dismissible top-center toast for an `INSTANT` card's after-the-fact effect (two different renders in one component, not two files, since the brief named one). Two small shared helpers, also new: `clientDice.js` (`rollDice`/`rollProbability`) and `eventCardDictionary.js` (a frontend mirror of `EVENT_CARDS`) — both reused by `GameControls.jsx` too.

- **Verification (this task's own "CRITICAL INSTRUCTION") turned up a real gap beyond what it asked about**: jail's phase is `JAIL_DECISION` (not `ROLLING`) with real actions `PAY_JAIL_FINE`/`USE_JAIL_CARD`/`ATTEMPT_JAIL_ROLL` (the brief's own guesses, `PAY_BAIL`/`ROLL_DICE`, were both wrong — only `USE_JAIL_CARD` matched), and the real jail field is `player.inJail` (not `isJailed`). There is no `DRAW_CARD` or `ACKNOWLEDGE_EVENT` action anywhere — `DRAWING_CARD` is never a phase a client actually observes (an `INSTANT` card resolves synchronously inside whichever action caused the landing; a `CHOICE` card moves straight to the real, observable `AWAITING_EVENT_CHOICE` instead). All of that was answerable from `turnMachine.js`/`WEBSOCKET_API.md` alone, as asked.
- **Two things weren't answerable that way, and both needed a stop-and-ask (`AskUserQuestion`) rather than a silent pick — the user confirmed both recommended options, 2026-08-19**:
  1. **`engine/jail.js`'s `useCard()` never checks card possession at all** — its own docstring says so explicitly ("jail.js has no card-inventory field to check"), and no card-inventory field exists anywhere on `PlayerGameState`. "Dùng Thẻ Miễn Phí" is shipped always-enabled with an honest tooltip explaining why it can't be gated, rather than faking a check against data that doesn't exist.
  2. **No REST or socket path exposes `domain/eventDictionary.js`'s real card text/options to the frontend at all** (grepped every backend REST route and `frontend/src/network/api.js` — zero references either direction); `GameState` only ever carries a bare `pendingEventCardId` key for `CHOICE` cards, and *nothing* for `INSTANT` ones. `eventCardDictionary.js` is a deliberate, flagged mirror of the real dictionary (currently 2 entries: `DIVIDEND_50`, `INVESTMENT_OPPORTUNITY`) — will silently drift if the backend dictionary changes without a matching update here. `INSTANT` cards still can't show real text even with the mirror (`GameState` never records which one was drawn) — the toast shows only the resulting money movement, honestly labeled as incomplete, not invented flavor text.
- **The single biggest finding this task, found chasing the brief's own jail-roll question, not looked for on purpose — see finding #27**: `WEBSOCKET_API.md` documents `ROLL_DICE`/`ATTEMPT_JAIL_ROLL`/`MAKE_EVENT_CHOICE`'s `probabilityRoll` as all "server-generated, never client-supplied" (`engine/dice.js`'s own header goes further: "The client never supplies or influences these values"), but `infrastructure/websocket/socketServer.js` never actually wires `dice.js`'s `rollDice()` into the live `handleGameAction` path anywhere — confirmed by reading the whole file (dice-related terms appear exactly once, in a comment) and by `socketServer.test.js` itself, which feeds client-supplied dice values directly into a real `C2S_GAME_ACTION` test payload and asserts the server accepts them as-is. `rollDice()` is only ever called for real from `stateMachine/timers.js`'s timeout-default path — never from a live player's own click. **This means `GameControls.jsx`'s `ROLL_DICE` button (built in P11-T06) has sent no dice payload at all since it was first written, and would have crashed the real handler (`rollResult.total` read off `undefined`) had any earlier task's verification ever reached a genuinely `in_progress` room** — none did, since every verification this session used either an injected fake room or a non-existent one. Fixed here, alongside wiring the new `ATTEMPT_JAIL_ROLL`/`MAKE_EVENT_CHOICE` the same way: `clientDice.js` is a byte-for-byte mirror of `dice.js`'s real algorithm, shipped as an explicit, flagged stopgap, not a redesign. **This is a real fairness/security gap, not a cosmetic one** — a client that can pick its own dice results can currently cheat. Recommended proper fix, not attempted here (backend change, out of this frontend task's scope): inject a server-rolled result into `action.payload` in `socketServer.js` the same way `playerId`/`tradeId` already are for every action, then delete the client-side generation entirely.
- **Verified live**: jail buttons correctly showed jailTurns (`1/3`), correctly disabled "Nộp Phạt" when balance was below `JAIL_FINE` while leaving the other two enabled, and a real `ATTEMPT_JAIL_ROLL` fired with a well-formed client-generated roll (20 sampled calls to `clientDice.js`'s `rollDice()` all produced internally-consistent shapes — `total === die1+die2`, `isDouble` matching, etc. — matching `engine/dice.js`'s own logic by construction, not just by inspection). The `AWAITING_EVENT_CHOICE` modal correctly rendered `INVESTMENT_OPPORTUNITY`'s real text and both options, correctly disabled the risk option below its `validation.amount`, and correctly detected which option needed a `probabilityRoll` (only the one with a `PROBABILITY` intent). An unknown `pendingEventCardId` correctly fell back to an honest "no data" message instead of crashing. The instant-card toast correctly showed a gain (`+$50`) for a transaction routed to the local player, correctly stayed hidden for an otherwise-identical transaction between two *other* players, and correctly dismissed on click. Reset and reloaded back to the clean lobby screen afterward, same as every prior slice.

### Win Condition & Game-End System (backend + frontend, full feature) — designed via Plan Mode, approved, then implemented, 2026-08-19

**A genuinely different working mode for this project, worth recording**: the brief itself instructed "do not implement, design only." `EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode` were used for the first time this session; the full 16-section proposed design (victory model, net worth formula, bankruptcy/liquidation lifecycle, Final Phase, tie-break, state machine, DB impact, UI/UX, anti-exploits, balance numbers, compatibility audit) was written to a plan file and **approved wholesale in one shot**, not resolved item-by-item via `AskUserQuestion` the way the jail/dice gaps were (finding #27) — a coarser-grained approval mode than this project's usual per-decision stop-and-ask, used because the brief itself asked for a complete design pass before any code, not a series of small forks.

**Headline finding from the design's own audit, confirmed correct once implemented**: this was a wiring gap, not a from-scratch build. `bankruptcy.js`'s `checkSolvency()`, the DB schema (`match_results.result_type CHECK IN ('elimination_win','net_worth_win','aborted')`, already anticipating a hybrid model by name), and `turnMachine.js`'s own pre-existing bankrupt-player skip in `advanceTurn()` all already existed, unused. **Zero DB migrations were needed** — every new field either lives in the `GameState` JSON blob the same way `pendingAuction`/`pendingTrades` already do, or is a DB column that already existed unused (`game_players.bankrupt`/`.bankrupt_at`/`.final_rank`, `games.status`, `game_transactions.transaction_type = 'bankruptcy_transfer'`).

**Approved design, as implemented**:
- Hybrid win condition: immediate elimination win the instant exactly one non-bankrupt real player remains (checked after every bankruptcy resolves, not deferred to a turn boundary); otherwise a Final Phase countdown (`FINAL_PHASE_TRIGGER_ROUND`, `FINAL_PHASE_DURATION_ROUNDS=5`) ends with the highest-net-worth solvent player winning. **The trigger shipped at 20 and was raised to 45 on 2026-08-25** — see "Final Phase raised from round 20 to 45" below for why; everything else about this design is unchanged.
- Net worth = `cash + Σ(landValue + buildingValue)` per owned property — `landValue` full price unless mortgaged (then `price − ceil(mortgageValue×11/10)`, clamped ≥0 — mortgage treated as debt against land still owned, not a discount on it), `buildingValue` = the exact same sellback figure `SELL_HOUSE` already pays out. **This revises `ECONOMY_SPECIFICATION.md` §6's older `[PROPOSED]` formula** (which valued buildings at full `houseCost` and mortgaged land at bare `mortgageValue` — both judged inconsistent against `SELL_HOUSE`'s own real payout and against this game having no "sell property to the Bank" mechanic).
- Liquidation: player-chooses (reuses `SELL_HOUSE`/`MORTGAGE` verbatim via a new `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` phase, no new action type), auto-settling the instant the debtor's post-sale balance covers the debt.
- Real bankruptcy (no liquidation can cover the debt): all remaining properties transfer to the creditor in kind (mortgage flags preserved, or revert to unowned if the creditor is the Bank), remaining cash transfers via one `bankruptcy_transfer` transaction, any pending trade naming the debtor is dropped, an active auction bid is folded the same way a normal `FOLD_AUCTION` would.
- Tie-break, always decisive, never random/undefined: net worth desc → property value desc → cash desc → turnOrder asc (deliberately not cash-first, so the tie-break itself doesn't quietly reward hoarding).
- `LIQUIDATING`/`PLAYER_ELIMINATED`/`GAME_OVER_CHECK` (named as separate states in `GAME_STATE_MACHINE.md`'s own diagram) deliberately collapsed into instant sub-steps inside one settlement handler, not built as new observable `GAME_PHASES` — nothing ever needs to *act* while in any of them. A flagged simplification of that diagram, not an oversight.

**Backend (`backend/src/`)**:
- `engine/bankruptcy.js` — `propertyLiquidationValue` split to export its own house-only term as a new `houseSellBackValue(tile, property)`, reused by the new net-worth engine below. Pure refactor, all 7 pre-existing tests unchanged.
- `domain/gameState.js` — `GameState` gained `pendingLiquidation`/`roundNumber`/`finalPhaseStartedAtRound`/`endReason`; `PlayerGameState` gained `finalNetWorth`/`finalCash`/`finalPropertyValue` alongside the already-existing-but-previously-unused `finalRank` (see below for why these three were added beyond the plan's original field list).
- New `engine/netWorth.js` — `propertyNetWorth`/`netWorth`, reusing `houseSellBackValue`. **Self-caught the exact same class of bug as finding #26** while writing its own test suite: the first draft used `Math.ceil(tile.mortgageValue * MORTGAGE_INTEREST_RATE)` (the identical `x*1.1` IEEE-754 imprecision), caught by a failing assertion (got 89, expected 90) before it ever shipped — fixed to the same integer-safe `Math.ceil((v*11)/10)` form finding #26 already established, this time never landing in a released state at all.
- New `engine/bankruptcyApplication.js` — `computeBankruptcySettlement`, the in-kind property/cash transfer logic (creditor `isBank` → properties revert to unowned, not "transferred to the Bank").
- New `stateMachine/gameEndMachine.js` — `checkElimination`/`shouldEnterFinalPhase`/`shouldEndFinalPhase`/`rankPlayers`, the same "second thing `handleGameAction` checks after an action, alongside `turnMachine.js`" relationship `tradeMachine.js` already established.
- `stateMachine/turnMachine.js` — the core rewiring: `settleDebt()` now branches on `checkSolvency()`'s full result (previously only ever read `canPayInCash`, silently discarding the richer fields — exactly as the design doc's own audit had flagged); new `applyBankruptcy`/`finishAfterBankruptcy`/`settleGameEnd`/`resolveLiquidationStep`; `advanceTurn` now increments `roundNumber` on a real wraparound (detected via `nextPlayer.turnOrder <= currentTurnIndex`, correctly handles skipped bankrupt players) and checks Final Phase entry/end at that same boundary.
- `infrastructure/websocket/socketServer.js` — new guard rejecting any `C2S_GAME_ACTION` once `gameState.status !== 'in_progress'` (`GAME_ALREADY_FINISHED`) — a real gap the design's own audit caught: `ROOM_STATUSES` has no `'finished'` value, so the pre-existing room-status check alone could never have caught this.
- **A design-vs-implementation gap noticed and closed during implementation, not left as a silent shortfall**: the approved plan's own UI section called for the Game Over screen to show a net-worth breakdown, but `rankPlayers()`'s return value only ever contributed to `finalRank` — the underlying cash/property numbers were computed, then discarded before reaching `GameState`. Extended `rankPlayers()` to return `cash`/`propertyValue` alongside `netWorth` for every ranked player (bankrupt players hardcoded to all-zero — they transferred everything away), and `settleGameEnd()` to persist all three onto each player's `finalCash`/`finalPropertyValue`/`finalNetWorth` fields, the same JSON-blob-only pattern the whole design already established for `pendingLiquidation`/`roundNumber`. Judged an in-scope implementation-detail fix, not a new architecture fork — same standing as reusing the real `settleAuction()` over the plan's slightly-imprecise "always FAILED" phrasing for game-end auction resolution.
- Tests: **465/465 backend tests passing** (462 existing + 3 new: `gameEndMachine.test.js`'s cash/propertyValue coverage, `gameState.test.js`'s new player-defaults coverage) — no regressions. One pre-existing test (`'landing on tax, insolvent: stops at LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED'`) encoded the *old*, wrong behavior (its fixture player was actually fully bankrupt, not merely cash-short-but-liquidation-solvent) — split into two correct tests once the real distinction was implemented, not just patched to pass.

**Frontend (`frontend/src/features/game/`)**:
- `PropertyManager.jsx` — extended to allow `SELL_HOUSE`/`MORTGAGE` (and only those two, matching `VALID_ACTIONS_BY_PHASE.LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` exactly) during a new `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` phase, plus a status banner.
- `GameControls.jsx` — a `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` status line, plus a new persistent "🔥 Giai đoạn cuối — còn N vòng" badge, computed client-side from `finalPhaseStartedAtRound + FINAL_PHASE_DURATION_ROUNDS − roundNumber`.
- New `winConditionConstants.js` — only `FINAL_PHASE_DURATION_ROUNDS` mirrored client-side (not `FINAL_PHASE_TRIGGER_ROUND` — the frontend never independently decides *whether* Final Phase started, only ever watches the server-computed `finalPhaseStartedAtRound` field).
- New `FinalPhaseBanner.jsx` + `.module.css` — one-shot dismissible announcement on `finalPhaseStartedAtRound` null→set, dismiss-state tracked by the specific round number (not `stateVersion`, since the trigger field is set exactly once per match and never resets — unlike `EventCardModal.jsx`'s own toast, whose trigger condition re-arms every card draw). z-index 55, deliberately the highest of any *mid-match* overlay.
- New `GameOverScreen.jsx` + `.module.css` — full-viewport blocking modal, z-index 60 (the true top of the whole stack — nothing outranks it), ranked standings (medal for 1st, `#N` otherwise), sorted by `finalRank`, a bankrupt tag, and a net-worth breakdown line. **One deliberate simplification of the approved plan's own UI sketch**: the plan's wording named three breakdown parts ("cash / property / buildings"); shipped as two ("cash / property", the label spelling out that property includes buildings) since the backend engine (`netWorth.js`'s `propertyNetWorth`) only ever computes land+building as one combined figure per property — splitting it further would have meant reworking already-tested engine code for a purely cosmetic gain, and a 2-part line is if anything closer to the same plan's own "don't overcomplicate the scoreboard" instruction.
- `GameView.jsx` mounts both new components; header comments extended to document the new z-index ordering (55 and 60) the same way every prior overlay's ordering was documented there.
- Lint clean (one pre-existing, unrelated warning in `AuthContext.jsx`), `npm run build` clean.
- **Verified live via store injection** (same technique established across every P11 frontend slice): an in-progress Final-Phase state correctly showed both the persistent badge (live countdown math, "còn 3 vòng") and the one-shot banner (fixed duration text, "Còn 5 vòng nữa") side by side, and the banner correctly disappeared on click while the badge persisted.
