A `status:'finished'` state correctly rendered three ranked players (🏆 for rank 1, correct breakdown numbers matching the injected `finalCash`/`finalPropertyValue`/`finalNetWorth`, a "· phá sản" tag on the bankrupt one), sorted correctly. **Confirmed the opaque-backdrop non-collision claim programmatically, not just visually** (the Browser pane wasn't displayed for a screenshot this session) — `document.elementFromPoint()` at the exact coordinates of `GameControls.jsx`'s now-stale "no UI for this phase" fallback line (still in the DOM, `gameState.phase` is `null` once the match ends) resolved to `GameOverScreen`'s own backdrop `div` (z-index 60), not the fallback text underneath, proving the cover is real in actual paint order, not assumed from CSS alone. The real backend (port 5000) was reachable throughout — `getRoom` calls for the synthetic injected `roomId` correctly 404'd against the real REST API and were silently caught, exactly as designed, falling back to raw playerId display. Store reset to `null`/`null` afterward, same courtesy as every prior slice.

**Deliberately not done in this pass, both already flagged in the approved design as open follow-ons, not silently dropped**:
- `LIQUIDATION_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (45s) auto-liquidate-on-timeout fallback — `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` currently has no timer at all; a player who never acts simply stalls the match indefinitely in that phase. `stateMachine/timers.js`'s `TimerManager` would need a new phase entry + `buildDefaultAction` branch, the same mechanism P10-T04 already wired for every other timed phase.
- `match_results` DB row persistence — the table/columns already exist and match this design's own vocabulary exactly, but nothing writes to it yet; deferred pending a read of `persistAndBroadcast`'s real shape in `socketServer.js`, not attempted blind.
- See finding #28 below for a related, adjacent gap found (not fixed) while implementing this feature.

### First full live playtest — a real 3-player match played start to finish, 2026-08-19

The single biggest standing gap this whole project — "no real multiplayer playtest has ever reached game-end" (flagged repeatedly: "Recommended next" #6/#7, finding #27's own writeup) — is closed. A throwaway bot-driver script (`backend/scripts/`, real Supabase accounts, real REST + Socket.IO, deleted after use — not committed) authenticated 3 real users, created/joined/started a real room, then drove a deliberately dumb bot (always buy if affordable, never build/trade, always end turn, fold every auction, mortgage to cover any liquidation, take the safe event-card option) through real actions until the match reached a genuine `status: 'finished'`.

**Result**: a real match played 25 full rounds, 206 real actions, 110.7 seconds — entered Final Phase exactly at round 20 (matching `FINAL_PHASE_TRIGGER_ROUND`), ended exactly at round 25 (`FINAL_PHASE_TRIGGER_ROUND + FINAL_PHASE_DURATION_ROUNDS`), `endReason: 'final_phase'`, three players ranked with sane, internally-consistent standings (`finalNetWorth === finalCash + finalPropertyValue` for all three, strictly descending). Zero unexpected rejections, zero hangs, zero anomalies. This is the first time in this project's history any of that has actually been observed end to end, not inferred from unit tests or partial live-verification.

**Found and fixed a real, significant, previously-undiscovered bug getting there — see finding #29 below**: `initializeGameState()` (`room.controller.js`) had hardcoded `properties: []` for every real game since Phase 08, with a comment claiming board tile data was still unreachable — true then, stale ever since Phase 11 actually seeded it. Every real game started via `POST /rooms/:id/start` has had an empty `properties` array this entire time, meaning any player landing on any property/transport/utility tile crashed the handler outright. Nothing before this playtest ever drove a real game deep enough into real, uninjected state to hit it.

**Three bugs found and fixed in the throwaway *script itself*, not the game** — worth recording since each is a real lesson for any future live-verification script, not just noise:
1. First hang: attaching a `S2C_STATE_UPDATE` listener to all 3 sockets (a room-wide broadcast) let one broadcast fire a shared single-slot resolver up to 3 times, occasionally resolving the *next* pending action's promise with a stale response.
2. Second hang, same symptom different cause: `initializeGameState()` shuffles turn order, so `gameState.players`' array order does not match `sockets[]`'s host/guest/p3 order — the script's player→socket map was built by array position instead of by real auth identity (`PlayerGameState.playerId` vs. `sessions[i].user.id`), silently routing actions through the wrong socket (most consequentially during `FLASH_AUCTION_ACTIVE`, where `PLACE_BID`/`FOLD_AUCTION` key off the real sender identity, unlike every other turn-scoped action).
3. After fixing both, a third silent stall persisted with no clear root cause in the shared-timeout mechanism itself — rather than keep chasing it, the whole response-correlation design was replaced with one-shot listeners registered fresh per call and torn down the instant that call settles, eliminating the shared mutable state entirely. Correct on the first try after the rewrite.

None of these three were bugs in the actual game — the unit test suite never exercised `initializeGameState()`'s real board-tile-construction path at all (every test builds its own correct `properties` fixture directly), which is exactly why this specific bug needed a real, full live playtest to surface, not more unit tests.

### Real board content — "Cờ Tỷ Phú Vĩnh Phát" seed data (Phase 13, 2026-08-19)

`backend/supabase/seed/boards.sql` rewritten in full — replaces every placeholder name/price/group with the real classic Vietnamese board's content, closing an item that's been on the "Open design decisions"/"Real board content" list since Phase 02.

**A real conflict surfaced before any seed data was written, resolved via `AskUserQuestion`**: the source material is a classic 40-tile board, but `ADAPTIVE_BOARD_DESIGN.md` locks 36 (Small)/44 (Large) tiles — an explicit, already-approved, already-implemented decision (`movement.js`'s own `APPROVED_TILE_COUNTS = [36, 44]`; `boards.id` even has a DB-level `CHECK (id IN ('small','large'))`), not something a data-seeding task should silently reopen or silently violate. User confirmed: adapt the real content onto the existing 36/44 structure, no core-logic or schema changes — the option that also happens to be the only one matching the task's own "do not rewrite core logic" instruction.

**Small (36)**: all 22 real properties kept — the real property count already matches Small's own locked property count exactly, nothing dropped there. Exactly 1 non-property tile dropped per side (4 total, symmetric with the 9→8 edge-tiles-per-side change): 2 of the 4 real named stations (kept Bến Xe Miền Tây/Miền Đông — the two most widely-recognized Ho Chi Minh City terminals of the four; a flagged judgment call, not a computed answer), plus 1 duplicate Cơ Hội instance and 1 duplicate Khí Vận instance (functionally identical to the ones kept, so nothing content-wise is actually lost).

**Large (44)**: all 36 real edge tiles kept unchanged (Large has *more* room than the source provides, so nothing needed dropping — including both stations Small had to cut) plus 4 placeholder property slots to reach 40 edge tiles, same `"Property 23"`-style naming the prior placeholder file already used for exactly this kind of gap. Consequence, flagged directly in the file's own header: Large's realized category counts (26 property/3 chance/3 fortune/2 tax/4 transport/2 utility) now differ slightly from `ADAPTIVE_BOARD_DESIGN.md`'s original table (28/2/2/3/3/2) — that table was authored for generic placeholder content with no real-world counts of its own; real content doesn't split into a ratio chosen before any real content existed.

**Two economic anchors in the brief corrected, not applied literally — flagged with the exact reasoning in the file itself**: the brief's numbers for Tân Kỳ Tân Quý ($400)/Lũy Bán Bích ($350) — rent $2/$4, house $50, mortgage $30 — are, digit for digit, the real classic Monopoly *cheapest*-tier figures (Mediterranean/Baltic Ave), not a coherent number for the board's two most expensive properties (a $2 rent on a $400 property would make it effectively free to land on). Re-applied instead to Nguyễn Huệ/Lê Lợi (the real $60 "Red" group — an exact price-tier match, not just the anchor), and gave the actual DarkBlue tier the real classic top-tier numbers instead (rent 50/35, house $200, mortgage $200/$175). A second anchor (Trường Chinh/Lê Đại Hành's house $100/mortgage $70,$80) was deliberately *not* overridden — merely non-standard vs. classic Monopoly's own numbers for that tier, not economically incoherent the way the first one was, and a genuine regional edition having its own numbers isn't by itself a reason to second-guess an explicitly-given value. Every other property's rent progression/house cost/mortgage — none given explicitly — uses the real, standard classic Monopoly progression for that exact price point (the brief's own prices already match that table's tiers exactly).

**Reused engine formulas rather than storing anything new**: station rent needed only `base_rent = 25` (no `rent_table`) — `calculateRent.js`'s own `baseRent × 2^(ownedCount−1)` formula, confirmed by reading it, already reproduces the brief's 25/50/100/200 progression exactly from that one value. Utility rent (4×/10× dice by ownership count) is likewise entirely computed by `calculateRent.js` already — utility tiles only needed `price`/`mortgage_value`. Income Tax's "10% of holdings or flat $200, player's choice" was seeded as flat $200 only — a percentage-choice branch would be new core logic (a new decision point in `resolveTile.js`'s `PAYING_TAX` path), out of this task's own explicit "seed data only" scope; flagged, not silently simplified.

**`group_id` populated for the first time** (8 real groups: red/cyan/purple/orange/yellow/green/blue/darkblue) — previously null everywhere in the live seed, which is exactly why `BUILD_HOUSE`'s full-group-ownership check has shown itself permanently disabled on the real board all session (see the `PropertyManager.jsx` slice's own finding, confirmed live earlier this session with an injected `groupId`). No code changes needed for this to start working — already confirmed correct against real preconditions once real group data exists, only the data itself was ever missing.

**Verified structurally before applying, then actually applied to the live database, same day, user confirmed.** Before applying: a local script confirmed, precisely, not by eye: 36/44 rows respectively, positions contiguous 0..N−1 with no duplicates on both boards, category counts and group counts exactly matching the design above, all 80 `board_tiles` data rows carrying exactly the 11 values the column list expects, and the file's real SQL (comments stripped) has balanced parens/quotes and exactly 3 statements. Both `board_tiles` `INSERT`s use `ON CONFLICT (board_id, "position") DO UPDATE`, so applying it cleanly replaced the existing P02-T03 placeholder rows in place (`npx supabase db query --file backend/supabase/seed/boards.sql --linked`, same command/project Phase 11's own seed application used) rather than failing on the table's own `UNIQUE` constraint.

**One real gotcha hit applying it, not a bug in the seed itself**: the already-running backend dev server didn't reflect the change at first — `server.js` reads `board_tiles` and caches `boardTilesByBoard` exactly once at process startup (`app.set('boardTilesByBoard', ...)`), so a server that was already running when the seed was applied keeps serving whatever it read at its own boot time until restarted. Same class of gotcha as findings #27/#28's own live-verification passes this session (a long-lived process not reflecting a change made after it started) — recognized immediately this time from that precedent, fixed with a clean restart. **Confirmed live afterward** via the real `GET /api/v1/boards/:boardId` endpoint against the real database: both boards return the real names/prices/`groupId`/`rentTable` (e.g., position 1 on both boards: `Nguyễn Huệ`, `group red`, `price 60`, `rentTable [10,30,90,160,250]`), 8 distinct real groups on both boards, exactly 0 placeholder-named properties on Small and 4 on Large as designed, and the exact right station subsets on each board (2 on Small, all 4 on Large).

### Phase 14 — Advanced Mechanics: building supply, Free Parking jackpot, Hostile Acquisition (2026-08-19)

A "Senior Backend Architect" brief asking for 3 new mechanics into the existing state machine, with its own closing instruction explicitly inviting pushback before or alongside implementation. All 3 resolved genuinely open items this project's docs had been carrying since Phase 06/07 (`GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §12/§14's own `[OPEN DESIGN DECISION]` tags, `BOARD_SPECIFICATION.md`'s Hostile Acquisition sketch) — implemented per the fresh brief, with every doc updated to CONFIRMED in place (§12/§12a/§14, `BOARD_SPECIFICATION.md`, `ECONOMY_SPECIFICATION.md` §3/§4, `WEBSOCKET_API.md` §1–§3), not left silently contradicting shipped code.

**Building supply scarcity** — `GameState.houseSupply`/`.hotelSupply` (32/12, shared match-wide, not per-player). `BUILD_HOUSE` now checks the relevant pool (`hotelSupply` specifically for a 4th-house→hotel conversion, `houseSupply` otherwise) before charging, rejecting `INSUFFICIENT_SUPPLY` at 0. `SELL_HOUSE` and a property forfeited to the Bank on bankruptcy both return buildings to the pool — bankruptcy's own property-reversion path previously only ever touched `ownerId`, never `upgradeLevel`; fixed alongside this as a directly-related correctness gap (a forfeited property now correctly resets to `upgradeLevel: 0` on Bank-reversion, so the supply count and the property's own displayed state can't disagree) — `mortgaged` deliberately left untouched, out of this specific fix's scope. Ownership transfer alone (Trade, Hostile Acquisition) never touches supply, only an actual build/sell/forfeit does.

**Free Parking jackpot** — `GameState.freeParkingJackpot` (starts 0). The brief's own "instead of disappearing into the Bank" phrasing doesn't survive contact with `applyTransaction`'s real shape (it requires two real `PlayerGameState` ids; there's no "jackpot player" to redirect a payment to) — resolved by keeping tax/jail-fine payments to the Bank byte-for-byte unchanged and adding a second, additive bookkeeping step (`feedFreeParkingJackpot`, called from all three real payment sites: `settleDebt`'s cash branch, `payJailFineOrLiquidate`'s cash branch, and `resolveLiquidationStep` for a tax/fine debt that clears later via liquidation — so a deferred debt still correctly feeds the jackpot once it settles). This isn't an improvised workaround — it's exactly what `ECONOMY_SPECIFICATION.md` §4 had already recommended for this variant before any of this was built ("funded by a real source... an accumulating pool of Tax/Fine payments the Bank already collected"). Landing on a `free_parking` tile with a non-zero jackpot pays the full amount via its own real `applyTransaction` (Bank → player, new type `free_parking_jackpot`) and resets the counter — added as `resolveLanding`'s new `POST_ACTIONS` branch, since `resolveTile.js`'s tile-type dispatch collapses `free_parking` into the same generic `POST_ACTIONS` outcome as several other tile types, so tile-type-specific behavior has to live as an inline check inside the landing resolver, not in the dispatcher itself. Rent payments never feed the jackpot (already a real player-to-player transfer with its own destination).

**Hostile Acquisition** — after paying rent on another player's property, `GameState.pendingHostileBuyoutPropertyId` is set (regardless of which of `settleDebt`'s 3 internal branches the payment took), making a new `HOSTILE_BUYOUT` action legal for the rest of that turn only — cleared unconditionally inside `advanceTurn()` (covers every real path: normal `END_TURN`, the doubles-bonus early return, and a forced jail-fine turn-advance, from one central place rather than scattered per-call-site). Cost is a strict `2.0× (tile.price + property.upgradeLevel × tile.houseCost)`, confirmed to need no hotel special-casing (`calculateBuildHouse` already charges a flat `houseCost` per build regardless of resulting level, so `upgradeLevel × houseCost` already uniformly represents total building spend). Rejected if the target belongs to a color group its owner fully monopolizes (`MONOPOLY_PROTECTED`) or the buyer can't afford it (`INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE`, code reused). Fully unilateral — one direct `applyTransaction` (buyer → owner, type `hostile_acquisition`, Bank uninvolved, same "genuine player-to-player transfer needs `applyTransaction` directly, not the Bank-mediated `applyIntents`" rule finding #24 already established for Trade), ownership transfers, back to `POST_ACTIONS`. **Deliberately supersedes, not layers onto, `BOARD_SPECIFICATION.md`'s older `[PROPOSED]` sketch** (a turn-threshold gate tied to Late-Game Escalation; a two-sided owner defense-fee/response-window handshake) — that sketch was itself still undecided, and the fresh brief was explicit and detailed enough to treat as superseding rather than something to silently reconcile or silently ignore; the divergence was flagged in prose before writing any code, and both docs now carry a struck-through record of what was considered and not adopted, not a silent deletion.

**DB**: one new migration, `0004_free_parking_jackpot_transaction_type.sql` — adds `'free_parking_jackpot'` to `game_transactions.transaction_type`'s CHECK constraint (looked up by real constraint name via `pg_constraint`/`pg_get_constraintdef`, not hardcoded, since Postgres's own auto-generated name for an unnamed inline CHECK isn't guaranteed stable across tooling/versions). `hostile_acquisition`/`rent_gamble` needed no migration — `0001_core_tables.sql` already anticipated both, pre-emptively, from the start. ~~Written and verified, not yet applied to the live database~~ — **APPLIED 2026-08-21**, on explicit user go-ahead ("ừ chạy đi"), same `npx supabase db query --file ... --linked` command/project (`qpbyfgzujwzueprzuurk`) the board-content seed used. Confirmed live via a direct follow-up query against `pg_get_constraintdef` — the real constraint now reads `CHECK ((transaction_type = ANY (ARRAY[..., 'free_parking_jackpot'::text])))`, all 17 values present. No remaining migration gap for Phase 14.

**Tests**: 20 new in `turnMachine.test.js` (every branch of all 3 mechanics — supply exhaustion for both houses and hotels, both directions of hotel-conversion supply accounting, both bankruptcy-to-Bank-returns-buildings and bankruptcy-to-player-leaves-them-alone, jackpot feeding from tax/jail-fine/deferred-liquidation but never rent, jackpot payout and the no-op zero-jackpot case, and all 4 `HOSTILE_BUYOUT` outcomes including the cleared-on-`END_TURN` case) + 1 in `gameState.test.js` (the 4 new fields' defaults and explicit overrides). **498/498 backend tests passing** (478 + 20 — the 478 baseline itself included clearing an unrelated false-positive: a stray, already-obsolete `scripts/test-create-room.mjs` scratch file from earlier in this session was being auto-discovered by `node --test`'s filename heuristic and deleted as debris, not a real regression). Two self-caught issues during implementation, neither ever shipped: an `advanceTurn()` edit that cleared `pendingHostileBuyoutPropertyId` into a new `state` variable but left one downstream line still spreading the stale original `gameState` (caught by re-reading the diff immediately after editing, before any test ran — would have silently undone the fix for the most common turn-advance path had it shipped); and the stray-file false positive just mentioned.

~~**Not done in the backend-only pass itself**~~: frontend UI for all 3 mechanics — **DONE 2026-08-20**, see "Phase 14 frontend slice" below. ~~Applying migration 0004 to the live database~~ — **DONE 2026-08-21**, see the DB paragraph above.

### Phase 14 frontend slice — supply/jackpot display + `HOSTILE_BUYOUT` button (2026-08-20)

Closes the frontend half of Phase 14's own "Not done" note above, same day as a user follow-up ("giờ làm gì tiếp"). No new components — extended the two existing components that already own this territory, matching this project's established "extend, don't multiply overlays" convention from the Win Condition frontend slice.

- **`PropertyManager.jsx`**: a new `.supplyRow` line ("🏠 Nhà còn: N · 🏨 Khách sạn còn: M"), shown only for `tile.tileType === 'property'` (supply is meaningless on a transport/utility panel), placed directly above the action button grid. **`buildReason()` gained the one client-side check this component's own precedent (the "full mirror of `turnMachine.js`'s real preconditions, not just examples" rule set during P11-T09) obligated but Phase 14's backend-only pass never added**: `INSUFFICIENT_SUPPLY`, branching the same way `handleBuildHouse` does — `property.upgradeLevel === MAX_UPGRADE_LEVEL - 1` (a build about to convert to a hotel) checks `hotelSupply`, otherwise `houseSupply`.
- **`GameControls.jsx`**: a persistent jackpot badge ("🅿️ Jackpot Bãi Đỗ Xe: $N"), same visual pattern as the existing Final Phase badge (a differently-colored pill), shown whenever `freeParkingJackpot > 0` and hidden at 0 — no dismiss state needed, it's not one-shot like `FinalPhaseBanner.jsx`. The `POST_ACTIONS` block's single "Kết Thúc Lượt" button became a row: a new "⚔️ Cưỡng Đoạt" button appears alongside it whenever `pendingHostileBuyoutPropertyId` is non-null, computing a full client-side preview (cost, and the same full-group-monopoly-ownership check `groupHoldingsFor` uses server-side) reproduced locally rather than imported, same "no shared package" standing as every other mirrored constant in this file. `HOSTILE_BUYOUT`'s payload is genuinely `{}` — this button's preview computation is display-only, nothing it computes is ever sent.
- **Live-verified via store injection** (same technique established across every P11 frontend slice this session): a fake in-progress game with `houseSupply: 3, hotelSupply: 0`, a property at `upgradeLevel: 4` (about to hotel-convert), `freeParkingJackpot: 350`, and `pendingHostileBuyoutPropertyId` pointing at an opponent-owned property in a 2-tile group they only partly own — confirmed the jackpot badge showed the exact amount, the supply line showed the exact counts, `Xây Nhà` was correctly disabled with title `"Hết khách sạn trong kho chung"` (proving the new supply check fires, not just the pre-existing affordability one), and `Cưỡng Đoạt` showed the exact `2×(price+upgradeLevel×houseCost)` cost and was enabled. Then flipped the group's other tile to the same opponent (full monopoly) and confirmed the button correctly flipped to disabled with title `"Ô này thuộc nhóm độc quyền, không thể cưỡng đoạt"`. Then cleared both fields to their empty defaults and confirmed the button disappeared entirely and the badge hid, while `Kết Thúc Lượt` remained. Reset store to `null`/`null` and hard-reloaded back to the clean lobby afterward, same courtesy as every prior slice. `npm run lint`/`npm run build` both clean (the one pre-existing `AuthContext.jsx` warning, unrelated, unchanged).
- **One tool-level hiccup, not a code issue**: two `javascript_tool` calls that mutated the store via `Object.assign`-style spreads returned `"Promise was collected"` instead of a result — retried with an equivalent plain-object-spread (`{ ...gs, ... }`) script and it worked immediately, so this reads as a transient tool/eval quirk rather than anything wrong with the page or the store; worth a retry-with-simpler-syntax first instinct if this recurs, not a debugging rabbit hole.
- **Still not done**: any real 2-player browser run of these 3 mechanics against a genuinely live backend + second real account (this session's whole Phase 14 verification, backend and frontend both, has been unit tests + single-tab store injection — same standing gap this project's docs have flagged for every other feature, not new here).

### Backlog batch — 7 items in one pass, 2026-08-21

User asked to bundle the remaining backlog ("gom hết lại làm 1 lần") rather than pick items one at a time. Worked through it sequentially, same rigor as every other slice this project's history uses (read the real code first, implement, test, verify against the full suite, document) — flagged two items as needing the user's own decision rather than guessed: the REST `Idempotency-Key` header mechanism (`API_CONTRACT.md`'s own words: "still-undesigned... needs your approval") was skipped entirely, not attempted blind.

**1. `LIQUIDATION_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (Win Condition design §E2, designed 2026-08-19, wired here)**: `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` previously had no timeout at all — a debtor who never acted could stall the match forever. `stateMachine/timers.js`'s `buildDefaultAction` gained a `boardTiles` parameter (a real signature change, needed since this is the first default action requiring property/tile data) and a new `buildLiquidationDefaultAction` helper: builds every currently-*valid* `SELL_HOUSE`/`MORTGAGE` target across the debtor's whole portfolio (correctly respecting the even-sell rule and per-group `GROUP_HAS_HOUSES`, not just "sell before mortgage" — a player can own one group with houses and a separate house-free group at the same time, and the cheaper liquidation could be in either), picks the single cheapest one regardless of action type — matching the approved design's own "cheapest... first" wording. 45s duration. `socketServer.js`'s `handleTurnTimeout` reordered so `boardTiles` is computed before `buildDefaultAction` is called (previously the other way around). 5 new tests (`timers.test.js`), including a defensive throw-test for the (structurally unreachable per `checkSolvency`'s own precondition) nothing-left-to-liquidate case.

**2. `match_results` persistence (Win Condition design, designed 2026-08-19, wired here)**: the table/columns/RLS existed since Phase 02, nothing ever wrote to it. New `gameRepository.js` export `saveMatchResult` — maps `GameState.endReason` (`'elimination'|'final_phase'`) to the schema's own `result_type` enum (`'elimination_win'|'net_worth_win'`; `'aborted'` has no mapping, since nothing sets that `endReason` value yet — §18's "Abandoned" ending is still unbuilt, throws rather than guesses if ever asked), winner is whichever player has `finalRank === 1`. Called once from `socketServer.js`'s `persistAndBroadcast`, right alongside the existing snapshot save, in its own separate try/catch (two independent writes to two different tables — either can fail without blocking the other or the broadcast). `onConflict: 'game_id'` keeps it safely re-runnable, matching `saveSnapshot`'s own upsert posture, even though the new `GAME_ALREADY_FINISHED` guard already makes a second real call structurally unreachable. 7 new tests (6 unit + 1 full-socket-pipeline integration proving `handleGameAction` really calls it, not just that the function works in isolation).

**3. `idempotencyKey` collision (finding #15/#20/#24, open since Phase 01-07, fixed here)**: `economy/applyTransaction.js`'s own `idempotencyKey` (`${gameId}:${stateVersion}`) is computed from `stateVersion` while it's still *this action's* old value for `transitionFn`'s entire call — so any action producing more than one real transaction (a two-sided `ACCEPT_TRADE`, Flash Auction settlement + Near-Miss rewards) handed back several transactions sharing the identical key. Not yet observable in production (no real `game_transactions` write exists yet — P13), but would silently collide once one does. **Fixed centrally in `stateMachine/idempotency.js`'s `applyWithIdempotency`** — the one place that already sees every transaction one external action produced, as a single array, in call order — rather than threading a per-call sequence number through every `applyTransaction` call site across `turnMachine.js`/`tradeMachine.js`: each transaction's `idempotencyKey` gets its own array-index suffix appended (`...:0`, `...:1`, ...), unique within the action and still globally unique across actions (the base key already differs per action). 3 new tests; one pre-existing test's fake transaction fixture needed a realistic `idempotencyKey` field added (it had none, so the fix's `${undefined}:0` would have silently passed a `deepEqual` against a similarly-wrong expectation — caught and given a real fixture value instead).

**4. AFK/reconnect (`GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §24/§25, both `[PROPOSED]` since the architecture doc, partially wired here)**: two distinct pieces.
   - **`RECONNECT_GRACE_SECONDS`'s real gap, closed** — not built as its own standalone 90s disconnect timer (which would have been strictly redundant: every phase timer is already shorter and already fires "regardless of connection status," §5). Instead closed the one real hole that made it seem necessary: `TURN_START` had no `TIMER_DURATIONS_SECONDS` entry at all, so a player disconnecting (or just idling) at the exact start of their own turn could stall the match forever — nothing else ever forces a `START_TURN`. Added `TURN_START: 15` + a trivial default (`{ type: 'START_TURN' }`, no real decision to synthesize). Every phase is now covered.
   - **`AFK_THRESHOLD_MISSED_TURNS`, partially wired** — new `PlayerGameState.missedTurnStreak` (raw counter, not a derived boolean), incremented specifically when a player's `POST_ACTIONS→END_TURN` was system-synthesized (a new `action.isSystemDefault` flag, set only by `handleTurnTimeout`, checked in `turnMachine.js`'s `END_TURN` case before `advanceTurn()` moves `currentTurnIndex` off the outgoing player), reset to 0 on a genuine click. **Deliberately narrower than the full spec, flagged in both `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` and the field's own JSDoc**: only the dominant `POST_ACTIONS` timeout path is tracked (not the rarer forced-3rd-jail-roll-failure or 3rd-consecutive-double turn-enders); and reaching the threshold does not yet make future turns resolve any faster — the "instant skip, no fresh grace period" behavior needs new trigger infrastructure this pass didn't build. 5 new tests (2 timer, 3 turnMachine).

**5. `STALE_ACTION` (`GAME_STATE_MACHINE.md` §6 point 2 / `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §26, `[PROPOSED]` since the architecture doc, wired here)**: "a client sends an old/stale action... rejected... where that matters (target-specific actions)" had no wire-contract field to even carry the check — `WEBSOCKET_API.md`'s `C2S_GAME_ACTION` never defined one. Added a genuinely **optional** `lastSeenStateVersion` field, checked uniformly whenever present (rejected with `STALE_ACTION` unless it exactly matches `gameState.stateVersion`) rather than guessing which specific `actionType`s count as "target-specific" enough to need it — a client opts in by including the field at all, backward compatible with every existing client/test that never sends it (nothing needed updating). 3 new tests (`socketServer.test.js`): stale rejected, matching accepted, omitted-entirely unaffected.

**6. `leave_room`/`kick_player` REST endpoints (`GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §4, `[CONFIRMED]` rules since Phase 04, `SECURITY_DESIGN.md`-adjacent "Known gaps" #2, no endpoint until now)**: `POST /:id/leave` (`API_CONTRACT.md` already fully specified this one, just unbuilt) and `POST /:id/kick` (new to this document — `API_CONTRACT.md` never had an entry at all; modeled on `leave`'s own already-approved shape). Both host-transfer-on-leave (to the next-joined remaining player) and real player removal.

   **Found and fixed a real, previously-latent bug getting there, not by code review — by the first test actually asserting on it**: `roomRepository.js`'s `updateRoom` had *never* supported removing a `room_players` row or changing `rooms.host_id`, even though its own JSDoc already (incorrectly) implied the latter was just out of scope by design. It only ever updated existing rows' `is_ready` or inserted new joiners — any player computed as "removed" in the caller's intended new list was silently left in the database forever. This was invisible until now because no prior caller (`joinRoom`, `setReady`) ever needed real removal — both only ever grow or modify the list, never shrink it. **Fixed `updateRoom` itself** (now genuinely deletes rows absent from the new list, and writes `host_id` when the caller supplies a changed one) rather than working around it in the two new controllers, since any future caller needing real removal would have hit the identical silent-no-op. `testUtils/fakeSupabase.js` (shared across every repository's tests) gained `.delete()` support, since nothing in this codebase had ever needed it before. 10 new controller tests, plus this fix's own coverage folded into 2 of them (a genuinely-removed-and-reconfirmed-gone assertion, not just "the call didn't throw").

**7. Rent Risk Choice (`BOARD_SPECIFICATION.md`, *proposed* since Phase 06, `[CONFIRMED]`/shipped here)** — the last remaining `[PROPOSED]` mechanic from Part 2, and the biggest single piece of this batch: a genuinely new interactive decision, not a gap-fill. `resolveLanding`'s `PAYING_RENT` case no longer settles immediately — whenever the landed-on tile has a real (non-Bank) owner, it now enters a new `RENT_RISK_DECISION` phase (new `GameState.pendingRentRiskChoice: { propertyId, ownerId, payerId, standardAmount }`) and waits for the *owner* — not the visiting/current-turn player, the same "acting player isn't necessarily `getCurrentPlayer`" exception Flash Auction already established — to send a new `RENT_RISK_CHOICE` action (`{ choice: 'STANDARD' | 'GAMBLE' }`). `STANDARD` settles the already-computed guaranteed amount exactly as rent always worked before; `GAMBLE` is a server-rolled 50/50 double-or-zero (`action.payload.gambleRoll`, server-generated only, same `serverGeneratedFields` treatment finding #27 already established for `ROLL_DICE`/`MAKE_EVENT_CHOICE`) — a losing roll settles no transaction at all, both because it's trivially zero-sum and because `applyTransaction` itself rejects a non-positive amount. `pendingHostileBuyoutPropertyId` eligibility (Phase 14) now gets set from `handleRentRiskChoice` once the amount is actually known, instead of unconditionally right after landing.

   **Two open questions from the original proposal, resolved with reasoned defaults, both flagged in `BOARD_SPECIFICATION.md` itself, not silently picked**: a 15s timer (matching `AWAITING_PURCHASE`'s own "one player decides, short window" shape), defaulting to the safe `STANDARD` choice on timeout (the proposal's own "probably yes, to avoid stalling") — never defaults *into* the gamble, same loss-averse-default posture every other timed choice in this codebase already uses. And: applies to **every** ownable tile, not narrowed to transport/utility as the proposal's own example suggested — the general reading needed zero extra tile-type-gating code (composes with `resolveTile.js`'s existing dispatch as-is) and fits the mechanic's own stated "fast-paced multiplayer interactions" goal better than restricting it to ~6 tiles out of 36-44.

   A real actor-identity subtlety, caught while implementing, not left implicit: the system-synthesized STANDARD-on-timeout default carries no `playerId` at all (unlike a real player action, `handleTurnTimeout` never injects one) — `handleRentRiskChoice`'s own `NOT_OWNER` check is skipped specifically when `action.isSystemDefault` is set, mirroring exactly how `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED`'s own default synthesis needed no actor identity either.

   **3 pre-existing tests broke and were fixed, not papered over** — each had assumed rent settles in one step (straight to `POST_ACTIONS`); each now drives the real two-step flow (land → assert `RENT_RISK_DECISION` → send a real `RENT_RISK_CHOICE` → assert the original expected outcome), rather than being loosened to tolerate either behavior. 11 new dedicated tests (7 `turnMachine.test.js`: gamble win/lose, `NOT_OWNER`, unknown `choice`, system-default bypass; 2 `timers.test.js`; 2 `socketServer.test.js`: server-generated `gambleRoll` discards a client-claimed one, and the owner-not-current-player exemption from finding #30's turn-ownership guard).

**Not built, flagged as explicit follow-ons**: ~~any frontend UI for Rent Risk Choice's owner decision~~ — **DONE 2026-08-21**, see "Frontend slice — `RentRiskChoice.jsx`" below (backend-only pass at the time, same scope boundary Phase 14's backend slice used before its own follow-on frontend slice); the REST `Idempotency-Key` header mechanism (genuinely skipped, needs the user's own decision per `API_CONTRACT.md`'s own words, not guessed); AFK's "instant skip" latency optimization (see item 4 above).

**Live playtest, same day, right after — a throwaway bot script (`backend/scripts/`, real Supabase accounts, real REST + Socket.IO, deleted after use) driving 3 real accounts through the live backend.** Not through the actual React UI (no new frontend UI exists yet for this batch's own mechanics either) — same standing as every prior live-verification pass in this project, real server/real database/real socket protocol, just not the rendered browser.

- **Confirmed working, cleanly**: `leave_room` (a real member left, room correctly shrank to 2); `kick_player` (self-kick correctly rejected `CANNOT_KICK_HOST`, a real kick of the 3rd account correctly removed them); `STALE_ACTION` (a deliberately-wrong `lastSeenStateVersion` correctly rejected before any dispatch); a real 2-player match played 120 real actions across ~11-12 rounds with zero server crashes, zero unhandled exceptions.
- **`RENT_RISK_DECISION` fired live, repeatedly** (5+ times across the run) — the owner correctly got the decision (not the visiting/current-turn player), both `STANDARD` and `GAMBLE` choices were exercised for real against real balances, amounts matched the real rent formula each time.
- **`LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` fired live** for the first time ever against this backend (a real debt the debtor's cash alone couldn't cover) — correctly stayed in that phase rather than crashing or silently failing when the (deliberately dumb) bot's own liquidation logic couldn't find a fully-clearing sequence of mortgages, ending the run with the debtor legitimately $7 short rather than the script forcing a fake resolution. **Not the same as verifying `LIQUIDATION_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`'s real 45s firing** — the bot kept actively (if unproductively) sending actions the whole time, so the timeout itself never got the chance to fire in this run; that specific mechanism's correctness rests on its own unit/integration test coverage (`timers.test.js`), not this live pass.
- **One real hiccup, environment-only, not a code bug**: the dev server (already running, started before this batch) needed restarting with `--watch` to pick up the day's changes — same "a long-lived process doesn't reflect a later edit" lesson this project has hit multiple times before, recognized and fixed immediately from precedent. A one-time `ECONNRESET` on the very first request was `--watch` restarting mid-request as it picked up the change; the retry succeeded cleanly. One pre-existing, already-known `profiles`-auto-creation-trigger race (a `rooms_host_id_fkey` violation) surfaced once in the server log from that same aborted first attempt — not a new regression, not chased further.
- **A real bug in the throwaway script itself, not the game — found and fixed before trusting the run's own output, same discipline this project's very first playtest script established**: `Promise.race`'s losing side never cleaned up its own socket listener, so a stale listener could catch a later, unrelated rejection and misattribute it in the script's own console output. Fixed (explicit cancellation of both race branches the instant either settles) before treating any of this run's specific per-action logging as reliable.

This closes the "Backlog batch" — all 7 items shipped, tested (unit + integration), documented, and now also live-verified against a real server for the pieces that specifically needed real multi-actor interaction to exercise at all.

**544/544 backend tests passing**, starting from 502 before this batch: 502→507 (item 1, `LIQUIDATION_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, +5)→514 (item 2, `match_results`, +7)→517 (item 3, `idempotencyKey`, +3)→522 (item 4, AFK/`TURN_START`, +5)→532 (item 6, `leave`/`kick`, +10)→535 (item 5, `STALE_ACTION`, +3)→544 (item 7, Rent Risk Choice, +9). Full suite re-run after every single item, not just at the end — every regression this batch caused (3, all from Rent Risk Choice's own settlement-timing change to 3 pre-existing tests) was caught and fixed the same pass it was introduced, never left for a later cleanup.

### Frontend slice — `RentRiskChoice.jsx` (Rent Risk Choice UI, 2026-08-21)

Closes "Backlog batch" item 7's own frontend follow-on (backend shipped/live-verified same day, no UI existed yet) — same "backend first, frontend slice after" pattern Phase 14 used, picked as the highest-value next task over the other open items (a real design-doc read + user go-ahead, not guessed).

**`frontend/src/features/game/RentRiskChoice.jsx` + `.module.css`** (new), mounted in `GameView.jsx` alongside the other three full-modal overlays. Verified against `turnMachine.js`/`WEBSOCKET_API.md` directly before writing anything (this project's own established discipline): `RENT_RISK_CHOICE`'s payload is genuinely `{ choice: 'STANDARD' | 'GAMBLE' }` only — `gambleRoll` is server-generated (`serverGeneratedFields`, finding #27's own precedent), so nothing here computes or sends one. `gameState.pendingRentRiskChoice` is `{ propertyId, ownerId, payerId, standardAmount }`, `ownerId`/`payerId` in the same `PlayerGameState.id` space every other pending-* field already uses.

- **Architecture choice, not specified anywhere, decided by analogy**: unlike `PropertyActionDrawer.jsx`'s single-actor corner drawer, this is a fourth full-viewport modal (z-index 46, `RentRiskChoice.module.css` — above `TradeWindow`'s 40 and `EventCardModal`'s 45, below `FlashAuction`'s 50). Reasoning: the deciding player (the property owner) is never the current-turn player — the same "acting player isn't necessarily `getCurrentPlayer`" case `PLACE_BID`/`FOLD_AUCTION` already established — and the phase gates the whole match (`VALID_ACTIONS_BY_PHASE.RENT_RISK_DECISION` has no other legal action) with two distinct invested players (the owner deciding, the payer waiting to see if they'll be gambled against), closer to Flash Auction's "whole room needs to see this" than to a single-actor decision. `GameControls.jsx`'s `HANDLED_PHASES` gained `'RENT_RISK_DECISION'`, matching `AWAITING_PURCHASE`/`AWAITING_EVENT_CHOICE`'s precedent (`FLASH_AUCTION_ACTIVE`'s own "left in the fallback list, covered by the opaque backdrop" exception was not repeated here — no reason to rely on implicit DOM-covering when the explicit-exclusion pattern is already the majority convention).
- Renders one of three views depending on the local player's role: the owner sees the property card (name/color/icon, reusing `tileVisuals.js`) plus "Nhận chắc chắn ($standardAmount)" / "🎲 Đánh cược (50%: $2×standardAmount · 50%: $0)" buttons; the payer sees a personalized "waiting for the owner to decide *your* rent" line; any other player sees the generic "waiting for the owner to decide" line. All three verified live (below).
- **Found and fixed a real, previously-unnoticed frontend bug while building this** (not by code review — by checking the property card's rendered color against the real seed data): `frontend/src/features/board/tileVisuals.js`'s `GROUP_COLORS` still keyed on the original placeholder slugs (`'group-1'`..`'group-8'`) from before real board content existed — the real seed applied 2026-08-19 uses `'red'/'cyan'/'purple'/'orange'/'yellow'/'green'/'blue'/'darkblue'` (`backend/supabase/seed/boards.sql`, confirmed via `boardRepository.js`'s direct `group_id` passthrough, no transform). The placeholder keys never matched any real tile's `groupId`, so **every property's color header has silently fallen back to the neutral default background on the live board since the real seed went live** — on every consumer of this shared file (`BoardTile.jsx`, `FlashAuction.jsx`, `PropertyActionDrawer.jsx`, `PropertyManager.jsx`, `TradeOfferColumn.jsx`, and now this component), not just this new one. `tileVisuals.js`'s own header comment had already flagged this exact TODO ("swap it for real values once `BOARD_SPECIFICATION.md`'s open item resolves") — the open item resolved two days ago and the comment was never revisited, same "stale comment outlives the blocker it described" shape as finding #29's `properties: []`. Fixed by remapping to the 8 real slugs with literal color-matched hex values (a `red` group now actually renders red, etc.) — corrected directly rather than left broken, since the answer was unambiguous (read the real seed file). Confirmed live via `getComputedStyle` on a real seeded property ("Nguyễn Huệ", group `red`): header background is `rgb(211, 47, 47)` (`#d32f2f`), matching the new mapping exactly.
- **Verified live, same store-injection technique as every other frontend slice this session** (`e2e-smoke-host@cobactyphu.dev`, a real room created via the real REST API — no second real account was needed since role-switching only requires editing the injected `pendingRentRiskChoice`/`properties[].ownerId`, not two authenticated sessions): all three roles (owner / the payer themself / an uninvolved third player) rendered the exact expected view and copy, with real `standardAmount`/`gambleAmount` values reflected correctly (`$10` standard / `$20` gamble, `$15`, `$25` across the three injected scenarios). A real `RENT_RISK_CHOICE` fired through the real authenticated socket on a real "Nhận chắc chắn" click (busy-lock engaged, then cleared on response) and was correctly rejected `ROOM_NOT_IN_PROGRESS` by the real backend (the underlying room genuinely never left `waiting_for_players` — same verification depth `GameControls.jsx`'s own `ROLL_DICE` slice used for the identical reason, P11-T06 above). Confirmed `GameControls.jsx`'s own per-turn status line (`"Đang chờ lượt của…"`) and the shared `lastError` line both still render underneath/alongside without a stray "chưa có giao diện điều khiển" fallback ever appearing, in both the payer's-own-turn and not-my-turn cases. Store reset to `null`/`null` and hard-reloaded back to the clean lobby afterward, same courtesy as every prior slice. `npm run lint`/`npm run build` both clean (131 modules, up from 113; the one pre-existing `AuthContext.jsx` warning, unrelated, unchanged).
- **Not a bug, worth recording**: the Lobby/Diagnostic screen briefly shows "🔴 Offline" for a moment right after login or a hard reload, before the socket handshake completes — confirmed this is just `connectionStatus`'s real `'disconnected'`→`'connected'` transition (network tab shows the real Socket.IO polling handshake succeeding a moment later), not a connectivity fault, while re-testing this session.
- No backend changes — this was a frontend-only slice, `backend/` untouched, 544/544 still the real count.

### Backlog batch #2 — Lobby push, event-card content, board animation, live 2-account verification (2026-08-21)

User asked to bundle the remaining "Recommended next" items into one more pass ("ok tiến hành đi làm hết 1 lần"), same framing as the original "Backlog batch." Four items shipped; explicitly skipped the items that need the user's own decision rather than guessing (same discipline as the original batch's own `Idempotency-Key` skip) — see the updated "Recommended next" list below for exactly what's still open and why.

**1. Lobby real-time push (`S2C_ROOM_UPDATED`)** — full wire contract documented in `WEBSOCKET_API.md` §6, not duplicated here. Wired into all five room-mutating REST endpoints (`join`/`ready`/`start`/`leave`/`kick`), not just the two `API_CONTRACT.md` originally named — every one of them changes what an already-connected lobby member is looking at, same underlying gap. `server.js` now captures `initSocketServer()`'s return value and does `app.set('io', io)`, the same DI pattern `boardTilesByBoard` already established, so `room.controller.js`'s plain REST handlers can reach the one real `io` instance. 5 new backend tests (`room.controller.test.js`, one per endpoint, asserting the exact emitted `roomId`/`roomStatus`, not just "something fired"). Frontend: `gameStore.js` gained `roomUpdatedAt`/`applyRoomUpdated`, `socketClient.js` listens and merges `roomStatus` into `roomState`, `Lobby.jsx` re-fetches immediately on the bump (3s poll kept as a fallback, not replaced). 549/549 backend tests at this point.

**A real, previously-unknown second gap found live-verifying this, fixed the same pass**: making `App.jsx` correctly switch to `GameView` the instant `roomStatus` reaches `in_progress` turned out to be only half the fix — `GameView`'s own children (`GameControls.jsx`/`GameBoard.jsx`) both render nothing until `currentGameState` exists, and nothing was ever emitting `C2S_RECONNECT` for a socket that had been sitting in the lobby continuously connected the whole time (Socket.IO's `'connect'` event, the only place `C2S_RECONNECT` was emitted, doesn't re-fire just because `roomStatus` changed — it only fires on a genuine transport (re)connect). Every player, including the host who clicked Start themselves, would have hit a blank `GameView` with no way to ever get their first `S2C_STATE_UPDATE`, until their socket happened to drop and reconnect for an unrelated reason. **Fixed in `socketClient.js`'s own `S2C_ROOM_UPDATED` handler**: when the pushed `roomStatus` is `in_progress` and `currentGameState` is still `null`, it now emits `C2S_RECONNECT` itself — covers every player uniformly (the push is sender-included, so the host's own socket runs the identical code path, no special-casing needed), guarded to fire exactly once per match. No dedicated automated test (this project still has no frontend test runner — same standing gap as every other frontend-only fix here); verified live instead, see item 4 below.

**2. Event card content (`GET /api/v1/event-cards`)** — closes `EventCardModal.jsx`'s own long-flagged drift risk: `frontend/src/features/game/eventCardDictionary.js` was a hand-maintained mirror of `backend/src/domain/eventDictionary.js`'s `EVENT_CARDS`, shipped on explicit instruction (P11-T11) because no REST/socket path exposed the real content at all. New `backend/src/api/controllers/eventCard.controller.js` + `eventCard.routes.js`, mounted at `/api/v1/event-cards` behind auth (same "static reference content, still not public" reasoning `board.controller.js` already established) — returns `{ cards: EVENT_CARDS }` verbatim, no filtering. `frontend/src/network/api.js` gained `getEventCards()`; `EventCardModal.jsx` fetches it on mount instead of importing the static mirror, which is deleted outright (not migrated, same "stopgap removed once the real fix lands" precedent `clientDice.js` already set for finding #27).

**Also closed the INSTANT-card half of the same gap, not just the CHOICE half**: `GameState` previously only ever recorded which card was drawn for the `CHOICE` case (`pendingEventCardId`) — an `INSTANT` card resolves synchronously inside the same action that drew it, so its identity was never retained anywhere, and the toast could only ever show the resulting money movement, never real text. New `GameState.lastDrawnEventCardId`, set by `turnMachine.js`'s `resolveDrawingCard()` on *both* branches (not just `CHOICE`), reset to `null` at `TURN_START` alongside `lastRollWasDouble` so a fresh turn never shows a stale draw. **One deliberately-not-covered edge case, documented in `domain/gameState.js`'s own JSDoc, not silently glossed over**: a 3rd-consecutive-double roll's `sentToJail` short-circuit in `moveAndResolve()` doesn't apply here (dice, not cards) — see item 3 below for the equivalent dice-side gap, which *does* touch this exact code path. 2 new backend tests (`eventCard.controller.test.js`) + 2 new assertions in existing `turnMachine.test.js` DRAWING_CARD tests. `EventCardModal.jsx`'s instant-card toast now shows the real card text when available, with the old "server doesn't send card content" caveat line removed (no longer true) and its now-dead CSS rule (`.toastCaveat`) deleted alongside it.

**3. Board animation — sliding tokens + real dice display** — the last item on this list's own long-standing "positions/tokens already update correctly, just without motion" note.

- **`GameState.lastRoll`** (new): the current player's real `{ die1, die2, total, isDouble }`, set by `moveAndResolve()` (shared by `ROLL_DICE` and a jail-escape `ATTEMPT_JAIL_ROLL`), reset to `null` at `TURN_START`. Exists only because, before this, the two individual die faces never reached the client in *any* form — `GameState` only ever carried the derived `lastRollWasDouble`/`currentDoublesStreak` booleans. **Same documented, deliberately-not-covered edge case as `lastDrawnEventCardId` above, this time the original one**: a 3rd-consecutive-double's `sentToJail` short-circuit does *not* set `lastRoll` — `advanceTurn()` immediately cascades into the *next* player's `startTurn()` within that same transition, which would reset it to `null` before any broadcast could show it, so setting it there would just be dead code. Judged not worth carrying a "just-jailed player's last roll" fact across a turn boundary for a rare edge case (three consecutive doubles) versus the dominant case (every ordinary roll, every doubles-bonus continuation) this field correctly covers. 3 new backend tests, including one that asserts this exact null-after-jailing behavior directly rather than leaving it unverified.
- **`GameControls.jsx`**: a new dice display (real Unicode die-face glyphs ⚀-⚅, a direct mapping of the real integers, not invented content) plus a "Đôi!" badge when `lastRoll.isDouble`, shown to every player for as long as the field stays non-null — the rest of that turn, per its own lifecycle. Re-keyed by `stateVersion` so its pop-in CSS animation replays on every fresh roll — the exact same key-to-replay-an-animation technique `FlashAuction.module.css`'s `.bidAmount` already established, not a new pattern.
- **Player tokens moved out of `BoardTile.jsx`'s per-tile rendering into a new absolutely-positioned overlay in `GameBoard.jsx`** — the actual reason no motion existed before: CSS Grid has no way to animate a `grid-row`/`grid-column` change (they're not interpolatable), so a token that's a grid-item child of "whichever tile currently owns it" can only ever teleport. The overlay positions each token with plain `left`/`top` percentages computed from the same `computeGridPosition()` tile placement already uses, so the two can never disagree about where a board position sits; a percentage change on a persistent, stably-`key`ed DOM node is something a CSS `transition` handles natively (`left`/`top`/`transform`, `GameBoard.module.css`'s new `.token` rule) — no animation library, matching this project's deliberately-minimal-dependencies stance. Multiple players sharing one tile are spread with a small per-index `transform: translateX()` offset, animated independently of the `left`/`top` transition. `PLAYER_COLORS`/`playerColor()`/`playerInitial()` moved from `BoardTile.jsx` into `tileVisuals.js` (same "plain constants, not a component" reasoning already documented there) so `GameBoard.jsx` can import them without tripping `oxlint`'s `react(only-export-components)` rule.
- **A second real, previously-unnoticed bug found and fixed while touching this same shared file**: `tileVisuals.js`'s `GROUP_COLORS` was already fixed to the real `red`/`cyan`/`purple`/... slugs earlier this session (see the `RentRiskChoice.jsx` slice above) — confirmed still correct, not re-broken by this move.
- No new backend tests needed for this item (frontend/CSS only); verified live instead, see item 4.

**4. Live verification — a real 2-account run over real sockets, plus real-browser DOM checks** — the closest this session got to the standing "real 2-player playtest through the actual browser UI" gap, without the shared-`localStorage`-per-origin problem a genuine 2-tab-same-browser test can't get around (`e2e-smoke-{host,guest}@cobactyphu.dev`, same fixed test accounts every prior live-verification pass in this project's history has used). Two complementary passes, both against the real running backend (restarted mid-session via its own `--watch` supervisor to pick up today's code — same "long-lived process, needs a restart to reflect new code" lesson this project has hit repeatedly, recognized immediately from precedent, including one transient `ECONNRESET` on the very first request, same as before):

- **A throwaway Node script** (`frontend/scripts/`, real `socket.io-client`, deleted after use, not committed — placed under `frontend/` rather than this project's usual `backend/scripts/` purely because that's where the dependency already lives) drove both accounts through the real REST + Socket.IO flow with **two independently-connected sockets watching each other**, not a single actor: confirmed the host's already-connected socket receives `S2C_ROOM_UPDATED` live when the guest joins via REST (no polling involved), confirmed the payload's `roomStatus` is correct at every step (`waiting_for_players` → `ready_check` → `in_progress`), and — the specific gap this batch's own headline feature exists to close — confirmed the **guest**, a non-acting player, receives `roomStatus: 'in_progress'` live the instant the host starts the match via REST. Also confirmed both sockets' initial resync (`C2S_RECONNECT`, simulating what the now-fixed `socketClient.js` does automatically) and drove real `ROLL_DICE` actions through real turn order (resolved via the real shuffled `PlayerGameState.playerId`/`turnOrder`, not array position — same id-space lesson this project's first live playtest already learned) confirming `gameState.lastRoll` carries real die faces in a real broadcast. 15/15 checks passed. Event-card draw specifically wasn't observed in this particular short run (probabilistic — not every short sequence of rolls lands on a chance/fortune tile); not chased further given the mechanism already has direct backend unit coverage and the REST endpoint was independently confirmed serving real content.
- **The real browser** (`e2e-smoke-host`, same store-injection technique as every prior frontend slice — hit the same orphaned-module-instance wrinkle `PropertyManager.jsx`'s own slice first documented, from HMR having bumped several files' versioned URLs mid-session; resolved the same way, via `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')` for the real loaded URL): confirmed the dice display renders the correct real glyphs (⚃⚃ for a rolled 4-4) with the "Đôi!" badge; confirmed `EventCardModal`'s CHOICE view now renders real fetched text ("Investment Opportunity: take a safe payout, or risk it for more.") instead of a hand-mirrored copy; confirmed the INSTANT toast renders real text too ("The Bank pays you a dividend of 50.") instead of the old generic placeholder; confirmed via `getComputedStyle`/inline-style inspection that the token overlay computes the exact right `left`/`top` percentage for a given tile position (cross-checked by hand against `computeGridPosition()`'s own math for positions 5/12/20), that two same-tile players get distinct `--token-offset-x` values, and that `transitionProperty` includes `left, top, transform` on every token — moving a player's `currentPosition` and re-reading the same DOM node's `left`/`top` confirmed the values update in place rather than the node remounting, which combined with the already-confirmed transition is what actually produces a smooth slide (not independently screen-recorded, same "DOM/computed-style-level proof when a live capture isn't available" standard this project's own Win Condition slice already used for its own opaque-backdrop claim). Store reset and hard-reloaded back to a clean lobby afterward, same courtesy as every prior slice.

`npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean throughout (130 modules, down 1 from deleting `eventCardDictionary.js`). **553/553 backend tests** (549 after item 1, +2 item 2's controller tests, +2 item 2's turnMachine assertions folded into existing tests so the count only moved from the 2 new ones, +3 item 3's `lastRoll` tests — 549→551→553).

**Explicitly skipped, not guessed** — same items already flagged as needing the user's own decision, re-confirmed still open, not re-attempted blind: the `Idempotency-Key` REST header mechanism; `game_state_snapshots.reason` for a freshly-started game's very first persist; cross-match wallet persistence (§19, never designed at all); `FLASH_AUCTION_WINDOW_SECONDS`/`FLASH_AUCTION_BID_RESET_SECONDS`'s real two-phase timer model (`TimerManager`'s current one-fixed-duration-per-phase design can't express it); AFK's "instant skip, no fresh grace period" latency optimization (needs new trigger infrastructure this pass didn't build, same scope note as the original batch's own item 4).

### Frontend slice — leave/kick room UI (2026-08-21)

User asked what to do next after "Backlog batch #2"; recommended this as the clearest remaining gap (backend `leave_room`/`kick_player` shipped, tested, and live-verified back in the original "Backlog batch" item 6 — zero frontend UI existed for either). Confirmed the gap was still real before writing anything: grepped the frontend for `leave`/`kick` and found nothing beyond a stray comment from the prior session's own Lobby-push writeup, not actual UI.

**`frontend/src/network/api.js`**: new `leaveRoom(token, roomId)` / `kickPlayer(token, roomId, targetPlayerId)`, mirroring `room.controller.js`'s real routes/payload shapes exactly (`targetPlayerId` is `profiles.id`, never a display name). Also enhanced the shared `request()` helper to attach the real `errorCode` onto the thrown `Error` as `.code` — previously only the prose `message` survived, which every existing caller already only ever used, so this is additive; needed here because `Lobby.jsx` has to tell "I'm no longer a member" (404 `NOT_FOUND`) apart from every other failure by more than string-matching a message.

**`frontend/src/features/lobby/Lobby.jsx`**: a "Rời phòng" (leave) button available to every member including the host (`GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §4's host-transfer-on-leave is entirely server-side, no client input needed for it), and a per-row "Đuổi" (kick) button rendered only for the host, only against non-host rows — mirrors the server's own `NOT_HOST`/`CANNOT_KICK_HOST` checks so a doomed click isn't even reachable from this UI, and a native `window.confirm()` guard before kicking specifically (not before leaving — leaving only ever affects the actor themselves, no confirmation needed).

**Closed an adjacent, real UX gap while building this, not left as a passive "next session" item**: a kicked player previously had no way to ever find out — `S2C_ROOM_UPDATED`'s payload only carries `roomStatus`, and a kicked player's own socket is still in that room's broadcast group (nothing evicts it — flagged, not fixed, in `WEBSOCKET_API.md` §6's own writeup, unchanged here). Reused existing infrastructure instead of adding new wire protocol: the same push already triggers `Lobby.jsx`'s own `refresh()` for everyone still watching that room, kicked player included, and `getRoom`'s own already-existing privacy rule (`API_CONTRACT.md`'s "not a member" reads identically to "room doesn't exist") means a kicked player's next `GET /rooms/:id` genuinely 404s `NOT_FOUND`. `refresh()`'s catch block now treats that specific code as "I'm not in this room anymore" — clears `roomState` (routing back to `LobbyDiagnostic`, the same three-way view split `App.jsx` already does) and leaves a new `roomExitNotice` (store field) for `LobbyDiagnostic.jsx` to show once and dismiss, instead of getting stuck displaying a raw error on a room screen the player has actually already been removed from.

**A real race found live-testing this same slice, fixed the same pass**: a poll tick (or a push-triggered `refresh()`) already in flight when the *local* player clicks "Rời phòng" can resolve *after* `handleLeave()`'s own `setRoomState(null)` — since leaving empties a solo-host room, that stale response's own membership check also now fails the exact same way a real kick would, so its catch block would incorrectly show the *kicked* wording over an entirely deliberate self-leave. Fixed by comparing each `refresh()` call's own captured `roomId` against whatever `roomState.roomId` actually is in the store *at the moment its response lands*, not trusting that nothing changed in between — a mismatch (including `roomState` already being `null`) means the response is stale and gets silently discarded rather than acted on. Reproduced live before the fix (a real leave click did show the kicked-wording notice), then confirmed gone after, waiting through the same timing window both times rather than assuming a quick recheck was conclusive.

**Verified live end to end** (`e2e-smoke-host`/`e2e-smoke-guest`, real accounts, `computer`-tool clicks intermittently failed to register again this session — same standing wrinkle noted in this file since P11-T07 — fell back to dispatching real DOM `click` events via `javascript_tool`, which worked reliably): created a real room, joined the guest via REST, confirmed the already-open host tab's roster updated live (the "Backlog batch #2" push, re-confirmed still working); the "Đuổi" button rendered in exactly the right place with exactly the right visibility (present on the guest's row, absent from the host's own); a real kick round-tripped through the real `kickPlayer()` call, the host's own roster updated to remove the guest via the same live push; independently confirmed via a direct REST call, using the guest's own real token, that their `GET /rooms/:id` now genuinely returns `404 NOT_FOUND` — the exact condition the new client-side handling depends on. A real "Rời phòng" click (solo host) correctly returned to `LobbyDiagnostic` with **no** exit notice, both before finding the race (where it incorrectly showed one) and after fixing it (where it correctly didn't). `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean (130 modules, unchanged). No backend changes — 553/553 backend tests untouched.

**Deliberately not built alongside this, same standing gap `WEBSOCKET_API.md` §6 already flagged**: forcibly evicting a kicked player's socket from the room's Socket.IO broadcast group (`socket.leave(roomId)`) — would need a new userId→socket registry reachable from a plain REST handler, real new infrastructure, not a small addition; the passive discovery path this slice built (next `GET /rooms/:id` 404s) is a complete, low-risk substitute for the frontend's own purposes, not a stopgap missing a piece.

### `GET /api/v1/auth/me` + frontend session resume (P03-T03, 2026-08-21)

Closed the longest-standing item on this document's own "Blocked" list. Rechecked the real blocker before writing anything, per this project's own established discipline: P03-T03 was blocked on "needs a live `profiles` row" — true when Phase 03 first scoped it, stale ever since real accounts started playing real games through this backend (this project's entire live-verification history since). The endpoint itself, though, had simply never been built.

**Scope call, not just "build the documented endpoint"**: `API_CONTRACT.md`'s own spec for this route returns `activeRoomId`/`activeGameId`, "derived by checking `room_players`/`game_players` for a non-terminal room/game" — specifically so a client that just reloaded can decide "lobby vs. board vs. home screen in one call." Building only the backend half here would have repeated this project's own recurring "shipped but never wired in" pattern (`calculateBuildHouse`, `TimerManager`, `eventCardDictionary.js`, ...) — user agreed up front to build both halves in the same pass.

**Backend**: new `backend/src/api/controllers/auth.controller.js` (`getMe`) + `auth.routes.js`, mounted at `/api/v1/auth` behind auth middleware like every other real route. Two new repository queries, following this project's existing "flat query, JS-side merge" style (`roomRepository.js`/`boardRepository.js`'s own convention, not PostgREST embedded-resource syntax):
- `roomRepository.js`'s `findActiveRoomIdForPlayer(supabase, playerId)` — every `room_players` row for this user, joined against `rooms`, excluding only `abandoned`; picks the most-recently-updated one if a player somehow belongs to more than one (a real possibility — no automatic cleanup removes a stale `room_players` row for someone who just closes their tab without calling `leave`).
- `gameRepository.js`'s `findActiveGameIdForRoom(supabase, roomId)` — see the real bug below for why this takes a `roomId`, not a `playerId`, unlike its sibling.

**A real, significant, previously-undiscovered bug — found live, not by review, and not specific to this endpoint**: the first version of the game-lookup queried `game_players` directly (`.eq('player_id', ...)`), matching what `DATABASE_DESIGN.md`'s schema appears to intend. It always returned `null`. Root cause, confirmed by grepping every source file in the backend: **nothing in this entire codebase has ever written a row to `game_players`** — or to `properties`, `game_transactions`, `game_actions`, or `game_events` either. `gameRepository.js`'s own `saveSnapshot()` is the *only* per-game Supabase writer that exists, and it only ever touches `games` (denormalized header fields) and `game_state_snapshots` (the full `GameState` — including the real `players[]` array — serialized into one JSONB blob). Every real `PlayerGameState`/`Property`/`Transaction` this project has ever produced has lived *only* in the in-memory hot store and that JSONB blob — the five normalized child tables the schema defines for them are fully migrated, RLS-enabled, and completely empty in the live database, and always have been. This was invisible until now because nothing had ever tried to query one of them from outside the snapshot blob before. Fixed by resolving `activeGameId` from `games.room_id` instead (`games` *is* reliably written; `room_id` is `UNIQUE`, so a room has at most one game across its lifetime) — sidesteps the empty tables entirely rather than working around them. **Flagged here as a standing fact for any future work**: querying `game_players`/`properties`/`game_transactions`/`game_actions`/`game_events` directly will silently return nothing regardless of real game activity; the only durable source for that data today is `game_state_snapshots.state`.

**Frontend**: `api.js` gained `getMe(token)`. New `App.jsx`-level `useSessionResume(user, session)` hook — attempted exactly once per login (a `useRef` guard, not an effect-dependency guard, so a later Supabase token refresh producing a new `session` object can't accidentally re-trigger it and stomp on room state the user has since built up normally through the UI), skipped entirely if `roomState` is already non-null (a real create/join already happened first). On a hit: `connectSocket()` (previously only ever called from `LobbyDiagnostic.jsx`'s own create/join clicks — a fresh login's socket was never proactively connected at all), then either `C2S_RECONNECT` (an active game — the real resync path, same event `socketClient.js`'s own `S2C_ROOM_UPDATED` handler already uses for "already connected when the match started") or `C2S_JOIN_ROOM` (lobby only — reusing `C2S_RECONNECT` there would fail its own `ROOM_NOT_IN_PROGRESS` check for nothing).

**Verified live end to end, twice, on genuinely fresh page loads** (not the same battle-scarred tab this whole session's testing had accumulated module-version cruft in — a fresh tab specifically to rule that class of artifact out): (1) a room with no game started yet correctly resumed into `Lobby`, showing the real join code, via `C2S_JOIN_ROOM`; (2) a room with a genuinely `in_progress` match correctly resumed straight into `GameView` — real board, real player tokens, real turn state — via `C2S_RECONNECT`, with `GET /auth/me`'s own response confirmed carrying the real `activeGameId` both before diagnosing the bug (`null`, wrong) and after fixing it (the real id, correct). Before landing on the real root cause, chased a red herring first (suspected an expired-token race from this session's own multi-hour age) and ruled it out by reproducing the exact same stuck-`Đang tải dữ liệu bàn cờ…` symptom on a brand-new tab with a guaranteed-fresh token — same "verify the actual cause, don't stop at a plausible one" discipline this project's history has needed before (finding #27's own dice bug, finding #29's `properties: []`).

**Deliberately not built**: rate limiting (`API_CONTRACT.md`: "60/min per user") — no rate-limiting middleware exists anywhere in this backend yet, and inventing one for a single endpoint would be new infrastructure this task never asked for; flagged, not silently skipped.

10 new backend tests (4 `roomRepository.test.js`, 3 `gameRepository.test.js`, 6 `auth.controller.test.js` — one net addition after replacing the broken `game_players`-keyed tests with correct `games.room_id`-keyed ones). **566/566 backend tests passing.** `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean.

### Frontend slice — isometric board + player HUD (2026-08-21)

User sent 3 reference screenshots of a different mobile board game (a tilted, pseudo-3D "isometric" board with player balance HUD cards in the corners) and asked for the same visual treatment: the flat top-down board rotated into a tilted/diamond view, the local player's own balance top-right, opponents stacked vertically down the left. Two implementation paths were flagged before starting — a simple flat 2D 45° rotation (safer, faster, no real depth) vs. a full CSS 3D isometric transform (closer to the reference, bigger rework, real legibility/cross-browser risk with this board's long Vietnamese tile names) — user picked the full 3D version explicitly via `AskUserQuestion` after the tradeoff was laid out, not guessed.

**`GameBoard.module.css`**: `.board` gained `transform: rotateX(55deg) rotateZ(45deg) scale(0.82)` (+ `transform-style: preserve-3d`) — the classic CSS-only isometric technique: rotate the whole flat grid back on X then spin it on Z, as one rigid unit, since it's the *container* being transformed, not each tile independently (tiles keep `grid-row`/`grid-column` placement exactly as before, unchanged). Deliberately **no** `perspective` on any ancestor — true isometric projection has no vanishing point (parallel edges stay parallel), so adding one would produce a *true-perspective* look instead, not the flat-shaded isometric one the reference images show. `.viewport` gained more padding + `align-items: center` (a tilted square's on-screen bounding box is wider and shorter than the original, needs more breathing room) and keeps its existing `overflow: auto` fallback for whatever doesn't fit.

**Legibility — the real risk flagged before starting, addressed directly**: `.board`'s rotation would otherwise skew every tile's text/icons along with it. `BoardTile.module.css`'s `.content` (and `GameBoard.module.css`'s `.token`/`.centerTitle`) each get the exact inverse transform, in reverse order — `rotateZ(-45deg) rotateX(-55deg)` — which cancels the parent's rotation for that one element specifically (verified by hand: composing `Rx(55)·Rz(45)` with a nested child's own `Rz(-45)·Rx(-55)` reduces to the identity, confirming this isn't just visually-plausible but mathematically exact, not an approximation). The tile's own background/border/color band stay tilted (part of the board's "floor"); only the readable content is billboarded upright — the standard "tilt the floor, billboard the label" isometric UI technique. `.token` additionally gets a small `translateZ(6px)` lift so player pieces read as standing *on* the board rather than painted flat onto it, matching the reference images' own raised-piece look.

**New `PlayerHud.jsx` + `.module.css`** — a flat, always-upright fixed overlay, deliberately *not* part of the tilted 3D scene (z-index 5: above the board, below `GameControls`' sticky bar and every modal). `.myCorner` (`position: fixed; top/right: 16px`) and `.opponentRail` (`position: fixed; top/left: 16px`, vertical stack) are exactly the two anchor points requested. Each card shows a token-colored avatar circle (reusing `tileVisuals.js`'s `playerColor`/`playerInitial` — the same colors the board's own tokens use, so a HUD card and its board piece are visually tied together), name (`GET /api/v1/rooms/:id` for the real display name, same independent-fetch pattern `GameControls.jsx`/`FlashAuction.jsx` already each use, not centralized — matches established precedent), balance, an offline dot (reusing the existing `offlinePlayerIds` store field), and an accent outline on whoever's currently the active turn.

**Verification note, stated plainly rather than overclaimed**: this session's Browser-pane tooling could not produce an actual screenshot (`screenshot failed: the Browser pane is not displayed` — the same limitation noted elsewhere in this document). Verified instead via `getComputedStyle`/`getBoundingClientRect` on the real running page: `.board`'s `transform` resolves to a real, non-degenerate `matrix3d(...)` (not `none`); `.content`'s own matrix is different from both `none` and the board's own matrix, confirming the counter-rotation is genuinely being applied, not accidentally omitted; `.myCorner`'s bounding rect sits flush against the viewport's top-right corner and `.opponentRail`'s against the top-left, exactly as specified; `PlayerHud` renders the real injected balances/names/offline state correctly. This confirms the mechanism is wired correctly and the math is exact, but is not the same as having actually seen the rendered pixels — flagged honestly as a gap in this pass's own verification, not silently presented as fully proven. Dev server picked up every change live via HMR throughout. No backend changes, no new tests (pure CSS/layout, nothing this project's backend test suite covers) — 566/566 backend tests unaffected, frontend `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean.

**Same day, follow-up — user sent a real screenshot of their own running app and named 3 concrete problems**, closing the verification gap the previous pass had flagged: (1) tile text hard to read, (2) the board getting covered when other panels appear, (3) wanted bigger corner tiles, (4) wanted nicer icons than plain emoji. All four addressed:

- **Text legibility**: `.content`'s counter-rotated "sign" turned out to genuinely need its own opaque background — an upright label's on-screen footprint is bigger than its source tile's own (now-tilted) footprint at these small sizes, so a neighboring tile's floor color/edge could visually bleed behind text. `.content` now gets `background: var(--bg)` + a small shadow (a real plate/sign, matching the reference images' own look) — and `.tile`'s pre-existing `overflow: hidden` had to be removed, since it was clipping that same "sign" whenever its counter-rotated footprint extended past the tile's own flat box.
