- **A real collision, not a hypothetical one**: `PlayerHud.jsx`'s new `.myCorner` and the *already-existing* `PropertyManager.module.css`'s `.panel` both claim `top:16px; right:16px` — `PropertyManager` (z-index 15) was rendering directly on top of and fully hiding the balance card whenever a property was selected. Found by re-checking every existing top-right-anchored component once this was pointed out, not just the one the user happened to screenshot. Fixed by moving `PropertyManager`'s own `top` to `84px` (both its base rule and its own `<900px` media-query override), with a matching `max-height` reduction so it never overflows short viewports.
- **Corner/board footprint**: `.board`'s outer ring of grid tracks (first/last row and column — where `computeGridPosition` always lands all 4 corners) got a bigger `minmax()` than the uniform interior tracks, making GO/Jail/Free Parking/Go-To-Jail genuinely bigger cells (confirmed live: 125×72px vs. 104×60px for an ordinary tile at the same zoom). Flagged tradeoff: this also makes edge tiles *directly adjacent* to a corner very slightly bigger in one dimension (an edge tile's own row or column is shared with its neighboring corner's track) — a true "only the 4 corner cells grow, every edge tile stays perfectly uniform" version would need restructuring `computeGridPosition`'s whole coordinate system, judged not worth the risk for this pass. Also reduced `.board`'s own `scale()` (0.82 → 0.72) and gave `.viewport` asymmetric padding (more on the right) — real overlap with the right-side-heavy fixed UI (HUD, `PropertyManager`, `PropertyActionDrawer`) was still possible at the old scale/centering, confirmed live at a realistic 1600×900 viewport (`rightClearance` went from negative at an artificially narrow 800px test window to +470px at 1600px — the negative number was this session's own too-narrow test viewport, not a real bug at desktop size, caught by deliberately re-testing at a realistic size rather than trusting the first, misleading number).
- **New `TileIcon.jsx`** (`frontend/src/features/board/`) — a hand-drawn SVG icon set (24×24 viewBox, `currentColor`, simple circle/rect/line/path primitives only, no complex bezier curves, deliberately: this session's tooling still can't produce a screenshot, so every icon had to be geometrically simple enough to reason about correctness by hand rather than needing a visual check) replacing the plain-emoji `TILE_ICON` map (`tileVisuals.js`, now deleted) and `BoardTile.jsx`'s own inline `CORNER_LABEL` emoji (🚔🅿️👮 → real jail-bars/parking-circle/handcuffs icons; GO stays text, matching real board convention). Swapped into all 6 places that rendered a tile icon, not just the board itself — `BoardTile.jsx`, `FlashAuction.jsx`, `PropertyActionDrawer.jsx`, `RentRiskChoice.jsx`, `PropertyManager.jsx` — so the same icon appears everywhere a tile type is shown, not just on the board.

**Verified live**: 13 real `<svg>` icon elements found on a real 36-tile board render, each with real child shape counts matching the hand-authored source (not empty/broken elements); `PlayerHud`'s `.myCorner` and `PropertyManager`'s `.panel` bounding rects confirmed non-overlapping; corner vs. ordinary tile size difference confirmed via real `getBoundingClientRect()` numbers; `.content`'s computed background/box-shadow confirmed present. Screenshot capture still not available in this session's tooling — same honest caveat as the previous pass, verified via computed geometry/DOM structure instead, not overclaimed as visually confirmed. `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean (133 modules, +1 for `TileIcon.jsx`). No backend changes.

**~~KNOWN BUG, NOT YET FIXED~~ — RESOLVED 2026-08-22** (see "Frontend fix — board sizing, scaling and legibility" below: the enlarged outer grid ring this was blamed on no longer exists, and all 4 corner contents now measure exactly centered in their own tile with 0px offset). Original report kept below for context. **Found from a second real user screenshot**: user reported the interface is "rất lỗi" (very buggy) and sent a screenshot before being interrupted mid-message, so the *specific* complaint wasn't fully captured in words — but the screenshot itself shows a real, describable problem: **the 4 corner tiles' own icon "sign" (`.content`, the same counter-rotated element the legibility fix above just added a background to) renders visually detached from its own tile**, floating well outside the board's own drawn diamond boundary near each of the 4 outer vertices (e.g., the Jail corner's icon box appears up near the browser chrome, well above the board itself; the Go-To-Jail corner's icon appears off to the right of the diamond's own right vertex, past the board edge). `PlayerHud` (top-right "Bạn"/top-left opponent cards) and the rest of the board — edge tiles, colors, the center title, dice/jackpot/controls — all look correctly positioned in the same screenshot; this is specifically a **corner-tile problem**, and specifically appeared alongside (very likely caused by) *this same pass's* corner-enlargement change.

**Working hypothesis for next session, not yet confirmed**: this pass's two corner-tile changes — bigger grid tracks (`GameBoard.module.css`'s `.board`, `minmax(100px, 1.5fr)` vs. the interior's `minmax(56px, 1fr)`) and the pre-existing counter-rotation technique (`BoardTile.module.css`'s `.content`, `rotateZ(-45deg) rotateX(-55deg)`) — likely interact badly specifically *because* corners are now much bigger: counter-rotating a box around its own center by a steep compound angle displaces that box's own geometric extent in 3D space by an amount that scales with the box's own size, so a dramatically bigger corner tile amplifies a "drift" effect that may have been present-but-negligible for small/uniform tiles all along. **This is also a real lesson about this pass's own verification, worth recording plainly**: the live checks done (bounding-rect sizes, computed transform matrices, non-`none` values) all individually came back correct, but none of them actually asked "does this element's on-screen position still look attached to its own parent tile from the viewer's perspective" — exactly the class of problem only a real rendered screenshot (which this session's tooling still cannot produce) or much more deliberate full-scene geometric reasoning would have caught. Computed-style spot-checks proved the *mechanism* was wired (rotation applied, non-degenerate matrices, no `none` transforms) but did not prove the *visual result* was correct — a real gap in how "verified" was used for this specific pass, flagged directly rather than glossed over.

**Candidate fixes to try next session, none attempted yet**: (a) stop enlarging corner *grid tracks* — instead keep every tile's own footprint uniform and only make a corner tile's *content* (icon size, font size) bigger, sidestepping the whole size-amplifies-drift interaction; (b) keep the bigger corner cells but move the counter-rotation to a smaller inner wrapper sized independently of the outer tile's own growth; (c) reduce how much bigger corners are, to shrink the drift to an unnoticeable amount rather than eliminating the interaction. (a) is probably the lowest-risk starting point given this session's own screenshot evidence that plain edge tiles (uniform, unenlarged) rendered correctly throughout.

**Session ended here at the user's own request** ("nén session này lại để tôi chuyển qua session mới" — compress this session so I can switch to a new one) before this bug could be investigated or fixed — this write-up is that handoff. Do not report the isometric board / corner-sizing work as fully working in a future session without first re-confirming this specific bug is actually fixed; the mechanism-level checks earlier in this section are real and still hold (rotation math, HUD/PropertyManager collision fix, icon rendering, legibility background) but the *corner-tile-specifically* result is currently known-broken.

**2026-08-22, follow-up investigation — could not reproduce, real findings recorded rather than either claiming fixed or leaving silent.** Injected a real fetched board + a fake in-progress `GameState` into the live store (same technique every prior frontend slice this session used) and measured all 4 real corner tiles' geometry directly: `.content`'s `getBoundingClientRect()` sat centered and fully contained within its own `.tile`'s bounds at every corner, with a comfortable ~20–48px margin on every side — not borderline, not touching the edge. Repeated at two materially different viewport sizes (1280×720 desktop, 834×1100 tablet-ish) with the same clean result both times. `getComputedStyle(content).transform` resolved to a real, well-formed, non-degenerate `matrix3d(...)` throughout. **This is the same class of check the original pass already flagged as an incomplete substitute for an actual screenshot** ("did not prove the visual result was correct") — this session's own Browser-pane tooling still cannot produce a real screenshot either (`screenshot failed: the Browser pane is not displayed`, same standing limitation), so this still isn't full visual confirmation.

Leading theory, not confirmed: the original report may have captured a **transient HMR mid-edit render**, not a persistent CSS defect — the user's screenshot was taken immediately after a live corner-enlargement CSS edit landed via Vite HMR, and this exact project has already hit genuine transient-HMR-staleness false alarms more than once before (`PropertyActionDrawer.jsx`'s slice, `PropertyManager.jsx`'s orphaned-module-instance wrinkle). A persistent geometry bug would be expected to reproduce consistently past some size/margin threshold, not "comfortable ~40px margin in careful, repeated, settled-render testing" — this doesn't rule out a real bug (e.g., a genuine Chrome-version- or zoom-level-specific 3D-transform rendering divergence between this dev environment's browser and the user's own), but the balance of evidence leans toward "not currently reproducing," not "confirmed still broken." **Deliberately did not ship a speculative fix** (e.g., adding `transform-style: preserve-3d` to `.tile` — a real, textbook-correct hardening for nested CSS 3D transform composition, and a candidate worth trying if a real repro ever lands) against a bug that couldn't be reproduced or confirmed — changing `.tile`'s stacking/3D behavior on a guess, for a problem that might not currently exist, was judged worse than asking. **Next step, not yet done**: get one fresh screenshot of a real board (a new/rejoined game, not the already-finished one this session's other fix was found on) from the user's own browser, on a normal (non-mid-edit) page load, to either close this out or give a real repro to work from.

### Frontend fix — stale socket auth token silently stuck every reconnect forever (2026-08-22)

User reported being stuck live on a real page (screenshot: "Xin chào, Taniel", `GameBoard.jsx`'s "Đang tải dữ liệu bàn cờ…" placeholder, indefinitely) — investigated as a live production-style bug on the user's own real session, not a synthetic report.

**Diagnosis, each step confirmed against the real running system before moving to the next, not guessed**: found the user's actual room (`f1a1fa7e-afd9-42d8-a565-8e1c23d8ecb6`, via a throwaway read-only script querying `profiles`/`room_players`/`rooms`/`games`/`game_state_snapshots` directly, deleted after use) — `rooms.status = 'in_progress'` but the real `games.status = 'finished'` (the match had genuinely already ended the previous evening, `ended_at` real). Called `gameRepository.resolveLiveGameState()` directly (same function `handleReconnect` uses) against this exact room in a fresh process — it returned a correct, complete finished `GameState` (boardId `small`, both real players resolvable). Fetched `GET /api/v1/boards/small` with a real live token — `200 OK`. **Both the cold-load path and the board REST endpoint the frontend depends on were already working correctly** — ruled out as the cause, not assumed.

**Real root cause, found by reading `network/socketClient.js`'s `connectSocket()` closely**: `io(SOCKET_URL, { auth: { token } })` passes a *plain object*, captured once at first-connect time. Socket.IO replays that exact frozen object on every future (re)connection attempt it ever makes — it does not re-evaluate `token`. Nothing in this codebase ever updated it afterward, even though `supabase-js`'s own background session refresh keeps the *underlying* Supabase session alive indefinitely. Once the original captured token's `exp` passed (a tab open longer than ~1hr — this one had been open since the previous evening) and the socket needed to (re)connect for any reason — here, the backend process itself being restarted, which drops every open connection — the handshake replayed the now-expired token; `createSocketAuthMiddleware`/`verifyJwtAsymmetric` correctly rejected it (`expired_token` → generic `Authentication error`), Socket.IO raised `connect_error` client-side — **and `attachListeners` had no `connect_error` listener at all**. The rejection vanished completely; Socket.IO's own automatic reconnection loop (`reconnection: true` by default) just kept silently retrying with the identical doomed token, forever. Compounding it: `connectionStatus` (which does track a 'reconnecting' state) is only ever read by `LobbyDiagnostic.jsx` — nowhere in the `GameView` tree — so even a correctly-tracked failure state had no way to reach the screen the user was actually looking at.

**Fix, three parts, all in `frontend/src/`**:
1. `network/socketClient.js`'s `connectSocket()`: `auth: { token }` → `auth: (cb) => cb({ token: authToken })` — a callback Socket.IO re-invokes fresh before *every* connection attempt (initial and every automatic retry), reading the current module-level `authToken` instead of a frozen closure value. New exported `updateAuthToken(token)` lets external code keep that value current without forcing a reconnect of an already-good connection.
2. `features/auth/AuthContext.jsx`: forwards every `session` — both the initial `getSession()` result and every `onAuthStateChange` firing (which already includes Supabase's own proactive `TOKEN_REFRESHED`, well before actual expiry) — into `updateAuthToken()`. This is the piece that actually keeps the value `connectSocket`'s new callback reads genuinely fresh.
3. `network/socketClient.js`'s `attachListeners`: added the missing `sock.on('connect_error', ...)` — logs it and sets `connectionStatus: 'reconnecting'` so a real handshake failure is at least observable/diagnosable instead of silently disappearing, same "surface it, don't swallow it" standard every other handler in this file already follows.

**Live-verified the actual mechanism, not just read the code**: via the live running app (real `e2e-smoke-host` session, same store/module-injection technique this project's frontend slices already established), confirmed `sock.io.opts.auth` is now `"function"` (was `"object"` before the fix); poisoned the live `authToken` then forced the transport closed the way an unexpected drop would (`sock.io.engine.close()`, not the client-initiated `.disconnect()`, which intentionally skips auto-reconnect) — a real `connect_error` fired with message `"Authentication error"`, now visible where before it vanished entirely; restored the real token via `updateAuthToken()` and confirmed a fresh `sock.connect()` attempt picks up the corrected value immediately and connects successfully. **One thing not fully chased down**: after the poisoned-token `connect_error`, Socket.IO's own automatic retry loop didn't visibly self-heal within an observed ~8s window even after the token was restored (`sock.active` read `false` afterward) — didn't dig into Socket.IO's own internal backoff/retry-eligibility rules for an explicit middleware `connect_error` specifically, since it's not load-bearing for the real fix: in normal operation `authToken` is kept correct *before* any (re)connect attempt ever happens (via `AuthContext`'s proactive forwarding), so the client should now essentially never present a genuinely stale token to the handshake at all, in contrast to before this fix, where it was guaranteed to eventually happen on any long-lived tab. Flagged here rather than silently claimed fully understood.

`npm run build`/`npm run lint` clean (133 modules — same count as before, no new files; the one pre-existing `AuthContext.jsx` fast-refresh warning, unrelated, unchanged, and confirmed live that an edit to that specific file does force a full page reload rather than a partial HMR patch — worth knowing, not itself a bug). No backend changes — 566/566 backend tests untouched, not re-run.

**Not fixed here, pre-existing and out of this bug's scope**: `handleReconnect` (`socketServer.js`) still has no equivalent of `handleGameAction`'s `GAME_ALREADY_FINISHED` guard — a room whose game already finished (like the one this bug was found on) still reads `rooms.status = 'in_progress'` forever (a real, already-known, separately-tracked gap, not this bug) and a reconnect into it succeeds and correctly delivers the finished `GameState`, which `GameOverScreen.jsx` correctly renders. This is actually the *right* outcome for a genuinely-finished match, not a bug — noted only so a future session doesn't conflate the two: once this fix reaches the user's tab (`AuthContext.jsx`'s own forced-reload-on-edit already means it likely already has, live, without the user needing to do anything), they should land on the real Game Over / final standings screen for their already-completed match, not back into active play.

### Frontend redesign — GameView 3-column "Empire Tycoon" layout (2026-08-22)

User shared a reference mockup (a different game's UI: navy+gold dashboard, left Players panel, center turn indicator + board + action bar, right Ledger event feed + property card) and asked for it, clarified via `AskUserQuestion` twice: the board itself stays the existing tilted CSS-3D isometric board, untouched — only the surrounding chrome changes; and the palette is a **fixed** navy/gold theme scoped to this screen only, not tied to the app's own light/dark-adaptive tokens. Planned via `EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode` (a genuinely large, multi-file, one-new-feature redesign) before implementing — full plan is this session's own plan-mode artifact, summarized here.

**What changed**: `GameView.jsx` went from a flat list of independently `position: fixed` corner overlays (the old `PlayerHud.jsx` top-left/top-right, `PropertyManager.jsx` top-right-below-that, `GameControls.jsx` a full-viewport sticky bottom bar) to a real 3-column shell (`GameView.module.css`, new `--et-*` custom properties scoped to `.shell` — not `index.css`). Every full-screen modal (`FlashAuction`/`TradeWindow`/`EventCardModal`/`RentRiskChoice`/`FinalPhaseBanner`/`GameOverScreen`/`PropertyActionDrawer`) is untouched, still mounted as plain siblings, still `position: fixed` at their own existing z-indices — composes fine over any layout underneath without needing to know the shell exists.

- **New `PlayersPanel.jsx`** (left column) — supersedes `PlayerHud.jsx` (deleted), a unified vertical list instead of a split "my corner"/"opponent rail". New here: a **Props count** per player (`gameState.properties.filter(p => p.ownerId === player.id).length`) — wasn't surfaced anywhere in this app before.
- **New `Ledger.jsx` + `ledgerFormat.js`** (right column, upper) — a genuinely new feature: an event-history feed. `S2C_STATE_UPDATE`'s own `transactions[]` was previously transient (`gameStore.js`'s `setGameState` overwrites it every broadcast, by design — a one-shot "what just changed" signal) and nothing accumulated it anywhere. New `gameStore.js` field `transactionLog` (capped at 50, newest-first) + `appendTransactionLog` action; `network/socketClient.js`'s `S2C_STATE_UPDATE` handler now also resolves each raw `Transaction` (`applyTransaction.js`'s real shape: `fromGamePlayerId`/`toGamePlayerId`/`amount`/`transactionType` only) into a stored log entry **once, at the moment it arrives** — not deferred to render time, since a later broadcast could move the same player again and silently corrupt an old entry's tile reference. `ledgerFormat.js` mirrors the backend's real `TRANSACTION_TYPES` (16 values) into label/icon/category/description — **honest scope limit, not papered over**: a `Transaction` record carries no property/tile reference at all; only `rent`/`purchase` can reliably recover one (the payer/buyer's own `currentPosition` in that same broadcast, since they just landed there), every other type's description omits a tile name rather than guessing one.
- **`PropertyManager.jsx` restyled, action logic completely untouched** (all four `*Reason()` gating functions, all four action handlers — byte-for-byte identical) — new content added to its JSX: a genuine rent-by-house-level table using real, already-existing-but-previously-unshown data (`tile.baseRent`/`tile.rentTable`, confirmed real via the live seed data), and an owner badge. Deliberately the **printed** table, not a live-recomputed "your rent right now" — reproducing `calculateRent.js`'s own full-group-ownership doubling bonus here too would risk this display-only card silently drifting from the real server-computed amount.
- **`GameControls.jsx` — zero logic changes**, `.module.css` only: the sticky full-viewport bottom bar becomes a pill hint + rounded button row sized to the center column. **Deliberately did not add a literal "Build" button** matching the reference's own 3-button row — the real game has no global build action (always property-scoped, needs a selected `propertyId`); inventing an always-visible button with no valid target would be exactly the "fabricated control" this project's docs repeatedly flag against. The bottom bar shows whatever `GameControls.jsx` already legitimately renders for the current phase, restyled, not a literal 3-button copy.
- **`App.jsx`**: the outer `<h1>`/greeting/logout header (unconditional before) is now suppressed while `gameHasStarted` — `GameView.jsx` renders its own top bar instead (same greeting text, same `supabase.auth.signOut()` call, just relocated + restyled into the navy shell), avoiding a mismatched double header.
- **One small, deliberate, flagged correction to `GameBoard.module.css`** (the one file the brief said stays untouched) — `.viewport`'s old asymmetric right padding (`40px 260px 40px 16px`) existed *specifically* to keep the board clear of the previous fixed-position corner overlays; those overlays no longer exist (real grid-column siblings now), so the padding was stale dead weight from a problem this redesign already removes structurally, not a deliberate property of "the board" worth preserving as-is — corrected to plain `24px`, flagged in place. The isometric transform math itself is untouched.
- Responsive: reuses the codebase's existing `@media (max-width: 900px)` convention — the 3-column grid stacks (Players becomes a horizontal scroll strip, Ledger/property card move below the board), rather than a new breakpoint scheme.

**Live-verified** via the same store-injection technique this session's own earlier investigation established (Browser pane + `javascript_tool`, `import()` and `.setState()` split into separate tool calls — avoids the `"Promise was collected"` quirk): injected a realistic 3-player game (2 owned properties in the same color group, one owned by "me", one by an opponent, `houseSupply`/`hotelSupply`/jackpot set, a 6-entry `transactionLog` covering `purchase`/`rent`/`pass_go_salary`/`tax`/`event_card`/`build`) — confirmed the shell's own colors resolve to the exact intended hex values (`getComputedStyle`, not just "a color got applied"), the board renders fully unchanged in the center, Props counts compute correctly per player, the Ledger renders all 6 entries with correct icon/color/description including the tile-name enrichment for `rent`/`purchase`, and — the most load-bearing check — **all four `PropertyManager` action buttons showed the exact expected disabled reasons** against the synthetic cross-player group state (`Cần sở hữu trọn bộ nhóm màu` / `Không có nhà để bán` / `Phải bán hết nhà trong nhóm trước` — correctly reflecting a *group-mate's* houses, not just the selected property's own — / `Chưa cầm cố`), confirming none of the untouched gating logic actually broke. Resize to 700px confirmed the responsive stack order and the Players list's row-direction switch. **One real gap found and fixed during this same verification pass**: `Ledger.jsx`'s own `nameFor` was missing the "is this the local player" special-case `PlayersPanel.jsx` already has — a ledger entry involving the local player showed their raw auth id instead of "Bạn"; fixed the same session, re-verified. `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean throughout (137 modules, net +3 new files/−2 deleted; the one pre-existing `AuthContext.jsx` warning, unrelated, unchanged). No backend changes — 566/566 backend tests untouched, not re-run.

**Not done, out of this pass's scope, not silently assumed**: no real 2-player browser run of this specific redesign yet (same standing gap as every other frontend feature in this project's history) — this pass's own verification was single-tab store injection only, same as most prior frontend slices before their own eventual live-multiplayer pass.

### Frontend fix — board sizing, scaling and legibility (2026-08-22)

User screenshot right after the GameView redesign: the board rendered but was "quá lỗi" — far too large for its frame, cut off at the bottom, text unreadable. Diagnosed by measuring the real DOM rather than eyeballing; three independent causes, all real:

**1. The core one — `transform` does not affect layout.** `.board` declared a fixed `width: min(1000px, board-size * 90px)` (900px for the Small board) + `aspect-ratio: 1`, so the browser reserved a 900×900 layout box — but the *rendered* isometric diamond is a completely different size and shape: `side × √2` wide (1273px) and `side × √2 × cos(55°)` tall (730px). So the diamond overhung its own layout box by ~187px on each side horizontally (clipped / forcing scroll) while the layout still reserved ~85px of dead vertical space top and bottom. `scale(0.72)` had been added earlier to paper over this by shrinking everything, which is also precisely why the text ended up unreadably small.
   **Fix**: derive `--side` from the genuinely available space with those two trig factors folded in, then reconcile the layout box with the rendered footprint via margins — positive horizontal (`0.2071 × side` each side, reserving the real overhang) and *negative* vertical (`-0.0944 × side`, collapsing the real dead space). The element's outer box now equals what's actually drawn. `scale()` deleted outright rather than retuned — folded into `--side` itself, so tiles/text render at true size instead of being shrunk after the fact.

**2. `#root` is capped at `1126px`** (`index.css`) — correct for the auth/lobby reading width, but it squeezed the whole 3-column game screen into a narrow centered column, leaving the board no room. Fixed with a standard centered-parent full-bleed breakout (`width: 100vw; margin-inline: calc(50% - 50vw)`) scoped to `GameView.module.css`'s `.shell` only — `Login`/`LobbyDiagnostic`/`Lobby` keep their 1126px width, rather than widening `#root` globally.

**3. `minmax(100px, …)` grid track minimums** set a floor (100 + 8×56 + 100 = 648px) the grid could never go below, so the board physically could not shrink to a computed smaller size — it would overflow instead. Now `minmax(0, 1fr)`; the explicit width sizes the grid, the tracks only divide it.

**Also in this pass**:
- **Uniform grid tracks** — the enlarged outer ring (`1.5fr` corners) is gone. It had two real problems: it silently enlarged every edge tile *adjacent* to a corner (they share a track — already a flagged wart), and it was the leading suspect behind the reported detached-corner-icon bug. Corners stay visually distinct by enlarging their own *content* instead (`.cornerLabel`/`.cornerIcon`), which is exactly candidate fix (a) from that bug's own earlier write-up. **Measured after: all 4 corner contents sit exactly centered in their own tile (0px center offset), fully inside the board bounds, corner and ordinary tracks identical (134px vs 134px)** — that bug is closed.
- **Board text now scales as one unit**: every tile size is `em` against a single `font-size: calc(var(--side) / 44)` on `.board`. Previously each element clamped independently against `vw`, so they did not track the board's actual rendered size at all.
- **Board surface colors pinned** (`--tile-surface`/`--tile-ink`/`--tile-line`/`--board-felt` on `.board`, consumed by `BoardTile.module.css` — custom properties inherit; CSS Modules only scope class *names*). They previously came from `index.css`'s OS-theme-adaptive tokens, so an OS-dark-mode user got a dark board inside the now deliberately-fixed dark navy shell, with no way to tell the two apart.
- **A genuine pre-existing bug found by measurement, not introduced here**: `.name` is a `-webkit-box` line-clamp element used as a flex item, and in that position its height resolves as a single line and is then clamped — so every two-line tile name had been silently truncated to one line even with room to spare (measured: name needed 24px, was given 13px, while its `.content` box still had 46px available). Fixed with `flex: none`. At 1366px this took clipped names from 21 → 2.

**Measured results** (real `getBoundingClientRect`, which is transform-aware, so these are the actually-rendered numbers — not inferred from CSS):
- At **1920×1080** (the user's own screen): side 945px, tile names **15.5px** (up from an effective ~8.6px — the old 12px clamp × the old 0.72 scale), diamond 1342×770 fitting inside its 1370×817 frame, fits both axes, no page scroll, zero tiles with overflowing content, 1 clipped name (the single longest).
- At **1366×768**: side 607px (up from 553 after also narrowing the side columns at a new intermediate breakpoint), names 9.9px, fits both axes, no page scroll.

**Follow-up the same day — tile layout reworked to match a real printed board** (user sent the current result next to a reference screenshot of Business Tour's isometric board, asking for that game's text handling and a bigger board). The remaining ugliness was structural, not a sizing issue:

- **Tile content no longer billboards upright.** `.content` used to be counter-rotated (`rotateZ(-45deg) rotateX(-55deg)`) onto an opaque plate so text faced the viewer. That produced a small floating sign whose on-screen footprint never matched its own tile — cramped, visually detached, and **the actual cause of the long-reported "corner tile icon looks detached" bug** (an earlier revision had wrongly blamed the enlarged corner grid ring for it). All counter-rotation and the plate background/shadow are gone; content lies flat on the board surface, so that whole class of mismatch is structurally impossible now. `.tile`'s `overflow: hidden` is restored alongside (it had only been removed to let that plate overflow deliberately).
- **Text runs parallel to its own edge**, as a real board prints it — horizontal on the top/bottom edges, `writing-mode: vertical-rl` + `rotate(180deg)` on the left/right ones (the plain `vertical-rl` alone reads downward-left, i.e. upside down once the board's own `rotateZ(45deg)` composes on top). New `computeEdge()` in `GameBoard.jsx` supplies the edge per tile — deliberately a separate function rather than an extra return value on `computeGridPosition`, which is correctness-critical and already verified.
- **The colour band now sits on the edge facing the board's centre**, per edge, via `flex-direction` (`column`/`column-reverse`/`row`/`row-reverse`) instead of always on top.
- **Rectangular tiles restored** (outer ring `1.45fr` vs inner `1fr`). Uniform tracks had made every edge tile square — both unlike a real board and much worse for fitting a long Vietnamese street name. This also makes the four corners naturally the largest, squarest cells with no special-casing.
- **Board enlarged**: side columns trimmed again (196/236, and 168/204 at the intermediate breakpoint) with `--side`'s budget kept in sync → 945px → **996px** at 1920.

**A real regression caught by measurement during this pass, fixed same pass**: the full-bleed breakout had used `width: 100vw; margin-inline: calc(50% - 50vw)`. `100vw` counts the vertical scrollbar, so the shell overflowed by ~15px → horizontal scrollbar → which stole viewport height → which made `min-height: 100vh` overflow → vertical scrollbar. A genuine circular scrollbar feedback loop, observed live at 1366px, not hypothetical. Replaced with `position: fixed; inset: 0`, which matches the real viewport with no scrollbar arithmetic (the sibling modals are themselves `position: fixed` and unaffected — nothing on `.shell` creates a containing block for them).

**Measured after the rework** — the text orientation was verified by measuring the *actual on-screen reading direction* (a `Range` over two adjacent glyphs, so the result survives every transform and writing-mode in the chain; an initial attempt using first-vs-last glyph was discarded once it became clear line-wrapping polluted the vector):
- Reading direction per edge: top/bottom **+30°**, left/right **−30°** — each exactly parallel to that edge's own on-screen angle, none upside down. Colour band confirmed facing the board centre on all four edges.
- Tiles genuinely rectangular and correctly oriented: bottom-edge 91×132 (narrow, deep), left-edge 132×91 (wide, shallow), corner 132×132.
- At **1920×1080**: side 996px, tile names **17.6px** (from an effective ~8.6px originally), diamond 1414×811 fitting its frame, **zero clipped names, zero overflowing content, no page scrollbars**.
- At **1366×768**: side 639px, names ~11px, fits both axes, no scrollbars, 0 clipped names, only 3 tiles with slight content overflow (down from 18 before this rework).
- All four corner contents: contained in their own tile, inside the board bounds, **0px centre offset** — the corner bug is closed.

**Third pass, same day — bigger board, bigger corners, real 3D dice** (user asked for all three):

- **New `features/board/DiceRoll.jsx` + `.module.css`** — two real CSS-3D dice that tumble onto the board and settle showing the rolled value, replacing the flat Unicode die glyphs (⚀-⚅) that lived in `GameControls`' bottom bar. Rendered *inside* `.board` so they occupy the board's own 3D space and land on its surface, counter-rotated (same trick `.token` already uses) so they read as objects standing on the board, with their own `perspective` — the board itself deliberately has none (true isometric), but dice are physical objects and a vanishing point is what sells them as cubes. Each cube is six real pip faces with opposite sides summing to 7, like a real die; a `@keyframes tumble` starts several whole turns away from the resting orientation and interpolates back, so the roll always ends on the correct face. Values are `gameState.lastRoll`'s real server-generated `die1`/`die2` — nothing here generates or influences a value (dice have been genuinely server-authoritative since finding #27). The old glyph display and its CSS were **deleted, not left alongside** — two renderings of the same `lastRoll` could visibly disagree mid-animation.
- **Bigger corners**: outer grid ring `1.45fr` → `1.7fr` (corners 132px → **153px** at 1920). Tuned by measurement, not guessed: `1.9fr` was tried first and rejected — the ring's depth is shared with every edge tile's *width* budget, so it bought 167px corners at the cost of squeezing edge tiles to 88px, which clipped 16 tile names.
- **Bigger board**: side columns and gaps trimmed again with `--side`'s budget kept in sync → 996px → **1028px** at 1920.
- **Two real regressions caught by measurement mid-pass, both fixed before finishing**: the first budget attempt (`100vw - 452px`) was ~12px optimistic and made the board genuinely overflow its frame (`fitsH: false`) — corrected to 466px after computing the real chrome. And the GO corner's text overflowed its tile because `.corner`'s own `align-items: center` sizes `.content` to its content height rather than the tile's (measured: a 37px box holding a 42px label); fixed with `align-self: stretch` on corner content.

**Measured after this pass** — dice correctness was verified by rolling four different values through the real component and, for each die, finding which face actually ends up toward the viewer (largest rendered area, since `backface-visibility: hidden` hides the rest) and counting its pips: **(6,1), (2,4), (6,6), (1,2) all landed showing exactly the rolled face**, and the "ĐÔI!" badge appeared only on the real double. Board state at **1920×1080**: side 1028, corners 153×153, edge tiles 90×153, **zero clipped names, zero overflowing content, no page scrollbars**, all four corner contents contained at 0px offset. At **1366×768**: side 661, also **zero clipped and zero overflowing** (the 3 overflowing tiles noted in the previous pass are now clean too).

`npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean (139 modules, +2 for the new dice component). No backend changes.

`npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean (137 modules, unchanged). No backend changes — 566/566 backend tests untouched, not re-run. **Still not done**: no real 2-player browser run of the redesigned screen (same standing gap as every other frontend feature here).

### Board camera, step-by-step movement, and zodiac player pieces (2026-08-22)

Four user requests in one pass, planned via `EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode` (multi-file, one item reaching the backend/DB): a zoomable/rotatable board camera, tile-by-tile token movement instead of a single glide, "where I was / where I am" position markers, and 12 con giáp (zodiac) player pieces — chosen in the lobby, randomly assigned if not — replacing the plain initial-letter tokens. Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`: do all of it in one pass, and apply the DB migration to the live project in the same pass.

**Backend (zodiac only — the other three items are frontend-only)**:
- New `backend/src/domain/zodiac.js` — the 12 real keys (`ZODIAC_KEYS`), `isValidZodiac`/`randomZodiac`.
- **`backend/supabase/migrations/0005_room_players_zodiac.sql`** — `room_players.zodiac text`, nullable, `CHECK` restricted to the 12 keys. **Applied live** the same session (`npx supabase db query --file ... --linked`, same project `qpbyfgzujwzueprzuurk` every prior migration used) and **confirmed by reading the real column back** (`information_schema.columns`: `zodiac | text | YES`), not trusted from the exit code — same discipline this project's own migration history already established.
- `roomRepository.js` — `fetchPlayers`/`createRoom`/`updateRoom` all thread `zodiac` through, same shape as `is_ready`.
- New `PATCH /api/v1/rooms/:id/zodiac` (`room.controller.js`'s `setZodiac`, `room.routes.js`) — modeled directly on `setReady`: same membership 404, same `JOINABLE_STATUSES` 409, same `S2C_ROOM_UPDATED` push. `null` explicitly clears a pick. **Duplicates across players are deliberately allowed** — no uniqueness check — matching the user's own explicit ask that two players picking the same animal render in different colours, not be blocked from picking it at all.
- `initializeGameState` (`room.controller.js`) assigns each `PlayerGameState.zodiac`: the lobby pick if valid, else `randomZodiac()`. `domain/gameState.js`'s `createPlayerGameState` gained the field (JSON-blob only, no `game_players` migration — nothing in this backend writes that table, an already-standing fact). Bank sentinel always null.
- Tests: 15 new (`zodiac.test.js`, `room.controller.test.js`'s `setZodiac`/`startGame` cases, `roomRepository.test.js`'s round-trip, `gameState.test.js`'s default) — **582/582 backend tests passing** (567 baseline + 15; one pre-existing test's exact-shape assertion needed `zodiac: null` added, not a real regression).

**Frontend**:
- **New `features/board/zodiac.js`** — the 12 animals mirrored (key/label/emoji), same "no shared package crosses the frontend/backend boundary" standing every other mirrored constant in this app already has. **Emoji, not hand-drawn SVG** — a deliberate, flagged exception to `TileIcon.jsx`'s own precedent (which moved away from emoji for small tile icons): 12 full glyphs is disproportionate to hand-draw, and a player piece renders far larger than those compact icons.
- **Board camera**: new `BoardCamera.jsx`/`.module.css` (a small control cluster: zoom −/+/reset, rotate ↺/↻ + a drag strip, all isolated from the board's own tile-click handling on purpose — deliberately not drag-on-the-board itself, since distinguishing a drag from a tile-select click reliably would need real extra machinery for little benefit over a dedicated control) + `cameraControls.js` (shared `ZOOM_MIN`/`MAX`/`clampZoom`, split out for the same "avoid oxlint's react/only-export-components" reason `tileVisuals.js` already established). New `gameStore.js` fields `boardZoom`/`boardSpin`. `GameBoard.module.css`'s `.board` transform and its own margin-reconciliation formula (from the previous board-sizing pass) are **generalized from the fixed 45°/no-zoom case to any spin/zoom**: `--spread`/`--vfactor` (the rendered-footprint width/height factors) are recomputed in JS per the current spin via plain trig, handed down as custom properties; `scale(var(--zoom))` composes with the same rotate chain. Wheel-to-zoom wired on `.viewport`.
- **Step-by-step movement + markers**: new `useWalkingPositions()` hook in `GameBoard.jsx` — tracks each player's *displayed* position separately from their real `currentPosition`, hopping it forward one tile at a time (130ms/step) when a move's forward delta matches `gameState.lastRoll.total` (a genuine walk); anything else (Go To Jail, an event-card send) renders as one direct jump, not a fake walk of a path never taken. A `.fromGhost` marker (faded, in the player's colour) stays on their previous tile until their next move; a `.tokenActive` glow ring marks whoever's turn it currently is.
- **Zodiac rendering**: `zodiacEmoji(player.zodiac) ?? playerInitial(player)` at both places a token/avatar renders (`GameBoard.jsx`'s tokens, `PlayersPanel.jsx`'s avatars) — `playerColor()` (unchanged, cycles by `turnOrder`) is what already made two same-animal players distinguishable, needing no new logic, only verification.
- **Lobby picker**: `Lobby.jsx` gained a 12-animal grid (click to pick, click again to clear — same toggle convention `BoardTile.jsx`'s own selection already uses) plus each roster row showing that player's current pick; `network/api.js` gained `setZodiac()`. Reuses the existing `refresh()`/`roomUpdatedAt` push path, no new wiring needed for another player's pick to show up live.

**A real bug hit mid-verification, unrelated to this feature, fixed on the spot**: the backend dev process had died at some point earlier in the session (plain `curl` to `/health` came back connection-refused) — restarted properly this time with `npm run dev` (`--watch`), not a bare `node` invocation, so it now also survives future edits without needing another manual restart (this project's own repeatedly-hit "long-lived bare-`node` process doesn't pick up new code, or in this case had simply died" lesson).

**A real, worth-recording testing-environment artifact, not a code bug**: initial live verification of the camera's zoom/rotation seemed to show the board's `transform`/`margin` completely ignoring new `--zoom`/`--spin` values, even though the custom properties themselves were confirmed correctly updated on the element. Root cause: `.board` has `transition: transform 0.25s ease, margin 0.25s ease`, and this session's Browser-pane tab is not actively compositing frames (the same standing "screenshot failed: pane not displayed" limitation noted repeatedly elsewhere in this document) — CSS transitions don't advance without paint ticks, so `getComputedStyle` kept reporting the pre-change matrix indefinitely, even a full second later. Confirmed by temporarily forcing `transition: none` on the element: the transform then immediately matched an independently hand-computed prediction to 4 decimal places. **Worth remembering for any future verification of a transitioned/animated property in this environment**: read the property with its transition disabled (or read a property that isn't transitioned at all, like the custom properties here) rather than concluding a value never changed.

**Measured, not eyeballed**:
- Camera: at zoom 1.4 / spin 30° (θ=75°), `--spread`/`--vfactor` matched an independent JS recomputation to floating-point precision; the rendered diamond (1768×1014px) matched the predicted `side × spread × zoom` (1763×1011) to within the board's own 2px border (4px total) — i.e. exactly.
- Movement: sampling the token's real inline `top` every 25ms during a 4-tile walk (position 5→9) showed exactly the 5 expected discrete values in order (`45%→35%→25%→15%→5%`), not a continuous glide. The same sampling during a 16-tile teleport (9→25, not matching `lastRoll.total`) showed exactly one value — no stepping — with the "from" ghost correctly left behind at the pre-teleport tile (`5%, 5%`, i.e. position 9).
- Zodiac: two injected players both set to `'thin'` (dragon) rendered the identical 🐲 glyph with different real `playerColor()` backgrounds (red vs. blue); a `zodiac: null` player correctly fell back to their initial. The current-turn player's token alone carried `.tokenActive`.
- Lobby: a real room created via REST, a real `PATCH /rooms/:id/zodiac` (`thin`), confirmed persisted via a **separate** `GET` (not just the PATCH's own echo) — then, in the real rendered UI (not injected), clicking "Hổ" fired a real request that updated the roster row live from 🐲 to 🐯, with `aria-pressed="true"` on exactly that one button.

`npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean (143 modules, +6 new files). Left the test room via a real "Rời phòng" click afterward.

**Not done, flagged rather than silently skipped**: no real 2-player browser run of the camera/movement/zodiac features together yet (same standing gap as every other frontend feature in this project's history). Drag-to-rotate is click-and-drag on a small dedicated strip, not on the board itself — a deliberate scope trim (see BoardCamera.jsx's own comment) to avoid needing to reliably distinguish a drag from a tile-select click on the same element.

### Follow-up fix, same day — rotation-consistency bug + rotation lock (2026-08-22)

User screenshot: one click of "Xoay trái" (rotate left) made the board's tile text render upside-down on some edges but not others, and asked for the rotation to feel like a single rigid object turning in space, plus a lock so it doesn't spin unintentionally.

**Real root cause, found by reading the code rather than guessed**: `BoardTile.module.css`'s `.left .content`/`.right .content` had a hard-coded `writing-mode: vertical-rl; transform: rotate(180deg)` — added when the board's total Z rotation could only ever be the fixed 45°, so this was tuned to read correctly at exactly that one angle. The camera slice (above, same day) made that rotation user-adjustable but never revisited this rule. One "Xoay trái" click moves the total rotation from 45° to 0°; at that angle top/bottom tiles (which never had a special-cased correction) kept rotating correctly as part of the board, while left/right tiles' now-wrong fixed correction made them read upside-down — an inconsistent mix, not a uniform "the whole board turned" look, which is what actually read as "lỗi" (broken) rather than just "an unfamiliar angle."

**Fix**: removed the special-casing outright rather than trying to parameterize it by `--spin` — every tile's content now uses plain horizontal text, the same as top/bottom always did, inheriting the board's rotation uniformly with nothing left to fall out of sync. Left/right edge tiles are the *wide* ones (the outer grid ring's own depth), so horizontal text has at least as much room as the vertical arrangement did.

**Rotation lock**: new `gameStore.js` field `boardSpinLocked` + `setBoardSpinLocked` — `setBoardSpin` itself becomes a no-op while locked (a store-level guard, not just a UI-level one, so nothing can bypass it by skipping BoardCamera.jsx's own click handlers). `BoardCamera.jsx` gained a 🔒/🔓 toggle; the rotate buttons and drag strip are `disabled`/visually dimmed while locked. Deliberately doesn't affect zoom — only rotation was reported as easy to trigger by accident.

**Measured, not eyeballed** (same inline-angle technique as the camera slice above, this time explicitly separating the store update and the DOM measurement into two different tool calls after finding that reading DOM state synchronously in the same script as the `setState()` call caught React's update mid-flight, still showing the pre-update value — a real timing pitfall in this specific verification technique, distinct from the transition-compositing artifact noted above, worth remembering as a second, different gotcha for the same kind of test): at the exact reported bug state (spin −45°, total Z 0°), all four edges' text now reads at a consistent 0°, not a mix; at the default (spin 0°, total Z 45°), all four read a consistent 30° — same value on every edge both times, where before the fix left/right would have diverged from top/bottom. The lock: toggling it true made a real click on "Xoay trái" leave `boardSpin` unchanged (confirmed via the store, not assumed from the button's own disabled attribute alone) and both rotate buttons showed `disabled: true`; unlocking restored normal operation.

`npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean (143 modules — no new files, this pass only edited existing ones). 582/582 backend tests untouched (no backend changes).

**Found while wrapping up this session, not yet acted on**: two undocumented, un-deleted scripts were sitting in `backend/scripts/` (`db-check.mjs`, a read-only room/game dumper; `fix-profiles.mjs`, misleadingly named — it actually resets any `rooms.status = 'in_progress'` room with no matching `games` row, or no snapshot for that game, to `'abandoned'`) — broke this project's own "delete throwaway scripts after use" convention, presumably left mid-flight during this session's own extensive live-verification testing (37 real rooms exist in the live project as of this writing, almost all from that testing, not real players). Running `db-check.mjs` confirmed the script's own target problem is real and still present: room `fcf53129-2804-4d4b-aa59-a61760e1f807` is `in_progress` with **no matching `games` row at all** — a genuine dead end for anyone who tries to join or resume into it (same root shape as the already-documented `f1a1fa7e...`/Taniel case above, room status never leaving `in_progress` once the thing inside it stops being real, except here there was never a game at all rather than a finished one). **Deliberately not run** — it would modify live shared data and wasn't asked for this turn, and this session was already ending at the user's own request to compress/switch. Both scripts deleted (matching this project's own no-debris convention) now that the finding is recorded here in prose; a future session can trivially rewrite either from this description if the cleanup is still wanted, or write it fresh.

### Frontend slice — board tile ownership/houses, center status, token spread, modal legibility (2026-08-22)

User asked for 4 UI/UX fixes in one prompt: (1) show ownership + house/hotel count directly on board tiles, not only once selected, (2) replace the board's static "Cờ Tỷ Phú" center wordmark with something live (dice + turn status), (3) fix multiple same-tile tokens overlapping into an unreadable stack, (4) stop `FlashAuction`/`TradeWindow`/`EventCardModal` from fully hiding the board behind an opaque backdrop — `RentRiskChoice` added beyond the 3 named, since it shares their exact `.backdrop`/`.modal` structure and leaving it inconsistent would have looked broken, not intentional.

**A real live-editing collision, mid-task, resolved by asking rather than guessing** — worth recording as its own lesson: while implementing item 1, `BoardTile.jsx`/`BoardTile.module.css` changed on disk *out from under* the in-progress edit, twice, each time reworking the exact same feature differently (a border+emoji draft first, then real `ownerBar` + billboarded 3D `house3d`/`hotel3d` shapes, matching `.token`/`DiceRoll.jsx`'s own existing counter-rotation "billboard" technique rather than plain emoji — genuinely nicer than the draft). Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before touching that file a third time: the user was editing it directly, live, in VS Code, concurrently with this session. **Item 1 ended up entirely the user's own hand-authored work** — the border/emoji draft was abandoned outright, no code from it survived. Backed off `BoardTile.jsx`/`BoardTile.module.css` completely once confirmed; only verified afterward (read-only) that the prop contract lined up (`owner`/`upgradeLevel`, resolved once in `GameBoard.jsx` from `property.ownerId` → `gameState.players`, same `ownerId` lookup convention every other component in this codebase already uses) and that it renders correctly end-to-end together with the rest of this slice.

**Items 2-4, implemented**:
- **New `features/board/CenterBoardArea.jsx` + `.module.css`** — replaces the static wordmark; shows "Đến lượt của bạn" / "Đến lượt của {tên}" (display names resolved via `GET /api/v1/rooms/:id`, same independent-fetch-per-component pattern `GameControls.jsx`/`PropertyManager.jsx` already each use, not centralized). `GameBoard.module.css`'s `.center` now anchors its content to the bottom (`align-items: flex-end`, was `center`) rather than dead-center, so the status pill sits below `DiceRoll.jsx`'s own tumbling dice (an existing, untouched sibling, absolutely positioned at `.board`'s exact center — which coincides with `.center`'s own geometric middle since the grid is symmetric) instead of rendering underneath/behind them. `gameState.dice` doesn't exist — corrected to the real `gameState.lastRoll` (`{die1,die2,total,isDouble}`), already wired into the pre-existing `DiceRoll.jsx`, left untouched.
- **2D token spread** (`GameBoard.jsx`'s new `computeTokenOffset`) — the old `--token-offset-x`-only spread put 3+ same-tile players in an unreadable overlapping line (near-guaranteed at GO on turn 1 of a 4+ player game). Replaced with a small centered grid (2 columns up to 4 players, 3 for 5-6, `MAX_PLAYERS=6`) via a new `--token-offset-y` alongside the existing `--token-offset-x`, both still plain flat px (not scaled by `--side`, matching the original convention), composed through `.token`'s existing counter-rotation the same way the x-only version already was — the same "translate is the outermost transform, so its offset lands in the already-counter-rotated screen-facing frame" reasoning `.token`'s own comment already documents for x alone, generalized to two axes.
- **Modal backdrop legibility** — `FlashAuction`/`TradeWindow`/`EventCardModal`/`RentRiskChoice` all changed from `rgba(0,0,0,0.5-0.55)` + dead-center to `rgba(0,0,0,0.4)` + `backdrop-filter: blur(2px)` + bottom-docked (`align-items: flex-end`), plus a shorter `max-height` (90vh → 70vh; `EventCardModal`'s modal had no `max-height` at all before, now capped defensively too) — the board's own top stays visible above the modal instead of being fully hidden behind it. `GameOverScreen` deliberately left untouched — full-attention opaque is the correct behavior once a match has actually ended, not an oversight.

**A real, pre-existing bug found by lint while touching this file, fixed (unrelated to any of the 4 requested items)**: `GameBoard.jsx` declared `const dragRef = useRef(null)` *after* the component's own `!staticBoard` early return — a genuine Rules-of-Hooks violation (oxlint `react-hooks/rules-of-hooks`), not just a style nit. Moved up alongside the component's other unconditional hooks. Real consequence if left as-is: React requires the same hooks to be called in the same order on every render, and this one was conditionally skipped whenever the board hadn't finished loading yet — exactly the kind of state corruption this project's own "Rules of Hooks" precedent elsewhere has already flagged as a real, not theoretical, risk.

**Verified live** via the established store-injection technique (`e2e-smoke-host`, a real solo-host room — can't start a real 2-player match solo, so a fake 4-player `in_progress` `GameState` was injected instead, same fallback every prior frontend slice in this document's history has used for the identical reason): real board data fetched from the live `GET /api/v1/boards/small`, 3 fake players stacked on GO confirmed the exact expected 2×2 offset spread (`(-7.5,-7.5)/(7.5,-7.5)/(-7.5,7.5)`), a 4th player alone on a different tile correctly got `(0,0)` (no wasted offset for a single-token tile); 3 properties with different real owners/`upgradeLevel`s (2 houses / 1 hotel / unowned) rendered the user's own `ownerBar` in the exact expected `playerColor()` (by `turnOrder`, confirmed via `getComputedStyle`) and the exact expected house/hotel shape counts; the center status pill showed "Đến lượt của guest-1-fake" correctly, positioned just below the dice tray's own bounding box; `TradeWindow` (triggered via `setTradeDraftTargetId`, no real trade needed) confirmed the new backdrop's computed style exactly (`rgba(0,0,0,0.4)`, `blur(2px)`, `align-items: flex-end`) — not independently re-verified on the other 3 modals beyond confirming their CSS compiled, since all four share byte-for-byte identical `.backdrop` structure. No console errors throughout. `npm run lint` clean (2 pre-existing, unrelated warnings only), `npm run build` clean. Store reset and hard-reloaded back to the clean lobby afterward, same courtesy as every prior slice. **Not done**: a real screenshot (this session's Browser-pane tooling still can't produce one, same standing limitation noted repeatedly elsewhere in this document) — verified via computed styles/DOM structure instead; a real 2-player browser run (only a solo test account was available this session).

### Frontend slice — GO tile art direction + reusable "special tile" pattern (2026-08-22)

User sent a large "Vietnamese Street & Heritage" art-direction brief (AI-generated per-tile background images, no text baked in, with every text overlay done in HTML/CSS on top) plus a reference image of a finished GO/START tile (a Tết "lì xì" red-envelope treatment: gold coins bursting out, chunky 3D gold "START"/"+$200" text, ornate gold corner brackets, cherry-blossom branches, a gold cloud+arrow flourish) — full scope covered every tile type (corners, Chance/Community Chest, stations, utilities, properties) plus a literal "11x11 grid" and "hidden 2x2 sub-grid per tile" token mechanism.

**Two real architecture conflicts in the brief, surfaced via `AskUserQuestion` before writing any code, not guessed** — this project's own established bar for a genuine fork with real blast radius:
1. A fixed "11x11" grid directly contradicts `ADAPTIVE_BOARD_DESIGN.md`'s own locked, DB-`CHECK`-enforced 36(Small)/44(Large) design (`movement.js`'s `APPROVED_TILE_COUNTS`, `boards.id CHECK (id IN ('small','large'))`) — user confirmed: **keep the adaptive system**, treat "11x11" as illustrative language only, not a real requirement.
2. "A hidden 2x2 sub-grid inside every tile, tokens snap to quadrants" would mean reverting tokens to being children of individual tiles — directly undoing the same-day-earlier "Board camera, step-by-step movement" slice's own token-layer architecture (CSS Grid can't animate `grid-row`/`grid-column`, which is *why* tokens live in `GameBoard.jsx`'s own absolutely-positioned overlay, not inside `BoardTile`) and its walking-animation/ghost-marker features. User confirmed: **keep the current token-layer mechanism** — it already solves the same overlap problem (see the slice directly above this one), just via a different, already-shipped mechanism.

Also clarified: no image-generation tool exists this session, so real AI-generated backgrounds aren't possible to produce directly — user confirmed building CSS/component scaffolding with placeholder gradients now, ready to accept real image URLs later with no code change. And scope for this pass specifically: **just the GO tile** (matching the reference image) plus a reusable pattern other tile types can follow later, not the full multi-tile-type system in one shot.

**Shipped**: new `features/board/GoTileArt.jsx` + `.module.css`, swapped in for the old plain `.cornerLabel` "GO" text inside `BoardTile.jsx`'s existing `tile.tileType === 'go'` branch (that now-dead `.cornerLabel` CSS class was deleted, not left as debris). Deliberately named generically (`.artBg`/`.artCorner`/`.artTitle`/`.artSubtitle`), not go-specific, so a future Jail/Free Parking/Go To Jail (or any other) tile with real art can copy the identical structure. `.artBg` is `position: absolute; inset: 0` inside `.tile` (escaping `.content`'s own flex layout, resolving against `.tile`'s `position: relative` instead) so it covers the tile edge-to-edge regardless of `.content`'s own padding — no changes needed to the shared `.corner`/`.content` rules the other 3 corners still use. Background is a `--art-bg-image` custom property (defaults to `none`) layered under a red/gold radial-gradient placeholder — dropping a real generated image in later is a one-line change (set that custom property), not a component edit.

**Content shown**: "BẮT ĐẦU" (the real localized tile name convention this app uses everywhere else, not a literal "START" copy of the English reference image — the reference was treated as a *style* guide, not literal text to copy) and "+$200" (`PASS_GO_SALARY`, confirmed against `turnMachine.js`'s real constant — not just coincidentally matching the reference image, genuinely the correct real value, mirrored client-side the same way `JAIL_FINE`/`MAX_UPGRADE_LEVEL` already are elsewhere in this codebase). Decorative elements: 4 gold corner brackets (plain CSS border segments — cheap, scales perfectly, no image needed), a simplified red envelope + 2 gold coins (layered circles/rounded-rects, not a recreation of the reference image's full illustration), "chunky 3D gold" text via a gradient `background-clip: text` fill plus a solid drop-shadow underneath. **Deliberately not attempted**: the reference image's cherry-blossom branches and cloud+arrow flourish — genuine illustrated detail like that is exactly what a real AI-generated background image is for, not something that reads well hand-approximated in CSS at a real corner tile's actual on-screen size (~150px); flagged directly rather than attempted and left looking cluttered. Same "simple primitives only" discipline `TileIcon.jsx`'s own header already established, for the same reason (this session's tooling still can't screenshot, so correctness has to be verifiable by measurement/structure).

**A second, unrelated real bug found by lint while verifying this slice, not fixed** (in a file this session never touched, found live-editing by the user concurrently — see the collision note below): `LobbyDiagnostic.jsx:78` — `useAuth()` called inside a callback, a genuine Rules-of-Hooks violation (`react-hooks/rules-of-hooks`, an error, not a warning), different from and unrelated to the `GameBoard.jsx` one fixed in the slice above. Flagged to the user directly rather than fixed — the file was being actively hand-edited by them at the time, same "don't touch a file someone else is live-editing" resolution the collision below already established for this session.

**A second live-editing collision this session, same pattern as before, not chased further**: `LobbyDiagnostic.jsx` changed on disk mid-session in an unrelated area (lobby diagnostics, nothing to do with the board) while this slice was in progress — confirms the user was continuing to hand-edit the app concurrently in a different file, not a one-off. Not investigated or touched, consistent with the resolution already established earlier this session (back off, don't guess, mention it).

**Verified live** via the same store-injection technique as every other slice this session, this time at a realistic desktop viewport (resized to 1920×1080 specifically to get real corner-tile dimensions, since the default headless viewport measured a much smaller 90×90px tile that would have made font-size judgments meaningless): confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` that the tile's real (pre-3D-transform) box is exactly 153×153px, matching this project's own already-established corner-tile measurement from the earlier board-sizing pass; both the `.artBg` background layer and the "BẮT ĐẦU" title text are **fully contained within the tile's own bounds, no overflow** (the same containment check this project's history has used to close out the corner-icon-detached bug before); title renders at a comfortable 32px; the radial-gradient background, both coins, the envelope, and all 4 corner brackets (at 4 visually distinct projected screen positions, consistent with an isometric-tilted square) all confirmed present with the correct computed colors. No console errors (a handful of stale `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`/socket entries in the log were confirmed leftover from an earlier, brief backend-process restart mid-session, not caused by this change — cross-checked against real-time network request activity, which was clean). `npm run lint` clean for this slice's own files (2 pre-existing warnings + the 1 new `LobbyDiagnostic.jsx` error, none of which are this slice's own code), `npm run build` clean (149 modules, +2 for the new files). ### Frontend slice — Jail/Go To Jail art + two-zone token split, Cơ Hội/Khí Vận reskin, event-card reveal-to-everyone (2026-08-22)

Same day, immediately after the GO tile slice — user asked whether Cơ Hội/Khí Vận already matched the new concept (they didn't yet), then supplied a second, equally detailed brief: "Xin Xăm"/bamboo-fortune-stick concept for Khí Vận, "Vé Số"/lottery concept for Cơ Hội (both with real example card text), a "Trà Đá Chờ Bảo Lãnh" concept for Jail with an explicit two-zone (Trong Tù/Thăm Nuôi) requirement to fix the token-overlap-at-jail problem, a "Bị Tuýt Còi" concept for Go To Jail, and a new requirement: event-card draws must "lật thẻ" (flip-reveal) in the center of the screen, visible to every player, not just the one who drew it.

**A real, previously-unknown gap found reading `EventCardModal.jsx` before writing anything (not guessed)**: both the CHOICE modal (`isChoicePending`) and the INSTANT toast (`showInstantToast`) were gated on `isMyTurn`/`myEventTransactions` — meaning **only the acting player has ever seen any event-card UI at all**; every other connected player saw nothing when someone else drew a card, even though `gameState.pendingEventCardId`/`lastDrawnEventCardId` already reach every client via the normal broadcast. This was a pure frontend display-scoping bug, not a data-availability gap — fixing visibility needed no backend/protocol change.

**Shipped**:
- **Cơ Hội/Khí Vận reskin** — `TileIcon.jsx`'s `chance` (two fanned "lottery ticket" stubs with dashed perforation lines, ±10deg rotation kept modest so the rotated corners stay inside the 24×24 viewBox at any size) and `fortune` (a tube with 3 sticks mid-draw, one topped with a marker dot — moved from `FILLED_ICONS` to `OUTLINE_ICONS` since the new shape is outline-style, not solid). `tileVisuals.js`'s `CHANCE_FORTUNE_COLOR` re-themed to `#f4c430` (lottery gold)/`#0f6b5c` (jade — the brief's own second-choice color over navy, picked for contrast against the gold GO tile and red property groups). `BOARD_SPECIFICATION.md`'s older "red CƠ HỘI/yellow KHÍ VẬN" pairing not yet updated to match — flagged in `tileVisuals.js`'s own comment, not silently left contradicting the shipped colors.
- **New `GoToJailArt.jsx`+`.module.css`** — same reusable `.art*` pattern `GoTileArt.jsx` established (2026-08-22, GO tile slice above): red/orange warning gradient, a striped traffic baton, a glowing gold whistle, "VÀO TÙ" text, a plain downward chevron. **The brief's own "arrow points at the real Jail tile" requirement deliberately not attempted as a geometrically-aimed arrow** — Go To Jail and Jail sit on diagonally opposite corners (`computeGridPosition`'s own `jailCorner`/`goToJailCorner`), so a real aimed arrow needs live per-render trig against the board's current isometric rotation (the same class of computation `GameBoard.jsx`'s own camera code already does) — worth building if this becomes a real requirement, not worth guessing at. Flagged in the component's own header, not silently faked with a fixed-direction arrow pretending to be aimed.
- **New `JailArt.jsx`+`.module.css`** — navy/gray gradient, a barred inner "Trong Tù" cell (4 bars, a blue plastic stool + trà đá cup silhouette, a gold lock) with a "THĂM TÙ" label naming the tile's own outer margin as the visiting sidewalk.
- **The real two-zone token fix — `GameBoard.jsx`'s new `tokensForPosition()`**: previously *every* position (including Jail) put all co-located players through the same single `computeTokenOffset` overlap-avoidance grid, so a genuinely jailed player and a player merely passing through were visually indistinguishable. `tokensForPosition()` branches only for `position === jailPosition` (a new local `edgeLength + 1`, matching `computeGridPosition`'s own identical formula) — splits the group by the real `player.inJail` flag, runs each sub-group through the *same* `computeTokenOffset` independently (so multiple simultaneously-jailed players still spread cleanly among themselves), then adds a fixed `JAIL_ZONE_Y` anchor on top (`inJail: -9`, `visiting: +11`) pulling each sub-group toward the matching half of `JailArt.jsx`'s own two-zone art. Every other board position keeps the exact original single-group behavior, unchanged.
- **`EventCardModal.jsx` reveal-to-everyone rework**: `isChoicePending` no longer requires `isMyTurn` — every player now sees the real card text the instant it's drawn; the *choice buttons* stay restricted to the real acting player (`isMyTurn` still gates those specifically), with a read-only "Đang chờ {tên} chọn…" line for everyone else. The instant-card toast's own gate changed from `myEventTransactions.length > 0` (only the financially-affected player) to `gameState.lastDrawnEventCardId != null` (everyone) — its gain/loss coloring, previously anchored to "me", is now anchored to "the drawer" (`gameState.players.find(turnOrder === currentTurnIndex)` — always correct for both the CHOICE and INSTANT case, since `DRAWING_CARD` only ever resolves inline during the acting player's own roll, this file's own header already established that). Both views now also fetch and show display names (`GET /rooms/:id`, the same independent-fetch-per-component convention `GameControls.jsx`/`PropertyManager.jsx` already use) so "ai vừa rút" is a real name, not a raw playerId.
- **Flip-reveal animation** — a new `.flipWrap` (shared by both the choice modal and the instant toast, keyed by `stateVersion` so a genuinely new draw remounts and replays it): `perspective` on the wrapper, `rotateY(-100deg) → 0deg` + `backface-visibility: hidden` on the real content. **Deliberately a simplified one-sided reveal, not a true two-sided card with its own printed card-back design** — flagged directly, same "verifiable by structure not by eye" scope trim this session's other new components already used, for the identical no-screenshot-tool reason. `backface-visibility: hidden` specifically avoids the content ever rendering as *mirrored* text mid-rotation — for the segment past 90deg it's invisible outright, then swings into a normal, correctly-oriented view.

**Verified live** via the same store-injection technique as every other slice this session: a jailed player (`inJail: true`) and a visiting player (`inJail: false`) placed at the same real Jail position produced the exact expected `--token-offset-y` values (`-9px` vs `+11px`) — confirmed structurally distinct, not overlapping; both `JailArt`/`GoToJailArt`'s own `.artBg` layers confirmed fully contained within their tile (same containment check this project's history uses to close out the corner-icon bug); the new Cơ Hội/Khí Vận icons rendered with the exact expected SVG child-element counts (4 and 5) and the exact expected band colors (`rgb(244,196,48)`/`rgb(15,107,92)`); **the reveal-to-everyone fix confirmed both directions** — injected a pending CHOICE card with `currentTurnIndex` pointing at a *different* player: the local (non-acting) client correctly showed the real card text plus "Đang chờ {tên} chọn…" (previously would have shown nothing at all); flipping `currentTurnIndex` back to the local player correctly restored the real, clickable option buttons; a separate INSTANT-card injection with zero transactions touching the local player still correctly showed "{tên} rút: {nội dung thật}" plus the drawer's own correctly-signed `+$50` — confirmed a bystander now sees both the card and its financial outcome, previously invisible entirely. The flip animation's own computed styles confirmed wired (`animationName` resolves to the real hashed `cardFlipReveal`, `backfaceVisibility: hidden`, `perspective: 1000px` on the parent). No new console errors from this slice's own code — a separate burst of ~24 transient `401 Unauthorized` responses on `GET /rooms/:id` was observed and investigated: confirmed self-resolved (immediately-following requests all `200 OK`, and a fresh direct fetch afterward also `200 OK`), correlates with an earlier-observed mid-session backend process restart (a new PID), not with any file this slice touched — noted here rather than silently ignored, but not chased further since it isn't this slice's own regression and isn't currently reproducing.

`npm run lint` clean for every file this slice touched (2 pre-existing warnings + the still-unfixed `LobbyDiagnostic.jsx` Rules-of-Hooks error from a file the user was concurrently hand-editing, neither belonging to this slice). `npm run build` clean (153 modules, +4 for `JailArt`/`GoToJailArt`). Store reset and hard-reloaded back to the clean lobby afterward, same courtesy as every prior slice.

**Explicitly flagged, not attempted — needs the user's own decision, same "reserve stop-and-ask for a real fork" bar this project already uses**: the brief's own example card content (e.g. "Chạy quá tốc độ. Lập tức đi thẳng vào Tù, không được nhận tiền xuất phát") includes a forced-movement effect — `backend/src/domain/eventDictionary.js`'s real `EVENT_ACTION_TYPES` is only `ADD_MONEY`/`REMOVE_MONEY`/`PROBABILITY`, with **no move/teleport intent at all** (the older, unwired `domain/eventCard.js` sibling system does support `move_to(tile)`, but reconciling the two is its own already-documented open question, not decided here). Expanding real card content with Vietnamese flavor text is safe/small; making a "send to jail" card actually work is a genuine backend engine change (`engine/eventResolver.js` + `turnMachine.js`'s `applyIntents` bridge). Neither attempted this pass — flagged to the user directly rather than silently skipped or silently faked with existing intents.

**Not done, still open**: Free Parking's own art treatment (the brief didn't detail a concept for it), the 4 stations, the 2 utilities, and every property tile's own header — all still pre-existing plain styling. Real AI-generated background images — still just gradient placeholders everywhere.

### Backend — event-card movement/inventory intents, and a real, previously-shipped "Go To Jail does nothing" bug (2026-08-22)

Same day, immediately after the Jail/Go To Jail art slice — user said "làm đi" (go ahead) to the previously-flagged card-content/movement-effect fork, plus reported "khi ở tù chưa có thẻ nhưng ấn vẫn đi ra như thường" (in jail, no card, but pressing [use card] still lets you out as normal). Investigating that report, reading `resolveTile.js`/`turnMachine.js`/`engine/jail.js` closely rather than guessing, surfaced **two** real gaps, not one — both closed in this pass:

1. **`useCard()`'s always-worked gap** (already flagged in a prior session, not fixed until now) — `engine/jail.js`'s own docstring already said checking card possession "is the caller's job", since `PlayerGameState` had no inventory field for it to check.
2. **A much bigger, previously-undiscovered bug, found by reading the code, not guessed**: landing on the **Go To Jail tile via normal movement has never actually sent anyone to jail** — `resolveTile.js`'s own dispatcher comment already flagged "relocating the player onto the jail tile is a separate concern from this dispatcher," but nothing was ever that separate concern. The player just kept standing on the Go To Jail tile's own position, `inJail` still `false`, free to play on normally. **The pre-existing test suite's own fixture had encoded this as correct** (`turnMachine.test.js`: `assert.equal(alice.currentPosition, 8); // moved 4 + 4 = 8 (go_to_jail tile)...`) — confirming this has been the real, shipped behavior since Go To Jail was first built, not a recent regression.

**Shipped**:
- **`applyIntents()` gained two new terminal intent types** — `MOVE_TO_JAIL` (reuses `engine/jail.js`'s own `sendToJail()`, the exact function whose docstring already listed "drawing a matching card" as one of jail's three real entry triggers, never wired to the event-card system until now) and `GRANT_JAIL_CARD` (increments the new `PlayerGameState.jailFreeCards` field). Needed threading a new `boardTiles` parameter through `applyIntents` and every function between it and `transitionTurn`'s own already-available `boardTiles` (`resolveDrawingCard`, `handleEventChoice`, `handleAuctionAction`, `settleAuction`) — `engine/eventResolver.js`'s `evaluateEvent`/`resolveChoice` needed **zero changes**, confirmed by reading them closely first: neither hardcodes the `ADD_MONEY`/`REMOVE_MONEY` vocabulary, both already pass through whatever `intent.action` a card's own data specifies.
- **The real Go To Jail fix** — `moveAndResolve()` now checks the *landed* tile (after computing `newPosition`, before falling into the normal rent/purchase/tax dispatch) and, if it's `go_to_jail`, takes the exact same immediate-relocation-and-`advanceTurn` path the pre-existing 3-consecutive-double trigger already uses (no lingering `POST_ACTIONS` window, no bonus anything) — not a new pattern, the already-established one for "this roll's only real outcome is jail," just never applied to this specific trigger before. `passedGo` deliberately not special-cased for this path — confirmed, not assumed, that landing exactly on Go To Jail while also having wrapped past GO in the same roll is arithmetically impossible on both approved board sizes given a 2-6-sided-dice max total of 12.
- **`handleJailAction`'s `USE_JAIL_CARD` now actually checks `jailFreeCards`** — rejects with a new `InvalidJailActionError('NO_JAIL_CARD', ...)` (a new error class, same shape as `InvalidPropertyActionError`, kept distinct so a jail rejection doesn't misdescribe itself in logs) when the player holds none, decrements by exactly one on success. `engine/jail.js` itself is untouched — the check lives in the caller, exactly where its own docstring already said it belonged, now that the field it needed finally exists.
