- **Real Vietnamese event-card content** — 5 new `EVENT_CARDS` entries (up from 2): 3 plain flavor cards using the user's own example text verbatim (`CHUNG_CU_MINI_THU_TIEN` +$100, `QUY_TO_DAN_PHO` -$50, `XE_CONG_NGHE_GIO_CAO_DIEM` +$50), plus the two new-capability flagships — `CHAY_QUA_TOC_DO` (the user's own "speeding → straight to jail" example, a real `MOVE_TO_JAIL` intent, no invented extra fine — the example only ever named the jail effect) and `VE_SO_TRUNG_AN_UI` (a `GRANT_JAIL_CARD` card, the one real source of a legitimately-held Get Out of Jail Free card). Not deck-split (chance vs. fortune) — an already-separately-documented gap, not resolved here, out of this pass's scope.
- **Frontend**: `GameControls.jsx`'s "Dùng Thẻ Miễn Phí" button now disabled unless `me.jailFreeCards > 0`, with an honest tooltip explaining why when it's 0 (replacing the old permanently-honest-but-permanently-enabled tooltip) and a `(N)` count suffix when held.

**Test fallout, all fixed properly, not loosened** — running the full suite for this work also surfaced **two completely unrelated pre-existing stale tests**, found only because the full suite was run, not touched otherwise:
- `resolveTile.test.js`: a test asserting landing on your own property resolves to `POST_ACTIONS` — the real code already returns `AWAITING_UPGRADE` for this case (a genuine existing feature, own-property landing shows a build popup), just never had its test updated.
- `turnMachine.test.js`: a `HOSTILE_BUYOUT` test asserting a successful 2.0x buyout *including* a property's building value — the real code already unconditionally rejects `HOUSE_PROTECTED` for any `upgradeLevel > 0` (its own comment: "User requirement: properties with ANY houses cannot be taken over"), making that specific scenario permanently unreachable. Split into two: a successful-buyout test on an unimproved property (preserving coverage of the exact-2.0x-price happy path) plus a new dedicated `HOUSE_PROTECTED` rejection test.

Directly caused by this session's own fixes, fixed the same way (updated, not loosened): 2 pre-existing `ATTEMPT_JAIL_ROLL`-with-doubles tests whose fixture roll happened to coincidentally land the escaping player exactly on the Go To Jail tile — before this fix that was harmless (the bug), after it, correctly sends them right back to jail, which is a real and correct consequence but not what those two tests meant to exercise. Moved their fixture's starting position so the same escape roll lands somewhere inert (`free_parking`) instead, and added a **new**, dedicated test that keeps the original position specifically to prove the "escape then immediately re-jailed" edge case is itself real and correct, not accidentally broken by the fixture change. Also fixed the pre-existing `USE_JAIL_CARD` happy-path test (now needs `jailFreeCards: 1` in its own fixture) and added a new `NO_JAIL_CARD` rejection test, plus two new tests exercising `MOVE_TO_JAIL`/`GRANT_JAIL_CARD` through the real `DRAWING_CARD` path (not just `JAIL_DECISION`'s own `USE_JAIL_CARD`, which never touches the event-card system at all).

**587/587 backend tests passing** (582 baseline + 3 from the test-fixing pass + 2 new event-card-intent tests). No backend lint script exists in this project (native test runner only, no separate lint tool configured for the backend). Frontend: `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean (153 modules, no new files this slice — `GameControls.jsx` only). Frontend gating verified live via store injection (`jailFreeCards: 0` → disabled with the honest tooltip; `jailFreeCards: 2` → enabled, "(2)" suffix) — the backend fixes themselves rest on the test suite, not a live playthrough, since **the running dev backend process was not restarted this pass** (plain `node`, not `--watch` — same standing "long-lived process doesn't reflect a later edit" fact this project's history has hit many times before) — flagged directly rather than silently assumed live.

**Not done, flagged rather than silently skipped**: deck-membership split (chance vs. fortune drawing from the same shared pool) — pre-existing, separate, not touched. A fuller card deck (16 cards per type, matching real Monopoly's own count) — only 5 new cards added this pass, covering the user's own given examples plus the two new mechanics, not an exhaustive content-authoring pass.

### Backend — the full 24-card "Cơ Hội/Khí Vận" deck, phases 1-3 of 4 (2026-08-22)

Same day — user shared a complete, economically-reasoned 24-card deck design doc (card-by-card text, effect, frequency, and an explicit money-distribution philosophy: 35-40% direct money, the rest movement/economy-modifier/risk cards, anti-snowball by design) and asked for an opinion before implementing. Real technical gap analysis (not guessed) against the actual `EVENT_ACTION_TYPES` vocabulary found only 2 of 24 cards worked with zero new engine capability — proposed a build order grouped by *shared engine capability* rather than card-by-card, confirmed by the user ("làm đi" — proceed in that order). Discovered while implementing that the originally-proposed grouping under-shared: K06/K10 (richest/poorest-pays) and K03/K04/K09/K12 (every/threshold-crossing player) all need the identical "resolve against a *computed player-set*" primitive — merged into one phase rather than split across two, flagged and explained before proceeding, not silently changed.

**Phase 1+2 (10 cards, no new persistent state)** — `EVENT_ACTION_TYPES` gained 5 new terminal intents (`ADD_MONEY_EACH_PLAYER`/`REMOVE_MONEY_EACH_PLAYER`/`REMOVE_MONEY_RICHEST`/`ADD_MONEY_POOREST`/`REMOVE_MONEY_IF_BALANCE_AT_LEAST`/`REMOVE_MONEY_PER_DEVELOPMENT` — 6, not 5, `REMOVE_MONEY_PER_DEVELOPMENT` is a genuinely per-player *computed* amount, K09's own "$25 × mỗi cấp độ nhà" driven by each player's real property holdings, not a flat card-data number like every other intent), all handled in `applyIntents()` via a new shared `eligiblePlayers()` helper (every real, non-bankrupt player — the same filter `handleDeclinePurchase`'s own `eligibleBidders` already used inline, now named and reused). A new optional `eligibility` field on `EventCardDictionaryEntry` (`{field: 'currentBalance', op: 'lt', value}`) closes C08's own "chỉ dùng nếu Cash < $300" — an ineligible draw is a real, revealed no-op (`lastDrawnEventCardId` still set, so the reveal-to-everyone UI still shows it), not silently swallowed. C09 (Luật Sư)'s own two sub-options collapsed into the existing `jailFreeCards`/`GRANT_JAIL_CARD` mechanic rather than a second, functionally-identical inventory system — flagged as a deliberate simplification given the brief's own two options describe the same real outcome once specified concretely.

**Phase 3 (6 more cards, 16/24 total) — the "temporary buff" pair of mechanisms**, deliberately two different shapes:
- **Global, round-scoped** (K01/K02 rent ±10%, K07/K08 build cost ±$50): new `GameState.rentModifierPercent`/`buildCostModifierAmount`, reset to 0 by `advanceTurn()` at the exact real round-wraparound boundary that function already computes (`roundNumber`'s own trigger) — "trong một vòng" (for the rest of the current round), not a separate timer. Layered onto `calculateRent.js`/`economy/propertyEconomy.js`'s own outputs at their `turnMachine.js` call sites — both pure functions stay untouched.
- **Player-specific, one-shot** (C07/C12-B next-build discount, C12-C next-rent discount): new `PlayerGameState.nextBuildDiscount`/`nextRentDiscount`, consumed by that exact player's next qualifying action (`handleBuildHouse`/the `PAYING_RENT` case) regardless of how many rounds that takes, stacking additively with the global modifiers where both apply.
- **C12 (Cơ Hội Cuối)** — the deck's own biggest comeback card, a 3-option CHOICE card with an eligibility gate. The brief's own gate is an OR of two conditions ("nằm trong nhóm net worth thấp nhất, hoặc cash dưới $200") — only the cash half is implemented; the `eligibility` field only supports a single-field check against the drawing player, and "lowest net worth among all players" needs a genuine cross-player comparison this pass didn't build. Flagged directly as a narrower gate than designed, not silently treated as equivalent.

**Testing**: every new intent/mechanism exercised through the real `DRAWING_CARD`/`BUILD_HOUSE`/`ROLL_DICE`→`PAYING_RENT` paths (not `applyIntents` directly — still unexported, same discipline the earlier `MOVE_TO_JAIL`/`GRANT_JAIL_CARD` tests already established), including tie-handling for K06/K10, the round-wraparound reset boundary (confirmed modifiers persist across a non-wrapping `END_TURN` and clear exactly on a wrapping one), and all 3 of C12's options plus its eligibility gate both ways. **605/605 backend tests passing** (587 baseline + 10 phase 1+2 + 8 phase 3). No backend lint script exists in this project.

**Not done — phase 4 of 4, the hardest, deliberately last**: C01/C02 (movement to a dynamically-computed tile — "1 of the next 3 tiles, player's choice" / "nearest unowned property ahead" — needs live board-state computation *and* re-triggering the destination tile's own landing effect, the most architecturally novel piece left), C03 (adjust the next dice roll ±1 — a real new player interaction inserted between rolling and landing resolution, not a passive modifier, why it was grouped here and not with phase 3's other buffs), C05/C11 (dice-face-tiered reward tables — the current `PROBABILITY` intent is binary success/failure only, can't express a 6-way per-face payout), C06/C10 (a loan/deferred-payment mechanic firing automatically at the start of a *future* turn — needs a new "pending obligation" GameState concept, nothing like it exists yet). **The running dev backend process was not restarted this pass either** (same standing fact as the jail/movement fix earlier this same day) — these fixes rest on the test suite, not live gameplay, until it is.

### Backend — the 24-card deck, phase 4 of 4 (partial): C01/C02/C05/C11 (2026-08-22)

Continuing the same day, user approved proceeding straight into phase 4 ("làm luôn đi"). Landed the 4 cards phase 3's own writeup called "the most architecturally novel piece left" plus the two dice-face-reward cards; **C03 and C06/C10 remain undone**, see below.

**New engine capability — card-driven movement that re-triggers a real landing resolution**, not just a field mutation: `EVENT_ACTION_TYPES` gained `MOVE_RELATIVE` (C01 "Lối Tắt" — move exactly N tiles forward, player's own choice of 1/2/3) and `MOVE_TO_NEAREST_UNOWNED_PROPERTY` (C02 "Chuyến Đi May Mắn" — scans forward up to one full lap for the first unowned buyable tile; a real no-op, not an error, when none exists). Both intercepted in their callers (`resolveDrawingCard`/`handleEventChoice`) *before* reaching `applyIntents()` — that function's own contract is "batch simple mutations, always end in POST_ACTIONS," which can't express "go re-run the destination tile's own effect." New shared `moveByStepsAndResolve()` reuses `movePlayer()` for the actual position math (so PASS_GO crediting is the same real code path as a dice roll, not reimplemented) and then calls `resolveLanding()` directly on the new position — the destination's own effect (buy prompt, rent, another card draw, jail, whatever) genuinely fires, not simulated. New `findNearestUnownedAhead()` helper does C02's own board scan against `BUYABLE_TILE_TYPES` (property/transport/utility) and real property ownership.

**New engine capability — per-die-face reward tables**: `EVENT_ACTION_TYPES` gained `DIE_FACE_REWARD` (C05 "Cơ Hội Đầu Tư", C11 "Cú Đánh Liều" — a 6-way payout keyed by which face came up, distinct from the pre-existing `PROBABILITY` intent which is binary success/failure only). `eventResolver.js`'s `resolveChoice()` took a new `dieFaceRoll` parameter (1-6), validated the same way `probabilityRoll` already is. `socketServer.js`'s `serverGeneratedFields()` generates it server-side the same way it already generates `probabilityRoll` — same external-randomness-injection point, no new plumbing. C11 has no real A-vs-B choice in the brief (pay $50, then roll) — modeled as a single-option CHOICE card anyway specifically to reuse this already-proven mechanism rather than build a second injection point for the INSTANT-card path.

**A real bug found and fixed before it ever shipped**: `movement.js`'s `movePlayer()` hard-validated `steps` as a real dice total (`2 ≤ steps ≤ 12`) — its own header comment had explicitly scoped it to dice-only movement, marking card-effect movement "out of scope for this task" when originally written. C01's own `OPT_1_TILE` (steps: 1) falls below that minimum, so choosing it would have thrown `RangeError` at runtime the first time any player picked "tiến 1 ô." Caught by writing the test for it (not by manual play — the dev backend was never restarted to try it live), fixed by loosening the lower bound to `steps ≥ 1` and updating both the doc comment and `movement.test.js`'s own now-stale "rejects steps below 2" test (replaced with a "rejects 0" test plus a new explicit "allows 1" test).

**Testing**: 12 new tests — MOVE_RELATIVE's plain move, its own PASS_GO-wrap case, and the single-tile-doesn't-throw regression test for the bug above; MOVE_TO_NEAREST_UNOWNED_PROPERTY's immediate-hit, skip-owned-tiles, and full-lap-no-op cases; every tier of both C05's and C11's die-face tables including C11's own stake deduction stacking on top; the stake-validation rejection; and a missing/invalid-`dieFaceRoll` rejection. **617/617 backend tests passing** (605 baseline + 11 new turnMachine tests + 1 net-new movement.js test).

**Not done — same two pieces flagged as undesigned at the start of phase 4**: C03 (adjust the next dice roll ±1) needs a real new player interaction inserted between rolling and landing resolution, not a passive modifier — no such insertion point exists in `turnMachine.js` today. C06/C10 (a loan/deferred-payment obligation firing automatically at the start of a *future* turn) needs a new "pending obligation" concept on `GameState` — nothing like it exists yet. Both remain exactly as scoped in the phase-3 writeup above; **20 of 24 cards are done**. **The running dev backend process still has not been restarted** (same standing fact carried through this entire day's work) — none of today's fixes (including the go_to_jail bug from earlier) are live in an actual running game yet, only proven by the test suite.

### Frontend — Trống Đồng centre art, big centre-screen card reveal, and card-effect visibility (2026-08-23)

User brief, four parts: (1) traditional-Vietnamese art for the middle of the board, (2) landing on Cơ Hội/Khí Vận must open a **big animated card dead-centre**, visible to every player, (3) cards whose effect lasts a round must be shown *chung* (shared) so everyone knows what's affecting the round, (4) cards a player *holds* must show in their own player slot, visible both to the holder and to everyone else. All four shipped; every piece reads real `GameState` fields already on the wire, so **zero backend or protocol changes were needed** — verified by reading `socketServer.js`'s broadcast (it emits the whole `gameState` unfiltered) rather than assumed.

**(1) Trống Đồng Đông Sơn medallion** — new `DongSonDrum` inside `features/board/CenterBoardArea.jsx`, one inline SVG on a 0-200 viewBox filling the board's `.center` cell. Concentric bands, exactly as the real artefact: a **14-ray** sun star (the Ngọc Lũ drum's real count), a dotted band, a band of 14 "vòng tròn đồng tâm" motifs, a band of **14 chim Lạc** flying counter-clockwise, a "răng lược" comb-tooth band, and a double rim, plus corner brackets framing the round medallion inside its square cell. Every point list is **computed** (`starPoints()`/`sawtoothPath()`/`ringAngles()`) rather than written as literal path strings — the same "verifiable by structure, not by eye" discipline `TileIcon.jsx`/`GoTileArt.jsx` already set, and the reason the counts could be checked in the live DOM afterwards. Deliberately restrained (opacity 0.5, engraved bronze on the board's own parchment felt) because `DiceRoll.jsx` lands its dice directly on the star — dice legibility outranks medallion contrast. `pointer-events: none`, so it can never swallow a tile click. `.center` (`GameBoard.module.css`) gained `position: relative` purely to anchor it.

**(2) Big centre-screen two-sided card** — `EventCardModal.jsx`/`.module.css` reworked. Both the CHOICE modal (was a 380px bottom-docked panel) and the INSTANT notice (was a small top-corner toast) are now the **same large dead-centre playing card**. This deliberately reverses the bottom-dock half of 2026-08-22's "don't hide the board" pass **for this component only** — the user has now explicitly asked for the opposite here; the backdrop stays translucent + blurred so the board is still legible, and `FlashAuction`/`TradeWindow`/`RentRiskChoice` are untouched. The flip is now a **genuine two-sided card** (a printed back with a "hồi văn" key-fret lattice + the deck's own `TileIcon`, rotating away to reveal the face) — closing the explicitly-flagged one-sided simplification from that same earlier pass. A CHOICE card keeps a real blocking backdrop (the turn genuinely waits on it); an INSTANT card blocks nothing, so its backdrop is `pointer-events: none` click-through and it auto-dismisses after 5s — it can never sit between the acting player and their own End Turn button.

**Cơ Hội vs Khí Vận** is a real distinction to a player but is **not a field on the card**: `eventDictionary.js` has one shared `EVENT_CARDS` map and one shared `gameState.eventDeck` for both tile types (the deck-membership split remains a separately-flagged backend gap). Derived instead from the tile the drawer is actually standing on, which is real and correct at draw time. **Known limit, flagged rather than papered over**: a card that MOVES the drawer (C01/C02) has already changed their position by the time the post-resolution notice renders, so the deck falls back to a neutral "Thẻ Sự Kiện" label there — never to a guessed one.

**(3) Shared round-effect rail** — new `features/game/RoundEffectsBar.jsx`, mounted in a new `.statusRail` in `GameView.jsx` directly under the existing "Vòng N" pill (which lost its own hand-placed absolute offset to the rail). Shows `GameState.rentModifierPercent` / `.buildCostModifierAmount` — the two genuinely global, round-scoped modifiers — with a "hết vòng này" duration note that is the literal truth, since `advanceTurn()` resets both at the real round-wraparound boundary. Renders nothing when neither is active. **Deliberately placed outside the isometric board rather than on the Trống Đồng**: `.center` inherits the board's `rotateX(55deg)/rotateZ(45deg)`, which skews everything drawn there — decorative art survives that fine, a number a player has to actually read does not.

**(4) Per-player held effects** — `PlayersPanel.jsx` now renders small gold "card" chips for `jailFreeCards` / `nextBuildDiscount` / `nextRentDiscount` on **every** player's card, not just the local one. The visible-to-opponents part is the point, and is exactly what the user asked for: a rival holding a Get-Out-Of-Jail card or a pending discount is real information the table should be able to play around. Each chip carries a `title` spelling out the real mechanic (including `nextRentDiscount`'s own `max` cap).

**Verification — measured, not eyeballed.** No screenshot tool was available again this session (the Browser pane doesn't composite frames), so everything below was proven structurally against the real running app with real board data (`GET /api/v1/boards/small`, real chance/fortune positions 2/6/20/30) and an injected `in_progress` GameState:
- **The flip is genuinely two-sided**: cancelling the animation and pinning the flipper at each end state, `document.elementFromPoint` at the card's exact centre returns an element owned by **BACK** at `rotateY(0)` and by **FACE** at `rotateY(180)` — `backface-visibility: hidden` really is showing exactly one side at a time.
- **The animation's own keyframes are exact**: an early measurement caught frame 0 and read 259×301 at cy=314 — precisely the `scale(0.72) translateY(-46px)` written in the keyframe. Seeking the animation to t=1000ms gave **360×418 centred at (640, 360) — the viewport's exact centre** on a 1280×720 window.
- **Deck derivation works both ways**: drawer on position 30 (fortune) rendered "KHÍ VẬN" with `border-top`/card-back `rgb(15,107,92)` (the jade `CHANCE_FORTUNE_COLOR.fortune`); drawer on position 20 (chance) rendered "CƠ HỘI" with `rgb(244,196,48)` (the gold `.chance`). Card text came from the real backend dictionary both times (C05's real two options; `CHUNG_CU_MINI_THU_TIEN`'s real text plus a real `+$120`).
- **The INSTANT card is genuinely non-blocking**: `backdropGhost` with `pointer-events: none` and a fully transparent background, while `.scene` keeps `pointer-events: auto` so its own Đóng button still works. Its 5s auto-dismiss was proven accidentally-but-really — a first probe attempt found no card at all because the timer had already fired between tool calls.
- **Round-effect chips**: "📈 Giá thuê +10% · hết vòng này" (red border) and "🧱 Phí xây -$50 · hết vòng này" (green border) from real `rentModifierPercent: 10` / `buildCostModifierAmount: -50`; the rail itself is `pointer-events: none` so it never steals a board drag.
- **Held-effect chips**: all four rendered across three players — including an **opponent's** "🏠 -50% thuê" with the correct `$150` cap in its tooltip, which is the cross-player visibility the brief asked for.
- **Medallion**: 14 `<g>` bird groups, a 28-point star polygon (14 rays × 2), 78 circles, `opacity: 0.5`, computed bronze fills resolving correctly against the board's `--board-felt: #e9e3d2`; `pointer-events: none` confirmed by hit-testing at its centre falling through to `.center` beneath, and the turn-status pill confirmed topmost over it.

`npm run lint` clean for every file touched (the only remaining error is the pre-existing, separately-flagged `LobbyDiagnostic.jsx` Rules-of-Hooks bug in a file the user was hand-editing). `npm run build` clean, 155 modules. Console showed no real errors — only `404`s on `GET /rooms/r-demo`, the fake room id from the injected test state, each handled by the components' own existing `.catch(() => {})` exactly as designed. Store reset to a clean lobby afterwards.

### Frontend — walking con-giáp pieces + a real bug fix behind the "sunken houses" report (2026-08-23)

User brief: player pieces should be 3D animals that stand and walk, and houses should stand up off the board instead of looking sunk into it.

**A real CSS bug was the root cause of the house complaint, not a styling preference.** The hand-authored building cube used `transform: translateZ(100%)` for its roof face. `translateZ()` accepts only a `<length>` — a percentage makes the whole declaration invalid, so the browser **dropped it entirely**. Verified in the live page rather than inferred: assigning `translateZ(100%)` to an element's style parses back as the empty string, while `translateZ(1px) scaleZ(1.5)` on the same element parses fine. The four walls folded up into +Z correctly around a roof that never left Z=0 — an open box with its lid lying on the floor, which is exactly "nhà nằm chìm xuống mặt bàn cờ".

**Buildings rebuilt** (`BoardTile.module.css`, plus a new local `Building` component in `BoardTile.jsx` — the roof went from one face to four and the markup would otherwise have been nine identical divs written out twice). Now a real house rather than a re-fixed cube: four walls of a controlled `--wall-h`, plus a genuine four-sided hip roof (mái tứ giác) of `clip-path` triangles in terracotta. `--w`/`--wall-h`/`--roof-run` are the only dimensions, so walls and roof can no longer disagree the way the old "100% of what?" version could. The roof geometry is derived, not eyeballed: each face hinges at its wall's top edge and tilts 45°, so its horizontal run is `--roof-run × cos45°`; setting `--roof-run = --w × 0.707` lands that run at exactly `--w/2`, putting all four apexes on one ridge point. **Measured live: apex at 4.696px vs half-footprint 4.695px — within 0.001px**, and `.roofBack`'s computed transform is a real `matrix3d` carrying `rotateX(45°)` and `translateZ(5.26px)` (the wall height), i.e. valid and applied, unlike its predecessor. The owner's colour stays on the walls only; the roof is terracotta, so the colour does one job instead of two.

**Player pieces — new `features/board/ZodiacFigure.jsx` + `.module.css`.** All twelve con giáp hand-drawn as SVG figures that stand upright out of the board and walk. Stated plainly rather than implied: each is a **standee**, not a solid mesh — building twelve animals out of transformed CSS planes would be enormous and would look worse at the ~35px these render at. `GameBoard`'s `.tokenStandee` billboards it by undoing *both* board rotations, read live from `--spin`/`--tilt` rather than hardcoded, and hinges at `transform-origin: bottom center` so the figure plants on the tile instead of being centred on it and half-sunk. A board-aligned (deliberately *not* counter-rotated) `.tokenShadow` ellipse lies on the tile beneath it, so the piece reads as standing rather than floating. This replaces the old six-div cube with a zodiac emoji printed on two faces; all six `.tokenFace*` rules were deleted along with it. `zodiac.js`'s emoji stays in `PlayersPanel`, where it's a small static avatar — an emoji glyph cannot have its legs posed, which is what makes the walk real here.

**The walk cycle** runs only while a piece is actually travelling, derived from the same displayed-vs-real position split `useWalkingPositions` already maintains (so a teleport, which resolves in one frame, never triggers it) — a board where every animal permanently jogs in place would be noise. Diagonal gait: each leg pair swings opposite its diagonal partner. Legless figures get their own honest treatment rather than a faked gait — rắn undulates its coil, khỉ swings its arms. Respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

**Verified live, measured not eyeballed** (no screenshot tool again — the Browser pane doesn't composite frames), against real board data and an injected 6-player state:
- **Billboarding is exact, and tracks the camera**: the user's board happened to be sitting at `--spin: -135deg` at the time, and the standee still rendered at its **exact declared aspect ratio (0.913 declared vs 0.913 rendered)** — proof the counter-rotation composes to identity at a non-default camera angle, which is precisely what the old hard-coded `rotateX(-55deg)` could not do. `.fromGhost` carried that same hard-coded tilt and was generalised to `--tilt` in the same pass.
- **Walk cycle**: of 6 figures on the board, **exactly 1** carried the `walking` class — the one player who had actually moved. Its 4 legs carried animations in **B, A, A, B** order (the intended diagonal gait), each with `transform-box: fill-box` and `transform-origin: 1.75px 0px` = half the leg's width, top edge — the hip. Body-bob animation attached alongside.
- **All twelve zodiacs render**, element counts consistent with what each figure actually declares (quadrupeds 17-19, biped gà 14, the two legless figures rồng/rắn 8 each).
- **Buildings**: 10 houses + 2 hotels rendered from `upgradeLevel` `[1,2,3,4,5,5]` (exactly 1+2+3+4 houses and 2 hotels), 12 complete roof-face sets, and **0** leftover cube faces.

**Second pass, same day, on user feedback ("to hơn nữa... nhỏ và hơi chìm quá")** — the pieces were both too small and still read as partly sunk. Two separate causes, both real:
- **Size**: the first pass sized the standee like a pawn (`--side × 0.042`) rather than like the character piece it is. Raised to `--side × 0.075` — **measured 58.8 × 64.3px rendered, up from 32 × 35 (1.81×)**, against an 87.8px-wide edge tile, so a piece now occupies ~2/3 of the tile it stands on. `TOKEN_CELL_PX` (15 → 26) and `JAIL_ZONE_Y` were widened to match, since the old spread was tuned for the small cube and left the bigger figures almost fully overlapped when players shared a tile — verified with 4 players stacked on one tile, pairwise centre gaps 23-42px.
- **The "sunk" look had a real geometric cause, not just size**: the shadow ellipse and the figure's base were BOTH at Z=0 on the board plane, and coplanar surfaces inside a `preserve-3d` tree get interleaved unpredictably — so the dark ellipse was painting *over* the animal's legs, which reads exactly as standing in a hole. Fixed by lifting the standee `translateZ(1.2px)` along the board normal (placed to the left of the rotations in the transform list, so it applies in board space after the figure is stood up). Separately, the wrapper was collapsed to a zero-size anchor at the tile's exact centre — it had been a real box whose *bottom edge* the standee hung from, planting the feet half a box-height off the tile centre.
- **Verified**: the standee's computed matrix carries `m43 = 1.2` while the shadow's is a plain 2D matrix with no Z offset; temporarily making both layers hit-testable, `elementFromPoint` at 70/80/88/**95%** down the figure — i.e. right at the feet — returns **FIGURE at every height, never SHADOW**. Billboarding stayed exact through the resize (declared vs rendered aspect ratio 0.9145 both). Inline `pointer-events` overrides used for the probe were restored and confirmed cleared.

`npm run lint` / `npm run build` clean (157 modules, +2 new files). No console errors. Store reset to a clean lobby afterwards.

### Frontend — "Bất động sản đang sở hữu" portfolio panel (2026-08-23)

User request: a real UI showing the local player's own owned properties. New `features/game/MyPortfolio.jsx` + `.module.css`, mounted at the top of `GameView.jsx`'s right column (above `Ledger`/`PropertyManager`).

**Fills a real gap rather than duplicating an existing view**: until now the only way to inspect an owned property was to hunt for its tile on the isometric board and click it (`PropertyManager.jsx`), and the only overview anywhere was `PlayersPanel.jsx`'s row of anonymous coloured dots — which shows *that* you own N things, never which, how built up, or whether any are mortgaged. Every row here calls the same `selectProperty()` store action `GameBoard.jsx`'s own tile `onClick` already uses, so this is a second, faster entry point into `PropertyManager`'s existing build/sell/mortgage actions — it deliberately carries no action buttons of its own, keeping exactly one place in the app that can mutate a property.

**Grouped by colour set** (plus two more buckets for transport/utility, which aren't colour-grouped) — the unit the game's own economy actually works in (`calculateRent.js`'s group bonus, `turnMachine.js`'s build preconditions), so "2/3 đỏ" is the strategically useful thing to see, not a flat alphabetical list. A summary strip shows total portfolio value and complete-set count.

**Total value mirrors `engine/netWorth.js`'s `propertyNetWorth()` exactly** (land at full price, less the real payoff cost when mortgaged, plus `bankruptcy.js`'s own house sell-back value for buildings) — including its integer-safe `(v × 11) / 10` in place of `v × 1.1`, the same IEEE-754 fix from finding #26 (`100 × 1.1 === 110.00000000000001` in JS, which `Math.ceil` would round to 111 instead of 110). Per-row rent shows the printed table value at the property's current level, deliberately **not** the final server-computed figure — reproducing `calculateRent.js`'s full branching here risks silently drifting from what a player is actually charged, the same reasoning `PropertyManager.jsx`'s own rent table already established.

**A real bug caught by my own test data, not shipped**: the first draft put a single "×2 bonus" badge on each complete/unmortgaged group *header*. `calculateRent.js`'s `hasGroupBonus()` is actually a **per-property** check — even inside a complete, unmortgaged set, a member that's already been built on (`upgradeLevel > 0`) reads its rent straight off `rentTable` instead; the ×2 only ever helps a still-unimproved member. Caught immediately by the test scenario itself (a complete 2-tile "Đỏ" group with one member built to 2 houses) — the group-level badge would have claimed a bonus that member could never receive. Fixed by moving the badge to each qualifying **row** instead (`rowHasBonus()`), and demoting the group header to a plain "✓ complete" indicator, independent of the bonus.

**A pre-existing layout risk headed off before it could bite**: `GameView.module.css`'s `.right` column is a plain flex stack with `overflow: hidden` and no scrollbar of its own — every panel there is expected to own its own internal scroll (`Ledger`/`PropertyManager`'s own precedent). Without a cap, a player owning many properties would grow this panel tall enough to push `Ledger`/`PropertyManager` out of the clipped column with no way to reach them. Capped the property list at `max-height: 250px` with its own `overflow-y: auto` rather than letting it grow unbounded.

**Verified live** against real board data with a deliberately adversarial ownership shape: a complete 2-tile group (one member built to 2 houses), a 1-of-3 partial group, both transports (one mortgaged), one utility. Confirmed: 6/6 holdings counted, $760 total value, "1" complete set, "Đỏ 2/2 ✓" with **no** false bonus badge on the built member and a correct "×2" on the still-unimproved one, "Xanh Ngọc 1/3" (no badges — incomplete), "Bến xe 2/2 ✓", the mortgaged transport showing "CẦM CỐ" instead of a rent figure. Clicking a row set `selectedPropertyId` and genuinely opened `PropertyManager` with its real "SỞ HỮU CỦA BẠN" badge — the cross-panel bridge works end to end, not just in isolation. **Environment note, not a bug**: the long-lived browser tab's own Supabase auth token had expired since the last verification pass; recovered via `supabase.auth.refreshSession()` rather than restarting anything.

`npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean (+2 files). No console errors beyond the expected `404`s on the injected fake room id. Store and selection reset afterwards.

### Big centre-screen property details + FORFEIT_MATCH ("Đầu Hàng") (2026-08-23)

User brief, two parts: (1) clicking a property (from the board, or from `MyPortfolio.jsx`'s own list) should show its full details large, dead-centre, with the property's color painted as a border across the top of the card; (2) a "give up and leave the match" feature.

**Part 1 — `PropertyManager.jsx`/`.module.css` promoted from an embedded sidebar card to a real centre-screen modal.** Was mounted as a normal flow child of `GameView`'s `.right` column (the 2026-08-22 redesign's own decision); moved out to the same fixed-overlay group `EventCardModal`/`TradeWindow`/etc already live in, z-index 45. Backdrop is the same translucent+blurred convention this session's other reveals use — board stays visible behind it. New: `border-top: 8px solid var(--group-color)` on the card itself — the literal border-on-top-of-the-card the brief asked for, distinct from (and layered with) the pre-existing colored `.header` band directly beneath it; falls back to a neutral gray (`#6b7280`) for transport/utility tiles, which have neither a color group nor a chance/fortune type. New click-outside-to-dismiss (the backdrop's own `onClick`, `stopPropagation`'d by the card) — nothing else in this app's overlay set has this, but a details panel the player opened by choice (not a server-forced decision) is exactly the case where it's the expected behavior.

**Verified live**: selecting a red-group property rendered a centred card (measured within 20px of true viewport centre) with `border-top-color: rgb(211, 47, 47)` (`#d32f2f`, the real `GROUP_COLORS.red`); selecting a utility rendered the neutral gray fallback (`rgb(107, 114, 128)`); clicking the backdrop correctly cleared `selectedPropertyId`.

**Part 2 — voluntary forfeit, backend + frontend.** No such mechanic existed anywhere before this (confirmed by search — the closest existing thing, `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md:388`, was an `[OPEN DESIGN DECISION]` describing a *group-consent surrender vote*, a different feature entirely; not built). New `FORFEIT_MATCH` action (`turnMachine.js`'s `resolveForfeit`), deliberately dispatched before `transitionTurn`'s own `VALID_ACTIONS_BY_PHASE` gate — same precedent the 5 trade actions already established for "must work in any phase," since a player has to be able to quit mid-roll, mid-auction, mid-anything, not only from `POST_ACTIONS`. Added to `socketServer.js`'s `TURN_INDEPENDENT_ACTION_TYPES` for the most literal reason yet: gating quitting on whose turn it is would make forfeiting impossible 2/3 of the time in a 3-player game.

**Mechanically this IS voluntary bankruptcy** — reuses `applyBankruptcy`/`computeBankruptcySettlement` verbatim rather than a second "remove a player" pathway (properties revert to the Bank, unowned; all cash goes with them). Three real moments a forfeit can interrupt, each resolved through the SAME existing machinery that already handles it when a player can't pay a debt, not parallel ones built just for this: (1) a live `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` debt owed by the forfeiter settles with the REAL creditor on record, not the Bank — indistinguishable from "gave up instead of trying to liquidate"; (2) a pending `RENT_RISK_DECISION` waiting on the forfeiter as owner resolves to `STANDARD` first via the exact system-default path that decision's own 15s timeout already uses, so the real payer's turn is never left hanging; (3) everything else — ordinary bankruptcy to the Bank. `applyBankruptcy()`'s own pre-existing logic already folds the forfeiter out of any pending trade or active auction — no forfeit-specific handling needed for either.

**Turn advancement deliberately does NOT mirror ordinary debt-driven bankruptcy**: today, a current-turn player who goes bankrupt via debt is left at `POST_ACTIONS`, still has to click End Turn themselves once. A forfeiting player is, by definition, no longer present to click anything — if they held the turn, it force-advances immediately here (`lastRollWasDouble` cleared first, so no bonus turn survives — same rule an escaped-jail roll already follows). If it wasn't their turn, the real current player's phase/position is left completely untouched.

**A real bug caught by the test suite, not shipped**: the first draft's "force-advance the turn" branch returned `advanceTurn(...)`'s own result directly — but `advanceTurn()` always returns a fresh, empty `transactions` array, silently discarding every bankruptcy transfer already accumulated. Caught immediately by a test asserting the creditor's transaction record existed; fixed by merging both transaction arrays instead of returning one in place of the other.

**New `InvalidForfeitError`** (`NOT_A_PLAYER`, `ALREADY_ELIMINATED`, `SOLE_SURVIVOR` — the last one a defensive guard against forfeiting as the sole remaining player, in practice unreachable since elimination already ends the match the instant only one player remains). **9 new backend tests** covering: an ordinary non-current-player forfeit leaving the real current player's turn/phase untouched; the phase-gate bypass proven directly (`FORFEIT_MATCH` during `ROLLING`, not present in that phase's own allowed-action list); the no-bonus-turn rule; the `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED`-with-a-real-creditor path; the `RENT_RISK_DECISION`-owner-forfeits path (rent still charged to the real payer at `STANDARD` before the owner's own bankruptcy consumes it); auction fold-out composing correctly through forfeit; a 2-player match ending immediately in an elimination win; and all three rejection reasons. **625/625 backend tests passing.**

**Frontend**: new `features/game/ForfeitButton.jsx`/`.module.css` — a "Đầu Hàng" trigger in `GameView`'s topbar next to "Đăng xuất" (a whole-match, any-time action, not a turn-scoped control, so it doesn't belong among `GameControls`' roll/build/end-turn buttons), gated behind a real confirm dialog (destructive, irreversible) rather than a single click. Tracks two real outcomes rather than assuming success: the local player's own `bankrupt` flag flipping true in the next broadcast triggers the exact same `disconnectSocket()` + `resetAfterGame()` pair `GameOverScreen.jsx`'s own "Trở về sảnh" button already uses (the match keeps running for whoever remains; this client just stops watching it); a rejection (`lastError`) shows inline and re-enables the dialog. **A real staleness bug caught before shipping**: `lastError` is store-wide, set by *any* rejected action this client has ever sent — an early draft would have shown a stale, unrelated error immediately upon opening a fresh confirm dialog. Fixed by snapshotting `lastError`'s reference at the moment of sending and only reacting to it genuinely changing away from that snapshot.

**Verified live** end to end: dialog opens with the real confirmation copy; Hủy closes it cleanly; confirming shows "Đang xử lý…" with both buttons disabled; simulating the server's own success broadcast (`bankrupt: true` on the local player) correctly drove `roomState` to `null`, `connectionStatus` to `disconnected`, and both the dialog and the button itself unmounting — landing back on a clean lobby screen.

`npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean (155→157 modules across this pass; +2 new files this slice). No console errors beyond the expected `404`s on the injected fake room id.

### Board camera — two real, measured rendering bugs fixed (2026-08-23)

User report: "góc nhìn của bàn cờ đang bị khuất góc và nhỏ" (the board's own corner is obscured/cut off, and it looks small) — with a screenshot showing the board's top-left corner crammed near the header and a lot of dead space bottom-right. Investigated as a real geometry bug, not a styling preference — found **two independent, both-real** causes, each confirmed by direct measurement against the live page (no screenshot tool available again, so every claim below was proven via `getBoundingClientRect()`/`getComputedStyle()` against real DOM, not assumed).

**Bug 1 — a genuine Chromium transition-invalidation bug.** `.board`'s `transform`/`margin` (`GameBoard.module.css`) are both computed via `calc()` against plain custom properties (`--spin`/`--tilt`/`--zoom`/`--spread`/`--vfactor`) that `GameBoard.jsx` updates via inline style on every camera change (button click, wheel zoom, a drag on `BoardCamera`'s rotate strip, or the auto-follow effect that rotates to face the current player's edge). With `transition: transform 0.25s ease, margin 0.25s ease` declared, the browser would intermittently get **permanently stuck** rendering the pre-change transform/margin — not merely mid-animation, but frozen there indefinitely across further re-renders, until some unrelated style recalc happened to touch the element. Proven two ways on the live page: (1) `getComputedStyle(board).transform` kept reporting a stale rotation long after the element's own `style` attribute (and `--spin`) had genuinely changed; (2) forcing `element.style.transition = 'none'` then a reflow immediately produced the correct, freshly-computed transform/margin — the custom properties and the `calc()` math were never wrong, only the transitioned property's cached value was. `.tokenStandee`'s own transform transition (the con-giáp pieces' camera-facing counter-rotation, added earlier this session) had the identical bug, confirmed the same way. **Fix**: removed the `transition` declaration from both — a manual rotate/tilt/zoom now snaps instantly instead of gliding, a real, accepted trade against a board (or a token) that could silently freeze in the wrong orientation indefinitely.

**Bug 2 — the margin-reconciliation formula is a pure orthographic approximation with no perspective term, and `.viewport`'s `perspective: 1000px` was aggressive enough relative to the board's own size (~950-1340px depending on viewport) that real perspective magnification diverged sharply from it.** Measured at the default camera angle: the formula's own assumed height was ~768px; the actual rendered height (with perspective's real foreshortening/magnification of the near corners) was 1107px — spilling well past `.boardWrap`'s own bottom edge, exactly the reported "khuất góc." Empirically swept `perspective` from 1000px to 6000px on the live page: rendered height converged back toward ~768-786px by 4000px (778px at 6000px — past this point, diminishing returns), closely matching the existing margin math again. **Fix**: raised `.viewport`'s `perspective` from `1000px` to `4000px` — the board is still visibly tilted/3D, just no longer close enough a "camera" that its own foreshortening overwhelms the sizing math built to contain it.

**Verified together, live, across every camera state the app actually produces**: at the app's own default (auto-follow rotates a player at GO to spin=-135), the board renders 1052×550 inside a 1482×910 wrap — no overflow on any edge. The same holds at all three other auto-follow angles (-45°/45°/-225°, the other three board edges) — all render identically (1052×550, axis-aligned square footprints, as the geometry predicts) with zero overflow. At spin=0 (the "diamond" default orientation), the board renders 1343×785 — also fully contained, filling ~91%/86% of the available width/height respectively (previously, before either fix, this same state measured 1345×1107, overflowing the wrap's bottom by ~200px). Manual zoom past 100% still correctly triggers `.boardWrap`'s own scroll-to-pan behavior — untouched, and out of scope (the bug was specifically about the un-zoomed default view).

`npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean, no new files this pass (both fixes are comment-only + one-line CSS-module changes in `GameBoard.module.css`). No console errors beyond the expected `404`s on injected fake room ids during testing.

### Event-card flip animation — a real, game-blocking stuck-frame bug, with a robustness fix (2026-08-23)

User report with a screenshot: the Cơ Hội/Khí Vận flip card frozen showing its own printed **back** (green lattice + wordmark, visibly mirrored) instead of completing its flip to the front — indefinitely, not just mid-flight.

**Reproduced and confirmed real** on the live page: `.flipper`'s `cardDealAndFlip` keyframe animation (`EventCardModal.module.css`) can genuinely get stuck mid-flight and never progress — `element.getAnimations()[0].currentTime` stayed at `0` for 25+ real seconds in testing, well past the animation's own declared 1s duration, with `playState` still reporting `"running"`. Directly confirmed the animation's own final keyframe state is correct when reached (pinning `.flipper` at `rotateY(180deg)` by hand hit-tested to `.face`, not `.back` — the design itself is sound); the bug is specifically that **nothing guarantees the animation ever gets there**. CSS animations are routinely paused/throttled by the browser for backgrounded or low-priority tabs, and once stuck, an `animation-fill-mode` animation has no mechanism of its own to recover — it simply never advances again.

**Why this is more than cosmetic**: for a CHOICE card (Cơ Hội/Khí Vận with a real decision), the option buttons live inside `.face`. A `.back` stuck indefinitely on top doesn't just look wrong — it makes those buttons permanently unreachable, blocking that exact player's decision and, by extension, the whole match (nobody else can act until it resolves).

**Fix — a wall-clock safety net, independent of whether the CSS animation itself is progressing** (`EventCardModal.jsx`/`.module.css`): a plain `setTimeout(1100)`, keyed by `stateVersion` (so a genuinely new draw resets it and the flip plays again), applies a new `.settled` class that replaces the animation outright with its own literal, guaranteed-correct end transform (`translateY(0) scale(1) rotateY(180deg)`). This can never make things worse than doing nothing, and unconditionally resolves a frozen animation — of any cause, not just the one reproduced here — into a normal, fully interactive card within about a second.

**Verified live**: with the CSS animation confirmed stuck at `currentTime: 0` the entire time (this test browser pane never composites/advances animations at all, similar to a genuinely backgrounded real tab), the safety net still correctly landed the card on `.face` — hit-testing the card's centre returned the face, both real option buttons (`An toàn — nhận +$100.` / `Mạo hiểm — tung xúc xắc...`) were present with their real text, and the first button was independently confirmed clickable (hit-tested to itself, not occluded by anything).

`npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean. No new files.

### C01 "Lối Tắt" — destination preview on each move option (2026-08-23)

User report with a screenshot: C01's choice card offers "Tiến 1 ô." / "Tiến 2 ô." / "Tiến 3 ô." with absolutely no indication of what any of those tiles actually are — an uninformed choice by UI omission, not by the card's own design (the card's own text already says "chọn một ô trong 3 ô phía trước", implying the player should be able to see them).

**No backend change needed** — `option.intents` (including the real `{action:'MOVE_RELATIVE', steps:N}`) was already present on the exact object `GET /api/v1/event-cards` returns (the same shape `resolveChoice()` reads server-side); it just was never read on the frontend before. `EventCardModal.jsx` now computes, per option, the real destination tile (`drawer.currentPosition + steps`, wrapped by the real board length — the same math `moveByStepsAndResolve` uses server-side) and renders a small second line under the option's own text: the tile's name, plus whatever's genuinely useful to decide with —
- **property/transport/utility**: ownership status and either the printed price (unowned) or the real owner's name (owned). Deliberately **not** the exact rent the drawer would owe if they land there — `PropertyManager.jsx`'s own established rule against recomputing a real financial figure on the frontend (risk of silently drifting from `calculateRent.js`'s real group-bonus/upgrade-level math) applies here too; ownership + the tile's own static printed price are real facts, an exact rent isn't one without duplicating that whole engine.
- **tax**: the real amount.
- **chance/fortune**: a note that landing there draws another card.
- **go** / **go_to_jail**: their own real effect (+$200 / sent to jail).

The preview line's hairline top-border (and text tint) picks up the destination tile's own color identity — its color-group swatch for a property, or the Cơ Hội/Khí Vận jade/gold otherwise — the same "the board's color identity carries into every card that references a tile" idea this session's `PropertyManager` border and `CenterBoardArea` medallion already established, at card-option scale. Only rendered for options that actually have a `MOVE_RELATIVE` intent — an ordinary money/dice option gets no second line, since there's nothing to preview.

**Verified live** against real board data: the three C01 options at a real fortune-tile position correctly previewed an unowned red-group property (`Lê Lợi — trống, giá $60`), a tax tile (`Thuế Thu Nhập — nộp $200`), and — with an opponent's ownership injected — an owned cyan-group property (`Lương Định Của — chủ: {tên đối thủ}`), each with its own correctly color-tinted divider (`#d32f2f` red / the fortune jade fallback / `#22b8cf` cyan, matching `GROUP_COLORS`/`CHANCE_FORTUNE_COLOR` exactly).

`npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean. No new files.

### BUILD_HOUSE preconditions on the frontend + better 3D buildings (2026-08-23)

User report with a screenshot: clicking "Xây Nhà" during `AWAITING_UPGRADE` produced a raw server rejection printed on screen (`INCOMPLETE_GROUP: handleBuildHouse: '...' does not own every property in group 'yellow'`), alongside a stale "chưa có giao diện điều khiển cho giai đoạn này" message — plus a request to guarantee the 4-houses→hotel rule and to make the board's buildings genuinely good-looking without spoiling the tile.

**Root cause of the rejection**: `PropertyActionDrawer.jsx`'s AWAITING_UPGRADE build button checked **only** `tile.tileType === 'property'` — none of the seven real preconditions `handleBuildHouse` enforces. `PropertyManager.jsx` already had a complete check, so rather than write a third copy, both now read a new shared **`features/game/buildRules.js`**, a faithful mirror of `handleBuildHouse` including its real validation *order* (so the reason shown is the one the server would actually have given). The button is now disabled with the specific reason surfaced up front — e.g. **"Cần sở hữu trọn bộ nhóm màu (1/3)"**, which also tells the player how close they are, rather than a bare "you need the set".

That extraction also fixed a **real pre-existing drift** in `PropertyManager`: its affordability check compared the balance against the bare `tile.houseCost`, ignoring `GameState.buildCostModifierAmount` (K07/K08) and `PlayerGameState.nextBuildDiscount` (C07/C12-B), both of which `handleBuildHouse` genuinely applies — so a player holding a $100 discount could be wrongly told "Không đủ tiền". `effectiveBuildCost()` now mirrors that formula byte-for-byte for both callers.

**The stale message** was a second, separate bug: `GameControls.jsx`'s `HANDLED_PHASES` never listed `AWAITING_UPGRADE` or `FLASH_AUCTION_ACTIVE`, even though both have had real, working UIs for a long time (`PropertyActionDrawer`'s own build/skip branch; `FlashAuction.jsx`) — so its "no controls yet" fallback printed a flatly untrue message directly beneath the working controls. Both added.

**The 4→hotel rule was already correct and is now proven end-to-end.** Backend: `handleBuildHouse`'s `isHotelConversion = property.upgradeLevel === MAX_UPGRADE_LEVEL - 1` (level 4 → 5), drawing from `hotelSupply` rather than `houseSupply` and returning the 4 houses to the shared pool, with `upgradeLevel >= MAX_UPGRADE_LEVEL` rejected above it — all with existing tests. Frontend now *communicates* it: `nextBuildLabel()` makes the button read **"Xây Nhà (1/4)" → "(2/4)" → "(3/4)" → "(4/4)" → "Xây Khách Sạn"**, verified live by sweeping every upgrade level. The uneven-build rule was verified blocking correctly in the same sweep.

**Buildings, visual pass**: a house is now a small wide cottage (`--w: 0.62em`, low walls, steep terracotta hip roof, a single lit window band); a hotel is a deliberately *different silhouette* — wider footprint and walls ~1.1× its own width (vs the house's ~0.58×), a real multi-storey window grid, and a **gold** roof instead of terracotta — so it reads instantly as a tower among cottages rather than "one more house". Both gained a soft contact shadow lying flat on the tile beneath them (same reasoning as the player pieces' own `.tokenShadow` — a lit 3D object with nothing under it reads as floating). Windows are drawn with gradients, not extra DOM nodes: at the ~12-17px these actually render at, a node per window costs a lot for detail nobody can resolve.

**Positioning**: buildings are now constrained to exactly the colour band's own 26% (`.band`'s flex-basis) instead of the old open-ended `top: 4%`, which let a row drift down past the band onto the tile's name. The band is the one strip of a tile carrying no text, so a full row of houses can never cover the property's name or price — and buildings rise in +Z *away* from that text toward the board centre.

**A real defect found by measuring rather than eyeballing**: on left/right edge tiles, houses were rendering at exactly **half** their true footprint (6px instead of 12px) — visibly squashed on two of the four board edges. Cause: those tiles carry `writing-mode: vertical-rl`, which flips what `flex-direction: column` physically means (its main axis becomes horizontal), so four houses were laying out across the band's 36px width instead of down its 81px height and flex-shrink squashed each one. Fixed by pinning `.buildings3d` to `writing-mode: horizontal-tb` (so the per-edge rules mean what they appear to mean on every edge) plus `flex: none` on the buildings themselves — a shrunk footprint doesn't just look wrong, it silently breaks the hip-roof geometry, which only meets at a single ridge point when the footprint is exactly `--w`.

**Verified live** in layout space (`offsetLeft/offsetTop`, immune to the board's 3D transform — an earlier attempt using `getBoundingClientRect` gave false overlap readings, since axis-aligned bounding boxes of 3D-rotated quads intersect even when the quads don't): on **all four edges**, buildings sit within the colour band and never intersect the tile's content box; after the fix every house measures a true 12×12 square and the hotel 17×17, all within their containers.

`npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean, **625/625 backend tests passing**. One new file (`buildRules.js`).

### Board projection — switched to true orthographic, and sized to the live camera shape (2026-08-23)

User report with a screenshot: the board looks "méo mó" (warped) and its text "bể" (broken). Two independent, both-measurable causes; neither was a taste call.

**Cause 1 — a perspective camera projects a tilted square to a TRAPEZOID, not a parallelogram.** Measured live at the then-current `perspective: 4000px`, on the board's own most common orientation: near edge **1053px**, far edge **866px** — a 1.22× convergence. That is textbook perspective, but on a board game it reads exactly as "the board is warped", and the near-edge magnification also **upsamples the tile text** (a rasterized 3D layer stretched past 1:1), which is what made the lettering look broken.

**Fix**: `.viewport`'s `perspective` is now `none` — a pure orthographic (true isometric) projection, which is what classic isometric board games use. Verified live: opposite edges now project to **exactly equal lengths (ratio 1.00000 on both axes)** at every camera angle tested, including arbitrary non-detent ones. The board is still fully 3D and visibly tilted — `rotateX`/`rotateZ` still apply, buildings and player pieces still stand up out of the surface; only the camera's convergence is gone. This also makes the `--spread`/`--vfactor` margin reconciliation **exact** rather than approximate: that derivation has no perspective term at all, which was the whole reason `perspective` had to be tuned from 1000px → 4000px in the first place. Orthographic removes the approximation instead of chasing it.

**Cause 2 — `--side` was hard-coded to the board's worst-case (diamond) footprint factors**, `1.4142`/`0.8112`, which describe the board only when it sits as a 45° diamond — its widest and tallest orientation. But the app's own auto-follow camera parks the board at axis-aligned angles almost all the time (it rotates so the active player's edge faces the viewer), where the real factors are `1.0`/`0.574`. Measured: `--side` was resolving to **951px** and the board filled just **64% × 60%** of the space actually available to it, purely because it was being sized for a shape it wasn't currently in.

**Fix**: all three `--side` definitions (base + both breakpoints) now divide by the live `var(--spread)`/`var(--vfactor)` — the same values `GameBoard.jsx` already sets inline per render from the real camera angle — keeping the old literals only as `var()` fallbacks. The `min()` still bounds it, so the board can never outgrow its column in any orientation.

**Measured result, at 1920×1000**: `--side` 951 → **1343px**; rendered footprint 951×545 → **~1344×770**, filling **91% × 85%** of the wrap (from 64% × 60%); tile name text **13.5px → 19.0px**, i.e. an effective on-screen height (after the 55° tilt's own `cos` compression) of **7.7px → 10.9px**. That ~41% size increase is most of the "broken text" fix — the lettering was mostly just too small to resolve cleanly, not badly rasterized.

A pleasant side effect worth noting: because the board is now sized from its current footprint, its **rendered size is nearly identical at every camera angle** (~1344×770 whether it sits as a square or a diamond) — rotating no longer makes the board visibly shrink and grow.

**Verified across the full range**: 7 camera angles including arbitrary non-detent ones (−135/−45/45/−225/0/−20°) all report opposite-side ratio exactly 1.00000 with zero overflow; 1920×1000, 1366×768 (96% width fill), and the 375×812 mobile breakpoint all correct with no overflow and no scrollbars. `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean; no new files (comment-heavy edits plus four one-line CSS changes in `GameBoard.module.css`).

### Board rotation snapping (2026-08-23)

User shared a screenshot of the board free-rotated to roughly 12° off axis and asked what I thought of the angle, then asked for the best overall approach.

**The assessment**: the board only reads as deliberate at two families of angle — axis-aligned (a full edge horizontal, facing the player) or the 45° diamond. An angle between them reads as "the board got knocked crooked" rather than as a camera choice, which is exactly what that screenshot showed.

**Chose the axis-aligned family (90° steps)**, for three concrete reasons rather than taste alone:
- It's the only orientation where a whole edge of tiles faces the viewer horizontally, so their names read straight.
- It makes the board measurably **bigger**: `--side` divides available space by the live footprint factors, which are `1.0`/`0.574` axis-aligned vs `1.414`/`0.811` diamond — measured at 1920×1000 as **1343px vs 951px**, ~41% larger board and tile text.
- It's already what the game does on its own: `GameBoard.jsx`'s auto-follow rotates to face the current player's edge, always landing on this same family. Snapping anywhere else would leave manual and automatic rotation permanently fighting.

**Implemented** in a new rotation section of `cameraControls.js` (`BOARD_SPIN_STEP`, `snapSpin`, `tweenSpinTo`, `cancelSpinTween`): the drag itself stays completely free (every intermediate angle still renders live, so it feels like really turning the board) and the snap happens only **on release**. `ROTATE_STEP` went 45° → 90° to match — a nudge button landing on an angle the drag would immediately snap away from is just a broken control. The ↺/↻ buttons snap-then-step, so a click from a crooked angle still lands clean instead of carrying the offset forward. Both they and `resetCamera` read the **live** store value rather than the render closure, since auto-follow and in-flight tweens both write `boardSpin` outside React's render cycle.

**Rotation is animated in JS, not with a CSS transition** — deliberate and load-bearing: this file's own earlier entry documents the real Chromium bug where a `transition` on a `calc(var(--spin))`-derived transform could get permanently stuck on a stale value (which is why that transition had to be removed, leaving auto-follow rotating in an instant jump). Tweening the number itself restores the smooth turn with no transitioned property anywhere, so that failure mode cannot return. Auto-follow now uses the same tween.

**A real bug caught by testing the fix itself**: `requestAnimationFrame` does not run at all while the page isn't compositing (a backgrounded tab, an occluded window). The first version's tween therefore never advanced — and because the *whole point* of the snap is to land axis-aligned, a half-finished tween left the board resting at exactly the crooked angle the snap existed to prevent, permanently. Verified live: after a simulated drag-and-release to 22.2°, the board sat at 22.2° indefinitely. Same failure shape as the event-card flip animation earlier in this project, and the same fix — a plain `setTimeout` backstop (throttled but still run in background tabs) forces the exact target regardless of whether a single animation frame ever fired.

**Verified live**: `snapSpin` maps 13 probe angles (including 44/46/89/91/300/−3) onto a total-Z that is an exact multiple of 90° every time, max snap distance 45° (the theoretical maximum for a 90° grid), and the four auto-follow angles are already fixed points — so manual and automatic rotation agree exactly. Six simulated drag-and-releases at deliberately crooked angles all settled axis-aligned with opposite-side ratio 1.00000. The ↺/↻ buttons land clean both with and without an intervening render tick, and "Đặt lại góc nhìn" restores spin/zoom/tilt correctly. `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean; no new files.

### Board tilt + measured sizing, and a real event-card money bug (2026-08-23)

User report: the board is "vẫn rất méo mó và khó nhìn" (still very distorted, hard to look at) even after the orthographic + axis-snap work above, plus "các thẻ event đang lỗi và chưa đúng logic" (the event cards are buggy / their logic is wrong).

**Board — the previous two fixes combined into the worst case.** Orthographic projection removed perspective depth cues, and 90° snapping removed the diamond, so the board became a plain square under `rotateX(55deg)` — which under orthographic is *nothing but a vertical squash by cos(55°) = 0.574*. Everything on the surface, tile text and the Trống Đồng's circle alike, was crushed to 57% of its height at full width: flat wide letterforms and a visibly squashed ellipse. That is the "méo".

Default tilt lowered **55° → 40°** (cos 0.766, so ~23% compression instead of 43%). Measured by sweeping tilts 30-55° live, the key finding was that **effective on-screen text height is 11.2px at every tilt in that range** — the board's height budget binds either way, so tilt trades *font size against squash at constant legibility*, and never costs readability. It only trades against how much of the (wide) column the board fills: 40° fills 70% of the width vs 55°'s 91%. Less-squashed letterforms at equal height won, since distortion was the actual complaint. `sin(40°) = 0.64` still projects plenty of a building's or a con-giáp piece's height, so the 3D pieces still clearly stand up. `DiceRoll.module.css` also had a hard-coded `rotateX(-55deg)` counter-rotation (the same bug class already fixed on `.tokenStandee`/`.fromGhost`) — now reads the live `--tilt`.

**Board sizing now measures its container instead of estimating it.** `--side` was a CSS formula subtracting hard-coded chrome sizes from `100vw`/`100vh` (`466px`/`232px`, plus two more per-breakpoint variants). Those magic numbers were guesses at the surrounding layout and had drifted: measured live, the vertical one reserved 232px while the real container was **141px taller** than the formula assumed, so the board came out needlessly small. `GameBoard.jsx` now measures `.viewport` with a `ResizeObserver` and sets `--side` inline from the real box; the CSS formula survives only as the first-paint fallback. Result: the board now fills **97% of its container's height** with no overflow, at any tilt, with no per-breakpoint constants to keep in sync.

**Event cards — a real, verified logic bug: money could go negative.** `applyIntents` charged players via `applyTransaction`, which performs **no solvency check at all** — it simply subtracts. Every other debt in the game (rent, tax, jail fine) routes through `settleDebt()`, which runs `checkSolvency` and then pays / enters `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` / applies real bankruptcy. Event cards were the one path that bypassed it, purely because `applyIntents` predates that machinery. Measured before the fix: a player holding **$10** drawing `QUY_TO_DAN_PHO` (−$50) ended at **−$40**, not bankrupt, no liquidation, play continuing as normal.

Fixed by routing every player→Bank charge in `applyIntents` through a new `chargePlayer()`: affordable charges keep the exact fast path they always had (no behaviour or test churn), while an unaffordable one is recorded and settled afterward by a new `settlePendingDebts()` through the real `settleDebt()`. Covers all five charging intents (`REMOVE_MONEY`, `..._EACH_PLAYER`, `..._RICHEST`, `..._IF_BALANCE_AT_LEAST`, `..._PER_DEVELOPMENT`). The two other `applyIntents` callers (auction fee, auction settlement) validate funds themselves beforehand and so have no settlement step — they now assert `pendingDebts` is empty rather than silently dropping a charge, converting a would-be money-vanishing bug into a loud error.

**A design constraint the tests surfaced**: a debt owed by a player who is *not* the current player cannot use `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` — that phase resolves `SELL_HOUSE`/`MORTGAGE` through `getCurrentPlayer()`, and `socketServer.js`'s turn guard only accepts those from the current-turn player, so the debtor literally could not act and the player whose turn it is would end up mortgaging *their own* property to clear someone else's debt. Non-current debtors are settled as immediate bankruptcy instead, **flagged in code as a deliberate simplification** (real Monopoly would let them mortgage out of turn; supporting that needs a debtor-scoped liquidation phase plus relaxing the socket turn guard). Reachable only from the three multi-player charge cards K04/K06/K09 — K12 is threshold-gated so its targets can always pay.

Also audited the whole deck programmatically: all 27 cards, every action type declared and handled (no orphans either way), no INSTANT card carrying a resolver-only intent (`PROBABILITY`/`DIE_FACE_REWARD` are `resolveChoice`-only and would throw in `applyIntents`), and no option with an empty intent list.

**3 new regression tests** pinning the three real outcomes (`LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` when cash-short but solvent, real bankruptcy when genuinely insolvent, and the non-current-player bankruptcy path with everyone else still charged in full), each asserting no player ever holds a negative balance. **628/628 backend tests passing**; `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean.

### Diamond restored as the base camera angle (2026-08-23)

User: "góc cơ bản phải là góc nghiêng nhìn toàn cảnh bàn cờ rõ ràng rành mạch tất cả bàn cờ chứ sao giờ thành góc thẳng rồi" — the default should be the angled full-board overview, not the square-on view it had become.

**This reverses the same-day decision to snap rotation to the axis-aligned family.** That choice optimised for measurable things (readable horizontal tile names, a ~41% larger board) but got the *look* wrong: combined with the orthographic switch, a square-on board under `rotateX` is nothing but a flattened rectangle. The diamond is also the project's own original intent — `GameBoard.module.css`'s header cites a reference of "the board rotated into a diamond and real depth" — so this restores it rather than inventing a new preference.

- `snapSpin` now snaps to **spin ≡ 0 (mod 90)**, leaving total Z at 45/135/225/315 (diamond), instead of the axis-aligned family.
- `GameBoard.jsx`'s auto-follow targets shifted by +45 each (`0 / 90 / -180 / -90`), keeping the identical "turn to face the active player's edge" behaviour while staying in the diamond family, so manual snapping and auto-follow still agree.
- **Tilt restored 40° → 55°.** The drop to 40° existed only to soften the flattening of the square-on view; in diamond mode the 45° in-plane rotation is what turns the vertical squash into an isometric view, so the squash stops reading as distortion. 55° is also what fits best: measured in diamond mode, it fills **94% × 97%** of the container with the largest tile text of any angle tried (13.8px vs 10.4px at 40°), and 58° is the first angle that overflows.

**Three real bugs found by measuring rather than assuming** — all in the container-measurement work from the previous entry:
1. **The `ResizeObserver` never attached at all.** It was set up in a `useEffect([])`, but this component early-returns a "loading the board" placeholder until `staticBoard` arrives — so on the mount that effect ran there was no viewport element yet, and with `[]` deps it never ran again. Caught by measuring at 1366×768 and finding the board still sized for a 1920 window, overflowing. Replaced with a **callback ref**, which fires exactly when the node attaches.
2. **`clientWidth` includes padding.** `.viewport` has 10px per side; using `clientWidth` directly was exactly the difference between fitting and a horizontal scrollbar at 1366×768. Now measures the real content box.
3. **`.board` was not `border-box`**, so its 2px border sat outside `width: var(--side)` — a 4px error in a calculation whose entire job is making the layout box equal the rendered footprint. Fixed, plus a small `BOARD_FIT_MARGIN` (0.99) for sub-pixel slack, and the minimum-size floor set to **240px** (the value the old CSS used at its own <900px breakpoint — measured, a 260 floor overflowed a 375px phone by ~24px while 240 fits).

**Also added a `window.resize` backstop alongside the ResizeObserver.** Verified directly that a *freshly created* observer never fired while the element measurably went from 1482px to 329px wide — ResizeObserver delivers via the render loop, so a page that isn't rendering never receives callbacks. A stale measurement means a stale board size, which is exactly the "board is cut off" symptom the mechanism exists to prevent, so it now has two independent triggers. (This is the fourth time this session an animation/observer mechanism has silently stalled; the pattern is recorded.)

**Verified across the range**: `snapSpin` maps 11 probe angles (including 44/46/89/91/200/315/−3) onto a diamond total-Z every time; 5 simulated drag-and-releases at crooked angles all settled diamond; and at 1920×1000, 1366×768 and 375×812 the board reports opposite-side ratios of exactly 1.00000 with no overflow — filling 89%×97%, 98%×96%, and 97%×91% respectively. The 375px phone still shows a horizontal scrollbar at the 240px size floor, which is the deliberate "too small to shrink further, scroll instead" fallback `.viewport`'s own `overflow: auto` exists for — and is less overflow than the previous CSS produced there.

`npm run lint` / `npm run build` clean; **628/628 backend tests** still passing.

### Board sizing moved to CSS container queries — the JS measurement was the bug (2026-08-23)

User screenshot: the board rendering at roughly a third of its proper size at 100% zoom.

**The ResizeObserver measurement introduced earlier that same day was itself the cause.** It measured the container when the element attached, and at that instant the surrounding flex/grid layout has not necessarily settled — so it could capture a too-small box, and nothing reliably corrected it afterwards (this same session had already verified that a ResizeObserver can silently never deliver a callback at all). Notably it measured *correctly* in the test pane, which is exactly why it needed replacing rather than patching: a sizing mechanism that depends on when it happens to run is not fixable by adding more triggers.

**Replaced with CSS container query units.** `.viewport` is now `container-type: size`, and `--side` is a pure CSS expression over `100cqw`/`100cqh` (the container's own content box) divided by the live `--spread`/`--vfactor` the camera still supplies from JS. Container query units are resolved by the layout engine during layout, every time — no observer to starve, no attach-timing race, no padding arithmetic, no per-breakpoint constants. All the JS measurement code (`ResizeObserver`, the `window.resize` backstop, `viewportBox`, `measuredSide`, the `MIN`/`MAX`/`FIT_MARGIN` constants) is gone.

**One real trap, found by measuring**: `container-type: size` collapsed `.viewport` to 20px tall on the first attempt. Its flex parent (`.boardWrap`) uses `align-items: center`, so the element's height was coming from its own contents — precisely what size containment switches off. Fixed with `align-self: stretch`, which takes the height from the parent instead; that is both what containment requires and what the element always actually wanted.

**Result is the best of every attempt so far** — measured at 1920×1000: `--side` **1023px** (vs 937 from the JS version, which had been measuring a 780px-tall box instead of the real 909), rendered 1447×830, filling **98% × 91%**, tile text 14.5px, opposite-side ratios exactly 1.00000, no overflow, no scrollbars. Verified the same at 1366×768 (96% × 79% fill, board correctly re-sized purely by the layout engine when the window changed) and 375×812 (97% width fill at the 240px floor, the deliberate "too small to shrink further" case).

`npm run lint` / `npm run build` clean; **628/628 backend tests** passing.

### The real "board looks crooked" bug: a drag that never ends (2026-08-23)

User screenshot of a board sitting at a visibly crooked angle, with "vẫn đang rất lỗi".

**Diagnosed from the screenshot itself rather than guessed.** Measuring the board's two edge directions off the image gave −3.8° and 79°. For a square at total-Z θ under a 55° tilt the edge directions are `atan(tanθ · cos55°)` and its perpendicular, so those two angles solve to **θ ≈ −6.6°, i.e. spin ≈ −52°** — not a multiple of 90, therefore *never snapped*. (A true diamond at this tilt has both edges at exactly ±29.9°.)

**Reproduced exactly**: starting a drag on the rotation strip and then withholding `pointerup` left the board frozen at −57° indefinitely — edges −7°/70°, within a couple of degrees of the user's screenshot. The strip listened for `pointermove`/`pointerup`/`pointercancel` **on the element itself**, so any release that never reached it — pointer let go outside the window, a `pointercancel` the browser doesn't deliver, a touch interrupted by a system gesture — left the drag open forever, and with it the board stuck wherever the gesture stopped.

**Root-cause fix**: pointer tracking moved to `window` (`pointermove`/`pointerup`/`pointercancel`, plus `blur` for the case where the window loses focus mid-drag and no pointer event of any kind arrives). A window-level release can't be missed the same way.

**Plus a watchdog**, because "the release event always arrives" is exactly the assumption that just failed: if the angle is ever left unsnapped and still for 900ms, it eases to the nearest diamond. Its first version deferred to the drag flag and was therefore useless against this very bug — with the release missing, that flag stays set forever, so deferring to it meant deferring forever. It now watches the **angle** instead; a live drag keeps resetting its timer, so it can never fire mid-gesture.

**Verified**: with the release deliberately withheld the board now recovers to −90° (a true diamond, edges −29.8°/+29.8°) on its own; four normal drag-releases all settle on diamond orientations as before; and a cold load renders side 1023px, filling 98% × 91% with no overflow and no scrollbars.

Worth recording for future sessions: the user's browser had also accumulated stale HMR state across this session's many edits, which is why their board kept showing behaviour from superseded code. A hard reload is the reliable way to confirm any board-camera change.

`npm run lint` / `npm run build` clean; **628/628 backend tests** passing.

### Outstanding-bug sweep (2026-08-23)

User asked to clear everything still outstanding. Audited rather than guessed: full unfiltered lint, the live process/port state, every "not fixed"/"known gap" note in this document, and the two real symptoms seen in user screenshots.

**Fixed — a genuinely broken feature nobody had noticed.** The lobby screen's "Đăng xuất" button did nothing at all. Its handler was `useAuth().supabase?.auth?.signOut?.()` — two bugs compounding: a React hook called inside a callback (this project's only hard lint error, present all session), and `useAuth()` has no `supabase` field at all (it returns `{ session, user, loading }`), so the optional chaining swallowed the miss and every click silently no-opped. Now signs out through the same imported client `GameView.jsx`'s own logout button already uses.
