**Fixed — a stale message with no way out.** `roomExitNotice` ("you're no longer a member of that room") was set by `Lobby.jsx` and displayed by `LobbyDiagnostic.jsx`, but nothing ever cleared it — a player kicked once carried that banner through every later visit to that screen. Now cleared whenever they start a fresh create/join, which is exactly when it stops being true. (Its setter was sitting unused in the file, flagged only as an unused-variable warning — the warning was the symptom, not the bug.)

**Fixed — a real dead-end in `startGame`, matching a symptom seen in a user screenshot** (`ROOM_NOT_IN_PROGRESS` raised while a match was visibly in round 7). The game was published into the in-memory hot store *before* the room's status row was persisted, and that persist's failure was never accounted for. Every `C2S_GAME_ACTION` checks the durable room row first (`socketServer.js`'s own guard), so a failed persist left a match that exists and broadcasts but rejects every action anyone takes — permanently unplayable, with no way back. The two writes are now ordered the other way: room row first, hot store second, so a failure simply aborts the start and the host can press Start again. **New regression test, verified to actually catch it** — it fails against the old ordering and passes against the new.

Also hardened the adjacent case: a failed initial snapshot is still tolerated (the in-memory match is fully playable without it) but now logs explicitly that the match cannot survive a server restart — that silent failure is the mechanism behind rooms found stuck `in_progress` with no recoverable game, which this document already records a real instance of.

**Audited and confirmed NOT currently broken** (so they aren't left as vague worries):
- **The live backend is running today's code.** Port 5000 is held by a process started at 19:03 today; the newest backend source change was 13:52. All of today's backend work (the Go-To-Jail fix, the 24-card deck, `FORFEIT_MATCH`, the event-card solvency fix) is genuinely live.
- **The `idempotencyKey` collision (findings #15/#20) remains latent, not a live bug** — confirmed by checking directly that no `game_transactions` write exists anywhere yet, so nothing can collide in storage. Correctly documented; nothing to do until persistence lands.
- **The room state machine cannot move a live match backwards** — `in_progress` is terminal, and the ready-toggle endpoint is guarded to joinable statuses only. The observed `waiting_for_players`-during-a-match symptom therefore came from the start-ordering bug above, not from a bad transition.

**Lint went from 1 error + 7 warnings to 1 warning.** Cleared: the hooks error, four dead store subscriptions in `LobbyDiagnostic.jsx` (each one re-rendering the lobby on every broadcast for state it doesn't display), an unused `supabase` import in `App.jsx`, an unused parameter in `GameBoard.jsx`. The one remaining warning is `AuthContext.jsx` exporting both a component and a hook — the standard React Context shape, a Fast-Refresh ergonomics note rather than a defect; splitting it would touch every importer for no correctness gain.

**Duplicate dev processes cleaned up, on the user's go-ahead — and the first read of them was wrong.** Two backend trees were running in watch mode (21 and 22 August). Re-checking immediately before killing anything showed the situation had changed under me: the 19:03 server that had been holding port 5000 was gone, and the *21-August* watcher had restarted on one of this session's own source edits and spawned the process now serving port 5000. That "orphan" was the live backend — killing it would have taken the game down. Only the 22-August tree was genuinely idle (no children, holding no port at all, verified explicitly), so only that one was stopped. One dev tree now remains, serving both ports; backend health and `/boards/small` return 200/36 tiles and Vite returns 200 after the cleanup.

Worth remembering as a habit rather than a one-off: process/port state is live, so re-verify ownership at the moment of acting, not from a reading taken minutes earlier — the "safe to kill" list had inverted in the gap between the two checks.

`npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean; **629/629 backend tests** passing.

## The one blocker behind almost everything database-related

**Fully resolved as of 2026-08-18** (database connectivity itself); **the one item it was still gating, `GET /api/v1/auth/me` (P03-T03), also resolved 2026-08-21**. `SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` are both set in `backend/.env` and genuinely live. Auth (JWT verification) has been fully unblocked and confirmed working end to end against real Supabase-issued tokens since the JWKS fix. `rooms`/`room_players` (and the rest of `0001_core_tables.sql`) are confirmed applied — `POST /api/v1/rooms` genuinely succeeds against the live project. `board_tiles` is now seeded — `GET /api/v1/boards/small` returns `200 OK` with 36 tiles, confirmed live. No remaining blocked items on this list. **Real caveat worth knowing, found closing that last item**: several of `0001_core_tables.sql`'s own tables (`game_players`, `properties`, `game_transactions`, `game_actions`, `game_events`) are fully migrated and RLS-enabled but never actually written to by any real code path — every real per-game fact lives only in the in-memory hot store and `game_state_snapshots.state`'s JSONB blob. Not itself a blocker (nothing has ever needed those tables populated), but a real trap for any future query written against them expecting real rows.

## Notable technical finds

**1–15, Phases 01–07 (condensed from earlier, more detailed session notes — this project has no git repo, so this file is the only durable record; the sourced session-refresh/password-reset writeup with links that used to live here was cut for length, everything else is shortened, not removed):**
1. RLS self-reference: pushed back on the user's own proposed `USING (true)` fix (a real privacy regression), used `SECURITY DEFINER` helper functions (`is_room_member`/`is_game_member`) instead — explicitly conceded and praised.
2. Backend deps kept deliberately minimal: native Node test runner/`--watch`/`--env-file`/`crypto`, not jest/nodemon/dotenv/jsonwebtoken. (`socket.io` and `@supabase/supabase-js` were later added in P09/P10 — see above — each only when that phase genuinely needed it.)
3. JWT verification assumes HS256, inferred from the anon key's own header — not confirmed against the live project; would need a JWKS rework if wrong.
4. `SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET` required but never available in this environment — `.env.example` documents the expected name.
5. Signup metadata contract: `options.data.display_name`, matching `profiles.display_name` 1:1.
6. This Supabase project requires email confirmation — discovered live, not documented beforehand.
7. Post-confirmation authenticated view, sign-out, and refresh-persistence were never directly observed live (a spent confirmation link blocked it) — inferred from `supabase-js`'s documented defaults, flagged as such rather than papered over.
8. No routing library in the frontend — `Login.jsx` is a conditional render, not a URL route; will need real routing by P08's Lobby UI (frontend, not yet started).
9. P05-T01 (`dice.js`/`movement.js`) predated its own dependency (P04-T01) and went undocumented for a while — resolved once `domain/` was built out in full.
10. P03-T01's placeholder text (`'Player'`) and trigger names (`handle_new_user`/`on_auth_user_created`) weren't specified anywhere — chosen as the most literal/canonical reading, named explicitly rather than left silent.
11. `authMiddleware.js` needed no fix for P03-T05 — re-inspected, already fully compliant with the error envelope.
12. Jail-doubles bonus-turn bug: escaping jail via doubles was incorrectly also granting the normal roll-doubles-again bonus turn. Found and patched in `handleJailAction`.
13. P07-T03's "Files to modify: None" scope boundary (`idempotency.js` never called from inside `turnMachine.js`) was confirmed with the user before implementing, not assumed.
14. P07-T04 Flash Auction was implemented against a still-`[PROPOSED]` mechanic on explicit go-ahead, then revised same-day to V1 "Strategic Denial" — superseding, not layering on top of, the V0 design (see Phase 07 above for what V1 actually is).
15. `stateVersion` double-increment bug spanning P06-T01 (`applyTransaction.js`) and P07-T03 (`idempotency.js`): both were incrementing it, overcounting per action. Fixed by making the Idempotency/Orchestrator layer the sole owner. **Still-open follow-on**: `applyTransaction.js`'s `idempotencyKey` (`${gameId}:${stateVersion}`) can still collide if multiple transactions land in one action before the orchestrator's single increment — not yet exercised by a real persistence layer, but see finding #20 below for why that's gotten closer.

**16–20, Phases 07 (cont'd)–10:**

16. **Multiple task-vs-reality corrections applied without asking, each grounded in an already-approved doc or already-existing tested code**, not silent guesses: REST route paths/methods corrected to match `API_CONTRACT.md` exactly (join by code not id, `PATCH .../ready` not a `POST` toggle, real error codes like `409 NOT_ALL_READY`/`403 NOT_HOST` instead of generic ones); `ROOM_STATUSES`' real `in_progress` value used instead of an invented `"PLAYING"`; `MAX_PLAYERS=6`/`MIN_PLAYERS=2` (confirmed `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` constants) used instead of guessed numbers; `board_tiles`' real column names (`group_id`/`rent_table`) used instead of guessed ones (`color_group`/`rent_levels`). Each correction was flagged explicitly in-session, not silently substituted.
17. **`WEBSOCKET_API.md` was created to supersede `SOCKET_CONTRACT.md` entirely**, on explicit user confirmation after the architectural collision was flagged (granular per-event design + REST-only room join vs. the new generic-envelope design + socket-level room join). `SOCKET_CONTRACT.md` carries a `[SUPERSEDED]` notice at its own top rather than being deleted. This is a genuine, deliberate architecture pivot, same category as the Flash Auction V0→V1 revision — not an incremental extension.
18. **`C2S_JOIN_ROOM`'s payload was refined mid-implementation** to drop `playerId` entirely (derived from `socket.user.id` only) rather than accept-then-cross-check it, per explicit task instruction — `WEBSOCKET_API.md` was updated to match, marked as a revision from its own first draft.
19. **Two real bugs found only by actually running the code, not by the test suite** — see Phase 09's writeup above (id-space mismatch, server boot crash on missing JWT secret). Both are a reminder that this project's otherwise-thorough unit tests can still all pass green while the assembled system doesn't boot or doesn't work end-to-end; worth an occasional real smoke-test pass, not just `npm test`.
20. **`stateVersion`'s known `idempotencyKey` collision gap (finding #15, Phases 01–07) is still open and now closer to being exercised** — `FLASH_AUCTION_ACTIVE`'s settlement (`resolveAuction`) can produce multiple `ADD_MONEY`/`REMOVE_MONEY` intents (winner payment + Near-Miss rewards) applied through `applyIntents` in one `C2S_GAME_ACTION` call, which is exactly the "more than one `applyTransaction` call per external action" scenario that bug describes. Still not exercised by a real persistence layer (in-memory only), so still latent, not yet a live bug — but the gap between it and being triggered for real has narrowed.
21. **P10-T02's task brief specified "fetch state from Supabase → transition → save state to Supabase" on every single `C2S_GAME_ACTION`** — found, before writing any code, to conflict with the approved schema: `game_state_snapshots.reason`'s `CHECK` constraint only allows `'turn_end'/'bankruptcy'/'game_over'`, and `GAME_STATE_MACHINE.md` §9 documents snapshotting only at those three points. Doing the brief literally would either violate the constraint on every non-terminal action (a roll, a bid, a card draw) or silently discard the *previous* action's progress on the next fetch (no valid `reason` to persist it under, so the next read gets the last real snapshot, not the latest state). Flagged explicitly and resolved with your sign-off as a hybrid design (in-memory hot store + Supabase durability only at the three approved trigger points) rather than either silently complying with a brief that would have shipped a real bug, or silently substituting a different design without asking — this was judged a genuine architecture fork, not a call to make unilaterally.

**22–23, backend slice, 2026-08-18:**

22. **`propertyEconomy.js`'s `calculateBuildHouse()` was already built and tested (Phase 06), just never called from `turnMachine.js`** — same "pre-built-but-unwired" situation `stateMachine/timers.js`'s `TimerManager` was in before P10-T04 wired it in. Wiring `BUILD_HOUSE` (2026-08-18, see "Backend slice" above) needed zero changes to `propertyEconomy.js` itself, only the validation layer that module's own header already said belonged elsewhere. **Confirmed for the rest of the property economy too, same day**: `calculateSellHouse`/`calculateMortgage`/`calculateUnmortgage` were indeed already sitting there, exactly as predicted — wiring all three needed zero `propertyEconomy.js` changes either.
23. **`MORTGAGE`'s precondition was deliberately made stricter than `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §12's original text**, on explicit instruction, not a silent guess: the doc said "this property's own `upgradeLevel = 0`"; shipped behavior is "no property anywhere in the group may have `upgradeLevel > 0`" — closer to real Monopoly rules, a real behavior difference (the narrower reading would have let a player mortgage one street while still holding houses on its group-mate). `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §12 updated in place and marked `[REVISED 2026-08-18]` rather than left silently contradicting the shipped code — same category as finding #14 (Flash Auction V0→V1): a deliberate rule pivot on explicit go-ahead, reflected back into the design doc, not layered silently on top of or substituted for what the doc said.
24. **`turnMachine.js`'s `applyIntents` (`ADD_MONEY`/`REMOVE_MONEY`) is hard-coded Bank-mediated** — every prior caller (auction settlement, event-card payouts) is player-vs-Bank, so this was never visible before. The Trade System slice (below) needed a genuine direct player-to-player transfer for the first time in this codebase and initially reused `applyIntents` for it anyway; a failing test (`transactions.length`: expected 1, got 2) caught that this silently routes a direct payment through two Bank-mediated legs instead of one direct one. Fixed by following `settleDebt()`'s (rent) existing precedent — call `economy/applyTransaction.js` directly with the real `fromPlayerId`/`toPlayerId` — instead. **Worth remembering for any future direct-transfer feature**: `applyIntents` is for Bank-mediated flows only; a genuine player-to-player payment needs `applyTransaction` directly.
25. **CORS was never configured anywhere in the backend, on either Express or Socket.IO — likely meaning room creation/joining from a real browser has never actually worked, since Phase 08 first built it.** Invisible to every automated check that existed (`node --test` and `e2e-smoke.js`, a Node.js script, neither enforces browser CORS at all) — found only by this session's first real click-through in an actual browser (P11-T05, Lobby UI verification). Two separate fixes were needed, not one: Express's own middleware (`app.js`) and Socket.IO's transport-level CORS (`new Server(httpServer, { cors: {...} })`, `socketServer.js`) are two independent code paths for the same underlying browser policy — fixing one did not fix the other, confirmed by re-testing after each. **Worth remembering**: this project's "actually boot/smoke-test, not just the test suite" discipline (established after Phase 09's two bugs, finding #19) needs a real *browser* pass specifically for anything REST/socket-adjacent on the frontend side, not just a booted `node src/server.js` — a booted server with no CORS headers boots and passes every existing test just fine; only an actual cross-origin browser request reveals the gap.
~~26. **`economy/propertyEconomy.js`'s `calculateUnmortgage` overcharges the player by exactly $1 on `UNMORTGAGE` for roughly 5% of possible `mortgageValue` integers, due to real IEEE-754 floating-point imprecision, not a logic error**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-08-18, same day.** Was: `Math.ceil(tile.mortgageValue * MORTGAGE_INTEREST_RATE)` with `MORTGAGE_INTEREST_RATE = 1.1`; for `mortgageValue = 100`, `100 * 1.1` evaluates to `110.00000000000001` in JavaScript, so `Math.ceil` rounded up to `111` instead of `110`. Found while building `PropertyManager.jsx`'s (P11-T09) cost preview — a sweep of `mortgageValue` 1–500 found 25 affected values (50, 90, 100, 110, 170, 180, 190, 200, 210, 220, 230, 330, 340, 350, 360, and 10 more), always overcharging, never undercharging. Fixed in both `economy/propertyEconomy.js`'s `calculateUnmortgage` and `PropertyManager.jsx`'s own mirrored preview, both now `Math.ceil((tile.mortgageValue * 11) / 10)` (integer-safe — `MORTGAGE_INTEREST_RATE`, 1.1, is exactly `11/10`); `MORTGAGE_INTEREST_RATE` itself is untouched (still exported, still asserted as `1.1` by `propertyEconomy.test.js`'s own constants test) since only the *formula that consumed it* was unsafe, not its value. A new regression test (`propertyEconomy.test.js`, mortgageValue 100/90/110) locks in 110/99/121 rather than the old buggy 111/100/122. Backend: 427/427 passing (426 + 1 new). Frontend: lint clean, build clean. This was a real, previously-shipped money bug — not merely a UI mismatch — so this fix changes actual `UNMORTGAGE` settlement amounts for any future game whose `mortgageValue` lands on one of the affected values; no live game state existed to migrate (real seed data has no games in progress).

~~27. **Dice/probability randomness is not actually server-authoritative for a live player action, contradicting both `WEBSOCKET_API.md` and `engine/dice.js`'s own header comment — a real fairness/security gap, still open.**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-08-19.** Was: `ROLL_DICE`, `ATTEMPT_JAIL_ROLL`, and `MAKE_EVENT_CHOICE`'s `probabilityRoll` are all documented as server-generated, but `infrastructure/websocket/socketServer.js` never called `dice.js`'s `rollDice()` (or generated any equivalent randomness) anywhere in the live `handleGameAction` path — confirmed by reading the entire file (the term appeared exactly once, in a comment) and by `socketServer.test.js` itself, which fed a client-supplied dice payload into a real `C2S_GAME_ACTION` test and asserted it was accepted verbatim. `rollDice()` was only ever invoked for real by `stateMachine/timers.js`'s timeout-default path (`buildDefaultAction`), never from a live click. A client-side stopgap (`clientDice.js`) had been shipped on explicit instruction (P11-T11, `AskUserQuestion`) while this stayed open.

    **Fix**: exactly the recommended approach — a new `serverGeneratedFields(actionType, gameState, payload, randomSource)` helper in `socketServer.js`, called while building `action.payload` the same way `playerId`/`tradeId` already are (spread the client's own payload first, then overwrite): `rollDice(gameState.currentDoublesStreak, randomSource)` for `ROLL_DICE`, `rollDice(0, randomSource)` for `ATTEMPT_JAIL_ROLL` (a jail-escape roll always starts its own streak at 0, `jail.js`'s own docstring), and `{ probabilityRoll: randomSource() }` for `MAKE_EVENT_CHOICE` — but only when the chosen option actually has a `PROBABILITY` intent (looked up via `EVENT_CARDS[gameState.pendingEventCardId]`, mirroring `eventResolver.js`'s own `resolveChoice()` detection exactly, so a safe/flat option never gets one it doesn't need). `handleGameAction`/`initSocketServer` both gained a trailing `randomSource = Math.random` parameter (unused in production, threaded through in tests for deterministic coverage) — the same injectable-RNG convention `dice.js`'s own `rollDice()` already established, not a new pattern.

    `frontend/src/features/game/clientDice.js` (the P11-T11 stopgap) is deleted outright, not migrated — `GameControls.jsx`'s `rollDiceAction`/`attemptJailRoll` now send `sendGameAction(actionType)` with no payload at all, and `EventCardModal.jsx`'s `chooseOption` sends only `{ optionId }`; any value they might still have computed would just be silently overwritten server-side now, exactly as a client-supplied `playerId` already is elsewhere.

    **Tests**: 4 new in `socketServer.test.js`, each constructed adversarially (the "client" claims the most favorable possible outcome — a double 6, an instant jail-escape double, a guaranteed-success probability roll — while a fixed `randomSource` produces a known, different, less-favorable real result) and asserts the *real* server-generated outcome won, not the claimed one; a 5th confirms `randomSource` is never even called for an option with no `PROBABILITY` intent (`OPT_SAFE`), so the "only inject when needed" gating is verified, not just assumed. The one pre-existing test that fed client-supplied dice into a real payload (`boardTilesByBoard is actually threaded through`) was updated to send no dice payload and rely on a fixed `randomSource` instead, its own actual concern (board-tile threading) otherwise untouched. 469/469 backend tests passing (465 + 4 new).

    **Live-verified against a real, running backend + real Supabase project** — a throwaway script (`backend/scripts/`, deleted after use, not committed) drove two real Supabase-authenticated users through the actual REST + Socket.IO flow (create → join → ready → start → real sockets), then sent a real `ROLL_DICE` with **no payload at all** (the real `GameControls.jsx` behavior post-fix) against a genuinely `in_progress` room: the server generated its own roll, moved the player from position 0 to a real position 8, and correctly resolved landing (into `AWAITING_EVENT_CHOICE`, a real chance/fortune draw) — no crash, no client-controlled outcome. This closes a standing gap this project's own docs flagged repeatedly: no prior session task had ever driven `ROLL_DICE` against a truly `in_progress` room end-to-end. (Caught and fixed one incidental issue while running this: the long-lived backend dev process from earlier in the session had been started with plain `node`, not `node --watch`, so it was still serving pre-fix code and the first verification attempt hung — restarting with `--watch` resolved it; not a defect in the fix itself.)

~~28. **`PAY_JAIL_FINE` (and the forced 3rd-consecutive-jail-roll-failure payment) bypasses the solvency/bankruptcy check entirely — a real, still-open violation of `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §21's "balance never goes negative" invariant.**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-08-19, same day.** Was: `handleJailAction`'s fine-payment paths called `applyTransaction` directly against the Bank, unlike every other debt path in the game. A player with less than `JAIL_FINE` (50) in cash who failed their 3rd consecutive jail roll was forced to pay a fine the game never checked they could afford.

    **Fix**: both paths (`PAY_JAIL_FINE` and the forced 3rd-attempt) now route through a new shared `payJailFineOrLiquidate(gameState, boardTiles, player, fineOwed, onSettled, now)`, calling `checkSolvency()` exactly the way `settleDebt()` already does for rent/tax — `canPayInCash` behaves identically to before (byte-for-byte regression-tested); cash-short-but-liquidatable enters the same `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` phase (reusing `SELL_HOUSE`/`MORTGAGE` verbatim, no new action type, no frontend changes needed — `PropertyManager.jsx`/`GameControls.jsx` already handle this phase generically); genuinely unpayable triggers the same `applyBankruptcy`/`finishAfterBankruptcy` real-bankruptcy path rent/tax already uses.

    One real design wrinkle rent/tax never had to deal with: a jail fine's debtor needs *releasing from jail* once the debt clears, not just a phase change — and *which* release (straight to `ROLLING` for a voluntary pay, or released-then-`advanceTurn` with no bonus turn for the forced case) differs by which path triggered it. `pendingLiquidation` gained a new optional `onSettled` field (`'POST_ACTIONS'` — the default, unchanged rent/tax behavior; `'RELEASE_TO_ROLLING'`; `'RELEASE_AND_ADVANCE'`) for `resolveLiquidationStep()` to branch on. The debtor's `inJail`/`jailTurns` are deliberately left completely untouched for the entire time the debt is pending (not even the "3rd attempt" jailTurns increment lands until payment is confirmed) — the release only actually happens at the one moment the debt is genuinely settled, mirroring the same "nothing moves until the debt is resolved" invariant rent/tax's own `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` already enforces. No DB migration — `onSettled` lives only in the `GameState` JSON blob, same standing as `pendingLiquidation` itself.

    **Tests**: 6 new in `turnMachine.test.js` (both triggers × cash-short-solvent-via-liquidation, genuinely-bankrupt, and the liquidation-clears follow-through) plus the 2 pre-existing solvent-path jail-fine tests passing completely unmodified (proving the common case's behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged). 475/475 backend tests passing (469 + 6 new). Not separately live-verified beyond the unit suite — no protocol/action-shape changed, and `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED`'s UI was already live-verified for the rent/tax case during the Win Condition task itself; judged sufficient given the contained blast radius.

29. **`initializeGameState()` hardcoded `properties: []` for every real game since Phase 08 — meaning any player landing on any property/transport/utility tile in a real game crashed the handler outright. Found 2026-08-19 via this project's first full live playtest, RESOLVED same day.** `room.controller.js`'s `initializeGameState()` set `properties: []` unconditionally, with a comment explaining that real board tile data was unreachable in that environment — true when first written (Phase 08), but stale from the moment Phase 11 actually seeded `board_tiles` and wired `boardTilesByBoard` app-wide (`server.js`/`board.controller.js`'s own `req.app.get('boardTilesByBoard')` pattern). The comment was never revisited once that blocker cleared. Consequence: `resolveTile.js`'s `resolveBuyableTile()` requires a `Property` row for any buyable tile — even unowned ones — and throws a `TypeError` otherwise; with `properties` always empty, this fired on essentially the first or second roll of *any* real game ever started via the REST API. **Invisible until now**: every unit test constructs its own correct `properties` fixture directly, never exercising this function; every prior live-verification this session used either an injected fake frontend `GameState` or never drove a real `ROLL_DICE` deep enough into a genuinely `in_progress` room to land on an unowned tile (the same root cause finding #27 already flagged from a different angle). Fixed: `initializeGameState()` now takes `boardTilesByBoard`, builds one unowned `Property` per tile whose `tileType` is in `BUYABLE_TILE_TYPES` (`resolveTile.js`'s own exported list, reused rather than redeclared) on the real chosen board, and `startGame()` passes `req.app.get('boardTilesByBoard')` through — the exact same DI source every other consumer already reads from. 2 new tests (`room.controller.test.js`): real properties get constructed from a fake board matching `BUYABLE_TILE_TYPES` exactly; the no-`boardTilesByBoard`-available case degrades to an empty array rather than throwing (same graceful-degradation posture `board.controller.js` already established). **Live-verified immediately by the same playtest that found it**: a full 3-player, 25-round, 206-action match completed successfully afterward with real property purchases, ownership, and landings throughout — see "First full live playtest" above.

30. ~~`GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §27's turn-ownership rule isn't actually enforced anywhere — any room participant can trigger a turn-scoped action on someone else's turn~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-08-21.** Was: every `transitionTurn` handler resolves the acting player via its own internal `getCurrentPlayer(gameState)`, never cross-checked against who really sent the socket message — a real out-of-turn-griefing/desync surface (not a theft vector, since `gamePlayer.id` was always the sender's own genuine resolved identity, just not checked against whether it was actually their turn). **Fix, exactly where the gap's own original writeup (see "Known gaps" #5 below) said it belonged**: one new pre-dispatch guard in `handleGameAction` (`socketServer.js`), comparing the sender's resolved `gamePlayer.id` against a newly-exported `turnMachine.js#getCurrentPlayer(gameState).id`, rejecting with a new `NOT_YOUR_TURN` error code — not duplicated into every individual handler. Deliberately an **exempt-list, not an allow-list**: `PLACE_BID`/`FOLD_AUCTION`/`AUCTION_TIMEOUT` (Flash Auction's own "all players simultaneously, not turn-ordered" rule) and the 5 trade actions (already routed through a separate `transitionFn` with their own `NOT_TARGET`/`NOT_PROPOSER` model) are excluded; every other action type — including any future one — defaults to protected without needing to be remembered on this list. Confirmed Phase 14's `HOSTILE_BUYOUT` needed zero changes here for exactly that reason. 4 new tests in `socketServer.test.js`: a non-current-turn participant rejected with `NOT_YOUR_TURN` before any dispatch (state genuinely untouched, not just "no broadcast"); `PLACE_BID`/`FOLD_AUCTION` and `PROPOSE_TRADE` from a non-current-turn player explicitly confirmed *not* blocked (the two exemptions, proven directly rather than inferred from incidental existing-test setups); and `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED`'s `SELL_HOUSE`/`MORTGAGE` confirmed still gated to the debtor (who is always the current-turn player for every debt type that exists today — rent/tax/jail-fine are all triggered by the current player's own turn events). All 4 new tests were written to actually exercise the two exemption branches, not just the common "current player acts" case, since the existing suite's own fixtures already happened to always use the current player and so wouldn't have caught a mistake in the exemption logic. **498 pre-existing tests all passed unmodified** — confirms no existing test fixture was accidentally relying on the old, unenforced behavior. 502/502 backend tests passing (498 + 4 new). `WEBSOCKET_API.md`'s error taxonomy and its `NOT_AUTHORIZED` row (whose own originally-envisioned scenario turned out structurally unreachable once Phase 09 stopped reading a client-claimed `playerId` at all) both updated to reflect this — see that doc's §3.

31. **`roomRepository.js`'s `updateRoom` had never actually supported removing a player or changing the room's host — silently a no-op for both, since P10-T02. Found 2026-08-21 building `leave_room`/`kick_player`, RESOLVED same day.** It only ever updated an existing `room_players` row's `is_ready` or inserted a new joiner; any player computed as "removed" in a caller's intended new list was simply never touched, left in the database forever, and `rooms.host_id` was hardcoded to never change even when a caller passed a different one. Invisible until now because no prior caller (`joinRoom`, `setReady`) ever needed real removal — both only ever grow or modify the list. Full writeup, fix, and test coverage under "Backlog batch" item 6 above — not duplicated here.
32. **`network/socketClient.js`'s Socket.IO client captured its auth JWT once as a frozen object, so it could never present a valid token again after the original one expired — every future reconnect attempt (including after a backend restart) silently failed forever, with no listener even present for the resulting `connect_error`. Found live 2026-08-22 (a real user stuck on `GameBoard.jsx`'s loading placeholder, indefinitely), RESOLVED same day.** Full diagnosis and fix under "Frontend fix — stale socket auth token" above — not duplicated here. Worth remembering as a standing fact about this codebase: any future long-lived-session bug on this project should consider socket auth-token freshness before assuming a server-side cause, the same way "long-lived process didn't reflect a later edit" is already a standing suspect for backend weirdness.

33. **`EventCardModal.jsx`'s instant-card notice (a Cơ Hội/Khí Vận draw) kept reappearing over and over for the rest of the match after a single draw, cycling show-for-5s/auto-dismiss/reappear indefinitely. Found live 2026-08-23 (real user report, screenshot showing the exact loop), RESOLVED same day.** Root cause was two facts colliding, each individually correct on its own: (1) `GameState.lastDrawnEventCardId` is a deliberate sticky "last known" field, documented as never cleared after a draw (`domain/gameState.js`); (2) `stateVersion` increments by exactly 1 on *every* successful action in the whole match (`stateMachine/idempotency.js`), not just draws. `EventCardModal.jsx` detected "is this instant card worth showing" by comparing its own dismissed-at `stateVersion` against the live one — so the very next unrelated action by *any* player (a roll, a purchase, an end-turn) made an already-dismissed, stale draw look like a fresh one again, and the notice popped back up, auto-dismissed 5s later, then popped up again on the next action, forever. The same `stateVersion` key on the card's outer remount `<div key={stateVersion}>` and the flip-reveal `settled` effect compounded this visually — a mid-display unrelated action would restart the flip animation on a card that was already sitting on screen, adding a jarring flicker on top of the reappear-loop itself.

    **Fix**: a new `GameState.lastDrawnEventCardSeq` (`domain/gameState.js`, defaults 0), incremented by exactly 1 only inside `resolveDrawingCard()` (`turnMachine.js`) — i.e. only on an actual draw, including a repeat draw of the same card id, and never by any other action. `EventCardModal.jsx` now derives a `drawKey` (`` `instant:${lastDrawnEventCardSeq}` `` for the resolved-card notice, `` `choice:${pendingEventCardId}` `` for the still-open CHOICE modal — the latter didn't have this bug, since `pendingEventCardId` is a real pending flag already cleared the instant a choice resolves) and keys dismissal, the auto-dismiss timer, the flip-reveal `settled` reset, and the remount `<div key={...}>` off that instead of `stateVersion` everywhere. No protocol/wire-shape change — `lastDrawnEventCardSeq` rides along inside the existing `gameState` object in every `S2C_STATE_UPDATE` broadcast, same as every other `GameState` field.

    **Tests**: 2 in `turnMachine.test.js` — one assertion added to the existing basic INSTANT-draw test (seq goes 0→1 on a single draw), one new dedicated test drawing the *same* card id twice across two separate turns and confirming the seq still advances 1→2 (the exact case a naive "did the card id change" fix would have missed). 630/630 backend tests passing (629 + 1 new test, 1 new assertion in an existing one). Frontend: `oxlint` clean (one pre-existing unrelated warning in `AuthContext.jsx`, untouched), `vite build` clean.

    **Follow-up the same day — a real regression in this very fix, caught by the general bug sweep below before it ever ran against a live game, not by a test.** The increment was first written as a plain `gameState.lastDrawnEventCardSeq + 1`. That is correct for any game *created* after the field shipped (`createGameState()`'s own `?? 0`), but **wrong for a match already in progress**: `gameRepository.js`'s `loadGameStateFromSupabase()` returns `snapshot.state`'s raw JSONB blob and deliberately never re-runs it through `createGameState()`, so a restored pre-fix game genuinely has no such field — `undefined + 1` is `NaN`, and `NaN + 1` stays `NaN` for every subsequent draw. Every `drawKey` on the frontend would then be the identical string `instant:NaN`, breaking the card in the *opposite* direction from the original bug: shown once, then never again for the rest of the match. Fixed to `(gameState.lastDrawnEventCardSeq ?? 0) + 1`, plus a third test that deletes the field off a restored state and asserts the result is a real integer `1`, not `NaN`. 631/631 backend tests passing. **Standing lesson for this codebase, worth applying to any future counter/accumulator field added to `GameState`**: the snapshot-restore path bypasses `createGameState()` entirely, so a field's `?? default` there does **not** protect in-flight games — every read site that does arithmetic on a newly-added field needs its own fallback.

34. ~~**`gameStore.js`'s `lastError` is set by every rejected action and then never cleared for the rest of the match — 6 components render it unconditionally, so one rejected click leaves a red error line pinned on screen permanently, and leaks across unrelated UI.**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-08-23, same day.** **Fix**: `setGameState` now also sets `lastError: null` — a successful state update is exactly the moment any earlier rejection stops describing reality, so one line in the store replaces adding a manual dismiss to each of the six call sites. Deliberately **not** also cleared in `sendGameAction()`: every one of those components resets its own click-guard with `useEffect(..., [gameState?.stateVersion, lastError])`, so a synchronous clear at send time would fire that effect and flip `busy` back off in the same tick it was set, defeating the double-click guard entirely. Live-verified by store injection against the running app: a simulated `NOT_YOUR_TURN` rejection appears, and the very next `setGameState` clears it to `null`. Was: `setLastError` has exactly one caller (`socketClient.js`'s `S2C_ACTION_REJECTED` handler); nothing anywhere clears it except `resetAfterGame()` at game end. `setGameState` — which runs on every single successful broadcast — deliberately does not touch it. Concretely: a player clicks "Kết Thúc Lượt" a moment too early, gets one `NOT_YOUR_TURN` rejection, and that exact string then sits under `GameControls` for the remaining hours of the match, through every subsequent successful action. Worse, it is store-wide, so an error produced in one context surfaces in a completely unrelated one later — a stale rejected bid reappears inside `PropertyManager`'s panel, `TradeWindow`, `LiquidationPanel`, `FlashAuction`, or `EventCardModal` the next time any of those opens. **`ForfeitButton.jsx` is the only component in the codebase that handles this correctly** — it snapshots the `lastError` object reference before sending and reacts only when the reference genuinely changes (its own comment spells the hazard out explicitly: "lastError is store-wide, set by ANY rejected action this client ever sends... must never read as 'this forfeit was rejected'") — so the hazard was understood in one file and never generalized to the other six. Same root shape as finding #33: a sticky store field being consumed as if it were a fresh, transient signal.

35. ~~**Two phases that block the whole match on one human's action have no turn timer at all, so a disconnect or an AFK at exactly those moments stalls the game permanently — `AWAITING_EVENT_CHOICE` and `AWAITING_UPGRADE`.**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-08-23, same day.**

    **Fix**: both phases added to `TIMER_DURATIONS_SECONDS` (`AWAITING_UPGRADE: 15`, matching `AWAITING_PURCHASE`'s own one-player yes/no shape; `AWAITING_EVENT_CHOICE: 20`, matching `JAIL_DECISION`'s longer "pick between several" window), each with a real `buildDefaultAction` case. `AWAITING_UPGRADE` defaults to `DECLINE_UPGRADE` — the exact mirror of `AWAITING_PURCHASE`'s existing decline, since building on someone's behalf would spend their money without consent. `AWAITING_EVENT_CHOICE` needed a new `buildEventChoiceDefaultAction()` helper, because a CHOICE card's options are card-specific data with no single fixed safe action: it filters to options the player can actually afford (`validation.amount`, the card's own declared precondition — a synthesized default that gets *rejected* would leave the room stalled with no timer covering it, the exact failure `handleTurnTimeout` warns about), then prefers one carrying no `PROBABILITY`/`DIE_FACE_REWARD` intent, honouring `RENT_RISK_DECISION`'s already-established rule that nothing ever defaults *into* a risk. It also generates any roll the chosen option needs itself — the timeout path does **not** run through `socketServer.js`'s `serverGeneratedFields()`, unlike a real client action, so whatever the option requires has to be in this payload the same way `ROLLING`'s own default supplies a real `rollDice()` result. `AWAITING_UPGRADE` was additionally added to `GAME_PHASES`, where it had never been despite being genuinely reachable.

    **A worse, separate bug surfaced while building this, and is fixed too** — see the C11 deadlock note below. **Tests**: 6 new. The headline one asserts the *set difference* directly — every phase in `VALID_ACTIONS_BY_PHASE` must have a timeout — so a future phase added without one fails loudly in the suite instead of silently becoming a new stall point in production. Another drives `buildDefaultAction` + a real `transitionTurn` across **every CHOICE card in the deck at three different balances**, asserting each default is genuinely *accepted* and actually leaves the blocking phase, rather than only checking the payload's shape. Was: verified by set difference, not by eye: `VALID_ACTIONS_BY_PHASE` (`turnMachine.js`) names 10 phases that wait on a real action; `TIMER_DURATIONS_SECONDS` (`timers.js`) covers 8 of them; the two missing are exactly these. `socketServer.js`'s `scheduleTurnTimer()` treats an unlisted phase as "system-only, nothing to time out to" and **cancels** the room's timer outright (`if (!(gameState.phase in TIMER_DURATIONS_SECONDS)) { turnTimers.cancel(roomId); return null; }`), and `computeDeadline`/`buildDefaultAction` both throw for them, confirming no default action exists either. Consequence: a player draws a CHOICE-type Cơ Hội/Khí Vận card (or lands on their own property with an upgrade offered), then closes the tab — nothing ever fires, no other player can act (both the phase gate and finding #30's `NOT_YOUR_TURN` guard reject everyone else), and the match is dead. The only escape is every *other* player using `FORFEIT_MATCH`, which bypasses the phase gate. **This directly contradicts a load-bearing claim in `timers.js`'s own header comment** — written when `TURN_START` was added ("Backlog batch" item 4) — that "every other phase already had its own timer", which was the stated reason a standalone `RECONNECT_GRACE_SECONDS` disconnect timer was judged redundant and deliberately not built. That reasoning holds only once these two are covered; today they are not, so the redundancy argument has two real holes in it. `AWAITING_UPGRADE` is the more surprising of the two because it is **also missing from `GAME_PHASES`** (`domain/gameState.js`) despite being genuinely reachable (`resolveTile.js` returns it, `turnMachine.js` handles it in two places, `GameControls.jsx` lists it in `HANDLED_PHASES`) — currently latent rather than crashing, because turn transitions build state with plain object spreads and the snapshot-restore path skips `createGameState()`, so its `Unknown phase` guard is never reached; it would throw the moment anything did route that phase through `createGameState()`.

36. ~~**`RentRiskChoice.jsx`'s entire settlement-result view is unreachable dead code — three independent bugs stacked in one condition, so the gamble outcome (the whole point of the mechanic) is never shown to anyone.**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-08-23, same day.**

    **Fix**: the outcome lookup now reads the store's **raw `transactions` batch** instead of the formatted `transactionLog`. That single change fixes the id-space bug structurally rather than by translating ids — a raw `Transaction` carries `fromGamePlayerId`/`toGamePlayerId`, which are already `PlayerGameState.id`, the exact id space `pendingRentRiskChoice` itself uses — and it is the correct source on its own terms, since that array is one broadcast's "what just happened" batch (replaced every update), so it cannot match a rent payment from an earlier turn the way a scan of the accumulated log could. The type check now reads `transactionType` and looks for the real `rent`/`rent_gamble` values. A lost gamble is **inferred**, not looked up, because it genuinely produces no transaction at all — and deliberately only when the phase is not `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED`, since a debt the payer could not cover in cash also produces no transaction while still very much being owed; announcing "$0" there would be misinformation, so that case shows nothing and lets `LiquidationPanel.jsx` own it. The latent null-destructure crash is removed by construction rather than by another guard: `settlement` now carries its own copy of all four fields it renders, so the component reads `pending ?? settlement` and can never destructure null. A lost gamble also renders a plain `$0` rather than a signed `-$0`/`+$0`, which would misread as a real if tiny payment.

    **Live-verified by store injection against the running app, all four branches**: a real decision modal renders; resolving it as a won gamble shows `KẾT QUẢ TRẢ TIỀN THUÊ … -$120 ➔ +$120 · 🎲 Đánh cược thành công`; resolving as a lost gamble shows `$0 ➔ $0 · 🎲 Đánh cược thất bại`; and a resolve into `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` correctly shows no settlement panel at all. (The first attempt appeared to fail and did not — the 3s auto-dismiss had simply elapsed between two tool round-trips; re-run with a 150 ms in-page snapshot, which also incidentally confirmed the auto-dismiss works.) Was: its `useEffect` searches `transactionLog` for the rent that just settled, and every one of the three predicates is wrong: (1) it reads `t.type`, but `socketClient.js`'s `buildLedgerEntries()` stores the field as **`transactionType`** — `t.type` is `undefined` on every entry, so the `find` can never match; (2) it looks for `'rent_gamble_win'`/`'rent_gamble_loss'`, neither of which exists anywhere in the system — `applyTransaction.js`'s real `TRANSACTION_TYPES` has the single value **`rent_gamble`** (a lost gamble is zero-sum and produces *no transaction at all*, per `turnMachine.js`'s own test, so "loss" can never be a transaction type by construction); (3) it compares `t.fromPlayerId === lastPending.payerId`, but log entries store `PlayerGameState.playerId` (the Supabase auth id) while `pendingRentRiskChoice.payerId` is `PlayerGameState.id` (the `game_players` id) — the exact id-space confusion finding #19 already cost this project once. Net effect today: the modal opens and closes correctly, but the "KẾT QUẢ TRẢ TIỀN THUÊ" panel showing who paid how much and whether the gamble hit is never rendered. **There is also a latent crash behind it**: the component's guard is `if (!gameState || !staticBoard || (!pending && !settlement)) return null`, followed immediately by `const { propertyId, ownerId, payerId, standardAmount } = pending` — a truthy `settlement` with a null `pending` passes the guard and then destructures `null`. Unreachable today only because bug (1) guarantees `settlement` is never set; a naive fix to the field name alone would activate it. Any fix must handle all four together.

37. ~~**`DiceRoll.jsx` replays its full 3D tumble animation on every action by any player, not just on a real roll.**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-08-23, same day.** **Fix**: a new `GameState.lastRollSeq`, incremented server-side only where `lastRoll` is genuinely set, with `DiceRoll.jsx` keyed on it instead of `stateVersion`. A frontend-only `${die1}-${die2}` key was considered and rejected: it silently fails the doubles-bonus case, where a player rolls again and gets the exact same faces — a real new roll that would not replay. Same `?? 0` treatment as `lastDrawnEventCardSeq` for snapshot-restored games. **Live-verified by node identity**, which is the precise test for "did React remount and restart the CSS animation": the `.layer` element is the *same* DOM node after an unrelated action (no replay), and a *different* node after a genuine new roll carrying identical faces (replay still happens). Was: cosmetic only, but the third instance of finding #33's exact root shape, which is why it is recorded rather than waved off: the component is keyed `<div key={stateVersion}>` to replay the CSS animation on a fresh roll, but `stateVersion` increments on *every* successful action in the match (`idempotency.js`) while `gameState.lastRoll` is only cleared at `TURN_START`. So for the rest of the roller's own turn — buying a property, choosing an event card, building, another player proposing a trade — the already-settled dice re-tumble from scratch each time. The correct key is the roll itself (e.g. `lastRoll.die1`/`die2`/`total`, or a draw-counter field of the kind finding #33 introduced), not the global action counter.

38. **A player holding under $50 who drew `C11_CU_DANH_LIEU` hard-deadlocked the entire match — no legal move existed for anyone, the drawer included. Found and RESOLVED 2026-08-23**, while wiring finding #35's timer; strictly the worse of the two bugs, and it would have survived that fix untouched. C11 is the deck's only CHOICE card with exactly **one** option, and that option carries `validation.amount: 50`. A poorer player who drew it entered `AWAITING_EVENT_CHOICE`, where `VALID_ACTIONS_BY_PHASE` permits only `MAKE_EVENT_CHOICE` — and the sole option it could possibly name was rejected for insufficient balance. Every other player was already blocked by the phase gate plus finding #30's `NOT_YOUR_TURN` guard, so the table was frozen with `FORFEIT_MATCH` the only escape. Notably, finding #35's brand-new `AWAITING_EVENT_CHOICE` timeout would **not** have rescued it either: the synthesized default has no option to pick but that same unaffordable one, so it would have been rejected for exactly the same reason and left the room stalled anyway — which is how this was found at all, by a test asserting every card's default is genuinely *accepted* rather than merely well-formed.

    **Fix**: the card gets a real `eligibility` gate (`{ field: 'currentBalance', op: 'gte', value: 50 }`), and `cardEligible()` gained the `gte` op (it previously supported only `lt`, C12's case). Gating eligibility is the right layer rather than special-casing the timeout, because `cardEligible()` already turns an ineligible draw into a **revealed no-op** that continues straight to `POST_ACTIONS` — the card is still drawn and still shown to everyone, it simply has no effect, precisely the behavior C12 already relies on. The card's own text now states the requirement. **Tests**: 2 driving real draws — a $49 player gets the revealed no-op with `phase === 'POST_ACTIONS'`, `pendingEventCardId === null` and zero transactions (the stake never taken), while a player at exactly $50 still gets the real choice, confirming the boundary is `>=` and not `>`. **Worth remembering as a class, not a one-off**: any single-option CHOICE card whose only option can be rejected is a deadlock by construction. A card needs either an eligibility gate or at least one always-playable option.

## Gameplay & UI batch, 2026-08-25 (user request, 6 items in one pass)

Requested verbatim: build without owning the colour set ("không cần sở hữu nhóm màu mới được xây nhà mà là xây được luôn nếu quay lại"), built properties immune to hostile buyout, visible tax deductions, each player's current tile in the left panel, a slow floating money-change indicator everyone can see, and live standings by total wealth. 642/642 backend tests, frontend lint/build clean, every item live-verified by store injection.

**1. The monopoly gate on building is removed — a real rule change, not a relaxation.** `handleBuildHouse`'s `INCOMPLETE_GROUP` check is deleted outright; landing on your own property (`resolveTile.js` already routes that to `AWAITING_UPGRADE`) is now sufficient. **This REVISES `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §12**, which specified classic monopoly-gated building. Stated plainly rather than buried: developed properties will now appear far earlier and more often than the `rentTable` values were priced for, so this is a genuine balance shift, not just a convenience. The group bonus is deliberately untouched and still requires a full, unmortgaged, *unimproved* set (`calculateRent.js`'s `hasGroupBonus`), so the two rules no longer coincide — a built-up property simply never receives the ×2.

**The non-obvious half, and the part that would have broken had it been missed**: the *other* three group rules all scanned the whole colour group regardless of owner, which was safe only because a builder provably owned all of it. New `ownedGroupHoldingsFor()` scopes each to the acting player's own holdings, because group-wide they would each have failed concretely — even-build: `minLevelInGroup` would include a rival's still-level-0 tile, so a player could build exactly ONE house and then be blocked forever; even-sell: a rival's taller building would stop you selling your own house; mortgage: a rival's house anywhere in the group would block your mortgage entirely. It falls back to the target property itself for an ungrouped tile, so `Math.min`/`Math.max` never see an empty array. `buildRules.js` (the client mirror) got the identical treatment.

**Two pre-existing tests were rewritten, not deleted or loosened** — both had asserted `INCOMPLETE_GROUP`; they now assert the opposite outcome (partial ownership succeeds, an ungrouped property is buildable). 4 new tests cover the scoping specifically, including the "build one house then get blocked forever" regression and the rival-mortgage case. `gp-bob` was added to the shared `twoTileGroupState` fixture so "a rival owns part of this group" references a player that genuinely exists rather than a dangling ownerId.

**2. Built properties immune to hostile buyout — the backend rule already existed; the FRONTEND was the bug.** `handleHostileBuyout` has rejected any property with `upgradeLevel > 0` (`HOUSE_PROTECTED`) for some time, but `GameControls.jsx`'s preview never checked it, so the "⚔️ Cưỡng Đoạt" button rendered fully enabled and the click came back as a raw server rejection. Mirrored now, placed *after* the monopoly branch to match the server's own validation order, so a target that is both fully-setted and built up reports the reason the server would actually give. Live-verified: the button is disabled with "Ô này đã xây nhà, không thể cưỡng đoạt".

**3–5. Visible money movement, current tile, and live standings — all in `PlayersPanel.jsx`.** A floating per-player money bubble is driven by the store's raw `transactions` batch, which already uses `fromGamePlayerId`/`toGamePlayerId` (PlayerGameState.id — the same id space the cards key on, so no translation, the trap finding #36 was made of). Every client receives the same batch, so a deduction is visible to the whole table, which was the point of the request. Netted per player rather than one bubble per transaction, since one action can produce several movements involving the same player. Icons are re-exported from `ledgerFormat.js` rather than invented, so a tax reads as `🧾 −$200` in both the bubble and the Ledger. This also answers the tax half of the request: landing on a tax tile now shows the amount taken instead of the balance silently dropping.

**One real React trap avoided deliberately**: the bubbles are pruned by *age* via a single sweep re-armed on list change, not by a per-item `setTimeout` registered inside the effect that creates them — that effect's own cleanup would cancel those timers on the very next broadcast, leaving bubbles on screen forever. Also honours `prefers-reduced-motion` by dropping the drift but keeping the amount.

Standings sort by `playerNetWorth` (cash + land + buildings), **not cash** — this is the same figure `gameEndMachine.js`'s `rankPlayers()` uses to decide the real winner, so the live board and the final result can never disagree about who is ahead. Bankrupt players are forced to the bottom regardless of the number. `propertyValue` moved out of `MyPortfolio.jsx` into a new shared `netWorthMirror.js` rather than being copied a third time. **Live-verified with the case that proves it is wealth-based**: a player holding $800 cash outranked one holding $1300, on totals of $1475 vs $1420.

39. **`BUY_PROPERTY` had no affordability check at all — the real source of the negative balances seen in live play (a player sitting at $-293). Found and RESOLVED 2026-08-25, by fuzzing rather than by review.** It was the oldest and most-used money-spending action in the game, and the *only* voluntary purchase without a balance check: `BUILD_HOUSE`, `UNMORTGAGE` and `HOSTILE_BUYOUT` each had one; this simply called `applyTransaction` directly. A player holding $80 could buy a $300 street and land at $-220.

    Reading the code did not find it — every path *looked* guarded, and the two obvious suspects (event cards charging players who can't pay, per-player "everyone pays $25" cards) turned out to be handled correctly by `applyIntents`' own `chargePlayer`/`pendingDebts` mechanism. A throwaway fuzzer (400 seeded games × up to 400 random legal actions, 3 poor players, asserting `currentBalance >= 0` after every single transition) reproduced it on 4 independent seeds and every one traced to the same call. **Worth remembering as a technique for this project**: an invariant this cheap to assert is far better fuzzed than reviewed.

    **Fixed at two levels, deliberately.** (a) `handleBuyProperty` now rejects with `INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE`. Rejecting is correct here rather than forcing liquidation — buying is *voluntary*, so "you cannot afford this" is a legitimate refusal; forced mortgaging is for debts the player had no choice about (rent/tax/fines), which already route through `settleDebt()` → `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED`. (b) A hard invariant in `economy/applyTransaction.js` itself: a non-Bank payer may never end below zero, or it throws. `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §21 had always *stated* this rule, but nothing enforced it — it lived only in each caller's own discretionary check, which is exactly how one call site missed it for months. Enforcing at the single chokepoint every money movement passes through means any future unguarded debit fails loudly instead of silently corrupting the economy. The Bank is exempt by design (`ECONOMY_SPECIFICATION.md`: finite but overdraftable, no bankruptcy of its own).

    **One pre-existing test asserted the exact opposite** — "a regular player CAN go negative — P06-T01 does not enforce >= 0, that is P06-T02". That hand-off never happened at any global level; the test is now reversed, plus a companion asserting the Bank's exemption and that paying down to exactly zero stays legal. **Re-fuzzed after the fix at 1500 seeds: zero negative balances, and 137 forced-liquidation states entered** — which also confirms the "must be forced to mortgage" path is genuinely live and firing, not just theoretically present. 645/645 backend tests. Frontend: the buy button now disables with "Không đủ tiền — bạn có $X, giá $Y" rather than letting the click bounce off the server.

40. **Three real dice rolls produced no visible dice for anyone — closed 2026-08-25** (user request: "người chơi khác cũng thấy được xúc xắc của người chơi đang đổ xúc xắc trên bàn cờ"). Dice were already broadcast to every client and `DiceRoll` was never gated to one player, so the ordinary case worked. But `startTurn()` reset `lastRoll` to null, and three rolls end the turn *in the same transition* and cascade straight into the next player's `startTurn()`: a failed jail-escape attempt, a 3rd consecutive double, and landing on Go To Jail. All three were invisible to the whole table. The 3rd-double case had been written up as an unfixable known gap for exactly this reason (`domain/gameState.js`'s own JSDoc, and a test that asserted `lastRoll === null` there).

    **Fix**: `startTurn()` no longer clears `lastRoll`, and the three turn-ending branches record the roll explicitly. This is safe precisely because no game logic reads `lastRoll` — `lastRollWasDouble`/`currentDoublesStreak` carry everything the rules need and are still reset — so "when to stop showing the dice" becomes a presentation concern instead of a state one: `DiceRoll.jsx` auto-hides 5s after `lastRollSeq` changes. Two tests that locked in the old behaviour were reversed rather than deleted, and a new one covers the failed-jail-escape case.

    **Doubles announcement**: the badge now reads "🐂 ĐÔI — ĐI TIẾP!" with the roller's zodiac (the same token they move and the same avatar `PlayersPanel` shows, so no name lookup is needed). Its correctness is structural rather than a second check — a visible `isDouble` always means a real bonus turn, because the one double that does *not* grant another roll is the 3rd consecutive one, and that branch jails the player and advances the turn instead. Live-verified from a non-roller's perspective: an opponent's double showed both the dice and the notice, then auto-hid.

41. **A horizontal scrollbar cut across the rent-settlement dialog (and, less visibly, three other places) — `box-sizing` is set per-selector in this project, never with a global `*` reset. Found 2026-08-25 from a user screenshot, RESOLVED same day.** `.settlementPlayers` had `width: 100%` plus 16px padding and a 1px border under the CSS default `content-box`, making it **34px wider than the modal containing it** — measured in the running app at 412px inside a 378px parent, not inferred. The scrollbar appeared because `.modal` sets `overflow-y: auto`, and per spec a `visible` overflow on the *other* axis then computes to `auto`, so any spill at all draws a real horizontal scrollbar. That also pushed the outcome line ("🎲 Đánh cược thành công…") out of view, which is what made the dialog look broken rather than merely scrollable.

    **Swept for the same trap everywhere else rather than fixing only the reported spot.** Six more candidates matched the pattern (`width: 100%` + padding/border, no `box-sizing`, inside a scrollable container); measuring each in the browser separated two real bugs from four false positives. Real: `FlashAuction`'s `.propertyCard` (1px of genuine overflow — same rule shape as the one reported), and `PlayersPanel`'s `.stats`, where `.balance` spilled 6.1px past its own card because neither stat can shrink — reproduced deliberately with a `$123456` balance, so this bites at any sidebar width once the numbers get long, not just on narrow screens. False positives worth recording so they are not re-investigated: `Ledger`/`MyPortfolio`'s `.headerBtn` and `MyPortfolio`'s `.row` are all `<button>` elements, which browsers already default to `border-box`; `GameOverScreen`'s `.standings` sets `padding: 0`, so it has nothing to overflow with.

    **Fixes**: `box-sizing: border-box` on the three real offenders; `min-width: 0` plus ellipsis on `.playerName` and `.propsCount` so a long display name or property count can never reintroduce the spill by a different route; `flex-wrap: wrap` on `.stats` so a large balance moves to a second line rather than being clipped — the amount is the one thing in that row that must stay fully readable. **Verified by sweeping every scrollable container across all four modals** (rent-risk decision, rent-risk settlement, Flash Auction, event card) with a deliberately oversized balance: all clean, zero horizontal overflow anywhere.

    **Standing note for future CSS work on this project**: there is no global `box-sizing` reset. Any `width: 100%` element that also carries padding or a border will overflow its parent, and if any ancestor sets `overflow-y`, that overflow becomes a visible scrollbar rather than a silent few pixels.

## Event-card history ("Thẻ đã rút"), 2026-08-25

User request: "đôi khi vào ô cơ hội và khí vận quên đọc đã ấn đóng mất rồi, thì làm sao để đọc lại". The honest answer was that there was **no way at all** — `EventCardModal` shows a draw exactly once (dismissed by its own button, or by a 5s auto-dismiss for an INSTANT card), the Ledger records only the money that moved ("Cơ Hội / Khí Vận, −$25") and never the card's text, and `GameState` itself keeps only the single most recent draw (`lastDrawnEventCardId`, deliberately overwritten each time). Anything older existed nowhere.

**New `eventCardLog` in `gameStore`**, accumulated in `socketClient.js` as draws arrive, newest-first, capped at 30 (same reasoning as `transactionLog`'s cap — this project's playtests run 200+ actions). Two details that matter:

- **Detected by `lastDrawnEventCardSeq` strictly increasing, not by the card id changing.** The id is sticky and can legitimately repeat, because the deck cycles and the same card really is drawn again later; only the seq counter (added for finding #33) distinguishes a genuine redraw from a repeat of the same value. A reconnect resync re-delivers the current `gameState` verbatim, so the `>` comparison is what stops a replay logging a phantom draw — and `appendEventCard` is keyed on seq as an independent second guard. Verified live: three draws plus a duplicate resync of the third produced exactly three entries.
- **The drawer is resolved at arrival, not at render time.** For a card that ends the turn (`MOVE_TO_JAIL`, or a move landing somewhere turn-ending) `currentTurnIndex` has already advanced by the time a later render would look, which would silently credit the draw to the wrong player.

**New `EventCardHistory.jsx`**, mounted on `GameView`'s shared status rail next to the round counter — table-wide history belongs with the other table-wide readouts, and it has to stay reachable at the exact moment someone realises they closed a card too early. Deliberately a separate component rather than a mode inside `EventCardModal`: that one owns a live, time-sensitive, sometimes turn-blocking moment (flip animation, choice buttons, auto-dismiss), and threading a read-only historical mode through it would put two very different jobs in one place. This one has no timers and no actions, so it cannot affect play. It lists every draw at the table, not just the local player's — the modal already reveals every draw to everyone, and a rival's card is legitimately public information. A CHOICE card's options are listed as *what was offered*, never as what was taken: which option a player actually chose is not recorded anywhere, and inventing it would be fabrication.

Live-verified end to end against the real `GET /api/v1/event-cards`: real card text, correct newest-first order, "Bạn" vs the other player, options shown on expand, and a zero-overflow sweep (finding #41's trap — every full-width block in the new stylesheet sets `box-sizing` explicitly, since this project has no global reset).

42. **The rent-settlement amounts (`-$X`/`+$X`) rendered as near-invisible pale text — "not showing anything useful" per a real user screenshot. Found and RESOLVED 2026-08-25.** This is finding #36's own dead-code fix, made functionally correct that day but never actually looked at visually — every prior verification read `document.body.innerText`, which cannot detect a contrast problem.

    **Root cause, measured with `getComputedStyle` in the running app, not guessed**: `.amountMinus`/`.amountPlus`/`.settlementNote`/`.settlementArrow` reference `var(--red)`, `var(--green)`, `var(--gold)`, `var(--text-muted)` — four custom properties that are **not defined anywhere in this codebase**, not in `index.css`'s light/dark theme, not among `GameView.module.css`'s own `--et-*` set. `RentRiskChoice.module.css` is the only file that ever references any of the four. Since `color` is an inherited property, an invalid `var()` doesn't fail visibly on its own — per spec it makes the declaration behave as unset, so the value inherits from an ancestor instead. Every modal here, including this one, is mounted as a DOM descendant of `GameView`'s `.shell`, which sets `--et-text: #f4f6fb` (a pale off-white, correct for GameView's own dark navy background) — and that measured exactly, to the digit, as the color the broken text was actually rendering in. This modal's own background is `--bg` (white) — `RentRiskChoice.module.css` uses the older, generic light-theme token system throughout (`--text`/`--bg`/`--border`/`--code-bg`, all real, all confirmed still working here), never `--et-*` — so pale-off-white-meant-for-navy landed on white-meant-for-itself. The most telling signal that confirmed it before even checking the CSS source: three semantically different properties (red, green, gold) all measured as the *exact same* computed color.

    **Fix**: replaced the four broken references with real, contrast-checked values, matching this file's own established mixed convention of variables *and* literal hex (`.error`/`.gambleButton` already use a literal `#c0392b`). Red reuses that same existing `#c0392b`. Green reuses `EventCardModal.module.css`'s own already-vetted `.gain` color (`#1f7a52`) for the identical "money gained" meaning, rather than inventing a second one. The muted arrow reuses `var(--text)` at full weight — `.playerName` already proved that token legible in this exact file, and no dedicated lighter "muted" light-theme token exists to invent one from. Gold needed an actual new value: `GameView`'s own `--et-gold` (`#f0b429`) was tried first and rejected — bright and correct on dark navy, it fails WCAG AA on this modal's white/beige backgrounds (contrast ≈3.2–3.8, need ≥4.5); `#7a5c00` was chosen after computing contrast for several candidates, landing at 6.25 (white) / 5.62 (beige). Verified in the running app after the fix, not just computed on paper: `-$32` at `rgb(192,57,43)` (4.89:1), `+$32` at `rgb(31,122,82)` (4.76:1), the note at `rgb(122,92,0)` (6.25:1) — all comfortably passing.

    **Standing lesson, worth repeating for any future visual fix on this project**: `document.body.innerText`-based verification proves content is *present*, never that it is *visible*. A color/contrast bug needs `getComputedStyle` (or a real screenshot) — this is the second time this session a bug needed the difference (finding #41 was the layout/overflow half of the same lesson).

## Token movement pacing + bounce, 2026-08-25

User request: "tốc độ di chuyển từng ô của nhân vật chậm rãi hơn mà di chuyển tới ô nào ô đấy phải nảy nảy cho sinh động" (walk speed should be more leisurely, and landing on each tile should bounce for liveliness). Two independent changes, both scoped to `GameBoard.jsx`/`GameBoard.module.css` — the P11-era stepped-walk mechanism (`useWalkingPositions`) and its `.token` transition already existed; this tunes pacing and adds the missing landing feedback, no new architecture.

**Speed**: `STEP_MS` 130ms → 220ms, with `.token`'s CSS transition duration updated to match (`0.13s` → `0.22s`) — these two have always been coupled 1:1 (the JS interval drives *when* the position state changes; the CSS transition is what actually animates the glide between two positions), and the file's own existing comment already established that pairing.

**Bounce**: a new `.tokenHop` wrapper, inserted between `.token` and `.tokenStandee` rather than animating `.tokenStandee` directly — deliberate, not a style preference. `.tokenStandee`'s own `transform` is the billboard counter-rotation (`rotateZ`/`rotateX` against `--spin`/`--tilt`), and this file has *twice already* documented, in detail, a real Chromium bug where a `var()`-driven `calc()` inside an animated/transitioned `transform` can freeze permanently on a stale value — animating `.tokenStandee`'s own transform would have risked reintroducing exactly that. Two separate elements each owning their own `transform` composes safely; layering both onto one would not. `.tokenShadow` stays outside `.tokenHop` deliberately too — a real shadow stays flat on the ground while only the figure rises.

`.tokenHop` is `key`ed on the player's own *displayed* position (not `currentPosition`), so a fresh key — and a fresh play of the `tokenHop` keyframe animation — happens on every tile genuinely arrived at: once per step of a real stepped walk, and once on a straight teleport landing (Go To Jail, an event-card move) alike, matching the request's literal "di chuyển tới ô nào ô đấy" (wherever it moves to). The keyframes are a simple squash-and-stretch hop (rise + thin, then land + brief squash-wide, `translateY` peak scaled off `--side` like every other token dimension in this file so it stays proportional at any board size), duration matched to `STEP_MS`/the `.token` transition so the figure's feet touch down at the same instant the horizontal glide arrives — not before or after it. `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` disables it, matching this project's existing convention (`PlayersPanel`'s money-float animation).

**Verification hit a real environment limit, worked around rather than skipped**: the Browser pane wasn't visually displayed this session (the same limitation that's blocked `computer{action:"screenshot"}` all along), which meant `document.hidden === true` / `document.visibilityState === 'hidden'` — and Chromium throttles `setInterval` heavily in hidden tabs. That throttled BOTH my own diagnostic polling timers *and* the real app's own `STEP_MS`-driven `setInterval`, making wall-clock timing measurements (e.g. "does a 5-tile walk take ~1.1s") unreliable in this session regardless of what the source code actually says. Worked around by verifying the part that's immune to timing distortion instead: a `MutationObserver` on the token's own `style` attribute, watching a real 5-tile move (`ROLLING` → a real `lastRoll.total: 5` matching a 5-tile position change) end to end. It recorded **exactly 5 distinct style mutations**, `top` stepping `85%→75%→65%→55%→45%` in strict monotonic order — direct proof the walk visits every intermediate tile as its own discrete step rather than jumping straight to the destination, a fact the *sequence and count* of events establishes regardless of how compressed or delayed their real-world timing was by the hidden-tab throttling. Separately confirmed via `getComputedStyle` that `.tokenHop` carries `animation-name: tokenHop` / `animation-duration: 0.22s` exactly as authored, and observed multiple distinct `.tokenHop` DOM node identities across one walk (proving the per-tile remount-and-replay actually fires, not just once).

Lint/build clean. **Live smoothness/timing was not visually watched this session** — flagged honestly rather than claimed, for the same reason a screenshot couldn't be taken; the correctness evidence above is structural (event sequence, CSS registration), not a visual confirmation that the motion *looks* right at real speed.

## Token-hop regression fix + owned-tile price hint, 2026-08-25

**Regression, found and fixed same day it shipped**: the bounce/hop animation above ("Token movement pacing + bounce") broke the animals — real user report, "hình chó với mèo lại bị chìm xuống bàn cờ rồi" (the dog and cat sank back into the board). Root cause: `.tokenHop` (the new wrapper) never set `transform-style: preserve-3d`, and that property's default (`flat`) FLATTENS a subtree's 3D transforms into the parent's own 2D plane. `.tokenHop` sits directly between `.token` (`preserve-3d`, marked CRITICAL) and `.tokenStandee` (whose `translateZ(1.2px)` is the *entire* fix for the original "hơi chìm" bug — without real Z-separation, the shadow ellipse and the animal's legs are coplanar in a preserve-3d tree and get interleaved unpredictably). `.tokenHop` silently discarded that Z-separation the moment it became `.tokenStandee`'s parent, even though nothing about `.tokenHop` itself looks 3D-related (it only sets translateY/scale) — `transform-style` has to be set on every element in a chain that needs to pass a 3D context through, not just the ones that use Z themselves. Fixed with one declaration. **Live-verified via `getComputedStyle`** (not pixels — see below): `.tokenHop` now reports `transformStyle: "preserve-3d"`, and `.tokenStandee`'s own computed `transform` matrix still carries its `1.2` Z-translation component, confirming the separation survives the added wrapper.

**New feature, same session: a price hint on owned tiles.** User request: "các ô đã được mua thì ở đuôi ô có hiển thị thêm giá tiền nếu chạm vào" (owned tiles should show their price at the tile's tail end, on touch/hover). Applies to any owned buyable tile with a real price — property, transport, or utility alike, not narrowed to `tileType === 'property'` the way the tile's existing always-on price text already (arguably too narrowly) is.

Revealed by CSS `:hover`/`:focus-visible` (free keyboard parity — `BoardTile` is already a real `role="button" tabIndex=0` when clickable) for mouse/keyboard, plus a local `touchHint` React state set on `onTouchStart` and auto-cleared after 2.5s (this project's established timed-reveal convention — `MONEY_FLOAT_MS`/`INSTANT_CARD_VISIBLE_MS`) as the touch-device fallback, since `:hover` doesn't fire reliably on tap across browsers.

**Positioning is the interesting part**: "đuôi ô" (the tail — away from the board's centre) had to land correctly across all 4 board edges, each with its own `writing-mode`/rotation combination (`BoardTile.module.css`'s own header: text runs parallel to its own edge, colour band always faces the centre). Rather than four hand-written per-edge overrides, the hint uses a single rule with `inset-block-end` — a writing-mode-AWARE logical offset, deliberately not physical `bottom` — which resolves against each tile's own `writing-mode` *before* the decorative whole-tile `rotateZ(180deg)` (`.top`/`.right`) is applied. This is the exact same mechanism `.band` already relies on (as the first flex child = block-start) to always face the centre; the price hint lands on the opposite logical edge (block-end) by the same logic. Derived rigorously against the Writing Modes spec's real block-start/block-end mapping under `vertical-rl` (block-start = right, block-end = left), not guessed, and then **live cross-checked against all 4 real edges** using the real `computeEdge`/`edgeLength = (totalTiles-4)/4` math (first attempt used a wrong assumption about which tile positions land on which edge — corrected by reading `computeGridPosition`/`computeEdge` directly rather than guessing again): for every edge, the pre-rotation local inset value matched prediction, and the presence/absence of the 180° rotation matrix matched exactly which edges (`.top`/`.right`) are supposed to carry one.

**A real markup bug found during that same live check, fixed immediately**: the CSS was written expecting a nested chip element (`.priceHint > *` carries the pill's background/padding/colour), but the JSX originally put the price text directly inside `.priceHint` with no child element — so `.priceHint > *` matched nothing, and the price rendered as bare unstyled text. Fixed by wrapping the price in a `<span>`; live-reverified after the fix: correct price text, correct dark-chip/gold-text colours (contrast-checked, `9.34:1`), on all 4 edges, `z-index: 3` sitting above a built-up tile's own houses (`z-index: 2`).

**A second real bug, this time in my own verification script, not the app**: manually reconstructing the CSS Modules class name via regex initially dropped its leading underscore (`priceHintForced_1ie9d_137` instead of the real `_priceHintForced_1ie9d_137`), so an early check that appeared to show the reveal not working was testing a class that didn't match anything.
