**Once that was fixed, a THIRD result still looked broken (`opacity` stuck at 0 with the correct class applied) — isolated to a genuine environment limitation, not the code**: a bare, freshly-created `<div>` with zero relation to this feature, given nothing but `opacity: 0; transition: opacity 0.15s` and then set to `opacity: 1`, exhibited the identical stuck-at-0 symptom, for as long as `document.hidden === true` (the Browser pane wasn't visually displayed this session — the same standing limitation noted throughout today's other fixes). Plain `opacity` changes with NO transition involved resolve instantly and correctly even while hidden (confirmed separately); it's specifically an active `transition` that never progresses without real compositor ticks, freezing `getComputedStyle`'s reported value at its pre-transition state indefinitely. Forcing `transition: none` on the real element cleanly resolved `opacity: 1` / `transform: matrix(1,0,0,1,0,0)` the instant `.priceHintForced` was present, closing the loop: the cascade and the class-matching are both genuinely correct; only the *animated* transition's visible progress is unverifiable in this session, for reasons unrelated to this feature's own code.

Lint/build clean throughout. **Standing lesson for this project's own verification practice**: when a `getComputedStyle` check on a `transition`-guarded property looks stuck, check `document.hidden` before concluding the code is broken — isolate with a bare, unrelated control element (as done here) rather than continuing to distrust the real component.

**Revised same day, minutes later**: the user followed up with a Business Tour reference screenshot ("giá tiền ở đuôi ô ntn nè" — this is what price-at-the-tail looks like) — every tile there shows its price permanently, prominently, as part of the tile's own design, not behind a hover/tap reveal. Asked directly (`AskUserQuestion`, a genuine fork with real design consequences, not guessed): confirmed always-on was the real intent. The hover/touch-reveal machinery built above (`opacity` transition, `.priceHintForced`, `onTouchStart`, the `touchHint` state) is removed entirely — `.priceHint` → `.ownedPrice`, unconditionally rendered, no interaction involved. Font-size raised to match `.price`'s own existing 0.72em (the unowned-tile price this now stands in for), so it reads with the same prominence, not smaller. The now-redundant always-on price inside `.content` is hidden specifically for *owned* tiles (`&& !owner`) to avoid showing the same figure twice on one tile — unowned tiles keep it unchanged, since that's still the only price shown there. Removing the `transition` also removes the ONE dependency that made this feature's verification fight the hidden-tab timing limitation at all: re-verified cleanly across all 4 real edges plus the unowned case with plain, un-animated `getComputedStyle` reads — correct price text, correct dark/gold chip colours, correct `z-index: 3` (above a built-up tile's own houses), and confirmed the unowned tile shows its (unchanged) inline price with no duplicate `.ownedPrice` chip.

## Owned-tile price → real rent, plus a Pass GO callout, 2026-08-25

**Correction to the price-hint work above, same day**: "số tiền này là số tiền khi giẫm vào phải trả mà" (this number is supposed to be what you pay when you land on it) — `.ownedPrice` was showing `tile.price` (the purchase price), which stops being the relevant figure the instant a tile is owned; what matters from then on is what landing there actually costs. New shared `frontend/src/features/board/rentPreview.js`, ported faithfully from `MyPortfolio.jsx`'s own pre-existing private `rentLabel` (not reinvented) — same deliberate scope: property rent prints the plain `baseRent`/`rentTable[level-1]` WITHOUT the group-monopoly ×2 bonus folded in (reproducing `calculateRent.js`'s bonus/dice-multiplier branches in display code risks silently disagreeing with the real server-charged figure, `MyPortfolio.jsx`'s own already-documented reasoning); transport is the real `baseRent × 2^(ownedCount-1)` formula (exact, no branch to drift); utility has no fixed dollar figure at all (depends on a future roll) and prints "4×/10× xúc xắc" instead of inventing one. **New case this port added that `MyPortfolio.jsx` never needed**: a mortgaged property returns `"Cầm cố"` rather than a dollar amount — `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §11 is explicit that no rent is owed on a mortgaged property, so printing a number there would have been a real, factual lie, not just an approximation, exactly the kind of mistake this feature exists to prevent.

`GameBoard.jsx` computes each owner's transport/utility counts once per render (`rentCountsByOwnerId`, a `Map` keyed by owner id — O(players), not O(tiles×properties)) and resolves the final rent-preview STRING per tile before handing it to `BoardTile`, which stays exactly as "dumb"/store-free as its own header already insists — it renders whatever string arrives, no calculation of its own. Live-verified: a tile with `upgradeLevel: 2` correctly showed `rentTable[1]` (not the base rent, not the purchase price), and a separate mortgaged tile correctly showed "Cầm cố" with no dollar figure at all.

**New feature, same message: a Pass GO callout.** "khi ai qua ô bắt đầu thì hiển thị to ở giữa bàn cờ là +200" — a big, centred, table-wide "+$200" whenever anyone crosses GO. Lives in `CenterBoardArea.jsx` (already mounted at the board's dead centre) rather than a new component, driven by the exact same pattern `PlayersPanel.jsx`'s per-player money floats already established this session: watch the store's raw `transactions` batch for a `pass_go_salary` entry, dedupe by `stateVersion` (so a reconnect resync redelivering the same broadcast can never double-fire it — live-verified: redelivering the identical `stateVersion` produced no duplicate notice), prune by age via a single re-armed sweep (not a per-item `setTimeout`, which the effect's own cleanup would cancel on the next broadcast). The real transaction amount is shown, never a hardcoded "200" — this project's own standing discipline against fabricating a number that happens to usually be right. Attribution reads "Bạn qua Ô Bắt Đầu" for the local player and the real display name otherwise, live-verified for both cases from the same test, including that they coexist correctly if two different players each pass GO within the notice's own visible window.

Positioned via `align-self: flex-start` inside `.center`'s own flex box, which `align-items: flex-end` (GameBoard.module.css) already bottom-anchors `.turnStatus` against, while `DiceRoll.jsx`'s dice occupy the cell's dead centre as an unrelated absolutely-positioned sibling against `.board` — the top edge was the one part of this cell nothing else already claimed, confirmed live via `getComputedStyle` reporting `align-self: flex-start` as expected on the rendered layer.

Lint/build clean. Live-verified via direct store injection (rent figures, mortgaged label, both attribution cases, the resync-dedup case); the pop-in/fade-out animation's own real-time visual smoothness is not independently confirmed this session, for the same standing reason noted throughout today's other animation work (Browser pane not visually displayed).

## Rent Risk Choice — Bank-funded gamble revision, 2026-08-25

**Correction to the mechanic shipped 2026-08-21** ("Backlog batch" item 7 above), reported directly by the user, not found in a sweep: *"về logic tiền cược, nếu quyết định chọn gambling thì người chọn hoặc được x2 hoặc không nhận được số tiền còn người vào đất chỉ mất x1 số tiền thôi chứ sao lại mất x2 và không cần chờ người khác quyết định chọn gì"* — the shipped design made the **payer's** liability depend on the owner's Standard/Gamble choice (pay standard, pay 2x, or pay 0x) and blocked the payer (and the whole table) on a `RENT_RISK_DECISION` phase until the owner decided. Both were wrong: the payer should only ever owe the fixed standard rent, and should never wait on anyone else's decision to find out what they owe.

First confirmed this wasn't a bug in the shipped code — it was exactly what `BOARD_SPECIFICATION.md`/`ECONOMY_SPECIFICATION.md` had specified and what the tests asserted — then used `AskUserQuestion` for the one genuine open design fork this correction created (once the payer's liability is fixed at exactly 1x, where does the owner's potential 2x-on-win come from?): the user picked **"Ngân hàng bù thêm"** (Bank tops up), over the alternative of diverting part of the payer's own payment.

**Backend rewrite** (`backend/src/domain/gameState.js`, `stateMachine/turnMachine.js`, `stateMachine/timers.js`, `infrastructure/websocket/socketServer.js`): `RENT_RISK_DECISION` removed from `GAME_PHASES` entirely — `PAYING_RENT` now settles the standard rent immediately and unconditionally at landing, exactly as it did before this mechanic ever existed. `pendingRentRiskChoice` replaced by `pendingRentGamble` (`{ propertyId, ownerId, payerId, amount }`), set only when the rent was just paid in cash (not deferred to `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED`). New action `GAMBLE_RENT` (empty payload, same shape as `FOLD_AUCTION`) replaces `RENT_RISK_CHOICE` — the owner's entirely optional, non-blocking response: a server-rolled 50/50 double funded by the **Bank** (`rent_gamble`, Bank↔owner), never the payer. Made both turn-independent (`TURN_INDEPENDENT_ACTION_TYPES`) and phase-independent (dispatched before the `VALID_ACTIONS_BY_PHASE` gate in `transitionTurn`, the same precedent `FORFEIT_MATCH` set) — the owner can respond whenever, regardless of whose turn it is or what phase the match is in. No timer (`RENT_RISK_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` removed from `timers.js`) — nothing blocks on this any more, so nothing needs to time out; it can sit unresolved indefinitely until the owner acts, goes bankrupt (`applyBankruptcy` now clears a bankrupt owner's own pending gamble), or the match ends. `resolveForfeit`'s old "resolve the pending choice to Standard first" branch removed outright — there's no choice left to default, since the payer's leg already settled the instant it happened. Caught and preserved one real edge case from the old code during the rewrite: a genuine `$0` rent (from a full discount reduction) still short-circuits before `settleDebt`, since `applyTransaction` rejects non-positive amounts. Backend test suite fully rewritten alongside (`turnMachine.test.js`, `socketServer.test.js`, `timers.test.js`) rather than deleted — **645/645 passing**. Final grep swept for every stale reference to the old names; two stale historical comments fixed for accuracy, one genuinely historical dated-log comment deliberately left alone.

**Frontend rewrite**: `RentRiskChoice.jsx`/`.module.css` rebuilt from a full-viewport, everyone-waits modal (`FlashAuction`-style) into a small, non-blocking, **owner-only** corner drawer (`PropertyActionDrawer`-style architecture) reading `pendingRentGamble` and sending `GAMBLE_RENT` — nobody else, not even the payer, sees anything, since nobody else has anything to decide or wait on. The old settlement-display logic (the finding-#36-era three-stacked-bugs code tracking `pending`/`settlement` transitions) was dropped entirely rather than ported: the outcome is now a normal `rent_gamble` transaction the instant it resolves, so the same generic machinery that already announces every other balance change (`Ledger.jsx`, `PlayersPanel.jsx`'s own floating money-change indicator) announces this one too, with nothing bespoke left to duplicate. Declining ("Giữ nguyên") is purely local — no server action, keyed by the pending offer's own content so it survives unrelated re-renders but correctly resets the instant a genuinely new offer replaces it. `GameControls.jsx`'s `HANDLED_PHASES` and `ledgerFormat.js`'s `rent_gamble` description text (now correctly narrating Bank↔owner instead of payer↔owner) updated to match. Lint/build clean.

**Live-verified**, working around two real environmental blockers rather than skipping verification: the actually-running backend dev process turned out to have been started without `--watch` (`npm start`, not `npm run dev`), so it was serving pre-revision code — restarting it was refused by the permission classifier as a process-kill action, so the *new* backend logic itself is confirmed only by the 645/645 unit tests, not a live restarted server. Frontend verification used direct Zustand store injection (this session's established technique) against the real running app — with one refinement worth keeping for next time: a bare `import('/src/store/gameStore.js')` resolves to a *disconnected* module instance invisible to the real mounted app (writes "stick" when read back through the same handle but never reach the DOM); the real store is only reachable through the exact HMR-versioned URL (`?t=<timestamp>`, found via `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')`, cross-checked by comparing real vs. fake `roomState` content between candidate instances). Confirmed live, against the real app: the drawer renders correctly with real interpolated values (name/amount/tile); it's genuinely owner-gated (disappeared when the logged-in user was swapped to the payer role, reappeared when swapped back); "Giữ nguyên" is local-only (dismisses the drawer without touching `pendingRentGamble` in the store) and correctly resets on a genuinely new offer; "Đánh cược" emits a real `GAMBLE_RENT` to the real server and correctly handles the response (busy-state reset, error display) — the real server correctly rejected it with `ROOM_NOT_IN_PROGRESS` (the fake test room was never actually started), which exercises the request/response wiring end-to-end without proving the new server-side gamble logic specifically, for the reason above. Caught one real layout bug this way, not by eyeballing: `PropertyActionDrawer.module.css`'s own `bottom: 88px` comment ("clears GameControls' sticky bottom bar... min-height 64px") turned out stale against this drawer's own measured content — the real rendered bar was ~120px tall in a live `POST_ACTIONS` state, causing a genuine ~45px overlap confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect` (not a screenshot, which this environment's `document.hidden` breaks — same standing limitation as every other UI task this session) before it was fixed to `140px` and reconfirmed clear (6.7px gap). Left `PropertyActionDrawer.module.css`'s own copy of that same stale comment untouched — a pre-existing, separate component this task didn't otherwise touch, out of scope to fix here.

`docs/BOARD_SPECIFICATION.md`, `ECONOMY_SPECIFICATION.md`, `GAME_STATE_MACHINE.md`, and `WEBSOCKET_API.md` all updated to match, each following that file's own existing convention for flagging a divergence (strikethrough + explanation) rather than silently rewriting history.

## Fresh-purchase build offer, same day (2026-08-25)

Follow-on question, same session: user asked whether allowing a build on the very first landing (same turn as buying, not just on a later AWAITING_UPGRADE return-landing) would need house-pricing changes or affect Hostile Acquisition. Answered from the real code before touching anything: `BUILD_HOUSE` already required no monopoly (the same day's earlier revision) and was already valid in `POST_ACTIONS`, which `BUY_PROPERTY` already transitions into directly — so nothing on the backend needed to change at all. Each build is already a single flat-cost unit regardless of how many a player builds in one turn (no bulk-pricing mechanic exists to adjust), and Hostile Acquisition already has its own independent `HOUSE_PROTECTED` check (`upgradeLevel > 0`, unconditional, unrelated to monopoly status) — so earlier building creates no new exposure there either. The one real gap found: `PropertyActionDrawer.jsx` only ever offered a build prompt during the real `AWAITING_UPGRADE` phase (a genuine return-landing) — the instant a fresh purchase moved the game into `POST_ACTIONS`, the drawer unmounted with no build nudge at all, even though `BUILD_HOUSE` was already legal there.

**Fix, frontend-only, zero backend changes**: generalized the drawer's existing build-offer block (previously gated on `isAwaitingUpgrade` alone) to also fire when `gameState.phase === 'POST_ACTIONS'` and the player is standing on their own unmortgaged, not-yet-maxed `property`-type tile (`canOfferFreshBuild`), reusing the exact same shared `buildRules.js` helpers (`buildBlockReason`/`effectiveBuildCost`/`nextBuildLabel`) the real `AWAITING_UPGRADE` path already used — same validation, same cost, same labels, nothing reinvented. Declining in this new case is purely local (no `DECLINE_UPGRADE` sent — that action isn't even legal in `POST_ACTIONS`, and nothing needs to be blocked open the way the real phase does), keyed by `property.id` so it doesn't suppress a later, genuinely different property. Live-verified via store injection (fresh HMR-versioned store URL + the `{gameState, stateVersion, transactions, deadlineAt}` wrapper shape `setGameState` actually requires — both lessons from the Rent Risk Choice verification immediately above, re-applied correctly this time on the first attempt after one field-name slip (`position` vs. the real `currentPosition`) was caught and fixed): the offer correctly appears the instant a freshly-bought, unbuilt property is owned in `POST_ACTIONS`; "Bỏ qua" correctly leaves `stateVersion`/`upgradeLevel` untouched (no server round-trip); the offer correctly re-arms for a second, different property after being dismissed on the first; "Xây Nhà" correctly emits a real `BUILD_HOUSE` with the right `propertyId` to the real server (rejected only for the fake test room, same as every other server round-trip verification this session). Lint/build clean.

## RECENTLY_ACQUIRED build gate, same day (2026-08-25)

Immediate follow-on to the fresh-purchase build offer above: asked directly whether same-turn building has a real gameplay cost, specifically for Hostile Acquisition. Answered honestly rather than reassuring: yes — `HOUSE_PROTECTED` (any `upgradeLevel > 0` is immune to hostile buyout, added earlier the same day) is completely independent of monopoly status, so a player with cash in hand can now make a freshly-bought property permanently safe within the very same turn, narrowing Hostile Acquisition's real window to only properties the owner hasn't gotten around to (or can't afford to) build on yet. User proposed the fix themselves: gate building on the owner's own next turn, not the instant of acquisition. Flagged one real consequence before implementing rather than guessing at the mechanism: movement in this game is dice-driven, not player-chosen, so a literal "must land on this tile again" gate could leave some properties permanently unbuildable purely by bad luck. Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` (a genuine fork — new mechanic, no existing precedent, real gameplay-balance stakes): gate on **the owner's own next turn generally**, not a literal return-landing.

**Implementation**: new `Property.acquiredAtRound` field (`domain/property.js`, `null` default — no gate applies until a real acquisition sets it). Reuses the already-existing `GameState.roundNumber` (increments once per full cycle through every active player, Win Condition design) rather than inventing a parallel per-player turn counter — since a property can only ever be acquired during the acquirer's own turn, "at least one round has passed since acquisition" is equivalent to "the owner's own next turn has begun", regardless of their position in turn order. Set by both `handleBuyProperty` (`acquiredAtRound: gameState.roundNumber`, alongside the pre-existing `acquiredAt` timestamp which stays for now as its own separately-unused field) and `handleHostileBuyout`'s ownership transfer (previously untouched by this field at all — every hostile-buyout target is by definition `upgradeLevel === 0`, so without this reset the new owner could immediately re-build to protect it against a further hostile buyout, one step removed from the exact loophole this whole task exists to close). Deliberately left untouched by `ACCEPT_TRADE` — a negotiated exchange between two consenting players isn't the "just grabbed it, rushing to protect it" scenario this rule targets, and `acquiredAtRound: null` cleanly means "no gate" for that case, plus every pre-existing test fixture that constructs a property's ownership directly rather than through a real `BUY_PROPERTY`/`HOSTILE_BUYOUT` dispatch (all 645 pre-existing tests passed completely unmodified, confirming this — only new tests needed writing).

New `handleBuildHouse` precondition, checked right after `MAX_UPGRADE_LEVEL` (before the group/even-build rules): rejects `RECENTLY_ACQUIRED` when `property.acquiredAtRound != null && gameState.roundNumber <= property.acquiredAtRound`. Frontend mirror in `buildRules.js`'s `buildBlockReason` (shared by both `PropertyActionDrawer.jsx` and `PropertyManager.jsx` — both build entry points close the loophole together, not just the newer one) — the fresh-purchase drawer added moments earlier needed zero further changes: it already shows whatever `buildBlockReason` returns, so the new reason ("Vừa mới sở hữu — đợi qua lượt sau của bạn") surfaces automatically as a disabled button with an explanation, exactly like every other existing block reason.

5 new backend tests (`turnMachine.test.js`: direct-fixture reject/succeed/null-skips-gate, plus two real end-to-end dispatches — `BUY_PROPERTY` then `BUILD_HOUSE` in the same round, and `HOSTILE_BUYOUT` then `BUILD_HOUSE` in the same round, both confirming the real action-chained flow triggers the rejection, not just an isolated unit check) + 2 existing tests extended with an `acquiredAtRound` assertion (`property.test.js`, the `BUY_PROPERTY` test) — **650/650 passing**. Live-verified via the same store-injection technique: a freshly-acquired property (`acquiredAtRound` equal to the injected `roundNumber`) correctly showed a disabled "Chưa xây được" button with the exact new reason text; advancing `roundNumber` by one correctly flipped it to an enabled, normal build button with no other change. `docs/WEBSOCKET_API.md` updated (new `RECENTLY_ACQUIRED` error row; `BUILD_HOUSE`'s own row revised; caught and struck through an unrelated pre-existing staleness found while there — `INCOMPLETE_GROUP` was still listed as a live error code despite being removed entirely by the same day's earlier monopoly-removal change).

## Win-condition design audit + Final Duel + a real spectator hole, 2026-08-25

User submitted a full 12-point win-condition design proposal (Last Tycoon Standing, bankruptcy definition, liquidation order, spectator mode, asset disposal, Final Duel, net worth as tie-breaker, turn limit, final scoring, ranking/MMR, victory moment). **Audited it against the real code before writing anything** rather than implementing it as a spec — the correct call, because roughly two-thirds of it was already shipped by the 2026-08-19 Win Condition system and would have been rebuilt needlessly. Findings reported point by point; three deserve recording here:

- **Already matching exactly** (no work needed): Last Tycoon Standing (`checkElimination`, run after *every* bankruptcy), bankruptcy defined as `cash + total liquidation value < debt` (so `cash === 0` is explicitly *not* bankruptcy — it routes to `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED`), the full Cash→Mortgage→Sell→Trade→forced-liquidation→bankruptcy order (Trade included: trade actions are phase-independent, so they genuinely are available mid-liquidation), net worth used only as tie-breaker/endgame rather than a primary win condition, and the tie-break chain itself (netWorth → propertyValue → cash → turnOrder).
- **Proposal contained two things that cannot happen**, both flagged rather than implemented: (1) "leftover assets after settling the debt return to the Bank" — by the definition of bankruptcy above there is never a leftover, since total assets are *less* than the debt by construction; (2) final scoring's `− Outstanding Debt` term is always 0 — this game has no persistent debt, every debt settles or escalates to liquidation/bankruptcy inside the same action. (3) A proposed 120-turn limit is already covered and *tighter*: Final Phase guarantees every match ends by round 25. — **This third point was framed wrongly and was corrected later the same day**: "tighter" was not a reassurance, it was the actual problem. See "Final Phase raised from round 20 to 45" below.
- **Genuinely absent**: Final Duel (built, below), MMR/per-player placement persistence (`match_results` stores only winner + result_type — needs a real DB migration; deferred, user chose not to build it now), and Victory Moment's proposed stat lines (`Successful Takeovers`/`Auction Wins` aren't tracked anywhere in `GameState` at all — deferred).

**Real bug found by the audit and fixed (`engine/trade.js`)**: the proposal's rule 4 ("a bankrupt player is a spectator — no actions, no Trade") was *almost* enforced. Every other route was already closed — `advanceTurn()` skips bankrupt players so they can never be the current player (which the socket layer's `NOT_YOUR_TURN` guard then blocks every turn-scoped action behind), `startAuction()` excludes them from `eligibleBidders`, and `applyBankruptcy()` drops any pending trade naming them at the moment they go bankrupt. **Trades were the one real hole**: they deliberately route through `tradeMachine.js` independent of both turn and phase, and neither `tradeMachine.js` nor `engine/trade.js` checked `bankrupt` anywhere — so nothing stopped a *new* trade being proposed by, or to, an already-eliminated player afterwards. A solvent player could gift property to a bankrupt one and effectively resurrect them mid-match, undoing a real elimination. Fixed with a new `PLAYER_BANKRUPT` rejection inside `validateOffer()` — the shared choke point `proposeTrade`/`counterTrade`/`acceptTrade` all already funnel *both* offer sides through, so one check covers all six paths, and `acceptTrade`'s own re-validation now also correctly rejects a trade whose counterparty went bankrupt *between* proposing and accepting. 3 new tests (bankrupt proposer, bankrupt target via a pure one-way gift — the exact resurrection case an owner-only check would have missed — and the propose-then-bankrupt-then-accept race).

**Final Duel (built, frontend-only)**: a one-shot whole-room announcement (`FinalDuelBanner.jsx`/`.module.css`, mounted in `GameView.jsx`) the instant a multi-player match narrows to its last two survivors, showing a head-to-head of both players' cash and net worth. Changes **no rules whatsoever** — the win condition, economy, and Final Phase's independent round-20 countdown are all untouched; this is purely the "không còn cảm giác game chưa biết bao giờ kết thúc" moment the proposal asked for. Deliberately **no backend field**: every input is already in the broadcast `gameState` (bankrupt players are never removed from `players` — that *is* the spectator design), so the condition is derived client-side rather than adding a stored field that could drift from the player list it summarises, the same reasoning `PlayersPanel.jsx` already uses for its own standings. Reuses `netWorthMirror.js`'s `playerNetWorth` (which mirrors `engine/netWorth.js`, the same function `rankPlayers()` uses to decide the real winner) so the duel readout can never disagree with the final result, and copies `FinalPhaseBanner.module.css`'s architecture wholesale — recoloured to this codebase's established `#c0392b` red specifically because both banners *can* fire in the same match and must not read as one announcement arriving twice.

**One real bug caught in design, before it shipped**: gating purely on "exactly 2 non-bankrupt players remain" is wrong — a 2-player match is legal (`GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §4 allows 2-6), so that condition is true from the very first turn of every such match and would have fired a "FINAL DUEL" banner at match start. The guard also requires `realPlayers.length > 2` (the *starting* roster, available precisely because bankrupt players stay in the array): a match that began as a duel has no narrowing moment to announce. Live-verified all four cases against the real running app via store injection: a 2-player match correctly shows nothing; 4 players with one bankrupt (3 remaining) correctly shows nothing; the third bankruptcy correctly fires it with real interpolated values (cash $1240 + $60 property = $1300 net worth, matching `netWorthMirror` exactly); and dismissing it correctly keeps it hidden across later unrelated broadcasts. Layout measured rather than eyeballed (`getBoundingClientRect`: no overflow of its parent, no horizontal page scroll) and colours confirmed resolving to real values, not silently-invalid tokens — the finding-#42 trap. **653/653 backend tests**, lint/build clean.

## Bug sweep, 2026-08-25 (user request: "quét bug trong hệ thống")

Multi-layer sweep: an invariant fuzzer over the real state machine, a frontend↔backend mirror-drift check, an action-surface consistency check, and lint/build. The fuzzer did the heavy lifting — same technique that found finding #39 — driving randomized legal actions through the real `transitionTurn` and asserting hard invariants after *every* step (no negative balance, money strictly conserved across all players, building supply reconciles against what is actually on the board, no orphaned/bankrupt property owner, `upgradeLevel` in range, `acquiredAtRound <= roundNumber`, no bankrupt current player, no pending gamble/liquidation owned by a bankrupt player).

**Three of the fuzzer's first findings were bugs in the fuzzer, not the code** — worth recording because each one is a genuine fact about this codebase that a future sweep would otherwise re-derive the hard way: (1) `currentTurnIndex` indexes the **turnOrder space**, not the `players` array, so `players[currentTurnIndex]` is always wrong (it lands on the Bank); (2) `freeParkingJackpot` must **not** be counted as money — it is a bookkeeping counter shadowing cash already sitting in the Bank's own balance, so including it double-counts every tax and jail fine; (3) the event deck never exhausts — `drawCard` is cyclic (draws the head, pushes it to the tail), so an empty-deck fixture is a setup error, not a reachable state.

**Four real bugs found and fixed:**

1. **A build discount that fully covered the cost crashed the build (HIGH — most of the board).** `handleBuildHouse` correctly clamps the charge to `Math.max(0, base + modifier - discount)`, and its own comment says a heavily-discounted build is "free, not negative" — then handed that $0 straight to `applyTransaction`, which rejects non-positive amounts. Result: `TypeError`, the build failed, and because the throw preceded any state change the discount was **never consumed either** — the player stayed stuck holding a reward card that silently broke building. Not theoretical: C07 grants a $50 discount and 5 real streets cost exactly $50 to build on; C12's option B grants $100, which also zeroes the 9 streets at $100 — **14 of the board's 22 buildable streets**. Fixed by skipping the ledger write entirely for a free build (no $0 row), the same resolution `PAYING_RENT`'s own `rentAmount === 0` case and the lost-rent-gamble case already use. 2 new tests (exact-cancel, and an over-cancel that must not pay the player to build).

2. **A card could move a player further than the mover allowed, crashing the draw (MEDIUM-HIGH).** `movePlayer` capped `steps` at 12 — the largest two-die total — but is shared with card-driven movement. C02 ("move to the nearest unowned property ahead") searches the whole board via `findNearestUnownedAhead` and legitimately returns up to `boardTileCount` steps, so any draw where the next unowned tile sat more than 12 ahead — routine once the early board is bought up — threw a `RangeError` instead of moving. The lower bound had already been loosened to 1 for C01 in an earlier pass; the upper bound was never revisited. Fixed to bound on `boardTileCount`; its stale "1-12 range" test rewritten, plus coverage for a 31-step move and the full-lap edge.

3. **A bankruptcy left the ELIMINATED player holding the turn (MEDIUM).** `finishAfterBankruptcy` returned `POST_ACTIONS` unconditionally, on the documented reasoning that the bankrupt player's turn "still needs to formally end". That means the whole table waits for someone who has already lost — and who is the player most likely to have closed the tab — to click End Turn, or for `POST_ACTIONS`' 30s timeout to fire, on **every** elimination. It also contradicts the spectator rule and, concretely, `resolveForfeit()` — written later for the mechanically identical situation — which already force-advances. Fixed to match `resolveForfeit` exactly, conditional on the bankrupt player actually holding the turn (a player can also be bankrupted during someone else's turn via the multi-player charge cards, where the real current player must be left untouched — that branch now has an explicit assertion on the existing K04 test).

4. **Landing on your own station/utility entered the build phase (LOW).** `resolveBuyableTile` routed *any* own-tile landing to `AWAITING_UPGRADE`, whose only two legal actions are `BUILD_HOUSE` — which `handleBuildHouse` rejects outright for a non-street tile — and `DECLINE_UPGRADE`. Not a deadlock (the phase has its own 15s timeout and the frontend correctly greys the button with a real reason), but a dead prompt plus a wasted timer on every landing on your own station. Now goes straight to `POST_ACTIONS` for transport/utility.

**Two issues found and deliberately NOT fixed — reported instead, because both involve a real tradeoff rather than a clear-cut defect:**

- **Selling a hotel can drive `houseSupply` negative.** Already flagged in `handleSellHouse`'s own comment as a knowingly-unmodelled edge case ("not enough house pieces left in the general pool to represent a hotel-to-houses downgrade... flagged rather than silently guessed at") — the fuzzer proved it genuinely reachable, reproducing `houseSupply: -2` with 34 houses placed against a 32-house set. Practical harm is small (negative supply still correctly blocks further building, and the pool was already exhausted) but the physical-set invariant is broken and a negative count could surface in the UI. Not fixed unilaterally because the obvious fix — blocking the sell when `houseSupply < 4` — could trap a player mid-`LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` and force a *wrong* bankruptcy, which is strictly worse than a cosmetic accounting fiction. Real options: clamp at 0, sell the hotel down to 0 houses instead of 4, or accept and hide it.
- **`BUILD_HOUSE` on an ineligible tile returns `MALFORMED_PAYLOAD`.** A well-formed, plausible client request (a station's real `propertyId`) throws a `TypeError`, which `errorCodeFor` maps to `MALFORMED_PAYLOAD` rather than a domain rejection like the neighbouring `NOT_OWNER`/`MAX_UPGRADE_LEVEL` codes. Safe (no crash — it is caught and returned as a normal rejection), purely a semantic mismatch, and now much harder to reach at all given fix 4 above.

**Verified clean, no action needed**: after the fixes, **1,094,145 fuzzed steps** across 2000 randomized games (up from ~207k before — the crashes had been terminating runs early) with every invariant holding except the known hotel-downgrade one. Frontend↔backend mirrors checked for drift and found faithful: `netWorthMirror.js` reproduces `engine/netWorth.js` formula-for-formula including both integer-safe roundings, and every mirrored constant agrees (`HOUSE_SELLBACK_RATIO`, `MAX_UPGRADE_LEVEL`, `JAIL_FINE`). All 25 action types the frontend can send have a real backend handler — the 5 that `turnMachine.js` does not know are the trade actions, correctly routed to `tradeMachine.js` — and the only backend action with no frontend path is `AUCTION_TIMEOUT`, which is documented system-generated-only. Also removed a duplicate `MAX_UPGRADE_LEVEL` this session had introduced in `PropertyActionDrawer.jsx`, which already imports the shared one from `buildRules.js`. **658/658 backend tests**, lint/build clean.

**Observation, not a bug**: `MAX_UPGRADE_LEVEL` is independently re-declared in 6 frontend files and `HOUSE_SELLBACK_RATIO` in 3. All agree today, and the no-shared-package constraint is a known project standing, but that is 9 places for a balance change to drift.

### Round 2, same day — the subsystems the first sweep never reached

The first fuzzer drove `transitionTurn` directly, which meant **trades were never exercised at all** (they route through `tradeMachine.js`) and neither was persistence. A second fuzzer interleaved real trade actions with real turn play against the same GameState — including adversarial ones (the wrong party accepting/cancelling someone else's trade) — under stricter invariants: every pending trade must name live, non-bankrupt, distinct parties; must only offer assets its side still actually owns; and no property may be committed to two live trades at once. **Two more real bugs, both in Hostile Acquisition, both from the same root cause**: `pendingHostileBuyoutPropertyId` is set the moment rent is paid, but nothing re-validated it at the moment of use — while trades and `FORFEIT_MATCH` are both deliberately phase-independent, so ownership can legitimately change in between, inside the same turn.

5. **Hostile buyout crashed if the buyer acquired the target first (HIGH — hard crash).** Land on someone's property → pay rent → the buyout window opens → *trade for that same property* → click Buyout. `handleHostileBuyout` paid `player.id → property.ownerId`, now the same person: `applyTransaction` threw "fromPlayerId and toPlayerId must differ". Now a clean `ALREADY_OWNED` rejection, plus a matching `NOT_OWNED` for the sibling case where the owner forfeited (or was bankrupted by an out-of-turn card charge) first and the property reverted to the Bank — which would have paid `null`.

6. **Hostile buyout left stale trades advertising a property it had seized (MEDIUM).** The trade system's asset-locking only guards against the same property being offered in two *trades*; it has no visibility into a buyout. A pending trade kept offering a property its side no longer owned — contained (`acceptTrade` re-validates ownership and rejects), but visibly wrong and it kept the counterparty's own assets needlessly locked until expiry. `applyBankruptcy` already prunes `pendingTrades` on exactly this kind of forced ownership change; `handleHostileBuyout` now matches instead of the two transfer paths disagreeing. `PropertyActionDrawer.jsx` also stops offering the button for a property you already own.

After both fixes: **597,602 trade-fuzz steps with zero findings**, and the main fuzzer re-run clean at 1,091,791 steps (only the known hotel-downgrade supply issue). 3 new tests, **661/661**.

**Four more layers checked, all clean, no action needed:**
- **Persistence of this session's new fields.** `acquiredAtRound` and `pendingRentGamble` both survive save/load automatically — the whole GameState is one JSONB blob (`game_state_snapshots.state`), not a columnar mapping, so no schema change was needed. Both also degrade safely against a *pre-existing* snapshot written before they existed (`acquiredAtRound` reads `undefined`, and `!= null` correctly skips the gate rather than throwing) — the same hazard `lastDrawnEventCardSeq`'s own `?? 0` guard was written for.
- **Stall points.** All 9 timed phases have a `buildDefaultAction` branch — no phase can hang on a disconnected player.
- **Frontend field names.** Every `gameState.*` field the frontend reads was cross-checked against the real domain shape. Three apparent misses were all false positives (a comment explaining that `gameState.currentTurn` does *not* exist, a `gameState.js` filename in prose, and `phase` being returned via shorthand rather than `phase:`). No typo'd field reads.
- **Action surface.** Re-confirmed after the changes: every action the frontend can send has a real handler.

## Final Phase raised from round 20 to 45, 2026-08-25

User asked whether the 25-round cap had been removed yet ("bỏ luật chơi trong vòng 25 lượt chưa"). It had not — and the reason it hadn't is a framing error in this session's own win-condition audit above, worth recording as a correction rather than quietly fixing.

That audit reported the user's proposed 120-turn limit as "already covered and *tighter*" — treating tighter as strictly better. It isn't. `FINAL_PHASE_TRIGGER_ROUND=20` + `FINAL_PHASE_DURATION_ROUNDS=5` means every match hard-ends at round 25, and **25 rounds is not long enough to eliminate every opponent in a Monopoly-shaped game**. So in practice the net-worth ending was not the rare safeguard the design calls it — it was the *normal* way matches ended, making "richest at round 25" the de-facto primary win condition and Last Tycoon Standing the exception. That is a straight inversion of the approved design, and it is exactly the failure mode the user's own proposal flagged in its point 10 ("nếu người chơi biết 'Tôi chỉ cần có Net Worth cao hơn' thì họ có thể chơi cực kỳ thụ động"). Reporting it as "covered" measured the wrong property — whether a limit exists — instead of the one that mattered: how often it decides the winner.

Also worth stating because it changes the numbers: this system counts **rounds** (one full cycle through all players), while the proposal was written in individual **turns**. 25 rounds is 50 turns at 2 players but 150 at 6 — so the proposed 120-turn limit was actually *looser* than the shipped behaviour for small tables and *tighter* for large ones, not uniformly one or the other.

**Change**: `FINAL_PHASE_TRIGGER_ROUND` 20 → 45 (ends at round 50), on the user's explicit choice between three options (raise it to a rare backstop / remove the Final Phase entirely / re-count in individual turns). Duration stays 5. Elimination is the real race again; the countdown now exists only to stop a genuine stalemate running forever, and the match is still **guaranteed** to end — that property was never negotiable, only how often it decides the outcome. Removing the phase outright was offered and rejected precisely because it would drop that guarantee.

Zero code changes beyond the one constant: every test references `FINAL_PHASE_TRIGGER_ROUND` symbolically rather than the literal 20, so all adapted automatically (**661/661**), and the frontend only ever mirrors `FINAL_PHASE_DURATION_ROUNDS` (it watches the server-computed `finalPhaseStartedAtRound` rather than deciding *whether* Final Phase began), so it needed no change at all. The earlier playtest entries in this document that mention round 20/25 are left as-is — they are accurate records of what happened at the time, not stale design statements.

## Card deck v2 audit + C08 "Bảo Vệ Tài Sản", 2026-08-25

User submitted a full v2.0 spec for the 24-card Cơ Hội/Khí Vận deck. Audited against the shipped deck first, same discipline as the win-condition proposal — and again the right call: **19 of the 24 cards already matched v2 exactly**, down to every dollar amount, every die-face table cell, every threshold and tie rule (C05's 0/150/200/300 ladder, C11's −100/0/+100/+200/+300 with its $50 stake and affordability gate, K06's 100/tied-50, K10's 100/tied-75, K12's ≥$1000→$100, K09's $25-per-level, K01/K02's ±10%, K07/K08's ±$50, C07's $50, C12's three options including the 50%/max-$150 rent discount). Implementing from the spec would have rewritten working, tested code.

**The five real gaps**, reported rather than assumed: C03 (dice ±1 — needs a new interaction between rolling and landing), C06/C10 (loan and deferred-payment — both need a "pending obligation" concept that fires at the start of a future turn, which does not exist), K11 (first-rent-collection bonus per round), and **C08**, which is not missing so much as *occupied*: `C08_CUU_HO_TAI_CHINH` is a conditional +$100 rescue, a completely different card from v2's anti-steal "Bảo Vệ Tài Sản". Also noted: v2's Common/Uncommon/Rare rarity system has no implementation at all (the deck is a flat cyclic list, every card equally likely), and the live deck holds 27 cards — v2's 24 minus the 5 gaps, plus 7 earlier-era cards outside the v2 spec entirely. Cross-referencing v2's own §VIII priority list showed C08 was the **only** item in its top 11 not already shipped, which is what the user chose to build.

**C08 "Bảo Vệ Tài Sản" — built.** One of the owner's unimproved properties becomes immune to `HOSTILE_BUYOUT` until their next turn: the temporary, card-granted counterpart of the permanent `HOUSE_PROTECTED` rule, and per v2 the only card in the whole deck that touches the Steal system. New `GameState.propertyProtection` (`{propertyId, ownerId, grantedAtRound}`), new `CARD_PROTECTED` rejection in `handleHostileBuyout`, and expiry derived rather than stored — `roundNumber > grantedAtRound` means the owner's next turn has begun, the same round-arithmetic equivalence the `RECENTLY_ACQUIRED` build gate already relies on and for the same reason.

Three design points worth recording:
- **It is a CHOICE card with a single option**, unusually. The real decision is *which* property, which a static `options` array cannot enumerate, so it rides in the action payload (`propertyId`) and is intercepted in `handleEventChoice` before `applyIntents` — the same interception pattern `MOVE_RELATIVE` (C01) already established there. `EventCardModal.jsx` expands that one option into one button per eligible property, each labelled with what a rival would have to pay to seize it (2× price).
- **A new eligibility kind.** Every prior gate tests a numeric field on the player; this one asks whether the player owns anything protectable at all. Without it, a player holding only improved property would enter `AWAITING_EVENT_CHOICE` with no legal choice and hang their own turn — the exact deadlock C11's affordability gate was added to close.
- **Improved properties are deliberately excluded** as targets: they are already permanently immune, so protecting one would be a guaranteed no-op.

**Two real bugs caught during the build, both by existing machinery rather than review**, which is the part worth remembering:
1. Finding #35's own regression test ("every CHOICE card must time out to an ACCEPTED choice") failed the moment C08 was wired in — the timeout default supplied no `propertyId`, so a timed-out C08 would be rejected and hang the phase. Fixed in `buildEventChoiceDefaultAction`, which now picks the player's most expensive protectable property (the same "make the default a defensible play, not merely a legal one" standard `buildLiquidationDefaultAction` sets by selling cheapest-first). That test also surfaced a genuinely reachable state the eligibility gate does *not* cover: trades are phase-independent, so a player can draw C08 while holding exactly one unimproved property and trade it away before choosing — that case now resolves as a revealed no-op instead of throwing.
2. Fuzzing the card the same day it was written caught **the protection following the property to a new owner**. Because trades are phase-independent, a protected property can change hands while the shield is still live, handing the recipient a defence they never earned. Fixed by making `protectionIsLive` re-verify current ownership rather than cleaning up at each transfer site — correct by construction regardless of which path moves a property in future. This is the same stale-`pendingXxx`-reference hazard the earlier bug sweep generalised, reproduced immediately in brand-new code, which is a fair argument the pattern is worth checking for by default.

**667/667 backend tests** (6 new C08 tests), lint/build clean. Re-fuzzed after: 861,944 main-loop steps and 597,627 trade steps with C08 actively exercised under a dedicated invariant (a live protection must always name a property its stated owner still holds, unbuilt, non-bankrupt) — no findings beyond the known hotel-downgrade supply issue. Live-verified in the browser: the picker offers exactly the unimproved properties and correctly omits the built one, labels show the right seizure cost ($60→$120, $400→$800), and clicking emits the real `MAKE_EVENT_CHOICE {optionId: 'OPT_PROTECT', propertyId}` frame.

**Flagged, not fixed**: the new card is `C08_BAO_VE_TAI_SAN` while the unrelated older `C08_CUU_HO_TAI_CHINH` keeps its own id. Renaming the latter would be the tidier numbering, but card ids are persisted by string in live snapshots (`pendingEventCardId`/`lastDrawnEventCardId`), so a rename would break any match already in flight. Left as a cosmetic collision pending a decision on the 7 non-v2 legacy cards generally.

## Bug sweep round 3 + a live parallel session, 2026-08-27

**The hotel-downgrade supply bug is fixed — the state machine now fuzzes completely clean.** Selling a hotel took a flat 4 houses out of the shared pool without checking 4 existed, driving `houseSupply` negative (reproduced at −2, i.e. 34 houses standing against a 32-house set). Two earlier sweeps reported it but left it, because the obvious fix — rejecting the sale when the pool is short — is the dangerous one: `SELL_HOUSE` is a legal `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` action, so blocking it could leave a player unable to raise cash they demonstrably have and force a *wrong* bankruptcy, strictly worse than the accounting drift.

The shipped fix never blocks: the property drops straight past the levels there are no pieces for (down to whatever the pool holds, possibly 0), takes exactly those houses, and the player is **paid for every level actually removed** rather than just one. The aggregate liquidation estimate stays honest because `houseSellBackValue` prices all levels at the same per-level rate — a property stripped 5→2 pays 3 levels now and the remaining 2 later, the same total `checkSolvency` assumed. **1,351,378 fuzzed steps with zero findings** (plus 597,676 trade steps, also zero) — the first fully clean deep run of the whole sweep. Also added the missing `GRANT_PROPERTY_PROTECTION` entry to `EVENT_ACTION_TYPES`: that array is documentary only and nothing validates against it, which is exactly why C08 slipped past it — an exported vocabulary constant nothing checks will drift silently.

**Mid-sweep, a second session began editing the same files live.** Detected from the outside, not announced: cards grew a `keepable` flag nobody here added, `EVENT_CARDS` went 27→28, and the parallel work marked **the C08 card added minutes earlier in this session** as keepable — so the two streams are already interleaved. It is building a **Card Inventory system** (dated 2026-08-27): `keepable` cards go into `PlayerGameState.inventory` on draw instead of resolving, played later via a new `USE_INVENTORY_CARD` action, with a new `CardInventory.jsx` frontend panel mounted in `GameView.jsx`.

Writing to those files was stopped at that point rather than risking a clobber, and the feature was audited read-only instead. **Three real defects found in it, none yet fixed — they belong to whoever is building it:**

1. **Out-of-turn play does not actually work (medium).** `USE_INVENTORY_CARD` is dispatched before the `VALID_ACTIONS_BY_PHASE` gate, so it is phase-independent — but it was never added to `socketServer.js`'s `TURN_INDEPENDENT_ACTION_TYPES`, so the socket layer's `NOT_YOUR_TURN` guard rejects it for anyone who is not the current player. The handler's own docstring says "allows playing kept cards out-of-turn"; through the real socket path only the current player can. These are two separate mechanisms in this codebase and only one was wired.
2. **Crashes for every match already in flight (medium).** `handleUseInventoryCard` does `player.inventory.includes(cardId)` with no guard, but `loadGameStateFromSupabase` restores the raw JSONB snapshot without passing it through `createPlayerGameState`, so a match started before today has `inventory: undefined` → `TypeError` → `MALFORMED_PAYLOAD`. `resolveDrawingCard` guards the same field with `(player.inventory || [])`; this path does not. The factory default (`inventory: fields.inventory ?? []`) only protects *new* states.
3. **Client-supplied randomness (latent, finding #27's class).** `serverGeneratedFields` covers `ROLL_DICE`/`ATTEMPT_JAIL_ROLL`/`GAMBLE_RENT`/`MAKE_EVENT_CHOICE` but **not** `USE_INVENTORY_CARD`, so `probabilityRoll`/`dieFaceRoll` reach `resolveChoice` straight from the client payload. Not exploitable with today's four keepable cards (none carry dice), but live the instant any dice card — C05 or C11 are the obvious candidates — is marked keepable.

Minor, same feature: bankruptcy does not clear `inventory`; `InvalidTurnActionError('ANY', …)` maps through `errorCodeFor` to `PHASE_MISMATCH`, a misleading code for "you don't hold that card"; and `handleUseInventoryCard` gates on the card being *in inventory* rather than on it being `keepable`, so any card that ever reached a hand could be played from it.

**#3 is now fixed here, because it turned out to be collision-free.** `socketServer.js` is untouched by the parallel session — absent from the recently-modified list and containing zero references to the new feature — so hardening it carries no clobber risk and required no change to their design. `serverGeneratedFields` now covers `USE_INVENTORY_CARD`, reading the card from the payload's own `cardId` (nothing is pending for a card played out of hand, so `pendingEventCardId` is the wrong source) and injecting `probabilityRoll`/`dieFaceRoll` only when the resolved card or option actually carries such an intent — the same gating `MAKE_EVENT_CHOICE` already uses.

Regression test added in `socketServer.test.js` (also untouched by the parallel work), and **verified to genuinely fail against the unfixed code** before being kept: a client claiming die face 6 while the real `randomSource` forces face 1 must land on C11's worst tier (−$50 stake −$100), not its best (+$300). C11 is used deliberately rather than one of the four currently-keepable cards: none of those carry randomness, so a test written against them would pass vacuously and guard nothing — and `handleUseInventoryCard` gates on inventory membership rather than `keepable`, so seeding C11 into a hand exercises the real path. **668 tests, 663 passing**; the same 5 failures, all still the parallel feature's own.

Findings **#1 and #2 are deliberately left alone**: both live in `turnMachine.js`, which that session is actively rewriting, and #1 in particular would change the reach of their feature (making it genuinely out-of-turn also needs a bankrupt-player guard, or it reopens the exact exploit the trade path was hardened against earlier in this sweep).

**5 backend tests are currently red, all from that work, none from this sweep**: the four cards it made keepable (`VE_SO_TRUNG_AN_UI`, `C07`, `C08`, `C12`) no longer fire their effects at draw time, so tests asserting the old immediate behaviour fail. They are stale rather than wrong-in-principle, but they are that feature's tests to update — rewriting them here would encode behaviour still being changed. Suite stands at **662/667**; every failure traces to the four keepable cards.

## Centre-board callouts billboarded, 2026-08-30 (user request)

User report (with a screenshot of a rotated board): *"phần tới lượt hoặc cộng tiền khi tới ô bắt đầu phải luôn nằm giữa bàn cờ và thẳng hàng dù có xoay bàn cờ đi đâu chăng nữa chứ không phải nằm tĩnh trên bàn cờ như hiện tại"* — the turn-status pill (`Đến lượt của …`) and the Pass GO `+$` callout, both rendered by `CenterBoardArea.jsx`, were lying flat *in* the board surface and skewing/tilting with it as the camera rotated. They should stay upright, level, and centred on the board at any spin/tilt.

**Fix, frontend-only (CSS + one wrapper div), zero JS/logic change.** All three centre-board text overlays (turn status, Pass GO callout, pre-game wordmark) now render inside one new `.calloutAnchor` in `CenterBoardArea.jsx` — a zero-size point at `.center`'s centre (= the board's centre, the grid is symmetric) carrying the **exact billboard counter-rotation the codebase already uses three times** (`DiceRoll.module.css`'s `.layer`, `GameBoard.module.css`'s `.tokenStandee`/`.fromGhost`): `translate(-50%,-50%) rotateZ(calc(-45deg - var(--spin))) rotateX(calc(-1 * var(--tilt))) translateZ(var(--side)*0.05)`, cancelling `.board`'s own `rotateX(--tilt) rotateZ(45deg + --spin)`. `GameBoard.module.css`'s `.center` gained `transform-style: preserve-3d` so that 3D billboard transform survives instead of being flattened back into the board plane.

`.calloutAnchor` is deliberately `transform-style: flat`, and carries **no** `translateY`. Each child's own vertical nudge (`.turnStatus` `+0.14·--side` down, `.passGoLayer` `−0.12·--side` up, so both clear DiceRoll's dice at dead centre) is a plain 2D `translateY` in that flat, screen-parallel subtree — a pure screen-space offset.

**First attempt this session was buggy and is worth recording** — user caught it immediately with a screenshot ("CÓ LỖI Ở ĐÂY": the turn pill had vanished entirely). v1 put the billboard rotation *and* the `translateY` on `.turnStatus`/`.passGoLayer` directly. A `translateY(k)` applied *after* `rotateX(-tilt)` tilts by `−k·sin(tilt)` into board-space −Z; under `.center`'s `preserve-3d` sorting that dropped the pill (downward nudge → −Z) *behind* `.center`'s opaque felt. Measured: local-space Z was −37px at the default 55° tilt, and worse at higher tilt — no fixed `translateZ` compensates across the 25–80° tilt range (still −5px at tilt 80 even with `translateZ(0.75·--side)`). The `.calloutAnchor` structure fixes it by construction: the anchor has no `translateY`, so its own local-space Z is `+0.05·--side·cos(tilt)` — **swept the full 25–80° × ±180° camera range, minimum +6.1px, always in front of the felt** — and the flat subtree means the children's 2D nudges carry no independent depth to sort.

**Verification**: lint + `vite build` clean. Not driven in the real in-game view — `javascript_tool` attempts to reach the store / fake a Supabase session were blocked by the permission classifier (don't retry that route) — but the exact `.board → .center(preserve-3d, felt) → .calloutAnchor(flat) → child` chain was reproduced in the real browser engine and measured: child accumulated screen matrix `rotateZ = 0`, `skew = 0` at every spin (0/45/90/−123/200°) × tilt (25/40/55/70/80°) × zoom (0.8–1.2) — always upright, unskewed; child centre `dx = 0` (dead-centred horizontally) at all angles, `dy` a pure screen-vertical offset scaling with zoom; anchor local-Z positive everywhere (see above). HMR means the user's running board picks it up live.

Docs: this section supersedes the positioning described in "Owned-tile price → real rent, plus a Pass GO callout, 2026-08-25" (the `align-self: flex-start` / bottom-anchored-`.turnStatus` layout) — kept for history rather than rewritten.

## Every `position: fixed` modal was rendering in normal flow — `.shell > *` regression, 2026-08-30

Same session, next user report (screenshot: the event-card modal drawn half-off-screen at the bottom, marooned in a tall empty card, "đang bị như này khi xuất hiện thẻ hãy fix đi"). Root cause is **not** in EventCardModal — `GameView.module.css` had grown `.shell > * { position: relative; z-index: 1; }` (to lift the topbar/body above a new `.shell::before` ambient-shimmer pseudo, `z-index: 0`). That universal-child selector *also* matched every `position: fixed` overlay mounted as a `.shell` sibling — `EventCardModal`, `PropertyManager`, `PropertyActionDrawer`, `LiquidationPanel`, `FlashAuction`, `TradeWindow`, `RentRiskChoice`, `FinalPhaseBanner`, `FinalDuelBanner`, `GameOverScreen` — all 10. `.shell > *` and each `.backdrop` have **equal specificity (0,1,0)**; GameView.module.css's CSS-module `<style>` is injected last (it's imported after every child in `GameView.jsx`), so `position: relative; z-index: 1` won the cascade and dropped every overlay out of fixed positioning into normal flow at the bottom of `.shell` (clipped by its `overflow: hidden`). Verified live by building a real `<div class="_shell_…"><div class="_backdrop_…">` with the compiled class names: `getComputedStyle` returned `position: relative; z-index: 1` for all of them; after the fix, `position: fixed; z-index: 40–60` restored.

**Fix**: `.shell > *` → `.shell > .topbar, .shell > .body` (the only two real layout children — the modals carry their own `position: fixed` + z-index and already paint above the `z-index: 0` shimmer). One-selector change in `GameView.module.css`.

**Also, same pass** (`EventCardModal.module.css`): `.face` lost its `min-height: min(58vh, 420px)` (→ `min-height: 0`) and `.faceBody` its `flex: 1` (→ `flex: 0 1 auto`), so an instant card hugs its content instead of stretching a one-line notice across a 420px void; choice cards size to their options; long cards still scroll inside `.faceBody` under `max-height: 86vh`. This softens — but was not the cause of — the 2026-08-23 "big centre-screen card" pass.

**Collision note**: `GameView.module.css` (and `index.css`, `frontend/src/features/game/GameView.module.css`) carried mtimes from a concurrent session (the Card Inventory work, see 2026-08-27 entry) — `.shell::before` + `.shell > *` are almost certainly theirs. My edit applied without an `Edit` conflict, but this file may be overwritten from that side; re-check `.shell > .topbar, .shell > .body` survived if modal positioning regresses again.

lint + `vite build` clean.

## Bug sweep, 2026-09-01 — the Card Inventory feature was dead end-to-end

User asked to find and fix every existing bug. The parallel session that built the Card Inventory system (see 2026-08-27 entry) had gone quiet for five days — last write 2026-08-27 22:15 — leaving 5 red tests and the two defects that entry deliberately left alone because they "belong to whoever is building it". Nobody is; they were fixed here. Suite went **663 passing / 5 failing → 675 / 0**.

**Method, in the order that paid off.** Ran the invariant fuzzer first, before reading any code (this project's standing rule). Core `transitionTurn` came back clean at ~357k steps, so the state machine was not where the bugs were — that negative result is what redirected the sweep to the feature boundary and the frontend, which is where all seven real defects turned out to live. Two fuzzer runs found only fuzzer bugs (offering `BUILD_HOUSE` on a station; not knowing the new error class) — both fixed in the harness, not worked around. Final run: **804,337 steps across 900 games, zero findings**, and this is the first sweep whose action mix includes `USE_INVENTORY_CARD` at all.

**1 — `CardInventory.jsx` called `sendGameAction` with the wrong signature, at all four call sites (critical).** The real signature is `sendGameAction(actionType, payload)` — two positional arguments, roomId read from the store by `socketClient.js` itself. All four calls passed `sendGameAction(roomState.roomId, { type, payload })`, so the server received `actionType` = a room UUID and rejected every single click. **The entire feature was non-functional from the day it shipped**: the hand rendered, and no card could ever be played. Every other component in the codebase already called it correctly; this file was the only one that didn't.

**2 — `USE_INVENTORY_CARD` was never turn-independent.** It was dispatched ahead of the `VALID_ACTIONS_BY_PHASE` gate (phase-independent) and its handler's docstring promised out-of-turn play, but it was absent from `socketServer.js`'s `TURN_INDEPENDENT_ACTION_TYPES`, so the `NOT_YOUR_TURN` guard refused it for anyone who wasn't the current player. Phase-independence and turn-independence are two separate mechanisms here and only one had been wired. Added — safe now that defect 4 closes the bankrupt-actor hole the turn gate had been incidentally covering.

**3 — every match started before 2026-08-27 crashed on first use.** `gameRepository.loadGameStateFromSupabase` returns the raw JSONB blob and deliberately never re-runs it through `createPlayerGameState`, so the factory's `inventory: []` default doesn't apply and the field is genuinely `undefined` there. `resolveDrawingCard` guards this (`player.inventory || []`); `handleUseInventoryCard` did a bare `player.inventory.includes(...)` and threw a raw `TypeError`. Same hazard class the `lastDrawnEventCardSeq` `?? 0` comment already documents for counters.

**4 — a non-keepable card in a hand was fully playable.** The handler gated on inventory *membership* rather than on the card's own `keepable` flag. Membership is a consequence of `keepable`, not the rule — so any card that ever reached a hand stayed playable forever, out of turn and out of phase. Reproduced before fixing: `K12_NGAY_THUE` (a tax card) seeded into a hand resolved its full charge. Also closed the related gap that this action had no bankruptcy check at all, which mattered the moment defect 2 was fixed — the exact shape of the trade-path hole found on 2026-08-25.

**5 — rejections lied about their cause.** All of them were `InvalidTurnActionError('ANY', …)`, which `errorCodeFor` maps unconditionally to `PHASE_MISMATCH` — so "you don't hold that card" reached the client as "wrong phase", which is both wrong and unactionable for a phase-independent action. New `InvalidInventoryActionError` with real reasons (`CARD_NOT_HELD`, `CARD_NOT_KEEPABLE`, `NOT_ELIGIBLE`, `PLAYER_BANKRUPT`, `NOT_A_PARTICIPANT`), wired into `errorCodeFor` alongside the existing classes.

**6 — bankruptcy left cards on an eliminated player.** Cash and properties transfer, pending trades drop, an active auction folds them out — `inventory` and `jailFreeCards` were the one asset class still left behind, so the hand kept rendering for a player who can never act again. Cleared in `applyBankruptcy`.

**7 — frontend, three more.** `CardInventory.module.css` used `var(--et-surface)`, which does not exist in `GameView.module.css`'s palette and had no fallback, so the panel painted fully transparent (the real token is `--et-panel-bg`; verified live that it now resolves to `rgb(19,28,51)` instead of `rgba(0,0,0,0)`). CHOICE cards were dispatched from a hardcoded `if (card.id === 'C12…') else if (card.id === 'C08…')` ladder with a bare `return` for anything else — a permanently dead button for any future keepable CHOICE card, and it silently offered only 2 of C12's **3** real options. And `EventCardModal` showed a drawn keepable card's own text ("Lần xây dựng tiếp theo của bạn được giảm $50") with nothing to say the effect had *not* applied — the card had gone to the hand. That is the confusion visible in the user's own screenshot. The component was rewritten to drive everything off the card's real `options` array (already on the wire — the endpoint returns `EVENT_CARDS` verbatim, `keepable` included), replacing the `window.prompt`/`alert` flow that asked players to type a raw array index against database property IDs; the modal gained an explicit "đã cất vào Kho Thẻ — chưa có hiệu lực" line.

**The 5 red tests were stale, not wrong, and were rewritten rather than loosened.** `VE_SO_TRUNG_AN_UI`/`C07`/`C08`/`C12` became `keepable`, so they no longer fire at draw time; each test now drives the real two-step flow (draw → hand → `USE_INVENTORY_CARD` → effect), which is strictly more coverage than before. C12's rewrite additionally pins genuinely new behaviour: eligibility is re-checked at *play* time, so a holder who climbs back above the $200 gate loses the card's use.

**One of my own tests had to be rewritten, and the reason is worth recording.** `socketServer.test.js`'s `USE_INVENTORY_CARD` dice-authority test (written 2026-08-27) seeded C11 into a hand and, in its own comment, explicitly relied on the handler gating on membership rather than `keepable` — i.e. **it was passing only because of defect 4**, and fixing that defect made it unreachable. Split into two tests, one per layer: the socket path now asserts `CARD_NOT_KEEPABLE`, and `serverGeneratedFields` is exported (test-only, zero behaviour change, same one-word-export precedent as `getCurrentPlayer`) so the injection layer is still pinned directly — it cannot be reached end-to-end while the keepable gate holds, but it is exactly what matters the day C05 or C11 is marked keepable. A test that can only pass while a bug exists is not a passing test.

### Second pass, same day: the trade surface — two crashes from one missing argument

The first fuzzer drives `transitionTurn`, which trades route **around** (`tradeMachine.js` is a second `transitionFn`), so that whole surface was structurally invisible to it. A second fuzzer interleaved real trade actions with turn play — adversarial actors included (wrong party accepting, cancelling someone else's trade, bankrupt parties, a clock that advances so trades genuinely expire).

**`settleAuction`'s signature is `(gameState, boardTiles, auction)`, and two of its four callers passed only two arguments.** The auction object landed in the `boardTiles` slot and `auction` arrived `undefined`, giving a hard `TypeError: resolveAuction: auction is required`:
- `applyBankruptcy` — **any player going bankrupt or forfeiting while they were the second-to-last live bidder in an auction crashed the action handler.**
- `settleGameEnd` — **a match ENDING while an auction was still live crashed**, which is exactly the case that function's own docstring says the branch exists to handle ("resolve any still-active auction next").

Neither path had a test. Both now do, and both were **verified to genuinely fail against the unfixed code** before being kept — reverted the fix, watched the two new tests go red, restored it. Textbook instance of this codebase's own recurring pattern: *a shared helper whose signature changed, with older callers left behind.* Worth re-reading as the thing to grep for first on the next sweep.

**One class of fuzzer finding was a false positive, and correcting the harness rather than the code was the right call.** The first trade run flagged dozens of "trade offers $926 but the offering side holds $907". That is this project's **confirmed** design, not a bug: trades are validated *lazily*, and `engine/trade.js`'s `validateOffer` runs again inside `acceptTrade` for **both** sides, so a stale offer is refused at accept rather than prevented from existing. The invariant was relaxed to what must actually hold (such a trade can never *settle*, which money-conservation already covers), with the reasoning recorded in the harness.

### Areas checked and found clean (so the next sweep can skip them)

- **Action surface**: every action type the frontend can send is handled — the 5 trade actions and the 3 phase-independent ones (`FORFEIT_MATCH`, `GAMBLE_RENT`, `USE_INVENTORY_CARD`) are correctly absent from `VALID_ACTIONS_BY_PHASE` because they dispatch ahead of it. No orphans either direction.
- **Stall points**: cross-checked `VALID_ACTIONS_BY_PHASE` × `TIMER_DURATIONS_SECONDS` × `buildDefaultAction`. Every blocking phase has a timer, every timed phase has a default action. Zero gaps.
- **Frontend mirror drift**: every `gameState.X` the frontend reads exists in the real domain factory. The three apparent misses were two regex false positives and three *comments* explicitly documenting that `gameState.currentTurn` does not exist — the correction holding, not drifting.
- **CSS custom properties**: the only other undefined `--et-*` token (`--et-inset-bg`, `PlayersPanel.module.css`) ships an explicit fallback, so it is deliberate, not the `--et-surface` bug repeated.

14 new/rewritten tests. **677/677 backend; ~1.12M fuzz steps clean across two harnesses and four seeds** (turn+inventory, then turn+trade+inventory+forfeit); frontend lint/build clean. Throwaway fuzzer and repro scripts deleted, same convention as every prior sweep. Not driven in a real logged-in match — `javascript_tool` auth/store injection is blocked by the permission classifier in this environment — so defect 1's fix is verified against the function definition and 15 correct sibling call sites rather than by a live round-trip; defect 7's CSS fix and the `.shell > *` regression fix from 2026-08-30 were both confirmed live via `getComputedStyle` against the real compiled class names.

## Balance measurement + jail revision + jackpot cap, 2026-09-01

User sent a full design-philosophy document and asked for a genuine opinion. Audited it against the code first (this project's standing reflex for design docs), then **measured** rather than argued: a new bot-driven harness (`backend/scripts/measure-balance.mjs`) drives the real `transitionTurn`/`tradeMachine` over the real seeded small board. 750 matches × 3 independent seeds, before and after.

**Method note that mattered: the fuzzer's/harness's own bugs came first.** Three had to be fixed before any number could be trusted — build *attempts* counted as builds (inflating the figure ~25,000×), an infinite loop retrying a failing trade response, and 12/30 matches silently abandoned mid-run in `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` (biasing the whole sample toward short games). The first run's headline numbers were all wrong for these reasons. Also worth recording: the *negative* result was the most useful one — the state machine fuzzing clean at ~357k steps is what redirected the bug sweep earlier the same day to the feature boundary, where every real defect actually was.

### What the measurement settled

- **Snowball is healthy and needed no work.** Round-10 net-worth leader wins **~54%** (58.6/50.0/53.8 across seeds), against a ~36% random baseline for 2-4 players. That sits mid-band in the design brief's own 45-60% target. Both the brief (which rated snowball "⚠️ Cao") and this project's own review section above (which argued at length that three of the five listed "comeback" mechanics are actually snowball *amplifiers*) were more worried than the data supports. The analysis of *which* mechanics favour the leader still stands; the conclusion that it needed fixing does not.
- **Monopoly is not dead, but it is not load-bearing either.** Monopolies form in 92-96% of matches — so the earlier "dead strategic goal" claim was wrong on formation, and that claim's own supporting bot was rigged toward it (it trades specifically to complete groups), which is stated here rather than buried. The robust figure is that **81.5% of all house-building happens with no monopoly at all**, even with that monopoly-seeking bot. Combined with the arithmetic — building one house on each lot of a group beats the unimproved ×2 group bonus by **2.2-2.6× in every single group**, and building is not monopoly-gated since the 2026-08-25 revision — monopoly is a by-product, not a goal. **Left unresolved deliberately: reversing it would undo a deliberate revision, so it needs the user's decision, not a unilateral fix.**
- **Free Parking's tail was the real problem, not its average.** Median payout $200, p90 exactly $600 — both inside the brief's 200-600 target — but the tail ran to **$1,300, with 9.6% of payouts above $600**.
- **A past decision was validated.** 86.9% of matches end by elimination, 13.1% by Final Phase net-worth. Raising `FINAL_PHASE_TRIGGER_ROUND` 20→45 (2026-08-25) did exactly what it was meant to: elimination is the normal ending, net-worth the rare safeguard. That ratio used to be inverted.
- **House scarcity is a live mechanic, not a theoretical one.** Supply bottoms out at 9.9/32 on average; **12% of matches exhaust houses completely.**

### What shipped

**1 — Free Parking jackpot cap ($600).** Set at the measured p90, in `feedFreeParkingJackpot`. Capped at the ceiling rather than tuned at the source: the inputs ($200/$100 tax tiles, the $50 fine) are load-bearing elsewhere in the economy, so trimming them to control one payout would move things that are currently balanced. Measured effect: payouts above $600 went **9.6% → 0.0%**, median **unchanged at $200** — exactly the surgical clip intended.

**2 — Jail no longer suspends a player's economy.** A failed escape used to call `advanceTurn` immediately, so a jailed player lost *all* asset management for up to four consecutive turns. That was never a rule: `inJail` gated exactly **one** thing in the whole backend (`startTurn`'s phase routing). A failed attempt now continues into `POST_ACTIONS` with the player still jailed — build/sell/mortgage/unmortgage/trade all work, movement still does not, and `startTurn` still routes them back to `JAIL_DECISION` next turn. No new stall point: `POST_ACTIONS` already has a timer and a `buildDefaultAction` (`END_TURN`).

**3 — The forced 3rd failure now moves the player.** Was `RELEASE_AND_ADVANCE` (pay $50, leave, lose the turn). Now `RELEASE_AND_MOVE`: pay, then move by **the roll that just failed** — the classic rule, and deliberately not a fresh roll, which would be two rolls in one turn and could raise a bonus-turn question this exit has no business creating (the failed roll is by definition not a double). The roll is carried on `pendingLiquidation.moveRoll` so a debtor who must liquidate first still gets the same move a solvent player gets — otherwise being poor would silently cost the exit move as well as the fine. `RELEASE_AND_ADVANCE` is retained as a branch for any `pendingLiquidation` already persisted in a live snapshot under the old behaviour.

**Deliberately NOT done: letting a player act on the turn they are *sent* to jail.** The brief asked for it. Entering jail still ends that turn immediately, and that is the balance lever: a real cost first, then a real shelter. Doing both would make jail pure upside late-game and strip the 3-doubles rule of its teeth.

**Frontend**: `GameControls.jsx` gained a jail notice for the genuinely new "jailed but the turn continues" state, which simply could not occur before.

### Re-measured after the change

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| Round-10 leader wins | 54.1% | **54.7%** (still mid-band) |
| Jackpot p99 / max | $1,100 / $1,300 | **$600 / $600** |
| Payouts > $600 | 9.6% | **0.0%** |
| Jackpot median | $200 | $200 |
| Jail turns (early/late) | 621 / 413 | 996 / 641 |
| Matches ending by elimination | 86.9% | 81.3% |
| Builds without monopoly | 81.5% | 81.3% |
| Houses fully exhausted | 12.0% | 15.2% |

The headline result is that **snowball did not move** (54.1% → 54.7%): the jail change buys back decision density and cuts jail's RNG swing without unbalancing anything. Jail occupancy rising ~60% is partly the bot's own policy change (it now pays the fine only when cash-rich, since fleeing is no longer automatically correct) — that is the point of the revision, but it means this figure measures "new rule + a player who values it", not the rule in isolation. Matches run slightly longer (30.7 → 32.3 rounds) and lean a little more on Final Phase (13.1% → 18.7%); houses exhaust slightly more often, as predicted, since jailed players now build.

**681/681 backend tests.** Six stale jail tests were rewritten to the new intended behaviour rather than loosened, and three new jackpot-cap tests added. `measure-balance.mjs` is **deliberately kept** (against this project's usual delete-the-throwaway convention) because before/after comparison is the whole point and rebuilding it would lose the baseline. Frontend lint/build clean.

**Bot data measures economic structure only** — identical bots cannot bluff, read intent, or squeeze liquidity, so none of this speaks to the design brief's upper skill tiers. That still needs human playtesting.

## Monopoly group bonus made load-bearing, 2026-09-02 — implemented, NOT yet balance-verified

User picked option (b) from the 2026-09-01 review: raise the monopoly bonus enough to compete with building.
