**The diagnosis sharpened the fix.** The problem was never the bonus's *size* — it was *where it applied*. `hasGroupBonus` returned false the moment `upgradeLevel !== 0`, so the group bonus and building were **mutually exclusive**: you got one or the other, and building won by 2.2-2.6× in every group. That shape is correct in classic Monopoly, where building is gated behind owning the group and the ×2 is a stepping stone toward houses. This project removed that gate on 2026-08-25, and with the gate gone the old shape quietly turned the bonus into a dead end. A bigger multiplier in the same wrong place would not have fixed it.

**Measured proof it was dead weight, and the measurement separated correlation from cause.** A new harness metric recomputes every rent payment with group data withheld and sums the difference. Across 250 matches: **0.3% of all rent paid ($1,992 of $666,281) was attributable to the group bonus.** This matters because a second new metric looked like the opposite result — players who ever completed a monopoly won **46%** of the time against **12.9%** for those who never did. That 3.6× gap is *owning lots of property* winning, not the bonus doing anything; the 0.3% is what tells the two apart. Recording both, and which one answers the question, is the point.

**The change**: a monopoly should MULTIPLY a developed group, not compete with developing it — you can hold both, and holding both should be the strongest position on the board. `GROUP_BONUS_MULTIPLIER` (2, unchanged in value) now applies at **every** upgrade level. Unimproved rent is untouched; what changed is that the bonus no longer evaporates when you build. `calculateRent.test.js` is fully green — the one stale test (which asserted the old unimproved-only rule) was rewritten, plus three new ones covering every level, the incomplete-group case, and the mortgaged-member case.

### VERIFIED 2026-09-02 — the collision resolved, suite green, measurement done

The concurrent session was the user themselves, and their Auction V2 work is complete. The suite was brought back to **690/690** with two fixes, both stale-test problems rather than behaviour problems:

- `socketServer.test.js`'s shared `buildAuctionGameState` fixture called `startAuction` without the new required `bankId`. **One line; it cleared every failure in that file (51/51).** Every one of those ~33 raw failures was this single missing argument, none about the behaviour each test asserted.
- Two `timers.test.js` integration tests asserted that an `AWAITING_PURCHASE` timeout opens a fee-charging auction (V1's behaviour). Rewritten to V2, and the rewrite pins a real design point rather than just following the code: **a timeout must never pick the paying branch on an absent player's behalf.** `buildDefaultAction` returns `DECLINE_PURCHASE` → the SKIP alias → free, `POST_ACTIONS`. Correct. Worth noting the gameplay consequence: under V1 an AFK player's land still reached the market via auction; under V2 it simply stays unowned.

**Measured after, with the bot taught to use `FORCE_AUCTION` (~45% of the time when it can afford the fee but not the property) so both V2 branches are exercised.** 250 matches × 3 seeds:

| | before (bonus unimproved-only) | after (bonus at every level) |
|---|---|---|
| **Rent attributable to the group bonus** | **0.3%** | **10.2%** (10.4 / 10.2 / 9.9) |
| Round-10 leader wins | 54.7% | **55.2%** (57.7 / 55.9 / 52.0) — still in band |
| Monopoly holders win | 46.0% | 47.6% (49.0 / 45.5 / 48.3) |
| Non-holders win | 12.9% | 14.0% |
| Match length | 30.7-32.3 rounds | 28.3 rounds |
| Ending by elimination | 81.3% | 93.8% |
| Jackpot payouts > $600 | 0.0% | 0.0% (cap holding) |
| Houses fully exhausted | 15.2% | 12.0% |

**The group bonus went from 0.3% to ~10% of all rent — a 34× increase — while snowball did not leave the target band.** That is the result the change was for: monopoly is now a load-bearing mechanic rather than a rounding error, and the 46%→49% win-rate correlation is now backed by a real causal channel instead of being pure confounding.

Two side effects worth watching rather than acting on: matches run ~2-4 rounds shorter and end by elimination far more often (81% → 94%), both consistent with more rent moving through the economy. If elimination-by-round-28 starts feeling abrupt in human play, the Final Phase trigger is the lever, not the bonus.

**Still not measured**: whether skilled players beat decent ones. Identical bots cannot answer that.

### (Historical) Why this was deferred at first

A concurrent session began rewriting the auction system mid-task (`auction.js`, `turnMachine.js`, timestamps 23:58-23:59 on 2026-09-01) — an **Auction V2 "Nhà Môi Giới"** feature: `DECLINE_PURCHASE` split into `SKIP_PURCHASE` (free, no auction) and `FORCE_AUCTION` (pay the fee, open an auction, 20% broker commission to the initiator via `calculateBrokerCommission`), with `DECLINE_PURCHASE` kept as a legacy alias now routing to the **no-auction** branch.

**97 backend tests are red, all theirs, one root cause**: `startAuction` gained a required `bankId` parameter for commission settlement and the shared `buildAuctionGameState` fixture was not updated (`TypeError: startAuction: bankId is required for V2 commission settlement`, 32 occurrences). Verified that **zero** failures touch rent, monopoly, jail, or the jackpot cap; `calculateRent.test.js` passes in full on its own.

Measuring the monopoly change right now would produce a **false result**, so it was not attempted. Their `DECLINE_PURCHASE` → skip change means declining no longer opens an auction at all, and this harness's bot declines often — so the simulated economy is now materially different from the 2026-09-01 baseline. Comparing new numbers against that baseline would attribute their auction rewrite to this rent change. The before/after has to wait until their work lands and the suite is green again.

**My own changes were checked and survived their writes** (`FREE_PARKING_JACKPOT_CAP`, `RELEASE_AND_MOVE`, the jail POST_ACTIONS branch all still present in `turnMachine.js`). No further writes were made to `turnMachine.js` or `auction.js` after the collision was detected — same posture as the 2026-08-27 Card Inventory collision.

**Detection note worth reusing**: the collision surfaced as *99 test failures that also appeared when my change was reverted*. Reverting to A/B the suite is what proved the failures were not mine — before that, the obvious (wrong) conclusion was that the rent change had broken 99 tests. `find . -newermt` plus "does this symbol exist that I never wrote" (`calculateBrokerCommission`, `handleSkipPurchase`) confirmed it.

## Auction fuzz sweep, 2026-09-02 — a permanent deadlock and a silent state corruption

Auction V2 and the group-bonus revision both touch money, and the last clean fuzz run (~1.12M steps) predated both. Re-fuzzing that surface found two real bugs in ~138k steps. **Neither was introduced by V2 or by the rent change — both were pre-existing and simply never fuzzed.**

### 1. Auction settlement could deadlock the match permanently

`settleAuction` asserted that the winner can always pay, reasoning that `placeBid` validates the balance and *"no money moves during FLASH_AUCTION_ACTIVE"*. **That second half has been false since trades became phase-independent (2026-08-18.)** A bidder can win at $1,400, hand $1,400 to someone else through a trade while the auction is still live, and arrive at settlement unable to pay.

The consequence was not a wrong number. The throw aborted the transition, so `pendingAuction` stayed set and the phase stayed `FLASH_AUCTION_ACTIVE`; `FOLD_AUCTION` threw again, and so did `AUCTION_TIMEOUT` — the phase's own timer default. **The match had no escape at all**, and a bidder could trigger it deliberately.

Fixed the way the identical bug in event cards was fixed on 2026-08-23: route the shortfall through `settlePendingDebts`, the same path every other debt uses, instead of asserting it away. `POST_ACTIONS` is set first so it stays the normal outcome, and the helper overrides it only if a debt genuinely forces `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` or ends the match — which also keeps `settleGameEnd`'s standings honest, since the charge is now really settled rather than asserted impossible. `now` threaded through `settleAuction`/`handleAuctionAction` and their four call sites. Verified end to end: the winner who dumped their cash goes **bankrupt**, the property stays unowned, and money conserves exactly.

### 2. Folding while leading still won you the auction

The deadlock fix turned the crash into a *wrong state*, which the fuzzer then caught as a new invariant violation: **a bankrupt player owning a property.** Root cause was one line — `foldBidder` removed the player from `activeBidders` and left `highestBidderId`/`currentBid` untouched. Two consequences:

- **A leader who folded still won and was charged.** `FOLD_AUCTION` was meaningless for the one player it matters most to.
- `applyBankruptcy` folds a bankrupt/forfeiting player out of a live auction, so an eliminated player could still be named winner and have `TRANSFER_PROPERTY` run **after** their own bankruptcy settlement had already redistributed everything they owned.

Folding now withdraws the folder's bids: the winner is read back off the append-only `bids` log restricted to bidders still in, falling back to the best surviving bid or to `FAILED` if none remains. `bids` itself is deliberately **not** pruned — Near-Miss eligibility is about who genuinely competed, and a player who bid and later folded still did.

**The whole suite stayed green through this fix (691/691), which is itself the finding**: no test covered folding while in the lead. Four new regression tests added at the engine level plus one integration test through a real `FORFEIT_MATCH`.

**695/695 backend tests. ~980k fuzz steps across three seeds, zero findings**, now covering FORCE_AUCTION, the Bank-funded broker commission, the jackpot cap, and rent doubling at every development level — none of which had ever been fuzzed. Frontend lint/build clean; throwaway fuzzer and repro deleted, `measure-balance.mjs` kept.

## Event deck: a recommendation of mine, measured and withdrawn — 2026-09-02

I had flagged the flat cyclic event deck (`drawCard` takes the head and pushes it to the tail) as a competitive-integrity problem: fixed order after the initial shuffle, therefore memorisable, therefore rewarding memory over judgement. I recommended fixing it and the user approved.

**Measured before touching it, and the measurement killed the recommendation.** Over 250 matches: **9.2 event draws per match** (max 27) against a 27-card deck — **0.34 deck cycles**, and only **0.4% of matches complete even one full cycle**. A player has to survive 27 draws before the order tells them anything, which happens in about one match in 250. The concern was real in principle and irrelevant in practice. **No change made.** The deck is shuffled per match at `room.controller.js:209`, which is where the meaningful randomness already is.

**The same number answers the design brief's §11 three-tier rarity proposal, better than the structural objection did.** At 9.2 draws from 27 cards, any given card already has roughly a one-in-three chance of appearing at all in a match — **every card is already effectively rare.** Layering rarity tiers on top would push Level 3 cards to something like one appearance per twenty matches, i.e. functionally nonexistent. The scarcity the brief wants is already supplied by draw volume; the mechanism it proposes would only make cards disappear.

## Market Information rail, 2026-09-02 (design brief §16)

Every figure §16 asks for already existed — it was scattered and invisible at the moment it mattered: the Free Parking jackpot lived in `GameControls`' bottom bar, building supply only inside `PropertyManager`'s build panel, and the live auction high only inside the `FlashAuction` modal. None of it was visible while deciding something else.

New `MarketInfo.jsx` on `GameView`'s existing shared status rail, beside the round counter, where table-wide readouts already live: houses `n/32`, hotels `n/12`, jackpot (when non-zero), and the current auction high (when one is running). The duplicate jackpot badge was removed from `GameControls` — one number, one source — and its now-orphaned `.jackpotBadge` CSS rule deleted rather than left as debris. `HOUSE_SUPPLY_TOTAL`/`HOTEL_SUPPLY_TOTAL` mirrored into `winConditionConstants.js` purely as the denominator; the live counts are always the server's own `gameState.houseSupply`/`hotelSupply`, never recomputed client-side.

**Held to §18's line deliberately: information, never decision.** Every chip is a plain fact a player could tally off the board by hand — showing it removes bookkeeping, not judgement. The low-supply marker is a neutral state colour that says "few left", never "so build now". The rail inherits its parent's `pointer-events: none` so it can never swallow a board drag.

Building supply is the figure that should actually change play now that it is visible: measured, ~50% of matches drop below 10 houses remaining and **12% exhaust them entirely**, so "can I still build later?" is a real question this answers — which is the §17 "economic warfare" mechanic becoming legible rather than a new one being added.

Verified live against the real compiled class names: every chip colour resolves to a real value (no repeat of the `--et-surface` undefined-token bug from 2026-09-01), and the orphaned `.jackpotBadge` rule is genuinely absent from the built CSS. 695/695 backend, frontend lint/build clean.

## Documentation drift sweep, 2026-09-02

Four behaviour changes landed in two days (jail economy, jackpot cap, group bonus, Auction V2) and none of the approved specs had been updated. That matters more here than on most projects: **this codebase's working discipline is "read the approved docs before writing anything", so a spec that lies actively misleads whoever picks up the next task** — several past findings in this very file came from exactly that.

Six real drifts, found by checking each claim against the code rather than eyeballing dates. Four were not merely stale but **actively wrong about current behaviour**:

1. **`GAME_STATE_MACHINE.md` + `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` state diagrams** — both still drew `JAIL_DECISION → END_TURN: stays in jail`. A failed escape has continued into `POST_ACTIONS` since 2026-09-01. Corrected in both, with a Mermaid `%%` comment carrying the reasoning rather than bloating the edge label.
2. **`ECONOMY_SPECIFICATION.md` §2** — described the group bonus as applying "before any houses are built", the exact rule reversed on 2026-09-02, and still tagged `[PROPOSED]` though it shipped long ago. Rewritten to `[CONFIRMED, REVISED]` with the measured before/after (0.3% → ~10.2% of all rent paid) as the justification.
3. **`GAME_STATE_MACHINE.md` §3** — stated that a folder keeps their bid and "the current high bidder may fold and still win". That is precisely the bug fixed on 2026-09-02. Corrected, keeping the bid-*history* rule (unchanged) clearly distinct from the bid-*withdrawal* rule (changed).
4. **`BOARD_SPECIFICATION.md` Flash Auction V1** — its "Implementation status" bullet still claimed the auction was "not yet wired into `turnMachine.js`" and "not yet reachable from a real turn". It has been wired since Phase 07 and has real frontend buttons. Corrected — and the one genuinely outstanding piece (the two-phase `FLASH_AUCTION_WINDOW_SECONDS`/`FLASH_AUCTION_BID_RESET_SECONDS` timer that `TimerManager` still cannot express, so the phase runs a flat 15s window) is called out explicitly so the correction does not overclaim in the other direction.

Plus two gaps rather than errors:

5. **`WEBSOCKET_API.md` documented neither `SKIP_PURCHASE` nor `FORCE_AUCTION`** — the two actions Auction V2 added. The wire contract listed only the legacy `DECLINE_PURCHASE`, still described with its V1 behaviour ("may charge a fee and start a Flash Auction"). All three rows are now documented, including that `DECLINE_PURCHASE` is an alias for the *free* branch, and that this silently changed what an `AWAITING_PURCHASE` **timeout** does — `buildDefaultAction` returns `DECLINE_PURCHASE`, so a timeout no longer charges an absent player a fee.
6. **A dangling citation.** `backend/src/engine/auction.js`'s own header cites `BOARD_SPECIFICATION.md §"Flash Auction V2"` — **a section that did not exist**. The code was the only description of the broker mechanic anywhere in the project. Written now: the three-way `BUY`/`SKIP`/`FORCE_AUCTION` decision, the Bank-funded 20% commission and *why* it is Bank-funded (the closed-economy invariant), the initiator's possibly-negative net position as intended risk rather than a flaw, the anti-abuse routing that always continues to `POST_ACTIONS`, and the changed timeout behaviour.

### Also verified, and found correct — a claim of mine that had never been checked

The balance harness leaves ~7 of 250 matches stuck in `AWAITING_EVENT_CHOICE`, which I had attributed to bot limitation without actually checking. Verified properly this pass: `buildEventChoiceDefaultAction` filters options to those the player can afford, prefers a riskless one, and supplies `probabilityRoll`/`dieFaceRoll`/`propertyId` as each option requires; `pickProtectionTarget` returns `undefined` when nothing is protectable, and the handler treats that as a revealed no-op rather than throwing. **The real game cannot stall there** — only the bot, which is not that careful. Claim confirmed rather than left as an assumption.

## Frontend / REST / auth / persistence sweep, 2026-09-02

Fourth sweep this session, aimed at ground the previous three never touched. Three areas came back clean; recording them so the next sweep can skip them rather than re-deriving.

**Frontend — clean.** Every `useGameStore` selector resolves to a real store field (zero orphans). No dead component files — every `.jsx` is imported somewhere. No hardcoded event-card or tile IDs left that could drift. The one surviving `window.confirm` is Lobby's kick guard, which is a deliberate confirmation on a destructive action, not leftover crudeness. An "unused CSS class" scan flagged `DiceRoll`'s `.face1-6` and `BoardTile`'s `.top/.bottom/.left/.right` — both false positives, accessed dynamically (`styles[\`face${value}\`]`, `styles[edge]`); worth noting so a future sweep does not "clean up" live rules.

**REST surface — clean.** The frontend calls exactly 11 paths; the backend exposes exactly 11 routes. Exact match, no orphans in either direction.

**Auth — clean.** Every `/api/v1` router is mounted behind `authMiddleware`; the only unauthenticated route is `/api/v1/health`, which is the documented carve-out. Socket.IO has its own auth middleware. `.env` is covered by `.gitignore` (`.env` + `.env.*`), and this project has no git repo anyway.

### One real latent hazard, analysis complete, decision NOT taken

`gameRepository.loadGameStateFromSupabase` returns `snapshot.state` — the raw JSONB blob — without routing it through `createGameState()`/`createPlayerGameState()`/`createProperty()`. Every field added to the domain after a match started is therefore `undefined` on that match, and the factory defaults never apply. **This has bitten twice already**: `lastDrawnEventCardSeq` going `NaN` (finding, 2026-08-23) and `player.inventory.includes()` throwing a raw `TypeError` (fixed 2026-09-01). Both were closed field-by-field with `?? 0` / `?? []` guards at each read site, which is the pattern this file keeps recommending — but the class itself is still open.

**The structural fix was investigated and is available.** Both factories were verified **exhaustive** against what the engine actually reads: every GameState field read anywhere in `stateMachine/`, `engine/`, `economy/` appears in `createGameState`'s returned object (the only two apparent misses, `lastAction` and `js`, are a test-only fixture field and a grep artifact), and the same holds for `createPlayerGameState` (its `playerId`/`isBank` are shorthand properties, which an earlier extraction missed). So routing the restore through the factories would drop nothing and would kill the whole bug class at both levels.

**Deliberately not shipped, and the reason is the tradeoff rather than the risk of dropped fields.** `createGameState` *validates* status/phase and `createPlayerGameState` validates the bank invariant, so after this change a corrupt snapshot would **throw on restore instead of loading into a degraded state** — a player who could previously rejoin into something odd would be unable to rejoin at all. That is arguably more correct and arguably worse UX, it touches every single match restore, and it cannot be verified end-to-end here (no Supabase auth in this environment, and the live dev database should not be experimented on). That combination — real blast radius, genuine behavioural fork, unverifiable locally — is this project's own stated bar for stopping and asking rather than deciding unilaterally.

## Player-facing notice panels replacing raw error dumps, 2026-09-02

User screenshot showed this on screen, in a Vietnamese game, during normal play:

```
MONOPOLY_PROTECTED: handleHostileBuyout: property
'f303cf51-b811-4072-bd56-ca25044206d4' belongs to a completed
color-group monopoly, protected from hostile buyout
```

A function name and a raw database UUID, in English. **Seven UI surfaces each rendered `{lastError.errorCode}: {lastError.message}` independently**, and that `message` is an internal developer string that was never meant to leave the server.

### The root cause was a button that should never have been clickable

The rejection only happened because `PropertyActionDrawer`'s "Cường đoạt" button checked **only** `upgradeLevel > 0` (HOUSE_PROTECTED) and knew nothing about MONOPOLY_PROTECTED. `GameControls` had the full precondition set all along. Two copies of the same rules, one incomplete — **exactly the drift `buildRules.js` was created to stop, recurring**.

Fixed the same way that was: a new shared `buyoutRules.js` (`buyoutTarget`, `buyoutBlockReason`) mirroring `handleHostileBuyout`, now the single source for both surfaces. It preserves the server's own precedence — MONOPOLY_PROTECTED is tested before HOUSE_PROTECTED, so a lot that is both fully setted and built reports the reason the server would actually give. The drawer's button is now disabled with the real reason shown beneath it, so this particular rejection can no longer be triggered at all.

### The notice system

`actionErrors.js` maps **every code in the real taxonomy** to a Vietnamese title and explanation. Codes were collected from the actual `errorCode:` emissions in `socketServer.js` plus every `reason` passed to each of the seven error classes — not guessed. Each entry says what happened *and* what to do instead; several teach a rule the UI had no other place to explain (why a completed monopoly is buyout-proof, why building must be even, why a freshly bought lot cannot be built on yet, that the 32 houses are shared table-wide). An unknown code falls back to a generic entry — a missing code is a gap to fill in that table, never a reason to show a player a stack-trace fragment.

`ActionNotice.jsx` renders it, with three tones that carry meaning rather than decoration:

- `rule` (gold) — you asked for something the rules don't allow. The common case, and deliberately **not** red: the old single red line trained players to read every ordinary refusal as something broken.
- `timing` (neutral) — legal move, wrong moment. Usually resolves itself.
- `fault` (red) — the only tone that gets alarm colouring.

A `compact` variant (title only) is used where the surface is already dense and time-pressured — the auction modal and the liquidation sidebar — so the explanation never pushes the controls the player needs out of reach.

Shared rather than fixed seven times locally, for the same reason `buildRules`/`buyoutRules` exist: a new error code now needs one entry and appears correctly everywhere. All eight raw renders are gone (`GameControls`, `PropertyManager`, `TradeWindow`, `LiquidationPanel`, `RentRiskChoice`, `EventCardModal`, `FlashAuction`, `ForfeitButton`), and the eight now-orphaned `.error`/`.errorLine` CSS rules were deleted rather than left as debris. `Lobby.module.css`'s own `.error` was checked and left alone — it is still live and belongs to a different screen.

Verified live against the real compiled class names: all three tones resolve to real colours inside the real `.shell` palette (no repeat of the `--et-surface` undefined-token bug), and no orphaned in-game error rule survives in the built CSS. Frontend lint/build clean.

### Follow-up same day: the even-build message was too terse

User pointed at "Phải xây đều — xây ô thấp nhất của bạn trong nhóm trước" on screen and asked what it meant. Two problems: "thấp nhất" (lowest) is ambiguous (lowest price? position?), and it named no specific lot. `buildBlockReason` now names the exact lot(s) that are behind and their house count ("Hãy xây ở Hùng Vương, Hậu Giang (đang chưa có nhà) cho bằng ô này trước"), and `actionErrors.js`'s `UNEVEN_BUILD`/`UNEVEN_SELL` bodies were expanded to actually explain the rule rather than just state it. General note: a rules message that only restates the rule name is barely better than the raw error code — say what the player should click next.

### Same day: the frontend still showed HALF the real rent on monopolised tiles

User asked whether owning the full colour group doubles rent. Yes — and answering it surfaced that the 2026-09-02 backend change (bonus applies at every level, not just unimproved) was never propagated to the frontend. Three surfaces still assumed the old rule:

- **`rentPreview.js` / the on-board tile badge** — this one is the real bug. Its own header says the number is "số tiền khi giẫm vào phải trả" (what you pay when you land here). It applied no bonus at all, so a monopolised tile showed exactly half what the server charges. Now folds in the ×2 via a new `ownsFullGroup` helper (the exact `hasGroupBonus` condition minus the removed upgradeLevel check) and labels it `$X (×2 độc quyền)`.
- **`MyPortfolio.jsx` `rowHasBonus`** — still gated on `property.upgradeLevel === 0`, and its comments explicitly reasoned that "the ×2 only ever helps a still-unimproved member" — reasoning the backend change inverted. Now `complete && noneMortgaged`, per-group; the row rent figure itself is doubled, the ×2 badge stays as the explanation.
- **`PropertyManager.jsx` rent table** — left as a static title-deed-style reference (that's a legitimate choice), but gained a one-line `×2 ở mọi mức nếu bạn giữ trọn bộ nhóm màu` caption so it doesn't silently mislead.

Verified: a 24-case sweep (every upgrade level × full-group × other-member-mortgaged) confirms the frontend preview now equals `calculateRent` exactly. Lint/build clean.

**Pattern worth noting: a backend rule change needs a frontend grep even when the frontend "only displays" — three display sites had hardcoded the old rule's shape, and one of them was actively lying about the single biggest number in the game.**

## Even-build rule: measured, and it does much less than it looks like — 2026-09-02

User asked whether to keep the even-build rule (in a colour group, raise every lot you own to the same level before pushing one higher) and whether it matters. A/B measured with a throwaway harness (`MEASURE_NO_EVEN_BUILD` env hatch, since removed): sensible bot and a deliberate "rush one monopoly lot to a hotel" bot, rule on vs off, 250 matches × 3 seeds.

| | rule ON | rule OFF |
|---|---|---|
| Round-10 leader wins | ~55% | ~55% |
| **Highest single rent charged (avg)** | **~$690** | **~$709** |
| Successful builds / match (sensible bot) | ~34.5 | ~35.4 |
| UNEVEN_BUILD blocks / match (sensible) | ~1.9 | 0 |
| UNEVEN_BUILD blocks / match (rusher) | **~19** | 0 |
| Houses fully exhausted | ~13.6% | ~10.5% |

**The rule prevents no exploit.** The single largest rent charged is unchanged whether it exists or not (~$700 both ways). It was expected to stop "dump everything into one monopoly lot for a huge ×2 hotel rent" — but that play isn't good in this system: building is not monopoly-gated (so single-lot rushing is possible either way), and the ×2 bonus applies at every level regardless of evenness, so spreading across three lots that opponents can each land on out-earns hotelling one. The rusher bot with the rule OFF just builds *more* (36.6/match) and games end *sooner*.

**What it does do, small:** forces spreading, which uses ~3 percentage points more houses, so the §17 house-scarcity / economic-warfare angle bites slightly harder with it on. And it wastes ~19 turns/match for a bot that fights it — a player who doesn't understand the rule hits that wall hard (the user asked what it meant, which is the tell).

**Recommendation given: lean toward removing it.** Its stated purpose (rent-rush prevention) is empty in this ruleset, it's a lone holdover from the monopoly-gated-building rule this game already dropped, and "easy to learn" is a stated design goal. The only real cost of removing is the mild house-scarcity dampening — kept if the user considers house-cornering a signature mechanic worth protecting. Left as the user's call; no code change made.

Also fixed a stale comment in `handleBuildHouse` that still claimed the group bonus "needs an unimproved set" and "a built-up property never gets the ×2" — both reversed on 2026-09-02.

### Follow-up: even-build rule removed — 2026-09-02

User: "vậy bỏ đi". Removed the even-build rule outright.

**Backend** — `turnMachine.js` `handleBuildHouse`: deleted the `minLevelInGroup` / `UNEVEN_BUILD` throw block (kept `GROUP_MORTGAGED`, `INSUFFICIENT_SUPPLY`, `MAX_UPGRADE_LEVEL`, `RECENTLY_ACQUIRED`). The `ownedGroupHoldingsFor` helper stays — even-sell and group-mortgage still use it. Stale comments updated in four places (header reason-code list, `handleBuildHouse` docstring, `ownedGroupHoldingsFor` docstring, the even-sell comment at line ~149).

**Frontend** — `buildRules.js` `buildBlockReason`: matching branch removed. `actionErrors.js`: `UNEVEN_BUILD` dictionary entry removed (placeholder comment left), `UNEVEN_SELL` body reworded, header comment fixed.

**Tests** — 3 even-build tests in `turnMachine.test.js` (~lines 1846–1908) rewritten from "asserts `UNEVEN_BUILD`" to "asserts uneven building is now allowed". Backend suite **694 pass / 0 fail**. Frontend `lint` + `build` clean.

**Confirmation fuzz** — 600 full matches (sensible + rusher bots), zero unexpected errors, invariants intact. Max within-group house gap now reaches 5 (was pinned at 0 by the rule — confirms removal is live). Highest single rent still ~$700 avg / ~$1900 absolute (a hotel on the top darkblue lot with the ×2 group bonus — expected, not runaway). Snowball ~54%, house-exhaustion 2–10% — all unchanged from baseline.

**Docs** — `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §12 preconditions cell, `WEBSOCKET_API.md` (`BUILD_HOUSE` row, `SELL_HOUSE` row, `UNEVEN_BUILD` struck through like `INCOMPLETE_GROUP`, `UNEVEN_SELL` row) all updated. `MEASURE_NO_EVEN_BUILD` env hatch and `scripts/measure-evenbuild.mjs` both deleted.

**Left standing, flagged to user:** `UNEVEN_SELL` (even-sell during liquidation) is now the only "even" constraint in the game — no matching build-side rule. Kept deliberately: it governs refund order during forced liquidation (a player shouldn't be able to strip one lot bare while its group-mates stay improved), which is a distinct concern from build pacing. Not touched; user can revisit if they want full symmetry.

### Follow-up: MORTGAGE precondition reverted to per-property — 2026-09-02

User: "chỉ cần ô đó hết nhà thì việc cầm cố ô đất đó hay không là tùy vào người chơi". Reverted the 2026-08-18 group-wide mortgage check.

**The rule now:** `MORTGAGE` only requires the *target property itself* to have `upgradeLevel === 0`. Houses on its colour-group-mates are irrelevant. Error code renamed `GROUP_HAS_HOUSES` → `PROPERTY_HAS_HOUSES` (pure passthrough via `errorCodeFor`, so the rename only touched the throw site + frontend dictionary + docs + 2 tests).

**Why it's safe** — mortgaging *any* member of a group already forfeits the `×2` monopoly bonus on the whole group until it's unmortgaged (`calculateRent.js`'s `hasGroupBonus` requires every member unmortgaged, at every level since the 2026-09-02 bonus revision). So a player who mortgages one bare lot beside their own built lots is paying a real, immediate price. Forcing them to tear down houses first added friction without protecting anything. `handleBuildHouse`'s `GROUP_MORTGAGED` check still fires — you can't build *up* a group while any member is mortgaged — so the interaction stays coherent.

**Changed:**
- `turnMachine.js` `handleMortgage` — group scan replaced with `property.upgradeLevel > MIN_UPGRADE_LEVEL`. `ownedGroupHoldingsFor` is no longer called here (still used by `handleSellHouse` even-sell + `handleBuildHouse` GROUP_MORTGAGED); its docstring + the file's SCOPE comment updated.
- `timers.js` `buildLiquidationDefaultAction` — the auto-liquidation candidate builder now offers `MORTGAGE` on any house-free lot (strictly more options during forced liquidation, each genuinely valid).
- Frontend: `PropertyManager.jsx` `mortgageReason()` per-property; `LiquidationPanel.jsx` `canMortgage` was already per-property (comment corrected); `actionErrors.js` `PROPERTY_HAS_HOUSES` entry reworded to explain the ×2-bonus cost.
- Tests: `turnMachine.test.js` — the "a different lot in the group blocks the mortgage" test flipped to "a house-free lot is mortgageable regardless of group-mates"; `timers.test.js` comments. **Backend 694 pass / 0 fail.** Frontend lint + build clean.
- Confirmation fuzz: 600 matches / ~220k steps / **0 findings**, with 535 mortgages deliberately placed on a bare lot beside a built group-mate — no invariant broke, and a mortgaged-lot-with-houses state remains impossible (you still can't mortgage a lot that itself has houses).
- Docs: `GAME_DESIGN_SPEC.md` §12 mortgage row (full history: per-property → 08-18 group-wide → 08-25 owner-scoped → 09-02 per-property), `WEBSOCKET_API.md` (`MORTGAGE`/`UNMORTGAGE` rows, `PROPERTY_HAS_HOUSES` error row).



## Full-system bug sweep — 2026-09-02 (after the even-build / mortgage rule changes)

User: "quét lại toàn hệ thống xem còn vấn đề lỗi gì không". Four real defects found and fixed; several suspected ones checked and cleared.

### What was checked and found CLEAN
- **Error-code coverage**: all 42 `reason` codes the server can throw are present in `actionErrors.js` (mechanical diff, not eyeballed). The 4 dictionary entries with no matching throw are `PHASE_MISMATCH`/`MALFORMED_PAYLOAD`/`INTERNAL_ERROR` (synthesized by `errorCodeFor`, never literal) and the deliberately-kept struck-through `INCOMPLETE_GROUP`.
- **Persistence factory exhaustiveness**: mechanically diffed every `gameState.*` / `player.*` field the engine reads against what `createGameState`/`createPlayerGameState` set. Three apparent misses (`phase`, `player.balance`, `js`) all turned out to be comment text or a differently-shaped assignment. Factories are exhaustive.
- **Phase coverage**: all 9 `VALID_ACTIONS_BY_PHASE` phases have frontend handling (`FLASH_AUCTION_ACTIVE` is gated with `!==`, which an early grep missed).
- **TradeWindow**: mirrors `lockedPropertyIds`/`lockedMoneyFor` exactly and caps money entry at the *available* (locked-adjusted) balance, not the raw one. Counter-depth gated. No drift.
- **FlashAuction**: bid buttons are always `currentBid + increment` and disabled on balance. No drift.
- **EventCardModal**: its protectable-property picker mirrors `protectablePropertiesFor` exactly.
- **Auction fee formula**: `PropertyActionDrawer`'s inlined `Math.ceil(Math.max(20, Math.min(80, price * 0.05)))` matches `calculateAuctionFee` byte-for-byte.
- **Newly-reachable state**: per-property mortgage made "a group with houses AND a mortgaged member" possible for the first time. Both `ownsFullGroup` (`rentPreview.js`) and `rowHasBonus` (`MyPortfolio.jsx`) already check `mortgaged`, so the ×2 display stays correct.

### Bug 1 — sell button blocked by a RIVAL's houses (severity: high)
`PropertyManager.jsx` computed `maxLevelInGroup` across the **whole colour group regardless of owner**, while `handleSellHouse` has scanned only the acting player's own holdings since 2026-08-25 (`ownedGroupHoldingsFor`). Since building stopped requiring the full group, split groups are normal — so a rival's taller building in your group disabled your own "Bán Nhà" button for a sale the server would have allowed.

**Worst case is severe**: `sellReason()` uses `phaseReason(true)`, so it is live during `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` — a player could be locked out of selling their own houses to raise cash and pushed toward an avoidable bankruptcy. (The 45s auto-liquidation fallback in `timers.js` was always correct, so the match could not permanently stall; the player just lost the choice.)

Fixed by reusing `buildRules.js`'s already-correct `ownedGroupHoldingsFor` — the same mirror the build side went through in 2026-08-25. Verified by running the real helper on a 3-lot split group: old logic → blocked, new logic → allowed, matching the server.

**This is the 4th time the same two-copies-of-a-rule pattern has produced a bug** (`buildRules.js`, `buyoutRules.js`, the CardInventory signature, now this). The 2026-08-25 fix explicitly said "`buildRules.js` (the client mirror) got the identical treatment" — but that file only covers BUILD, and PropertyManager's own sell-side derivation was never revisited.

### Bug 2 — liquidation panel offered sales the server rejects (severity: medium)
`LiquidationPanel.jsx`'s `canSell` checked mortgaged / has-houses / tile-type but **skipped the even-sell rule entirely**, so it offered a "Bán Nhà" button the server answers with `UNEVEN_SELL` — during the one phase where a wasted click costs the most. Refactored `canSell`/`canMortgage` into `sellBlockReason`/`mortgageBlockReason` (the `buildRules.js` idiom, returning the reason rather than a boolean) so the tooltip states the real rule instead of re-deriving a guess. Even-sell scoped to the player's own lots, matching the server.

### Bug 3 — jail fine button removed a legal move (severity: medium)
`GameControls.jsx` hard-disabled "Nộp Phạt" whenever `currentBalance < JAIL_FINE`. The server does **not** reject a cash-short fine: `payJailFineOrLiquidate` routes it through `checkSolvency` into `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` so the player can sell/mortgage their way to the $50. A jailed player rich in property but short on cash was forced to keep rolling for doubles instead of buying their way out — exactly the choice the jail design is built around.

Now enabled whenever the player has anything to liquidate, with a tooltip warning that liquidation will follow. Deliberately **not** a copy of `checkSolvency`'s arithmetic — that would be a fifth rule mirror to drift; only the genuinely hopeless case (no cash, nothing to liquidate, where the server's third branch is immediate bankruptcy) stays disabled so a stray click cannot self-bankrupt.

### Bug 4 — auction-fee rejection reported the wrong reason (severity: low)
`handleForceAuction` threw `InvalidTurnActionError` when the initiator could not afford the fee. `errorCodeFor()` maps that class to `PHASE_MISMATCH`, so the player was shown **"Chưa tới bước này — không phải ở giai đoạn hiện tại của lượt"** when the actual problem was money. The message string already said `INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE`; only the class was wrong. `WEBSOCKET_API.md` had documented `INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE` all along — the code, not the doc, was out of contract.

Reachable only via a race (the drawer mirrors the fee formula correctly and disables the button), but the wrong class made that race unreadable when it did happen. Now throws `InvalidPropertyActionError('INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE', …)`.

**Why it hid for so long, and the general lesson:** its test was a bare `assert.throws(() => …)` with no predicate. A throws-anything assertion cannot catch a wrong-reason bug. Tightened to assert class + reason. Audited every other `InvalidTurnActionError` throw in the codebase — the only two left are genuine phase guards (`throw new InvalidTurnActionError(gameState.phase, action.type)`), so this was the sole instance. **52 bare `assert.throws()` calls remain across the suite**; most are domain factories where the class carries no player-facing meaning, but they are a known blind spot for exactly this defect class.

### Result
Backend **694 pass / 0 fail**. Frontend lint + build clean. No engine/state-machine invariant defects found this pass — the three highest-severity findings were all frontend gating drifting from server rules, which remains this codebase's dominant bug shape.

### Second sweep pass, same day — a reproducible ROOM DEADLOCK (bug 5)

Asked again for anything left. Went after the worst available failure mode first: a timeout default action the state machine then rejects. `handleTurnTimeout` logs it and returns with the timer already cleared, so the room is left in a phase **with no timer covering it** — the code's own comment names this as the reason it logs loudly. Audited all 9 phases' defaults; the payload-dependent ones (`LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED`, `AWAITING_EVENT_CHOICE`) are the only ones that can be built wrong, and one of them could be.

**`C11_CU_DANH_LIEU` could freeze a room permanently.** The card is eligibility-gated at `currentBalance >= 50` on draw and has exactly **one** option, costing $50. But trades — and `GAMBLE_RENT` and `USE_INVENTORY_CARD` — are deliberately phase-independent, so the drawer's cash can fall below $50 *after* the draw, while the card is still pending. From there:

- `AWAITING_EVENT_CHOICE` permits exactly one action, `MAKE_EVENT_CHOICE`, and every call threw `INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE` — **the player had no legal move at all**;
- `buildEventChoiceDefaultAction` fell back to the full option list when nothing was affordable (`pool = affordable.length > 0 ? affordable : options`), so the timeout synthesized that same doomed choice;
- `handleTurnTimeout` caught the rejection, logged, returned — timer gone, phase unchanged. **Frozen for every player in the room**, not just the drawer, and unrecoverable if they went AFK. (A trade could still rescue them, since trades bypass the phase — but that requires a willing opponent and an attentive player.)

Reproduced end to end before fixing (balance $500 → default accepted → `POST_ACTIONS`; balance $40 → `EventChoiceError/INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE`, default inapplicable), then re-run after fixing to confirm both paths reach `POST_ACTIONS`.

**Same root shape as the `settleAuction` deadlock fixed 2026-09-01**: a guard written when money could not move during that phase, left unrevisited after trades became phase-independent. Worth treating as a standing review question — *"what happens to this invariant if the player's balance changes mid-phase?"* — rather than a one-off.

**Fix**: `handleEventChoice` now fizzles a card **nobody can afford** into a no-op straight to `POST_ACTIONS` — the same "an ineligible draw is an honest *you drew this but weren't eligible* no-op" semantics `eligibility` already documents, applied to affordability lost after the draw. Deliberately narrow: choosing an unaffordable option **while an affordable one exists** is still real invalid input and still rejected with `INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE`. Both halves have tests; `timers.js`'s fallback keeps the default well-formed and now has a comment explaining why both halves are needed.

The pre-existing test `MAKE_EVENT_CHOICE (C11): OPT_GAMBLE is rejected when the player cannot afford the $50 stake` was **asserting the deadlock** — it passed for weeks precisely because the frozen behaviour was the tested behaviour. Rewritten to assert the fizzle.

### Also cleared this pass (no defect)
`buildDefaultAction`'s other 8 phases; `buildLiquidationDefaultAction` (its candidate list got *strictly larger* with the per-property mortgage change, so it is safer than before, and it was already correctly owner-scoped); the `affordable` filter's field (`o.validation?.amount`) genuinely matches what `resolveChoice` checks; only 1 of 6 CHOICE cards has a cost gate on every option.

### ⚠ Concurrent session: 6 auction tests are RED and it is not from this work
Mid-sweep, the parallel session changed `handleForceAuction`'s bidder eligibility in `turnMachine.js`:

```
- // Every real, non-bankrupt player is eligible to bid — including the initiator (unchanged from V1).
- const eligibleBidders = afterFee.players.filter((p) => !p.isBank && !p.bankrupt).map((p) => p.id);
+ // Every real, non-bankrupt player EXCEPT the initiator is eligible to bid
+ // (V2 Broker rule: The auction host cannot bid on their own auction).
+ const eligibleBidders = afterFee.players.filter((p) => !p.isBank && !p.bankrupt && p.id !== player.id).map((p) => p.id);
```

A real design change (the host can no longer bid on their own auction), but its 6 tests still asserted the initiator is an active bidder, so they failed — the first failure's diff was literally the initiator missing from `activeBidders`. Reported rather than silently "fixed", since guessing at another session's in-flight intent is how two people overwrite each other in a repo with no git.

**Resolved on the user's instruction ("cập nhật đi") — the tests now match the new rule. Suite back to 696/696.**

The interesting part was that the fix was *not* mechanical. Excluding the host means **every auction has one fewer bidder than there are players**, so two test helpers no longer produced the situations their names promised: the 2-player state gave a solo bidder, and the 3-player state gave two — leaving nothing to exercise "a bidder leaves and more than one still remains". Renamed the helpers for how many players can actually **bid** (`soloBidderAuctionState` / `twoBidderAuctionState` / `threeBidderAuctionState`) and added a fourth player (Dave) so the three-bidder case still exists. Test-by-test:

- `FORCE_AUCTION` happy path — `activeBidders` is now `['gp-bob']`, host excluded.
- `FOLD_AUCTION` "more than one remains" and `FORFEIT_MATCH` "other bidders remain" — moved up to the new 4-player helper; both now end on `['gp-bob', 'gp-dave']`.
- `FOLD_AUCTION` "folds down to the last bidder" and the fold-triggered Near-Miss test — each lost a step, because Alice can no longer fold out of an auction she was never in; Carol's fold is now the one that resolves it. Confirms in passing that **a folded bidder still collects Near-Miss** (it is computed from the `bids` log, not from who is still standing) — noted inline, since that is easy to break later.
- `AUCTION_TIMEOUT` Near-Miss test — one-line assertion change; every bid in it was already Bob's or Carol's, so all the money assertions held unchanged.

Also propagated beyond the tests, which the change had not yet reached: `GAME_STATE_MACHINE.md` §Flash Auction still said bids come from "any player… including the player who just declined", and `BOARD_SPECIFICATION.md`'s Broker section did not state the rule at all — both corrected, including the consequence that **at a two-player table forcing an auction leaves exactly one eligible bidder**. `PropertyActionDrawer`'s "Mở sàn" tooltip now warns the host they will not be able to bid *before* they pay the fee — material information that was missing. (`FlashAuction.jsx` already handled it: the parallel session had added an `iAmInitiator` branch, so the frontend was correct, just undocumented.)

### Measured: what the V2 Broker rule actually costs and buys — 2026-09-02

A/B'd the host-cannot-bid rule against V1 (host bids like anyone else). Both arms were **frozen snapshots of `backend/src` copied into a scratch dir**, one line patched in the copy — the live tree was never touched, so the parallel session's edits could not perturb a run. 150 matches per cell, 2/3/4 players, identical bot policy in both arms.

**Two harness bugs had to be fixed before any number meant anything**, both worth remembering:
1. The bots valued a lot at 0.85 × price, but a Flash Auction **opens at 100% of base price** and every bid must strictly exceed the standing one — so no bot could ever legally bid and *both* arms reported a meaningless 100% failure rate. Valuation now starts at the price itself (what you would pay landing on it) and scales with real group synergy.
2. The bots folded whenever they did not want to raise — including while **leading**. Since the 2026-09-01 revision, folding *withdraws the folder's own bids*, so a leading bidder who folds throws away the lot they had already won. Fixed to let the clock run (`AUCTION_TIMEOUT`) instead, which is how a real auction ends.

#### The exploit the rule closes: host shill-bidding
A host who can afford the lot forces an auction anyway, then bids it up to just under the best genuine bidder's ceiling — inflating the winner's payment and therefore their own 20% commission. Per auction:

| | 2p V1 | 2p V2 | 3p V1 | 3p V2 | 4p V1 | 4p V2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Host net (commission − fee) | −$0.8 | **−$5.2** | +$10.9 | **+$6.6** | +$22.1 | **+$16.3** |
| Winning bid premium over base | 8.9% | **5.0%** | 10.8% | **8.3%** | 13.1% | **9.9%** |
| Host wins their own auction | 12.4% | **0%** | 14.4% | **0%** | 15.1% | **0%** |

Shill-bidding was worth a consistent **+$4 to +$6 per auction** and inflated settling prices by **~3 percentage points**. Real, repeatable at every table size, and the rule removes it completely.

#### What it costs ordinary play: nearly nothing
With bots that force auctions only on lots they cannot afford themselves, **the 2-player arms are numerically identical** (12.1% settled, host net −$14.4, both). The reason is structural: the trigger for forcing is "I cannot afford this", and the opening bid is the full base price — so a host who forces can never afford to bid either. At 3p the host's net moves −$1.8 → −$4.9; at 4p +$10.1 → +$11.2. No meaningful cost.

**Verdict: keep the rule.** It removes a consistent edge and costs essentially nothing.

#### Separate, pre-existing problem this uncovered: FORCE_AUCTION is a bad move at 2 players
Normal play, 2 players: **87.9% of forced auctions FAIL** (fee burned, nobody gets the lot), and **100% of the ones that settle go to the opponent at the minimum raise (base + $10)**. Average host result **−$14.4 per auction**. Not caused by the Broker rule — identical in both arms — but by three things compounding:
- the auction **opens at 100% of base price**, so no lot can ever be won below list;
- with one opponent there is **no competition**, so the price never moves past the minimum raise;
- that single bidder often cannot afford base+10, so the auction simply fails and the fee is lost.

Forcing an auction heads-up is therefore close to a pure $20 donation. Options if this is worth changing: reopen below base price (V0 used 50%), scale or refund the fee when only one bidder is eligible, or disable `FORCE_AUCTION` at a 2-player table. **No change made — this is a design decision.**

#### UX gap found while fixing the harness — FIXED
`foldBidder` withdraws the folder's own bids, so **a sole leading bidder who folds forfeits a lot they had already won** and the auction resolves FAILED. At a 2-player table the opponent is *always* the sole bidder (the Broker rule bars the host), so this is the ordinary shape of a heads-up auction rather than a corner case — and `FlashAuction.jsx`'s "Bỏ cuộc (Fold)" button offered it with no warning and no disabled state. The engine is behaving as designed; the button was not telling the truth about the consequence.

Fixed with a two-step confirm on the fold button, armed only when folding actually costs something:
- **not leading** → unchanged, one click, no warning (folding there is free);
- **leading, others still active** → warns the bid will be *withdrawn* and the lead passes to the best surviving bid (wording checked against `auction.test.js`'s "a leader who folds withdraws their bid and the auction falls back to the best surviving one" — the auction *continues*, so the copy deliberately does not claim the lot changes hands);
- **leading and the sole bidder** → warns the auction will FAIL with nobody getting the property, and points out that simply waiting for the timer wins it.

The confirmation disarms on any `stateVersion`/`lastError` change, since being outbid or left alone invalidates the warning it is showing. **Deliberately a confirm rather than a disabled button** — folding while ahead is a legal action, and disabling it would repeat the jail-fine bug fixed earlier the same day, where the UI deleted a move the server allows.

*Caveat on all of the above: bot policy, not human play. The per-auction economics are the meaningful output; "auctions per match" is an artifact of an arbitrary 0.6 trigger probability and predicts nothing about real frequency.*

### Why FORCE_AUCTION is dead heads-up, and the one lever that fixes it — 2026-09-02

Followed up "is there a way to balance this at 2 players". Diagnosed the failures rather than guessing at a fix.

**Every single heads-up failure is "the sole bidder could not afford it" — never "they didn't want it".** 100.0% of failures, at every bidder-reserve policy tested. The failure profile is the giveaway: average base price **$268**, sole bidder's cash **$333**, sole bidder's own valuation of the lot **$339**. They value it *above* list and still cannot pay, because the host only forces an auction when the host themselves is cash-short — and at a 2-player table both players hit that squeeze in the same phase of the game.

Checked this is not a bot artifact by varying how much cash the bidder insists on keeping:

| bidder keeps in reserve | $250 | $100 | $0 |
|---|---|---|---|
| auctions that settle | 11.5% | 20.4% | 28.6% |
| failures that are "can't afford" | 100% | 100% | 100% |

Even a reckless bidder holding nothing back fails 71% of the time. The diagnosis is structural, not a policy artifact.

**The obvious fix does not work.** Opening below base price does raise the settle rate (at 50%: 39% → 75% depending on reserve) — but the broker commission is a percentage of that same, now-lower price, while the fee is a flat $20. The host's expected value per auction:

| | settle rate | avg winning bid | commission | fee | **host EV** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| today (open at 100%) | 11.5% | $234 | $46.8 | $20 | **−$14.6** |
| open at 50%, fee kept | 39.1% | ~$143 | $28.6 | $20 | **−$8.8** |
| open at 100%, **fee waived** | 11.5% | $234 | $46.8 | $0 | **+$5.4** |

Lowering the opening bid trades a rare large commission for a frequent small one and never escapes the flat fee — it also hands the opponent lots at half price, which is worse for the host than the problem being solved. **The fee, not the opening bid, is what makes heads-up forcing a losing move.**

**Recommendation: waive the auction fee when fewer than 2 players are eligible to bid.** Rationale rather than a number: the fee buys brokerage of a *contest*, and with the Broker rule excluding the host, a 2-player table structurally has exactly one bidder — there is no contest to broker, so there is nothing to charge for. It leaves the fee doing its documented anti-abuse job at 3+ players, where competition genuinely exists, instead of repealing it globally.

Deliberately **not** recommending a refund-on-FAILED rule, which fixes the same arithmetic: `BOARD_SPECIFICATION.md` explicitly keeps the fee unrefunded to prevent "fee-free market research", and a global refund would repeal that. The eligible-bidder condition is narrower and does not touch it.

What heads-up forcing becomes under the waiver: *"I cannot afford this — take it at list price now, or nobody gets it."* Costless to attempt, but not free of consequence: the opponent may take a lot they wanted. Rough exchange when they do — opponent pays $277 cash for something they value at $339 (+$62), host receives $55 commission from the Bank — which is close to even, and the host risks nothing when the opponent cannot pay.

**DECIDED 2026-09-02: no change. Closed, not deferred.** User's call — "thôi kệ đi, coi như 2 người không cần đấu giá": a 2-player table simply is not the mode Flash Auction is for, and that is an acceptable shape rather than a defect to engineer around. Nothing above is pending work; it is kept as the *reasoning* for why heads-up forcing is a weak move, so a future session recognises the measurement has already been done and does not reopen it. The one thing that did ship from this thread is the fold-button warning above, which stands on its own.

## Surrender did not end the match — 2026-09-02

User report: *"hiện ấn đầu hàng nhưng không kết thúc ván đấu"*.

**The engine was never wrong.** Probed the real state machine for both halves of the 2-player case — the current-turn player forfeiting and the non-current one — and both return `status: 'finished'`, `phase: null`, `endReason: 'elimination'`, with final ranks assigned. `playerId` was fine too: `ForfeitButton` sends an empty payload, but `socketServer.js` injects `playerId: gamePlayer.id` into every action, so the handler always had the right actor.

**The client threw the result away.** `ForfeitButton.jsx` watched for its own `bankrupt` flag and, the moment it arrived, called `disconnectSocket()` + `resetAfterGame()`. `resetAfterGame` nulls `currentGameState`, and `GameOverScreen` bails on `status !== 'finished'` — so the screen that was about to show the outcome could never render. The player was dropped straight back to the lobby with no result and no final rank: from their side, indistinguishable from the match simply not ending.

That auto-leave was written for the case it describes — a 3+ player match that **carries on without you**, where staying connected to a game you are no longer in is pointless. It was just never conditioned on that actually being true. Fixed by leaving only while the match is still running; when your own surrender ended it, stay and let `GameOverScreen` (which owns its own "Trở về sảnh") show the result.

**Second defect in the same dialog, found while fixing the first.** The confirmation read *"Trận đấu tiếp tục với những người chơi còn lại"* unconditionally — at a 2-player table that is wrong every single time, since surrendering hands the opponent an immediate elimination win. The dialog now derives `survivors` exactly the way `resolveForfeit` derives `stillStanding`, and when you are the second-to-last player it says so: title becomes "Đầu hàng và kết thúc trận đấu?" and the body states the match ends at once and the remaining player wins.

**Test gap this exposed.** The only end-the-match forfeit test had the **non-current** player forfeit — but a real player presses "Đầu Hàng" on their own turn, which takes `resolveForfeit`'s separate `wasCurrentPlayer` branch. That branch was uncovered while a bug report pointed straight at it. Added a test pinning it (survivor gets `finalRank` 1, forfeiter 2 and `bankrupt`). Suite **697 pass / 0 fail**, frontend lint + build clean.

**Worth generalising:** the engine being right and the suite being green did not mean the feature worked. Three of the last four defects in this project have been a correct server result that the client discarded, mis-gated, or mis-described — that boundary, not the state machine, is where this codebase actually breaks.

### Follow-up: the forfeit dialog rendered *behind* the board — 2026-09-02

User screenshot: the confirm dialog painting under the 3D board, text and buttons unreadable. Same failure family as the `.shell > *` modal bug, and it turns out the fix for that one is what left this case behind.

**Why.** `.shell > .topbar, .shell > .body { position: relative; z-index: 1 }` makes **both** a stacking context at the *same* level, so `.body` — later in the DOM — paints over `.topbar` unconditionally. Every other modal in the app is a **direct child of `.shell`**, which is exactly what lets its z-index mean anything. `ForfeitButton` cannot be: its trigger belongs in the topbar. So `.backdrop { z-index: 55 }` was being resolved *inside* `.topbar` and could never exceed it — no z-index value would have worked.

**Fixed with a portal to `<body>`**, not a bigger number: the board is 3D-transformed, so a z-index arms race inside that subtree is unwinnable in principle. This is the first `createPortal` in the codebase; the reasoning is recorded at the call site so it does not read as an arbitrary deviation from the direct-child-of-`.shell` convention.

**Second defect the portal itself created, caught before shipping.** The dialog and its nested `ActionNotice` read six `--et-*` custom properties, and those were declared on `.shell`. Portalling to `<body>` puts the dialog *outside* that subtree, so every one would have resolved to nothing — an unstyled transparent panel, the exact failure the undefined `--et-surface` token produced on the Card Inventory panel. Moved the palette from `.shell` to `:root`. It remains a **single definition, not a copy**, so there is nothing to drift; Login/Lobby simply never reference these names. Verified in the built CSS rather than assumed: `:root{--et-bg:…}` is emitted global and unhashed with all 11 tokens, while `.shell` still hashes to `._shell_m1y8t_9`.

**Checked for siblings, found one latent but not broken.** `EventCardHistory` is the only other component that renders a full-screen `position: fixed` backdrop while nested rather than being a direct child of `.shell`. It sits in `.statusRail` (`position: absolute; z-index: 1`), which *is* a stacking context — but the rail's z-index 1 beats the board's `auto`, so its backdrop does paint above the board today. Left alone deliberately: it works, and the user reported nothing. It is fragile though — giving `.boardWrap` any z-index ≥ 1 would break it exactly the way the forfeit dialog broke.

**The general rule this codebase keeps re-learning:** a `z-index` is only ever compared against siblings inside the *nearest stacking context*. When an overlay renders behind something it should cover, the question is never "is the number big enough" but "which stacking context is this number being resolved in".

## "Some event cards don't do their thing" — 2026-09-02

User report. Audited the whole system rather than guessing, and the engine turned out to be entirely clean — the defect was a result readout that lied.

### The engine is fine — proven, not assumed
Two throwaway harnesses drove every card through the real state machine:

1. **Draw path** — all **28** cards, each dealt from a stacked deck onto a real landing, diffing every field a card could plausibly touch (both players' cash, Bank cash, position, jail, jail cards, inventory, build/rent discounts, rent & build modifiers, protection, jackpot, ownership/levels). **Every card produced its effect. Zero dead cards.** Each card's own `eligibility` gate was satisfied first, so "no effect" could never just mean "correctly gated out".
2. **Inventory path** — the four `keepable` cards (`VE_SO_TRUNG_AN_UI`, `C07_GIAM_GIA_XAY_DUNG`, `C08_BAO_VE_TAI_SAN`, `C12_CO_HOI_CUOI`) do **not** resolve on draw; they bank into `inventory` and only act when played via `USE_INVENTORY_CARD`. Drove all four, and all three of C12's options, through that path too. **All worked.**

Also cleared along the way: every intent action the deck declares has a handler (19 distinct actions, none orphaned); `cardEligible` handles all three gate shapes in use (`lt`, `gte`, `OWNS_PROTECTABLE_PROPERTY`); `serverGeneratedFields` injects `probabilityRoll`/`dieFaceRoll` for **both** `MAKE_EVENT_CHOICE` and `USE_INVENTORY_CARD`; `GET /api/v1/event-cards` returns `EVENT_CARDS` verbatim; the old hand-maintained `frontend/.../eventCardDictionary.js` mirror (a documented drift hazard) is gone; and `CardInventory.jsx` correctly gives option-less keepable cards their own "Sử dụng thẻ" button rather than deriving buttons only from an empty `options` array.

### The actual bug: the die-face result panel read fields that do not exist
`EventCardModal.jsx` computed the card's outcome from `t.toPlayerId` / `t.fromPlayerId`. A transaction on the wire has **`toGamePlayerId` / `fromGamePlayerId`** (`economy/applyTransaction.js`) — `fromPlayerId`/`toPlayerId` is `ledgerFormat.js`'s *separate, already-mapped* entry shape, produced by `buildLedgerEntries`. Both filters therefore matched nothing, `net` was always `0`, and the panel announced **"Không nhận được thêm tiền!"** on every die-face card — including a $300 win.

That is exactly the reported symptom: the two dice cards, **C05 "Cơ Hội Đầu Tư"** and **C11 "Cú Đánh Liều"**, looked like they did nothing. The money had moved correctly the whole time; only the readout was wrong. The `.amounts` block **twelve lines below in the same file** already used the correct field names — a one-file, one-block inconsistency.

Also compared against `drawer` rather than `me`: the panel is not gated on `isMyTurn`, so a spectator was measuring the wrong player's money.

### Second defect in the same block
The panel rendered whenever the card *has* any die-face option — not whether the chosen one rolled. Picking C05's safe option (no roll) still showed it, displaying the **movement dice from earlier in the turn** as if they were the card's result. `handleEventChoice` only overwrites `lastRoll` when a roll actually happened, synthesising it as `{die1: face, die2: 0}`, while a real movement roll always has both dice in 1..6 — so the panel is now gated on `lastRoll.die2 === 0`.

### Note
Both defects are in a block whose style (inline `style={{…}}`, different formatting from the rest of the file) marks it as recent work from the parallel session — consistent with the user seeing this only now. Frontend lint + build clean; no backend change was needed.

**Fourth consecutive defect at the same boundary:** a correct server result that the client discarded, mis-gated, or mis-described. The engine being right and the suite being green keeps not being enough.

### Two things found when asked "is everything fixed?" — 2026-09-02

#### Fixed: the topbar z-index bump was overlaying six modals
While the forfeit dialog was being portalled (above), `.shell > .topbar` was independently raised from `z-index: 1` to **50** — a reasonable attempt at the same bug from the other direction, since the dialog is nested *inside* the topbar and was capped by it. But 50 lands in the middle of the modal range, so every `position: fixed` overlay below it started rendering **under the header**: TradeWindow 40, EventCardModal 45, PropertyManager 45, EventCardHistory 48, LiquidationPanel 48, PropertyActionDrawer 20, RentRiskChoice 20. In practice the header — including **"Đầu Hàng" and "Đăng xuất"** — sat on top of an open modal's backdrop and stayed clickable.

Set to **2** instead: above `.body` (which is 1 and later in the DOM — the one thing z-index 1 genuinely could not do), below every overlay. The forfeit dialog does not need the height any more, since the portal takes it out of this stacking context entirely. Verified in the built CSS (`_topbar_…{z-index:2`), not just the source.

#### NOT fixed, flagged: the auction starting-price input does nothing
`PropertyActionDrawer.jsx` now renders a **"Giá khởi điểm đấu giá"** number input above the "Mở sàn" button. The value is held in local state and never leaves the component — the button calls `act('FORCE_AUCTION')`, and `act` sends `{ propertyId }` only. The server would ignore it regardless: `handleForceAuction` derives everything from the tile, `calculateAuctionFee(tile.price)` and `startAuction(property.id, tile.price, …)`, with no custom-open-price parameter anywhere in the contract.

So a player can type a starting price, see it accepted, and have the auction open at the printed price every time. **Left alone deliberately** — this is a half-built feature from the parallel session (its own comment dates it 2026-09-02) and finishing it means a real design decision plus a backend change (`WEBSOCKET_API.md`'s `FORCE_AUCTION` payload is `{}` today), not a bug fix. It also interacts with the heads-up auction economics measured above: a custom opening price is one of the levers that discussion considered and the user closed.

## FORCE_AUCTION opening price: finished, and a crash found on the way — 2026-09-03

Asked to finish the "Giá khởi điểm đấu giá" input the parallel session had left half-built.

### A live crash in the same block
`currentAuctionFee` and `cannotAffordAuction` were **read by the JSX and never declared anywhere** — a `ReferenceError` thrown while rendering `AWAITING_PURCHASE`, i.e. every single time any player landed on an unowned buyable tile. `oxlint` did not flag it and the build does not do that analysis, so both passed clean over a component that could not render. Defined properly, alongside the opening-price band, in one place the three call sites share.

### What the feature does now
`FORCE_AUCTION` takes an optional integer `basePrice`. Omitted → opens at the printed price, exactly the old behaviour. Supplied → must be inside **`[ceil(price × 0.5), price]`**, else `INVALID_BASE_PRICE`.

Three decisions worth recording, because none of them is obvious:

- **The floor is the load-bearing one.** With no floor a host could open a $400 lot at $1. Heads-up that is only self-harm — the Broker rule bars them from bidding, so they would be gifting their opponent — but at 3+ players it is a **collusion tool**: open low, let an ally take it for pocket change. Rival bidders can compete the price back up, which dampens it, but nothing guarantees they are solvent at that moment. `MIN_AUCTION_OPEN_RATIO = 0.5` is deliberately **V0's own original fixed opening bid**, reused rather than invented, so the number has a precedent in this game rather than being a fresh guess.
- **The ceiling is cheap insurance.** Opening above the printed price cannot attract a bid the printed price would not; it only manufactures a guaranteed FAILED auction that still burns the fee — and skipping the purchase already does that for free.
- **The fee is charged on the printed price, never on the chosen opening.** The half-built version computed `calculateAuctionFee(basePrice)`, so discounting the opening quietly discounted the host's own cost too: the auction most generous to the buyer would also have been the cheapest one to run. The fee prices the *service*, which is identical whatever the host opens at. This has its own regression test.

Also fixed: `handleForceAuction`'s signature already took `action`, but the dispatch call site passed only two arguments, so `action` was permanently `undefined` and the field could never have arrived even once the client sent it.

### Coverage
Five new backend tests (in-band value honoured; discount does not touch the fee; below-floor rejected including the exact off-by-one at `floor − 1`; above-printed and non-integer rejected; omitting it still opens at the printed price). **702 pass / 0 fail.** `INVALID_BASE_PRICE` added to `actionErrors.js` — the mechanical server-vs-dictionary diff is back to **43/43, none missing**. Client clamps to the same band and sends the clamped value, so a half-typed number can never become a rejected action; clamping happens on blur, not per keystroke, because clamping as you type makes the field impossible to edit. `WEBSOCKET_API.md` (payload + error taxonomy) and `BOARD_SPECIFICATION.md` updated.

This is the lever the heads-up measurement identified — at 2 players 88% of forced auctions failed because the sole eligible bidder could not afford `printed + $10` — now available to players rather than as a global rule change, which is what the user had closed.

## "No way to use a kept card" — 2026-09-03

User could not find any path to play a `keepable` card. Checked the whole chain before changing anything; **no hard functional break exists in the play path**:

- `USE_INVENTORY_CARD` is accepted by the server in **all 9 phases, on anyone's turn** (probed each combination directly) — it is in `TURN_INDEPENDENT_ACTION_TYPES` and never phase-gated, so nothing server-side ever refuses it.
- All four keepable cards, and all three of C12's options, resolve correctly through it (audited the day before).
- `CardInventory` is mounted in GameView's right rail — the "THẺ SỰ KIỆN" panel visible in the user's own screenshot — expanded by default, and builds correct payloads (`optionId` for CHOICE cards, `propertyId` for the protection card, plain `{cardId}` for the option-less ones).
- `GET /api/v1/event-cards` is wired behind `authMiddleware` in `app.js` and returns `EVENT_CARDS` verbatim.

So the defect is not "the action fails" — it is that the path is invisible, and that one failure mode removes it silently.

### 1. The hand had no affordance
Each card rendered as an icon + text row, visually identical to the read-only chips in the panels stacked above it (`MyPortfolio`, and the `Ledger` below). Nothing indicated a row was clickable, and the play controls appeared **only after** clicking — in a detail block further down a rail that is itself `overflow-y: auto`. A player scanning that column has no reason to think the list does anything. Added a `Dùng ▸` cue pinned to the right edge of each row (flipping to `▾` when open) and a one-line "Chạm vào một thẻ để dùng nó." above the hand.

### 2. A failed dictionary fetch killed the panel silently
`getEventCards`'s handler was `.catch(() => {})`, annotated "real card text stays unavailable; the id-based fallback below still renders". The hand does still render — `c?.text ?? cardId` — but `openCard` is looked up as `cardsData[openCardId]`, so with an empty map **clicking a card opens nothing at all**. The result is precisely the reported symptom: a visible list of cards that cannot be used, with no error, no retry, and nothing on screen suggesting anything had gone wrong. A single transient 401 or network blip on mount was enough, permanently, for the life of the component.

Now tracked as `loadFailed`: the panel says so plainly and offers a **Thử lại** button (a `reloadNonce` in the effect's dependency array re-runs the fetch). The effect also got a `cancelled` guard so a late response cannot write into an unmounted component.

### Note on confidence
Neither fix is verifiable from here — reaching a live inventory needs a real multiplayer session with Supabase auth, which this project's own rules keep off the dev database. The chain above was verified by probing the engine directly and by reading every link; what could not be verified is which of the two the user actually hit. Both are real defects either way.

## The game now proposes a card when the moment calls for it — 2026-09-03

User request: *"hãy có phép hệ thống tự đề xuất cho người chơi nếu vào tình huống cần dùng thẻ, ví dụ như khi vào tù mà đang có thẻ ra tù"*.

### Why the jail example is worse than it looks
The deck has **two different jail mechanisms**, and only one of them was reachable:
- `C09_LUAT_SU` is *not* keepable — it increments `jailFreeCards` the instant it is drawn, and GameControls' jail row already has a button bound to that counter.
- `VE_SO_TRUNG_AN_UI` **is** keepable. Holding it does **not** give you a jail card. You must play it out of the inventory first, which grants the counter, and only then can you spend it to leave. Two steps, in two different panels, with nothing linking them.

So a jailed player could be holding their own way out and have no way to find that out. That is the gap this closes.

### New `cardSuggestions.js`
A pure function returning which held cards are worth playing *right now*, most pressing first. Matching is on the card's own **intents**, walked through options, probability branches and die-face tables — not on card ids. Ids would work for today's four keepable cards and then silently stop covering the fifth; that is the exact drift the deleted `eventCardDictionary.js` mirror was removed for.

Rules, each firing only where the effect is **actionable now** rather than merely relevant someday:

| Situation | Card | Urgency |
|---|---|---|
| In jail with **zero** jail cards | grants `GRANT_JAIL_CARD` | urgent |
| You are the debtor in `LIQUIDATION_REQUIRED` | has an `ADD_MONEY` outcome | urgent |
| A hostile buyout is pending **against your own unimproved lot** | grants `GRANT_PROPERTY_PROTECTION` | urgent |
| You are being offered a build and have no discount active | grants `GRANT_NEXT_BUILD_DISCOUNT` | hint |

The protection rule is worth noting: it fires on **someone else's turn**, which is legal precisely because `USE_INVENTORY_CARD` is turn-independent (`TURN_INDEPENDENT_ACTION_TYPES`) — a defence the player had no way of knowing was available.

Protection is deliberately *not* suggested merely for owning something unimproved, and the build hint is deliberately quiet: this panel is on screen every single turn, so a nag there costs more than a missed discount.

### Surfacing
`CardInventory` shows the prompt with a **"Mở thẻ này"** button that opens the card directly, so the play controls are one click from the suggestion. An urgent suggestion also **forces the panel open** — recorded per card id, so collapsing it dismisses that one suggestion without suppressing the next, genuinely different one, and the collapse control never appears broken by springing back open. Suggested cards carry a ★ in the hand so the prompt and the row it refers to are visibly the same thing.

### Verified
All ten scenarios driven against the real `EVENT_CARDS`, including **five negative cases** — already holding a jail card, not in jail, someone *else* liquidating, a buyout aimed at a lot that is not yours, a build discount already active. None of them produces a suggestion.

### Known limit
The prompt lives in the Card Inventory panel in the right rail, **not inside the jail dialog itself**. Putting it there needs the card dictionary in the store — `EventCardModal` and `CardInventory` currently fetch `/api/v1/event-cards` independently, so GameControls would be a third copy. Worth doing as a small cleanup, not smuggled into this change.

## Kept cards are now genuinely usable — 2026-09-03

User asked twice, and the honest answer to the first ask was "no". A `keepable` card did nothing on draw, the play path was a side-rail panel with no affordance, and the sharpest case — sitting in jail holding your own way out — made the player leave the jail dialog, find a different panel, play the card there, and come back. This closes that.

### The card dictionary now lives in the store
`GET /api/v1/event-cards` was fetched **independently by three components** (EventCardModal, CardInventory, EventCardHistory) and GameControls would have been a fourth. Each was its own failure point with its own `.catch(() => {})`. Moved to `gameStore.eventCards`, fetched once per session in `socketClient.js` by `ensureEventCardsLoaded()` — the exact pattern `ensureStaticBoardLoaded()` already uses, including the free retry: it runs on every `S2C_STATE_UPDATE`, guards on "map is non-empty", so a failed fetch simply succeeds on the next broadcast. All four components now read one shared copy. The retry machinery I had added to CardInventory the day before (`loadFailed` / `reloadNonce` / a manual "Thử lại" button) is gone — the store handles it.

### The jail dialog now offers the card directly
